{"id": "d9c00d11e044517b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:18:13.110254+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C45 removes every graph-rank and memory restriction from the positive-flow obstruction under two explicit aggregate correlation signs. For arbitrary real nonnegative a_ij, if adjacent gradients are nonpositively correlated and each next gradient is nonpositively correlated with the whole strictly older weighted gradient sum, then m>=512,N>=16 gives score_free/K<7(3/4)^(N/2)<7/9 and limsup B_n score_free<=14/(9e)<1. All pairwise nonpositive gradients are included; some positive remote pairs are also allowed when they cancel in the sum. Every old/future normal, true distance, primitive defect and free endpoint is retained. Unverified candidate, not unrestricted W.", "evidence": "# C45. Arbitrary positive forward incidence under an aggregate correlation sign\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier, numerical solver or sweep.\nAll ingredients are proved here; no earlier candidate is a correctness premise.\n\nConsider the near-critical ruler and arbitrary nonnegative forward transfers,\nwith ANY number of parents, ANY memory length, and NO factorization. Assume\nthat adjacent phase gradients have nonpositive inner products and that each\nnext gradient has nonpositive inner product with the weighted sum of all\nstrictly older gradients, as written explicitly below. In particular this\nholds when EVERY pair of distinct phase gradients has nonpositive inner product.\n\nFor m>=512 and N>=16 phases, every legal such flow and every full terminal\ncompletion satisfy\n\n    score_free/K<7(3/4)^(N/2)<7/9.\n\nTherefore limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/(9e)<1, and this limit superior\nis zero if N grows. These sign conditions are essential hypotheses of this\ncandidate; no unrestricted objective upper bound is asserted.\n\n## 1. Real geometry, all normal tests, and the exact sign hypothesis\n\nSet lambda=1+sqrt(F2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Fix 0<=r<=m-4, let\nk=m-r, N=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M, A=1+q, and put\n\n q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nThe symbol nu_2 denotes binary valuation. The N ordinary phases are exactly\nthe physical H_r copies, with mass M-1; each nonfinal phase is followed by\nthe actual checkpoint1+Mq_i.\n\nChoose arbitrary transfers a_ij>=0 for i<j and masses\n\n    R_0>0, R_j=sum_(i<j)a_ij>0 for j>=1, E_i=R_i^2.\n\nIn the real space R^N with standard orthonormal basis define\n\n p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nIts gradient is Euclidean projection onto C, by the projection variational\ninequality and maximizing-difference inequalities. That projection is\nnonexpansive; thus F_C is convex and1-smooth.\n\nFor i>=0 and j>i put r_j^(i)=sum_(l<i)a_lj, an actual inherited coordinate\nmass. The exact phase state is\n\n    X_i=R_i e_i+sum_(j>i)r_j^(i)e_j,\n\nwhere the root sum vanishes. Subtracting T_i p_i exhausts coordinate i and\ncreates X_(i+1); every individual physical step is retained.\n\nDefine\n\n V_i=E_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij r_j^(i),\n C_i=sum_(j>i)a_ij[r_j^(i)+a_ij],\n Delta_ij=a_ij R_j-sum_(l>j)a_il a_jl, i<j.               (F1)\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\n    Delta_ij=-T_i T_j <p_i,p_j>.                         (F2)\n\nFor the complete normal test put z=(ordinary partial mass+1)/M in[1/M,1].\nMultiplying all normal values by M, the own value is\n\n    [(tau_i-z)V_i-z C_i]/tau_i^2,                        (F3)\n\nand a future-j value is\n\n    [sum_(l<i)Delta_lj+(z/tau_i)Delta_ij]/tau_j.           (F4)\n\nPast normal values are nonpositive since their positive coordinates have\nbeen exhausted and all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nComparing(F3) with zero and EVERY(F4) at BOTH z=1/M and z=1 is necessary\nand sufficient for all actual queries. All differences are affine. At the\nlast phase t>=1 suffices because there is no outgoing transfer or future\nnormal. This keeps the full old-query and future-query test explicit.\n\nEvery legal nonterminal phase has\n\n    q_i V_i>=C_i>=0.                                    (F5)\n\nIn fact V_i>0: if C_i>0 use(F5); if C_i=0 all outgoing transfers vanish\nand V_i=E_i>0. At the terminal phase V_(N-1)=E_(N-1)>0.\n\nThe additional sign hypothesis, for i=1,...,N-2, is\n\n F_i:=sum_(l<i)Delta_(l,i+1)>=0,\n G_i:=Delta_(i,i+1)>=0.                                 (F6)\n\nBy(F2), this means <p_i,p_(i+1)><=0 and\n<sum_(l<i)T_l p_l,p_(i+1)><=0. Thus it is IMPLIED by all pairwise\nnonpositive gradient correlations, but allows some positive older pair\ncorrelations if the entire indicated sum still has the stated sign.\nNo assumption that (F6) follows from arbitrary positive transfers is made.\n\nThe class is nonempty. For example take only root-to-later transfers\na_0j=R_j and no outgoing transfers from j>=1. Set E_(N-1)=1, then choose\nE_i backward large enough that\nq_i E_i/tau_i^2>=max_(j>i) E_j/tau_j. Finally choose\nqE_0>=sum_(j>=1)E_j+A max_(j>=1)E_j/tau_j.\nThese give every endpoint normal above, and their correlations obey(F6).\n\nEvery original queried value is <x,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The polar inequalities\n<p_i,z><=0 imply backward that every coordinate of z is nonpositive.\nConsequently0 is the actual closest minimizer to R_0e_0, with distance R_0.\nWriting htot=sum H_m, the original objective and primitive defect are\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2t^2E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (F7)\n\nHere V without a subscript is the project primitive defect; V_i in(F1)\nis the phase energy numerator. Formula(F7) follows from the terminal state\nand the exact identity\n2hF+h(1-h)||g||^2=||x||^2-||x-hg||^2 along the original trajectory.\n\n## 2. The boundary transfer uses only the two aggregate signs\n\nSet Z_i=V_i/tau_i>0. At the final phase Z_(N-1)=E_(N-1)/t.\nBy direct summation in(F1),\n\n    F_i+G_i=V_(i+1).                                    (F8)\n\nThe current squared-transfer cost also satisfies\n\n C_i>=a_(i,i+1)R_(i+1)>=Delta_(i,i+1)=G_i,                (F9)\n\nbecause R_(i+1)=r_(i+1)^(i)+a_(i,i+1) and all products omitted from\nC_i are nonnegative; the second inequality discards the shared-tail sum\nsubtracted in Delta.\n\nThe endpoint future-i+1 comparison(F3)-(F4), together with(F9), gives\n\n q_i V_i>=C_i+tau_i[tau_i F_i+G_i]/tau_(i+1)\n >=[tau_i^2 F_i+(tau_i+tau_(i+1))G_i]/tau_(i+1).\n\nUsing(F6),(F8), this implies\n\n Z_(i+1)/Z_i\n <=q_i tau_i(F_i+G_i)/\n      [tau_i^2 F_i+(tau_i+tau_(i+1))G_i]\n <=max(q_i/tau_i, q_i tau_i/(tau_i+tau_(i+1))).           (F10)\n\nThe denominator is positive since F_i+G_i=V_(i+1)>0. The last ratio\nis a positive denominator-weighted average of its endpoint ratios.\n\nAt the root, V_1=Delta_01>0, while\nC_0=sum_(j>0)a_0j^2>=R_1^2>=Delta_01.\nIts endpoint comparison with phase1 therefore gives\n\n    Z_1/E_0<=q/(A+tau_1).                                (F11)\n\nNo factorization, finite memory, or scalar common direction was used.\nThe only sign step is the weighted-ratio bound under(F6).\n## 3. Multiply the exact ruler factors\n\nThe mass recursion gives, with b=a/(c-2) in(0,1),\n\n    M_j=1+sum H_j=b c^j+(1-b)2^j.\n\nConsequently M_m<=Mc^k and a<=q<c-2. For m>=512 use\n\n 2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16,\n cq>=1-1/m>=99/100,\n qc<=c(c-2)<1+8/m<=65/64.                                (10)\n\nFor example lambda<169/70 and a>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5\ngive the first lower bound. The expansion\nc(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2 gives the last one.\n\nAt a LOW phase tau_i=A and tau_(i+1)>=1. Since A q<1,\n\n    A^2<A+tau_(i+1),\n\nso the first alternative in(F10) is larger and the factor is at most q/A.\n\nAt a HIGH phase tau_i=B=1+Q with Q>=99/100, its next phase is low,\ntau_(i+1)=A. The bounds imply BQ>A, so B^2>B+A and the second\nalternative in(F10) is larger. The factor is at most QB/(B+A).\n\nWrite B_1=1+cq=tau_1 (not the benchmark with the same letter).\nThere are N/2-1 high phases, with levels summing to N-k-1, and the\nsame number of low phases after the root. Thus (F10)-(F11) give\n\n Z_(N-1)/E_0\n <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)]\n               *product_(high i) tau_i/(tau_i+A)\n <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)].                  (11)\n\nThe count follows directly from 2^(k-ell-1) high checkpoints at level\nell=1,...,k-1. No asymptotic replacement of the physical word is made.\n\n## 4. Full terminal completion and the actual distance\n\nLet f be ANY convex1-smooth completion retaining all queried jets and\nthe optimum(0,0,0), including the maximal completion in accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf r>=1, every p_i is actually queried. Interpolation to any new\nminimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0, since the retained value is\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The triangular inequalities force its coordinates\nnonpositive, so0 remains the true closest minimizer at distance R_0.\nAdditional orthogonal dimensions cannot lower the distance.\n\nThe last physical step is h_last=1+a. Its actual retained query gives\n\n 2f(x_n)/E_0\n <=[2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a yields 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nPut K=1/(2M_m-1). Use M_m<=Mc^k and(11):\n\n score_free/K\n <[4A/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <2(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (12)\n\nThe last inequality uses B_1>A.\n\nIf r=0, the old terminal gradient is UNQUERIED. Use instead the\nLipschitz-gradient lower bound\n\n    dist(x_0,argmin f)^2>=||p_0||^2>=E_0/A^2.             (13)\n\nAt the final query i=N-2, put\nv=r_(i+1)^(i)/R_(i+1) in[0,1]. Its position is\nR_i e_i+v R_(i+1)e_(i+1), and its gradient is\n[R_i e_i-(1-v)R_(i+1)e_(i+1)]/A. One smoothness step of length A gives\n\n 2f(x_n)<=(1-A^(-2))E_i\n +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(i+1)\n <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.                                       (14)\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive for A<23/16.\n\nWrite b=a_(i,i+1) and d=r_(i+1)^(i). The final low phase's\nown zero comparison says qE_i>=A b d+b^2. Since Z_i=(E_i-bd)/A,\nit follows that\n\n    Z_i>=E_i/A^2.                                       (15)\n\nIndeed bd<=qE_i/A, and A-q=1 then proves the displayed bound.\n\nRepeat the product(F10)-(F11) but omit the final low factor q/A. It yields\n\n Z_(N-2)/E_0\n <=q^(N-2)c^(N-k-1)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-2)].\n\nCombine(13)-(15), multiply by K^(-1)<2c^k, and use A^2-1=q(2+q):\n\n score_free/K\n <[2(2+q)A^4/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <7(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (16)\n\nFor the numerical rational constant, A+B_1>3 and\n2(39/16)(23/16)^4/3=21827598/3145728<7.\n\nNo unqueried normal has been used to constrain the new minimizer.\nThe terminal bound follows only from retained queries and their actual\nphysical last displacement.\n\n## 5. Uniform payoff and the original exact benchmark\n\nSet z0=65/64>1. Then (qc)^(N-1)<=z0^N even when qc<1.\nAlso\n\n    z0^2/A<(65/64)^2/(7/5)=21125/28672<3/4.\n\nEquations(12),(16) prove\n\n    score_free/K<7(3/4)^(N/2).\n\nFor N>=16, (3/4)^4=81/256<1/3 gives score_free/K<7/9.\nIf N grows, the ratio tends to zero uniformly in all nonnegative transfers obeying(F6), energies, r, terminal margin and full completion.\n\nFor the exact benchmark use accepted b98bb807d514e80d and write\nrho_s=log_2(lambda). Put F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1. The second derivative of\nlog F(exp w) equals\n\n rho_s*z*(1+z)^(rho_s-2)*\n [(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s*z]/F(z)^2>0.\n\nThus F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2 by midpoint convexity in w. Comparing the\npositive root of t^2-(X+Y)t-XY with\n(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s proves\n\n    Omega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\n\nInduction in the accepted primitive recursion gives\n1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s. Since the accepted dominant join\nGamma(X,D)=2X+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8XD)/2 is at most4X+D,\nits recursion then gives B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\nThe displayed exact mass formula gives M_m/lambda^m->exp(1), hence\n\n    limsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1).\n\nWe conclude\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/[9 exp(1)]<1,\n\nwith limit superior zero whenever N tends to infinity.\n\n## 6. Scope and the sign needed to escape\n\nThis excludes every legal nonnegative forward-incidence construction\nsatisfying(F6), regardless of memory length, number of parents, graph size,\nor factorization rank. It includes all pairwise nonpositive-gradient\nfamilies, but also permits positive older pairs that cancel in the\nspecified weighted sum. All original phases and full terminal completion\nare covered.\n\nAn escaping positive-incidence witness must violate at least one of these\ntwo aggregate signs somewhere: it needs either a positive adjacent\ngradient correlation or a positive correlation of the next gradient with\nthe entire strictly older weighted gradient sum. A positive remote pair\nalone is insufficient if the aggregate signs still hold.\n\nThis is a necessary condition within the displayed forward-incidence\ngeometry, not a construction attaining the benchmark. Arbitrary flows\nviolating(F6), signed transfers, and reactivation of old coordinates remain\nopen. It is not an upper bound on unrestricted W or a proof of the original\nexact-profile theorem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["61d9c9f5d216500d", "19f9dda7a15723b3", "8e8cc2d8c1739221"]}, "glossary": {"a_ij": "Arbitrary nonnegative mass sent from phase i to coordinate j, with j>i.", "R_j": "Total incoming mass sum_(i<j)a_ij for j>=1; R_0 is the independent initial mass.", "r_j^(i)": "Inherited coordinate-j mass sum_(l<i)a_lj before phase i.", "Delta_ij": "a_ij R_j-sum_(k>j)a_ik a_jk=-T_i T_j<p_i,p_j>.", "V_i": "R_i^2-sum_(j>i)a_ij r_j^(i), distinct from the unindexed primitive defect V.", "C_i": "sum_(j>i)a_ij(r_j^(i)+a_ij), the full squared-transfer cost.", "F_i": "sum_(l<i)Delta_(l,i+1), whose nonnegativity is the old aggregate correlation condition.", "G_i": "Delta_(i,i+1), whose nonnegativity is the adjacent correlation condition.", "Z_i": "Positive boundary energy V_i/tau_i.", "K": "Canonical Huber score 1/(2M_m-1), where M_m=1+sum H_m.", "score_free": "Actual normalized objective 2f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 of any convex1-smooth completion retaining every query and the optimum."}}
{"id": "f07e30eaf6cc2f95", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:22:26.451736+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Uniform whole-family obstruction for arbitrary horizon-dependent unequal quadratic rays on the ACTUAL near-critical ruler: every scalar f(x)=u x^2/2 for x>=0, v x^2/2 for x<=0, 0<=u,v<=1, has exact normalized objective <=E_m<(3/4)K and limsup B_(2^m-1) E_m<=2/e^2<1. Finite orthogonal direct sums also obey the bound. The new proof uses exact sign-reset/swap dynamics; it does not cover spatially varying curvatures or unrestricted W. Candidate only.", "evidence": "# Unequal quadratic rays on the actual critical ruler\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No solver, trajectory sweep or\nfact_submit is allocated. All new claims are proved together here. The only\naccepted input is the benchmark recurrence in b98bb807d514e80d. This result\ndoes not bound the unrestricted worst-case objective or all scalar functions.\n\n## 1. Whole-family statement\n\nFor an integer m>=128 keep the following parameters fixed during the run:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    r=c-2, d_j=1+a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1,\n    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=S_j+1, K=1/(2S_m+1).\n\nThe H_j here are schedules, not the conjugate quadratics in the project\nglossary. For ANY two curvatures u,v in[0,1], define one fixed derivative\nand objective by\n\n    g(x)=u x for x>=0, g(x)=v x for x<=0,\n    f(x)=u x^2/2 for x>=0, f(x)=v x^2/2 for x<=0.       (1)\n\nThe derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz; f is convex,\ndifferentiable and1-smooth, with minimizer0. For positive curvatures the\nminimizer is unique. Both curvatures may depend arbitrarily on m. For every\nnonzero starting point, exact GD with H_m satisfies\n\n  2f(x_n)/x_0^2 <= E_m := max{\n       1/(2e S_m),\n       d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),\n       r^2/(2e S_(m-1))\n  } < (3/4)K.                                         (2)\n\nFor the exact benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1,\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) E_m <= 2/e^2 <1.     (3)\n\nThe same upper bound holds for arbitrary finite orthogonal direct sums\nof (1), with positive coordinate curvatures, translations of their unique\nminimizer and arbitrary orthogonal changes of basis. Thus independent\none-sided quadratic curvatures, including horizon-dependent unequal cores\nthat extend over the entire rays, cannot supply the target witness here.\n\n## 2. Parameter estimates and the scalar product\n\nDirectly from the ruler recurrence,\n\n    M_j=(a/r)c^j+(1-a/r)2^j.                           (4)\n\nFor m>=128 the following deliberately loose rational bounds hold:\n\n    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,\n    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,\n    ac=1-1/m>7/8, 0<a<r<1/2, cr<5/4.                 (5)\n\nFor example lambda<17/7 gives c<2193/896<49/20.\nThen a>(127/128)/(49/20)>3/8; also\na=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2<r.\nEquation(4) implies\n\n    S_j >= a c^j/r-1,\n    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j.                         (6)\n\nLet\n\n    P_j(t)=product_(l=0)^(j-1)(1-d_l t)^(2^(j-l-1)),\n    P_0(t)=1.                                        (7)\n\nBefore the first level crossing for curvature t all these factors are\npositive and\n\n    0<P_j(t)<=exp(-S_j t).                            (8)\n\nWe first prove (2) when u>=v>0 and neither d_j u nor d_j v equals1.\nReflection deals with v>u. Positive homogeneity reduces initial points\nto+1 and-1. Finally, finite compositions of the continuous maps\nx -> x-d_j g(x), and the objective itself, are continuous in u,v.\nThis extends the proved inequalities to zero factors and zero curvatures.\n\nDefine p and q as the first nonnegative levels with d_p u>1 and d_q v>1,\nrespectively. They exist because a>0,c>1,u,v>0, and p<=q.\n\n## 3. Same crossing level: two swapping maps\n\nSuppose p=q<m. H_p preserves both signs, and H_(p+1) swaps them.\nWrite A_j for the magnitude at the end of H_j starting at+1, and B_j\nfor that starting at-1. At j=p+1,\n\n    A_(p+1)=(d_p u-1)P_p(u)P_p(v),\n    B_(p+1)=(d_p v-1)P_p(u)P_p(v).                    (9)\n\nAll subsequent H_j also swap signs: the three maps H_j, the central\nd_j step and H_j each swap. Consequently, for j>=p+1,\n\n    A_(j+1)=(d_j v-1)A_j^2,\n    B_(j+1)=(d_j u-1)B_j^2.                          (10)\n\nFor either of these recurrences, let t be the output-ray curvature\n(v for A, u for B), let Z_j be its magnitude, and put\n\n    Y_j=c a c^j t Z_j.\n\nAn exact substitution in(10) gives\n\n    Y_(j+1)=[1-(1-t)/(a c^j t)]Y_j^2 <= Y_j^2.        (11)\n\nThe bracket lies in[0,1] because all these levels cross and t<=1.\n\nIt remains to control the actual entry value, including both curvatures.\nIf p=0, (9) gives\n\n    c a c v A_1=c^2 a v(d_0 u-1)<=(ac)^2 v<=1,\n\nand the other starting sign has the symmetric bound.\nIf p>=1, then u,v<1/d_(p-1)<=1/d_0<8/11. Put\nU=a c^p u and V=a c^p v. From(6),(8),(9),\n\n    c a c^(p+1) v A_(p+1)\n       <=c^2 U V exp(-(U+V)/r+u+v)\n       <=(cr)^2 exp(u+v-2)<1.                        (12)\n\nThe middle inequality maximizes z exp(-z/r) at z=r. The last uses\ncr<5/4 and exp(6/11)>1+6/11+18/121=205/121>25/16.\nThe bound for B is identical with u,v exchanged. Iterating(11) proves\nY_m<=1 for both initial signs. Since the terminal curvature t crosses\nat a level<=m-1, t>1/d_(m-1), and hence\n\n    2f(x_n)=t Z_m^2\n       <=1/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t)\n       <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)).                  (13)\n\n## 4. Different crossing levels: an exact sign reset\n\nSuppose p<q and p<m. The level-p step maps the high-curvature ray to\nthe low-curvature ray and preserves the low ray. Thus H_(p+1) maps\nBOTH signs to the low ray. Every subsequent H_j still has this\nconstant sign map, because the final H_j in H_(j+1) returns to that\nray regardless of what the central step does.\n\nFor p+1<=j<=q, low-ray initial data have not yet crossed, so\n\n    B_j=P_j(v),\n    A_j=D_p P_j(v)/[P_p(v)(1-d_p v)],\n    D_p=(d_p u-1)P_p(u).                             (14)\n\nOne can also obtain this identity by splitting the actual run into its\ninitial high-ray H_p and d_p step, followed by low-ray steps of total\nmass S_j-S_p-d_p. No queries or spatial pieces are chosen independently.\n\nFor j>=q the central step crosses from low to high, after which H_j\nreturns to low. The exact magnitude recurrences are\n\n    A_(j+1)=(d_j v-1)A_j^2,\n    B_(j+1)=(d_j v-1)A_j B_j.                        (15)\n\nIn particular, with Z_j=max(A_j,B_j),\nZ_(j+1)<=(d_j v-1)Z_j^2. Therefore the scaled Y_j=c a c^j v Z_j\nagain satisfies(11), with <= in place of its first equality.\n\nAssume first q<m. We prove the needed entry Y_q<=1.\nFor B_q=P_q(v), q>=1 and v<1/d_(q-1)<=1/d_0<8/11. With\nV=a c^q v, (6),(8) give\n\n    c V B_q <= c V exp(-V/r+v)\n               <=cr exp(v-1)<1,                    (16)\n\nwhere exp(3/11)>1+3/11>5/4 was used.\n\nFor A_q observe that S_q-S_p-d_p>=S_(q-1), because p<=q-1 and\nS_p+d_p<=S_(q-1)+d_(q-1). Equation(14) therefore gives\n\n    A_q<=D_p exp(-v S_(q-1)).                        (17)\n\nIf p>=1, setting U=a c^p u and using(6),(8) yields\n\n    D_p<=U exp(-U/r+u)<=r exp(u-1).\n\nSince u,v<8/11, (17) implies\n\n    c V A_q<=c r exp(u-1)V exp(-V/(cr)+v)\n                <=(cr)^2 exp(u+v-2)<1.              (18)\n\nIf p=0,q=1, the entry is exact:\nA_1=d_0u-1<=a, and c a c v A_1<=(ac)^2v<=1.\nIf p=0,q>=2, use D_0<=a and v<1/d_0<8/11 in(17) to get\n\n    c V A_q<=a c^2 r exp(v-1)\n                <=cr exp(v-1)<1,                    (19)\n\nbecause ac<1. Equations(16),(18),(19) cover every entry regime. Now\n(15),(11) give Y_m<=1. The terminal ray is low, whose curvature\nv>1/d_(m-1), so (13) applies to BOTH starting signs.\n\nAssume instead q>=m. Starting on the low ray never crosses and gives\nv P_m(v)^2<=1/(2e S_m) by(8). Starting on the high ray crosses once\nat level p, after which every actual step preserves the low ray. The\nexact decomposition underlying(14) and the proved bound D_p<=r give\n\n    A_m<=r exp[-v(S_m-S_p-d_p)]\n         <=r exp(-v S_(m-1)).\n\nConsequently\n\n    2f(x_n)=v A_m^2<=r^2/(2e S_(m-1)).               (20)\n\nFinally, if p>=m neither ray crosses: (8) gives the first term in(2)\nfor each initial sign. This completes all generic cases, and continuity\nas explained above completes (2)'s non-strict upper bound.\n\n## 5. Uniform finite and benchmark constants\n\nEach of the three entries in E_m is less than(3/4)K.\nFor the first, S_m>=1 and e>2 give\n\n    (2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4.\n\nFor the second, M_m<=c^m and ac>7/8 imply\n\n (2S_m+1)d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))\n  <= 2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]\n  <= 16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                      (21)\n\nHere c>2,m>=3 bound the first summand, and c>12/5 bounds the second.\nFor the third, put S=S_(m-1)>=1. Since S_m<=c(S+1)-1, c<5/2,r<1/2,\n\n    (2S_m+1)r^2/(2e S)\n       <=r^2(4c-1)/(2e)<9/(8e)<3/4.                 (22)\n\nFor completeness, derive the benchmark bound directly from the accepted\nrecurrence rather than use another unverified candidate as a premise.\nWrite rho=log_2(lambda), C_N=S_circle(N)+1. Its mass join is\nOmega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2. The dominant join is\nG(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nFor t>0 set F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. With t=exp(z), differentiation gives\n\n (d^2/dz^2)log F(exp(z))\n =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive because rho>1. Convexity at z,-z therefore\ngives F(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and D=(1+t)^rho B, this\nsays (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. It follows that\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho. Induction in the accepted\nprimitive recurrence yields C_N<=(N+1)^rho. Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,\ninduction in the accepted dominant recurrence gives\n\n    B_N<=1+4N^rho.                                  (23)\n\nAt a split N=k+l+1 the induction uses\n(k+1)^rho+l^rho<=(k+l+1)^rho; this holds for nonnegative arguments\nbecause rho>1. The base N=0 is B_0=1.\n\nEquation(4) and the actual horizon-dependent parameters give\n\n    c^m/lambda^m -> e,\n    S_m/c^m ->1, S_(m-1)/c^(m-1)->1,\n    a->1/lambda, r->1/lambda.\n\nMultiplying the three entries in E_m by the upper bound(23), at\nN=2^m-1, gives limits at most, respectively,\n\n    2/e^2, 4/(e lambda^2), 2/(e^2 lambda).           (24)\n\nThe first is the largest: lambda>1 and 2e<lambda^2. One elementary\ncheck is e<49/18<11/4 while lambda^2>29/5>11/2. The e bound follows\nby bounding the factorial tail from3 onward by a geometric series\nwith first term1/6 and ratio1/4. This proves(3), uniformly over all\nhorizon-dependent choices of curvatures and starting signs.\n\nFor finite orthogonal direct sums, the GD recursions separate by\ncoordinate and the normalized terminal objective is a weighted average\nof coordinate normalized objectives, with weights proportional to their\nsquared initial displacements. This proves the stated extension.\n\n## 6. Scope and audit\n\nThe crucial new mechanism is the exact constant-sign map when the two\ncurvatures have different crossing levels. It is not an assertion that\nall scalar maps are controlled by their largest secant. Spatially varying\nderivatives can give amplitude-dependent crossing levels, so their\nrecursive maps need not be sign-constant and equations(14)-(15) fail.\n\nNo saturated initial jet, equal-ray curvature, fixed-m-independent\ncurvature or small-core-curvature assumption was made. Zero factors\nand zero curvatures are included by an actual continuity argument.\nThe objective in every case is the complete area in(1), with true\ninitial distance to the unique minimizer for u,v>0. This is a precisely\nscoped obstruction, not the original all-schedule theorem.\n\nThe literature check used Matlas for the sign-switching model; its hits\nwere not applicable. The exact local copy of Altschuler--Parrilo,\narXiv:2309.16530, Section2 and the opening of Section3, was read for\nthe ruler recursion and the importance of full trajectory consistency.\nNo external theorem is a premise of this proof; the altered ruler's\nmass formula and all estimates were rederived above.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["b3f126ee134c432a", "e08ee0674ce368d3", "63a60cbd357b04c2"], "local_artifact": "research/max_unequal_ray_quadratic_closure.md"}, "glossary": {"u": "One ray's quadratic curvature in this candidate, in[0,1].", "v": "The other ray's quadratic curvature in this candidate, in[0,1].", "p_flip": "First ruler level whose step times the higher ray curvature exceeds1.", "q_flip": "First ruler level whose step times the lower ray curvature exceeds1.", "E_m": "Maximum of the three explicit objective upper bounds in equation(2) of this candidate.", "r_gap": "The positive gap c-2, written r locally in the proof.", "A_j_ray": "Output magnitude of H_j from the high-curvature initial ray, written A_j locally.", "B_j_ray": "Output magnitude of H_j from the low-curvature initial ray, written B_j locally."}}
{"id": "88b640ae31a56475", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:22:26.952623+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C45 stronger scope: arbitrary nonnegative forward flows need NO adjacent-correlation sign and NO low-phase sign. It suffices that at each HIGH phase i the next low gradient p_(i+1) has nonpositive correlation with sum_(l<i)T_l p_l. Under only this condition the same complete free-terminal bound score/K<7(3/4)^(N/2) holds (m>=512,N>=16). Thus an escaping positive-flow witness must have <X_i,p_(i+1)><0 before some high phase, using an older shared-coordinate residual; positive adjacent correlations alone cannot escape. Full standalone proof supplied, still unverified.", "evidence": "# C45, enlarged scope. Only high-phase older-history signs are needed\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier, numerical solver or sweep.\nAll ingredients are proved here; no earlier candidate is a correctness premise.\n\nConsider the near-critical ruler and arbitrary nonnegative forward transfers,\nwith ANY number of parents, ANY memory length, and NO factorization. Assume ONLY that after each HIGH phase, the next gradient has nonpositive\ninner product with the weighted sum of all strictly older phase gradients,\nas written explicitly below. Every adjacent gradient correlation is free,\nand every low-phase correlation is free. In particular the hypothesis holds\nwhen EVERY pair of distinct phase gradients has nonpositive inner product.\n\nFor m>=512 and N>=16 phases, every legal such flow and every full terminal\ncompletion satisfy\n\n    score_free/K<7(3/4)^(N/2)<7/9.\n\nTherefore limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/(9e)<1, and this limit superior\nis zero if N grows. These sign conditions are essential hypotheses of this\ncandidate; no unrestricted objective upper bound is asserted.\n\n## 1. Real geometry, all normal tests, and the exact sign hypothesis\n\nSet lambda=1+sqrt(F2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Fix 0<=r<=m-4, let\nk=m-r, N=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M, A=1+q, and put\n\n q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nThe symbol nu_2 denotes binary valuation. The N ordinary phases are exactly\nthe physical H_r copies, with mass M-1; each nonfinal phase is followed by\nthe actual checkpoint1+Mq_i.\n\nChoose arbitrary transfers a_ij>=0 for i<j and masses\n\n    R_0>0, R_j=sum_(i<j)a_ij>0 for j>=1, E_i=R_i^2.\n\nIn the real space R^N with standard orthonormal basis define\n\n p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nIts gradient is Euclidean projection onto C, by the projection variational\ninequality and maximizing-difference inequalities. That projection is\nnonexpansive; thus F_C is convex and1-smooth.\n\nFor i>=0 and j>i put r_j^(i)=sum_(l<i)a_lj, an actual inherited coordinate\nmass. The exact phase state is\n\n    X_i=R_i e_i+sum_(j>i)r_j^(i)e_j,\n\nwhere the root sum vanishes. Subtracting T_i p_i exhausts coordinate i and\ncreates X_(i+1); every individual physical step is retained.\n\nDefine\n\n V_i=E_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij r_j^(i),\n C_i=sum_(j>i)a_ij[r_j^(i)+a_ij],\n Delta_ij=a_ij R_j-sum_(l>j)a_il a_jl, i<j.               (F1)\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\n    Delta_ij=-T_i T_j <p_i,p_j>.                         (F2)\n\nFor the complete normal test put z=(ordinary partial mass+1)/M in[1/M,1].\nMultiplying all normal values by M, the own value is\n\n    [(tau_i-z)V_i-z C_i]/tau_i^2,                        (F3)\n\nand a future-j value is\n\n    [sum_(l<i)Delta_lj+(z/tau_i)Delta_ij]/tau_j.           (F4)\n\nPast normal values are nonpositive since their positive coordinates have\nbeen exhausted and all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nComparing(F3) with zero and EVERY(F4) at BOTH z=1/M and z=1 is necessary\nand sufficient for all actual queries. All differences are affine. At the\nlast phase t>=1 suffices because there is no outgoing transfer or future\nnormal. This keeps the full old-query and future-query test explicit.\n\nEvery legal nonterminal phase has\n\n    q_i V_i>=C_i>=0.                                    (F5)\n\nIn fact V_i>0: if C_i>0 use(F5); if C_i=0 all outgoing transfers vanish\nand V_i=E_i>0. At the terminal phase V_(N-1)=E_(N-1)>0.\n\nThe additional sign hypothesis is imposed ONLY when i is a HIGH phase,\nnamely odd i with 1<=i<=N-3:\n\n    F_i:=sum_(l<i)Delta_(l,i+1)>=0.                      (F6)\n\nAlways write G_i=Delta_(i,i+1), but impose NO sign on G_i. By(F2),\ncondition(F6) is exactly <sum_(l<i)T_l p_l,p_(i+1)><=0 at high phases.\nIt follows from pairwise nonpositive correlations, but permits arbitrary\npositive adjacent correlations and cancellations among older pairs.\nNo assumption that(F6) follows from arbitrary positive transfers is made.\n\nThe class is nonempty. For example take only root-to-later transfers\na_0j=R_j and no outgoing transfers from j>=1. Set E_(N-1)=1, then choose\nE_i backward large enough that\nq_i E_i/tau_i^2>=max_(j>i) E_j/tau_j. Finally choose\nqE_0>=sum_(j>=1)E_j+A max_(j>=1)E_j/tau_j.\nThese give every endpoint normal above, and their correlations obey(F6).\n\nEvery original queried value is <x,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The polar inequalities\n<p_i,z><=0 imply backward that every coordinate of z is nonpositive.\nConsequently0 is the actual closest minimizer to R_0e_0, with distance R_0.\nWriting htot=sum H_m, the original objective and primitive defect are\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2t^2E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (F7)\n\nHere V without a subscript is the project primitive defect; V_i in(F1)\nis the phase energy numerator. Formula(F7) follows from the terminal state\nand the exact identity\n2hF+h(1-h)||g||^2=||x||^2-||x-hg||^2 along the original trajectory.\n\n## 2. All low phases are automatic; high phases need only the older sum\n\nSet Z_i=V_i/tau_i>0. At the final phase Z_(N-1)=E_(N-1)/t.\nBy direct summation in(F1),\n\n    F_i+G_i=V_(i+1).                                    (F8)\n\nThe current squared-transfer cost also satisfies\n\n C_i>=a_(i,i+1)R_(i+1)>=Delta_(i,i+1)=G_i,                (F9)\n\nbecause R_(i+1)=r_(i+1)^(i)+a_(i,i+1) and all products omitted from\nC_i are nonnegative; the second inequality discards the shared-tail sum\nsubtracted in Delta.\n\nThe endpoint future-i+1 comparison(F3)-(F4), together with\nC_i>=max(0,G_i), gives\n\n q_i V_i >=[tau_i^2 V_(i+1)-tau_i q_i G_i\n                              +tau_(i+1)max(0,G_i)]/tau_(i+1).   (F9a)\n\nIf G_i<=0, the two extra terms are nonnegative. Thus\n\n    Z_(i+1)/Z_i<=q_i/tau_i.                             (F10a)\n\nIf G_i>=0, the extra coefficient of G_i in(F9a) is\nDelta_cap=tau_(i+1)-tau_i q_i.\nAt a LOW phase tau_i=A and Delta_cap>=1-Aq>0, so(F10a)\nholds for arbitrary F_i and G_i, without ANY correlation-sign condition.\n\nAt a HIGH phase tau_i=B=1+Q and tau_(i+1)=A, one has\nDelta_cap=A-BQ<0. The only hypothesis(F6) gives\nG_i<=F_i+G_i=V_(i+1). Multiplication by the negative Delta_cap\ntherefore bounds the right side of(F9a) below by\n[B^2+A-BQ]V_(i+1)/A=(A+B)V_(i+1)/A. Consequently\n\n    Z_(i+1)/Z_i<=q_i tau_i/(tau_i+tau_(i+1)).            (F10b)\n\nThe elementary parameter signs Aq<1 and BQ>A are established below.\nWhen G_i<=0 at a high phase, (F10a) is stronger since BQ>A.\nThus in all cases the same phasewise transfer bound holds:\n\n Z_(i+1)/Z_i\n <=max(q_i/tau_i, q_i tau_i/(tau_i+tau_(i+1))).           (F10)\n\nNo adjacent-correlation sign is required, and no low-phase sign is required.\n\nAt the root, V_1=Delta_01>0, while\nC_0=sum_(j>0)a_0j^2>=R_1^2>=Delta_01.\nIts endpoint comparison with phase1 therefore gives\n\n    Z_1/E_0<=q/(A+tau_1).                                (F11)\n\nNo factorization, finite memory, or scalar common direction was used.\nThe only extra sign is the older-sum hypothesis(F6) at high phases.\n\n## 3. Multiply the exact ruler factors\n\nThe mass recursion gives, with b=a/(c-2) in(0,1),\n\n    M_j=1+sum H_j=b c^j+(1-b)2^j.\n\nConsequently M_m<=Mc^k and a<=q<c-2. For m>=512 use\n\n 2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16,\n cq>=1-1/m>=99/100,\n qc<=c(c-2)<1+8/m<=65/64.                                (10)\n\nFor example lambda<169/70 and a>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5\ngive the first lower bound. The expansion\nc(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2 gives the last one.\n\nAt a LOW phase tau_i=A and tau_(i+1)>=1. Since A q<1,\n\n    A^2<A+tau_(i+1),\n\nso the first alternative in(F10) is larger and the factor is at most q/A.\n\nAt a HIGH phase tau_i=B=1+Q with Q>=99/100, its next phase is low,\ntau_(i+1)=A. The bounds imply BQ>A, so B^2>B+A and the second\nalternative in(F10) is larger. The factor is at most QB/(B+A).\n\nWrite B_1=1+cq=tau_1 (not the benchmark with the same letter).\nThere are N/2-1 high phases, with levels summing to N-k-1, and the\nsame number of low phases after the root. Thus (F10)-(F11) give\n\n Z_(N-1)/E_0\n <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)]\n               *product_(high i) tau_i/(tau_i+A)\n <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)].                  (11)\n\nThe count follows directly from 2^(k-ell-1) high checkpoints at level\nell=1,...,k-1. No asymptotic replacement of the physical word is made.\n\n## 4. Full terminal completion and the actual distance\n\nLet f be ANY convex1-smooth completion retaining all queried jets and\nthe optimum(0,0,0), including the maximal completion in accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf r>=1, every p_i is actually queried. Interpolation to any new\nminimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0, since the retained value is\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The triangular inequalities force its coordinates\nnonpositive, so0 remains the true closest minimizer at distance R_0.\nAdditional orthogonal dimensions cannot lower the distance.\n\nThe last physical step is h_last=1+a. Its actual retained query gives\n\n 2f(x_n)/E_0\n <=[2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a yields 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nPut K=1/(2M_m-1). Use M_m<=Mc^k and(11):\n\n score_free/K\n <[4A/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <2(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (12)\n\nThe last inequality uses B_1>A.\n\nIf r=0, the old terminal gradient is UNQUERIED. Use instead the\nLipschitz-gradient lower bound\n\n    dist(x_0,argmin f)^2>=||p_0||^2>=E_0/A^2.             (13)\n\nAt the final query i=N-2, put\nv=r_(i+1)^(i)/R_(i+1) in[0,1]. Its position is\nR_i e_i+v R_(i+1)e_(i+1), and its gradient is\n[R_i e_i-(1-v)R_(i+1)e_(i+1)]/A. One smoothness step of length A gives\n\n 2f(x_n)<=(1-A^(-2))E_i\n +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(i+1)\n <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.                                       (14)\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive for A<23/16.\n\nWrite b=a_(i,i+1) and d=r_(i+1)^(i). The final low phase's\nown zero comparison says qE_i>=A b d+b^2. Since Z_i=(E_i-bd)/A,\nit follows that\n\n    Z_i>=E_i/A^2.                                       (15)\n\nIndeed bd<=qE_i/A, and A-q=1 then proves the displayed bound.\n\nRepeat the product(F10)-(F11) but omit the final low factor q/A. It yields\n\n Z_(N-2)/E_0\n <=q^(N-2)c^(N-k-1)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-2)].\n\nCombine(13)-(15), multiply by K^(-1)<2c^k, and use A^2-1=q(2+q):\n\n score_free/K\n <[2(2+q)A^4/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <7(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (16)\n\nFor the numerical rational constant, A+B_1>3 and\n2(39/16)(23/16)^4/3=21827598/3145728<7.\n\nNo unqueried normal has been used to constrain the new minimizer.\nThe terminal bound follows only from retained queries and their actual\nphysical last displacement.\n\n## 5. Uniform payoff and the original exact benchmark\n\nSet z0=65/64>1. Then (qc)^(N-1)<=z0^N even when qc<1.\nAlso\n\n    z0^2/A<(65/64)^2/(7/5)=21125/28672<3/4.\n\nEquations(12),(16) prove\n\n    score_free/K<7(3/4)^(N/2).\n\nFor N>=16, (3/4)^4=81/256<1/3 gives score_free/K<7/9.\nIf N grows, the ratio tends to zero uniformly in all nonnegative transfers obeying(F6), energies, r, terminal margin and full completion.\n\nFor the exact benchmark use accepted b98bb807d514e80d and write\nrho_s=log_2(lambda). Put F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1. The second derivative of\nlog F(exp w) equals\n\n rho_s*z*(1+z)^(rho_s-2)*\n [(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s*z]/F(z)^2>0.\n\nThus F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2 by midpoint convexity in w. Comparing the\npositive root of t^2-(X+Y)t-XY with\n(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s proves\n\n    Omega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\n\nInduction in the accepted primitive recursion gives\n1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s. Since the accepted dominant join\nGamma(X,D)=2X+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8XD)/2 is at most4X+D,\nits recursion then gives B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\nThe displayed exact mass formula gives M_m/lambda^m->exp(1), hence\n\n    limsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1).\n\nWe conclude\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=14/[9 exp(1)]<1,\n\nwith limit superior zero whenever N tends to infinity.\n\n## 6. Scope and the only surviving sign\n\nThis excludes every legal nonnegative forward-incidence construction\nsatisfying(F6) at HIGH phases only, regardless of memory length, number\nof parents, graph size, or factorization rank. Every adjacent gradient\ncorrelation is unrestricted; all low-phase aggregate correlations are\nunrestricted. Some or all distant pairs may be positive, provided the\nspecified older sum at each high phase has nonpositive correlation.\nAll original physical phases and the free terminal completion are covered.\n\nAn escaping construction must therefore have at least one high phase i\nwhere\n\n    <sum_(l<i)T_l p_l,p_(i+1)> >0.                       (S)\n\nBecause x_0-X_i=sum_(l<i)T_l p_l and <x_0,p_(i+1)>=0 in this\npositive triangular geometry, (S) is equivalent to\n\n    <X_i,p_(i+1)> <0.\n\nSo the future low gradient must be negative on the actual state before\nthat high phase, even though <X_(i+1),p_(i+1)>>0 at its own phase.\nThe high phase must make use of an older shared-coordinate residual.\nA positive adjacent or remote pair, by itself, is insufficient.\n\nThis necessary condition is specific to the displayed forward-incidence\ngeometry. It is not a witness attaining the benchmark, does not cover\narbitrary flows violating(F6), signed transfers or reactivated coordinates,\nand does not upper-bound unrestricted W. The original exact-profile\ntarget remains unresolved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["d9c00d11e044517b", "19f9dda7a15723b3", "8e8cc2d8c1739221"]}, "glossary": {"F_i": "sum_(l<i)Delta_(l,i+1)=-T_(i+1)<sum_(l<i)T_l p_l,p_(i+1)>; required nonnegative only at high phases.", "G_i": "Delta_(i,i+1), whose sign is now completely unrestricted.", "Delta_ij": "a_ij R_j-sum_(k>j)a_ik a_jk=-T_iT_j<p_i,p_j>.", "X_i": "Actual state at the start of phase i, before its physical H_r block.", "V_i": "R_i^2-sum_(j>i)a_ij r_j^(i)>0, the phase energy numerator.", "Z_i": "V_i/tau_i, the positive phase energy used in the transfer inequality.", "C_i": "sum_(j>i)a_ij(r_j^(i)+a_ij), which is at least max(0,G_i).", "Delta_cap": "tau_(i+1)-tau_i q_i, positive for low phases and negative for high phases on this word.", "K": "1/(2M_m-1), canonical Huber score.", "score_free": "Normalized objective of any convex1-smooth completion retaining all queried jets and the optimum."}}
{"id": "849a06cf1649008b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:31:32.567424+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C47 closes a scalable memory-three ladder that REALLY violates the high-history sign at every high phase with an interior split. Each low phase feeds the next two odd coordinates, and each high phase feeds its next low coordinate; all split fractions and energies are independent. Combining both high future normals cancels the positive remote-correlation credit exactly. For m>=512,N>=64, EVERY legal member and full terminal completion has score_free/K<5(4/5)^(N/4)<1/5, hence limsup B_n score_free<=2/(5e)<1 and growing N gives zero. Candidate only; arbitrary three-predecessor flows remain open.", "evidence": "# C47. The full memory-three older-preload ladder still loses\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate; no verifier or numerical computation.\nEvery ingredient used is proved below. The construction really has positive\nremote correlations and violates the older-history sign at every high phase\nwith an interior split. It is a different mechanism from two-predecessor\nflows and from the conditional high-history obstruction.\n\nFor m>=512 and N=2^(m-r)>=64, every legal member of the entire ladder\nfamily below, with every split independently chosen, and every full terminal\ncompletion satisfies\n\n    score_free/K <5(4/5)^(N/4)<1/5.\n\nHence limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=2/(5e)<1, and the limit superior is\nzero when N grows. This excludes this whole ladder, not arbitrary memory\nthree or general positive flows.\n\n## 1. The actual word and real shared-coordinate ladder\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).\nChoose 0<=r<=m-6, let k=m-r, N=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M,\nA=1+q, and J=N/2-1. There are low phases2j and odd phases2j+1,\nj=0,...,J. For j<J the odd phase is a high checkpoint phase, with\n\n    Q_j=q c^(nu_2(2j+2)), B_j=1+Q_j.\n\nThe last odd phase is terminal; put B_J=t>=1.\nEvery phase contains the original H_r block of mass M-1. Low checkpoints\nhave size1+Mq; high checkpoints have size1+M Q_j. All physical steps\nremain in their original order.\n\nChoose theta_0=0 and arbitrary independent theta_j in[0,1] for1<=j<=J.\nChoose positive coordinate energies L_j,O_j. In the real space R^N let\n\n p_(2j)=[sqrt(L_j)e_(2j)\n       -(1-theta_j)sqrt(O_j)e_(2j+1)\n       -theta_(j+1)sqrt(O_(j+1))e_(2j+3)]/(MA),\n\nomitting the last term when j=J. For j<J put\n\n p_(2j+1)=[sqrt(O_j)e_(2j+1)-sqrt(L_(j+1))e_(2j+2)]/(MB_j),\n\nand put p_(2J+1)=sqrt(O_J)e_(2J+1)/(Mt).\nEvery high phase sends the entire next low mass. Every low phase splits\nits outgoing mass between the next and the following odd coordinate.\nEach odd coordinate j>=1 merges theta_j of its mass sent two low phases\nearlier with the remaining1-theta_j sent by the immediately preceding low\nphase. Thus its parents are physical phases three and one steps earlier.\n\nDefine the single function\n\n    C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n    F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nProjection onto C is its gradient, by the projection variational inequality\nand maximizing-difference inequalities. That projection is nonexpansive,\nso F_C is convex and1-smooth.\n\nStart at X_0=sqrt(L_0)e_0. The exact low-phase and high-phase states are\n\n X_(2j)=sqrt(L_j)e_(2j)+theta_j sqrt(O_j)e_(2j+1),\n X_(2j+1)=sqrt(O_j)e_(2j+1)\n                     +theta_(j+1)sqrt(O_(j+1))e_(2j+3),\n\nwith the last high state's second term omitted. Subtracting total phase\namount MA p_(2j) or MB_j p_(2j+1) gives the next displayed state.\nEvery individual step inside an H_r block uses the same displayed gradient.\n\n## 2. Every old/future normal, without discarding the preload\n\nHere are the necessary and sufficient endpoint inequalities. At a low phase\nj define\n\n    chi(theta,B)=(1+q theta)(1-theta+A/B).\n\nIts own normal dominates the current odd future normal exactly when\n\n q L_j>=chi(theta_j,B_j)O_j+theta_(j+1)^2 O_(j+1).         (1)\n\nIt dominates the following odd future normal exactly when\n\n q L_j>=(1-theta_j)(1+qtheta_j)O_j\n       +theta_(j+1)[theta_(j+1)+A/B_(j+1)]O_(j+1).        (2)\n\nAt the final low phase omit all O_(j+1) terms and comparison(2).\nThe intervening future low normal is nonpositive, since its only overlap\nis the negative coefficient on the newly preloaded odd coordinate.\nEvery other future normal is zero. The current odd comparison in(1)\nis positive, so it also enforces the zero-vertex comparison.\n\nAt a high phase j<J, write B=B_j, Q=Q_j,\ntheta=theta_(j+1), B'=B_(j+1). Its own endpoint normal is\n(Q O_j-L_(j+1))/B^2 after multiplication by M.\nIts next low and next odd normals give, respectively,\n\n Q O_j>=(1+B/A)L_(j+1)\n              -(B^2/A)theta(1-theta)O_(j+1),             (3)\n Q O_j>=L_(j+1)+(B^2 theta/B')O_(j+1).                   (4)\n\nAll farther future normals vanish. Equation(4) includes the zero test.\n\nEvery past normal is nonpositive: its positive coordinate has been\nexhausted and all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nInside each ordinary block the own normal decreases affinely with the\npartial mass. Each future normal that can be positive increases affinely\nor remains constant. The negative future low normal in a low phase\nremains nonpositive. Therefore (1)-(4) at the block endpoints prove every\nactual physical query, not just the endpoint. The final phase is legal\nwhen t>=1 because its own normal is nonnegative and all past normals\nare nonpositive.\n\nThe class is nonempty for every chosen split sequence: fix O_J=1,\nchoose L_J large enough for(1), choose O_(J-1) for(3)-(4), then continue\nbackward, choosing each low and high energy for its finite displayed\ntests. All coefficients on the new current energy are positive.\n\nAll old coordinate residuals are explicit. In particular, at any high\nphase j with0<theta_(j+1)<1,\n\n <X_(2j+1),p_(2j+2)>\n   =-theta_(j+1)(1-theta_(j+1))O_(j+1)/(MA)<0.            (5)\n\nThe preceding low gradient and the next low gradient have positive\ninner product theta_(j+1)(1-theta_(j+1))O_(j+1)/(M^2A^2).\nThus the family genuinely uses the older-history credit absent from\nnonpositive-history classes; it does not satisfy that condition by accident.\n\nTriangular polar inequalities force all minimizer coordinates nonpositive.\nHence0 is the true closest minimizer, with squared initial distance L_0.\nFor htot=sum H_m the original objective and primitive defect are\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)O_J/(M^2t^2L_0),\n V/L_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 O_J/L_0.                (6)\n\nThe defect follows by telescoping the exact identity\n2hF+h(1-h)||g||^2=||x||^2-||x-hg||^2 along these queries.\n\n## 3. Two future comparisons cancel the positive-history credit\n\nUse(1) at the following low phase and discard only its nonnegative\nO_(j+2) term:\n\n    q L_(j+1)>=chi(theta,B')O_(j+1).\n\nSubstitute into both(3),(4). With\n\n P=(1+B/A)chi(theta,B')-(qB^2/A)theta(1-theta),\n S=chi(theta,B')+qB^2 theta/B',\n\nwe obtain\n\n    qQ O_j>=max(P,S)O_(j+1).                             (7)\n\nA nonnegative weighted combination removes the troublesome sign:\n\n P+[B'(1-theta)/A]S\n    =[1+B/A+B'(1-theta)/A]chi(theta,B').\n\nDivide by1+B'(1-theta)/A and cancel the exact factor\nA+B'(1-theta) from chi. This gives\n\n max(P,S)>=(1+qtheta)[A+B+B'(1-theta)]/B'.                 (8)\n\nConsequently the denominator for the normalized odd energy Y_j=O_j/B_j is\n\n d=(B'/B)max(P,S)\n   >=(1+qtheta)[1+A/B+(B'/B)(1-theta)].\n\nThe final expression is a concave quadratic in theta, so its minimum\non[0,1] is at an endpoint:\n\n    d>=min(1+(A+B')/B, A(1+A/B))>=1.                    (9)\n\nNo split equality or stationarity was imposed. Equations(7)-(9) prove\n\n    Y_(j+1)/Y_j<=qQ_j/d.                                 (10)\n\n## 4. Uniform ruler factors and initial normalization\n\nWrite M_l=1+sum H_l. Its recursion gives\n\n M_l=b c^l+(1-b)2^l, b=a/(c-2) in(0,1).\n\nThus M_m<=M c^k and a<=q<c-2. For m>=512,\n\n 2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16, A<23/16,\n cq>=1-1/m>=99/100,\n qc<=c(c-2)<1+8/m<=65/64.                                (11)\n\nThe lower a bound follows, for example, from\na>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5 using lambda<169/70.\nFor the last bound expand\nc(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2.\n\nAt a level-one high phase B=1+cq<=129/64. Since B'>=1,\nqB<903/1024<1<A+1 implies\n\n    1+(A+B')/B>=A.\n\nThe other endpoint in(9) is also at least A, so every level-one high\npays d>=A. Every other high pays at least1.\n\nAt the initial low phase theta_0=0, and(1) gives\n\n    Y_0/L_0<=q/(A+B_0), B_0=1+cq.                        (12)\n\nThere are N/2-1 high passages in(10), including N/4 level-one passages.\nTheir levels sum to N-k-1, by the exact counts2^(k-ell-1) of each level.\nMultiplying(10)-(12), using d>=A on level one, gives\n\n    O_J/(tL_0)\n      <=(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_0)A^(N/4)].               (13)\n\nThis is the actual final energy of the single fixed function.\n\n## 5. Free terminal completion and actual closest distance\n\nRetain all original queried jets and the optimum(0,0,0), and let f be\nANY convex1-smooth terminal completion, including the maximal one of\naccepted1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf r>=1, every displayed p_i is queried. Interpolation to a new\nminimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0 from the retained value\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The triangular inequalities force all its\ncoordinates nonpositive, so0 remains the actual closest minimizer,\nat squared distance L_0.\n\nThe last physical step is h_last=1+a. Smoothness at its retained query\ngives\n\n 2f(x_n)/L_0\n <=[2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]O_J/(M^2t^2L_0)\n <2O_J/(MtL_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a implies 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nWith K=1/(2M_m-1), M_m<=Mc^k and(13), it follows that\n\n    score_free/K<2(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/4).                   (14)\n\nHere we used4/(A+B_0)<2.\n\nIf r=0, the old terminal normal is unqueried. The initial retained\ngradient supplies the correct replacement distance bound\n\n    dist(x_0,argmin f)^2>=||p_0||^2>=L_0/A^2.\n\nAt the last low query, with theta=theta_J, the position is\nsqrt(L_J)e_(N-2)+theta sqrt(O_J)e_(N-1) and the gradient is\n[sqrt(L_J)e_(N-2)-(1-theta)sqrt(O_J)e_(N-1)]/A.\nIts actual step has length A. Smoothness gives\n\n 2f(x_n)<=(1-A^(-2))L_J\n +[-2theta(1-theta)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-theta)^2]O_J\n <=(1-A^(-2))L_J,\n\nsince A<23/16. Hence score_free<=(A^2-1)L_J/L_0.\n\nAt the preceding high, Q_(J-1)=cq and B_(J-1)=B_0.\nIts zero comparison gives L_J<=Q_(J-1)O_(J-1).\nRepeat(10)-(12) but omit this final level-one passage. The result is\n\n L_J/L_0\n <=B_0 q^(N-2)c^(N-k-1)/[(A+B_0)A^(N/4-1)].\n\nUsing K^(-1)<2c^k and A^2-1=q(2+q) gives\n\n score_free/K\n <[2(2+q)B_0 A/(A+B_0)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/4)\n <5(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/4).                                  (15)\n\nThe explicit rational upper bound for the prefactor is\n2(39/16)(129/64)(23/16)/3=231426/49152<5, since A+B_0>3.\nThis argument does not constrain a new minimizer by the unqueried\nterminal gradient.\n\n## 6. Actual benchmark comparison\n\nSet z0=65/64>1, so(qc)^(N-1)<=z0^N even if qc<1. The rational estimate\n\n    z0^4/A<(65/64)^4/(7/5)\n           =89253125/117440512<4/5\n\nand(14)-(15) prove\n\n    score_free/K<5(4/5)^(N/4).\n\nFor N>=64, (4/5)^8=65536/390625<1/5, hence the bound is below1/5.\nIt tends to zero uniformly when N grows.\n\nTo retain the exact benchmark, use accepted b98bb807d514e80d and set\nrho_s=log_2(lambda). For F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1, the second derivative\nof log F(exp w) is\n\n rho_s*z*(1+z)^(rho_s-2)*\n [(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s*z]/F(z)^2>0.\n\nMidpoint convexity therefore gives F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2.\nComparing the positive root of t^2-(X+Y)t-XY then gives the accepted\nprimitive join bound\nOmega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\nInduction in its recursion yields1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s.\nThe dominant join obeys Gamma(X,D)<=4X+D, so its recursion gives\nB_n<=1+4n^rho_s. The explicit mass formula above yields\nM_m/lambda^m->exp(1), and consequently\n\n    limsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1).\n\nWe conclude limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=2/[5exp(1)]<1, with\nlimit superior zero if N tends to infinity.\n\n## 7. Scope\n\nEvery low-to-odd split is independent and may vary with horizon, level\nand location. Every compatible positive energy, every terminal margin\nand every full completion is allowed. At interior splits the next low\ngradient is actually negative on the previous high state, so the proof\ndoes not rely on a nonpositive-history assumption. The cancellation\nin(8) uses BOTH future normals and pays for this real positive\nremote-correlation mechanism.\n\nThe family still restricts each high phase to one next-low child and\neach low phase to the next and following odd coordinates. General\nthree-predecessor transfers, multiple nonproportional long-range source\nprofiles, signed transfers and reactivated coordinates remain outside\nit. This is not an unrestricted W upper certificate or a resolution of\nthe original exact-profile target.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["a7fdf1bf620a7148", "48c6a02c16281f04", "88b640ae31a56475"]}, "glossary": {"L_j": "Squared coordinate mass of low phase2j.", "O_j": "Squared coordinate mass of odd phase2j+1.", "theta_j": "Fraction of odd coordinate j preloaded by the low phase three physical phase indices earlier; theta_0=0, every later theta independent in[0,1].", "B_j": "1+q c^(nu_2(2j+2)) at high phase j, and t at the final odd phase; distinct from benchmark B_n.", "Q_j": "High checkpoint excess divided by M.", "chi(theta,B)": "(1+q theta)(1-theta+A/B), the low first-future normal coefficient.", "P": "First high-future scalar coefficient after inserting the next low normal.", "S": "Second high-future scalar coefficient after inserting the next low normal.", "Y_j": "O_j/B_j, normalized odd coordinate energy.", "N": "Number2^(m-r) of actual H_r phases.", "K": "Canonical Huber score1/(2M_m-1).", "score_free": "Actual normalized objective of any convex1-smooth completion retaining every original query and the optimum."}}
{"id": "f42b4c43d2ea0c3a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:33:32.229412+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Complete JOINT covariance obstruction on ANY retained schedule of sum S>0: every normal probability mixture with Re z>=0 and S|z|<=1/3, arbitrary jointly legal values, and every free b in[0,2] obeys2F<=1/[2S+1+b(2-b)]. Same exact bound for explicit REAL PSD rank-one coupling between TWO DISTINCT complex blocks: arbitrary amplitude if the projection aligns with z1-z2; arbitrary orientation if rho<=w1w2/144 (or449w1w2/256 at radius1/4). Includes an actual nonnormal R5 realization with all ordered constraints strictly checked and fixed-F payoff>=3/(64S) for ALL real b. On the actual near-critical word whole-class limsup ratio<=2/e; no whole-normal/arbitrary-coupling/actual-W claim.", "evidence": "# Joint covariance and an explicit nonnormal rank-one coupling\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate; no routine fact_submit allocation,\nnumerical solver, or new worker round. Accepted inputs are\n1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and free completion), dfde2fa326c908bf\n(polynomial notation), and b98bb807d514e80d (benchmark recursions).\nAll new geometry, inequalities, and construction checks appear below.\n\n## 1. Exact whole-class bounds on any retained schedule\n\nLet h_0,...,h_(q-1)>=0 have sum S>0. Let\nP_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), 0<=i<=q, and phi_i(z)=zP_i(z).\nFor ANY finite probability weights w_z at complex parameters satisfying\n\n  Re z>=0,             S|z|<=1/3,                       (1)\n\nform the real orthogonal-block vectors\nx_i=(sqrt(w_z)Re P_i(z),sqrt(w_z)Im P_i(z))_z and\ng_i=(sqrt(w_z)Re phi_i(z),sqrt(w_z)Im phi_i(z))_z.\nThus x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i and ||x_0||=1.\n\nAssume arbitrary JOINT legal values f_i, including the optimum (0,0,0).\nFor every interpolating convex 1-smooth F with minimum zero, and every\ngenuinely free terminal step b in [0,2],\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)<=1/[2S+1+b(2-b)].                        (2)\n\nThe statement is about all weights and all joint values; it does not infer\nlegality of the separate modes. A reflected complex block has the same\nGram data and fixes the initial direction. Thus we may take Im z>=0\nindependently in each normal block when proving this theorem.\n\nThere is also a specific nonnormal extension. Take TWO different parameters\nz_1,z_2 with Re z_l,Im z_l>=0 and S|z_l|<=1/3, and weights w_1,w_2>0\nwith sum one. Take\na complex number omega with |omega|=1, and rho_coup>=0. Append the coordinate\n\n  x_i^(5)=sqrt(rho_coup) Re[omega(P_i(z_1)-P_i(z_2))],\n  g_i^(5)=sqrt(rho_coup) Re[omega(phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2))]   (3)\n\nto the four normal coordinates. It starts at zero, so ||x_0||=1 exactly.\nThe same bound (2) holds for EVERY jointly legal prefix of these actual\nfive-dimensional vectors under either of the following conditions:\n\n  (a) omega(z_1-z_2)>0 is real; rho_coup is arbitrary, or\n  (b) rho_coup<=w_1 w_2/144; omega is arbitrary.             (4)\n\nThese are whole coupling classes, with no bound on the conditioning in (a).\nAt the smaller radius S|z_l|<=1/4, condition (b) improves to\n\n  rho_coup<=(449/256)w_1w_2.                              (5)\n\nOnly this explicit finite-dimensional rank-one coupling is claimed;\narbitrary cross-mode covariance is not being reparameterized.\n\n## 2. Divided differences and a finite covariance identity\n\nWork in the convex quarter disk Re z,Im z>=0, |z|S<=R. For every factor,\n\n  |1-hz|^2=1-2h Re z+h^2|z|^2<=exp(h^2|z|^2).\n\nAll prefix products and products omitting any factors therefore have\nmodulus <=exp(R^2/2), since sum h^2<=S^2. Telescoping P_i-1 and\ndifferentiating its product give\n\n  |P_i-1|<=|z|S exp(R^2/2),\n  |zP_i'|<=|z|S exp(R^2/2),\n  |phi_i'-1|<=2R exp(R^2/2).                             (6)\n\nFor R=1/3, exp(1/18)<=18/17 by positive power series comparison.\nThus all derivatives lie in the disk centered at 1 of radius\nd=12/17<1/sqrt(2). For R=1/4 one may instead take d=16/31.\n\nFor any two distinct z,v in the quarter disk, define\na_i(z,v)=[phi_i(z)-phi_i(v)]/(z-v). Integrating phi_i' along the straight\nsegment between them shows |a_i-1|<=d. A complex number a in this disk\nhas positive real part and |arg a|<=arcsin d. Indeed\n2Re a>=|a|^2+1-d^2>=2|a|sqrt(1-d^2).\nConsequently, for every i,j and every z,v,\n\n  Re[(phi_i(z)-phi_i(v))conj(phi_j(z)-phi_j(v))]\n   >=(1-2d^2)|z-v|^2 |a_i(z,v)||a_j(z,v)|>=0.             (7)\n\nThe coincident-parameter case is zero. At R=1/3, 1-2d^2=1/289.\n\nWe also need the signs of the means, not just covariance. Write nonzero\nz=r exp(i theta), 0<=theta<=pi/2. Since hr<=R<=1/3, each factor\nhas positive real part. Its negative argument is\natan(h r sin theta/(1-h r cos theta)). The sum of these arguments is at most\n\n  rS sin theta/(1-rS cos theta)\n      <=[R/(1-R)]theta<=theta/2.\n\nThus arg phi_i(z) lies in [theta/2,theta]; both Re phi_i(z) and\nIm phi_i(z) are nonnegative. The zero parameter is immediate.\n\nLet expectation denote the finite weighted sum and put mu_i=E phi_i.\nA direct expansion, with z,v independent under the same weights, gives\n\n  Re[E(phi_i conj(phi_j))-mu_i conj(mu_j)]\n   =(1/2)E_(z,v) Re[(phi_i(z)-phi_i(v))\n                                conj(phi_j(z)-phi_j(v))]>=0.  (8)\n\nSet e=x_0, G_i=<e,g_i>=Re mu_i, u_i=g_i-G_i e. Then G_i>=0 and\n\n  <u_i,u_j>\n    =Re[E(phi_i conj(phi_j))-mu_i conj(mu_j)]\n                                  +(Im mu_i)(Im mu_j)>=0. (9)\n\nThis establishes one joint cone directly from the probability law. It is\nnot the union of separate single-mode legality statements.\n\n## 3. The explicit PSD rank-one coupling\n\nIn real coordinates y=(y_1,y_2,y_3,y_4), let\nv=(Re omega,-Im omega,-Re omega,Im omega). The map T:R^4->R^5 is\n\n  T y=(sqrt(w_1)y_1,sqrt(w_1)y_2,\n       sqrt(w_2)y_3,sqrt(w_2)y_4,sqrt(rho_coup)<v,y>).\n\nIts Gram metric is exactly\n\n  T^T T=diag(w_1,w_1,w_2,w_2)+rho_coup vv^T,\n\nwhich is real positive definite for w_1,w_2>0. Formula (3) is precisely\nT applied to the two polynomial blocks, so it gives actual vectors and\na fixed linear realization. The fifth coordinate of e=x_0 is zero.\nIt does not change G_i; it adds rho_coup U_i U_j to (9), where\n\n  U_i=Re[omega(phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2))].\n\nUnder (4a), U_i=|z_1-z_2| Re a_i(z_1,z_2)>0, so the cone is preserved\nfor every rho_coup>=0.\n\nFor arbitrary orientation, put A=phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2) and\nB=phi_j(z_1)-phi_j(z_2). The elementary identity\n\n  Re(omega A) Re(omega B)\n    =(Re(A conj B)+Re(omega^2 A B))/2\n    >=(Re(A conj B)-|A||B|)/2\n    >=-d^2 |A||B|\n\nfollows from (7). The two-point covariance term in (8) equals\nw_1w_2 Re(A conj B), which is at least\nw_1w_2(1-2d^2)|A||B|. The imaginary-mean term is nonnegative.\nThus every transverse pair remains nonnegative whenever\n\n  rho_coup <= [(1-2d^2)/d^2] w_1w_2.                    (10)\n\nAt d=12/17 the ratio is 1/144; at d=16/31 it is 449/256.\nThis proves (4b)--(5).\n\nFor clarity these vectors really permit a nonnormal operator. Let\nZ=diag(C(z_1),C(z_2)), where\nC(alpha+i beta)=[[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]].\nOn R^5 set A_coup=T Z (T^T T)^(-1)T^T, zero on the orthogonal complement\nof im T. Then A_coup x_i=g_i. If rho_coup>0 and, for example, omega=1\nand z_1,z_2 have the same positive imaginary part but different real\nparts, its complex eigenvectors T(e_1-i e_2) and T(e_3-i e_4) have\ndistinct eigenvalues and inner product -rho_coup!=0. Hence A_coup is\nnot normal. To justify that implication directly, normality gives\n||(A-lambda I)u||=||(A^*-conj(lambda)I)u||; an eigenvector for lambda\nis then also an adjoint eigenvector, forcing eigenvectors of distinct\neigenvalues to be orthogonal. No generic covariance kernel is used.\n\n## 4. Exact cone bound with all queried anchors retained\n\nHere is the entire cone argument, so no earlier unverified result is a\nproof premise. It applies to any legal queried data with ||x_0||=1 and\n\n  g_i=G_i e+u_i,  G_i>=0, <e,u_i>=0, <u_i,u_j>>=0.\n\nPut X_i=1-sum_(t<i)h_t G_t, W_i=sum_(t<i)h_t u_t, so\nx_i=X_i e-W_i and X_i>=X_q. Add the two actual inequalities\nQ_(q,i)<=0 and Q_(i,star)<=0 from accepted 1252b8823bb5a290:\n\n  f_q<=<g_q,x_q>+<g_i-g_q,x_i>\n                         -||g_i||^2/2-||g_i-g_q||^2/2.   (11)\n\nFor i=q the optimum inequality alone gives the same formula.\nThe transverse part of the first two terms is exactly\n\n  -<u_q,W_q-W_i>-<u_i,W_i><=0.\n\nThe transverse contributions of the two squared norms are nonpositive\nas well. Choose an actual index i minimizing G_i and write p=G_i>=0.\nThen G_q>=p, X_i>=X_q, and X_q<=1-Sp, whence\n\n  2f_q<=2p-(2S+1)p^2-(G_q-p)^2.                          (12)\n\nFor b in [0,2], smoothness subtracts b(2-b)||g_q||^2 from (12).\nSince G_q>=p>=0, this subtracts at least b(2-b)p^2. Completing the\nsquare in p proves (2), for every interpolant and its free terminal\ngradient. No interpolation constraints within the prefix were deleted.\n\n## 5. A concrete legal, nonnormal, positively coupled family\n\nLet m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nd_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), and n=2^m-1.\nKeep a,c fixed within the horizon, remove the ORIGINAL last d_0 step\nfrom H_m, and retain all queries through q=n-1. Its sum S exceeds 100.\nPut\n\n  z_1=(6+2i sqrt(7))/(64S),   z_2=(3+2i sqrt(7))/(64S),\n  omega=1.\n\nBoth lie in (1), with |z_1|=1/(8S), |z_2|=sqrt(37)/(64S), and their\ndifference is real positive. Thus the whole coupling class (4a) applies.\nThe following choice proves it contains actually legal examples with\nrho_coup>0, not just formal vectors.\n\nFor the base z_1 write alpha=3/(32S), sigma=1/(64S^2), p_i=P_i(z_1).\nAll factors contract and 7/8<=|p_i|<=1. Define\nf_0^base=3alpha/4 and\nf_(i+1)^base=f_i^base-h_i sigma(1-alpha h_i/2)|p_i|^2.\nThen 7alpha/12<=f_i^base<=3alpha/4, because the decrements total at\nmost sigma S=alpha/6.\n\nEvery base ordered residual, including the optimum, is <=-delta with\ndelta=alpha sigma/8. Here are the full estimates. For i<j set\nT_seg=sum_(i<=s<j)h_s. Telescoping contracting factors gives\n\n  |p_j-p_i|>=(15/16)|z_1|T_seg |p_i|.\n\nThe signed area of the closed polygon i,...,j,i is\nsigma sum_(i<=s<t<j)h_s h_t Im(conj(p_s)p_t).\nTelescoping the subproduct minus its conjugate bounds\n|Im(conj(p_s)p_t)|<=|p_i|^2 |Im z_1| T_seg.\nThus |beta Area|<=sigma^2 T_seg^3 |p_i|^2/2.\nSubtracting direct-edge work from summed trapezoidal work shows that\nthe two ordered slacks are\n(kappa/2)|p_j-p_i|^2 +/- beta Area. With\nkappa>=5alpha/6 and sigma T_seg<=alpha/6, both are at least\n\n  [1125/3072-1/12]alpha sigma T_seg^2|p_i|^2\n                              >(alpha sigma/4)T_seg^2|p_i|^2>delta.\n\nThe last inequality uses every actual step >1 and |p_i|>=7/8.\nThe optimum lower slack is >=7alpha/12-sigma/2>alpha/2.\nThe optimum upper slack is >=alpha[1/64-49/(768S)]>alpha/100>delta.\n\nPut M=exp(S), K=4M^2, K_row=16M^2, and choose\n\n  epsilon=delta/[8(K+delta)], w_1=1-epsilon, w_2=epsilon,\n  rho_coup=delta/(8K_row)>0, f_i=(1-epsilon)f_i^base.\n\nGive the second normal block temporary zero values. Since all relevant\n|z|<=1, its ordered residuals are <=K. The two normal blocks therefore\nhave joint residual at most -delta+epsilon(K+delta)=-7delta/8.\nFor the added unscaled row with position Re(P_i(z_1)-P_i(z_2))\nand gradient Re(phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2)), each position\nand gradient has magnitude <=2M. With zero temporary values, its\nordered residual is at most K_row. Adding the PSD row contributes at\nmost rho_coup K_row=delta/8, so EVERY distinct ordered residual obeys\n\n  Q_ij<=-3delta/4<0,                                    (13)\n\nincluding every optimum pair. All cycles hold on the SAME coupled data.\n\nThe actual interpolant in R^5 is explicitly\n\n  H(v)=max_(i in {star,0,...,q})\n       {<g_i,x_i>-f_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2},\n  F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.\n\nAccepted 1252b8823bb5a290 makes it finite, convex, differentiable and\n1-smooth, with F(0)=min F=0 and every queried jet retained.\nThe chosen initial distance remains exactly one.\n\n## 6. Fixed-function payoff for every real final step\n\nLet Z_test be the orthogonal projection of the first normal block of x_q\nonto the line perpendicular to its corresponding queried gradient,\nwith zero coordinates in the other normal block and in the coupled row.\nThen <Z_test,g_q>=0,\n||Z_test||^2>=(7/8)(49/64)(7/16)>2/7. Set v_test=alpha Z_test.\n\nFor the optimum anchor,\n2<x_q,v_test>-||v_test||^2=(2alpha-alpha^2)||Z_test||^2>=alpha/2.\nFor every finite anchor put y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nHere\n\n  ||g_i||^2<=sigma+epsilon M^2+4rho_coup M^2\n              <=sigma+delta/16<=2sigma,\n\nand the first-block norm of x_q-y_i is <=(1+1/S)/8<=101/800.\nThus 2delta_i>=49alpha/48-2sigma and\n\n  2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,v_test>-||v_test||^2\n    >=alpha[49/48-1/300-101/400-3/3200]>alpha/2.\n\nBoth the extra block and the coupled row have zero inner product with\nv_test. Evaluating the same conjugate F at v_test proves\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)>=alpha/2=3/(64S)\n\nfor EVERY real b, since v_test is orthogonal to g_q. Every actual\nanchor, including the optimum, was checked. The true terminal gradient\nis unrestricted.\n\n## 7. Exact benchmark normalization and scope\n\nFor the actual near-critical word, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nd_j=1+a c^j, n=2^m-1, and\nS=2^m-1+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)-d_0. Thus S/lambda^m->e.\nThe bound (2) includes the original final b=d_0 in [0,2].\n\nThe exact recursions of b98bb807d514e80d give C_j=S_circle(j)+1 and\nB_j via Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2 and\nG(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2. With rho=log_2lambda,\nV(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 has\n\n  d^2/ds^2 log V(exp(s))\n   =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0,\n  t=exp(s).\n\nConvexity at s,-s gives V(t)V(1/t)>=2, which yields\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho after substituting\nt=(A/B)^(1/rho) and expanding (L-A)(L-B)>=2AB.\nInduction gives C_j<=(j+1)^rho; G(A,B)<=4A+B then gives\nB_j<=1+4j^rho. Balanced primitive splits and a dominant split with\nright length zero also give B_(2^m-1)>=2lambda^(m-1).\nConsequently, uniformly over the whole normal and specified coupled\nclasses and every b in [0,2],\n\n  limsup B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=2/e<1.\n\nThe explicit nonnormal R^5 construction, for every real b, satisfies\n\n  liminf B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(32lambda e)>0.\n\nThese are exact normalization losses, not a sharp unrestricted profile\nclaim. No all-b sharpness of the upper bound is asserted.\n\nThe unrestricted rank-one coupling is still open: a sufficiently large\nunaligned row can have opposite signs at different queries, and the\njoint transverse-cone argument then requires additional analysis.\nThe original all-schedule/all-horizon target remains unproved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["0576f736952b6e97"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/joint_covariance_rank_one_coupling.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "plan_id": "joint_covariance_rank_one_coupling", "verification_status": "unverified_no_routine_allocation"}, "glossary": {"phi_i": "Scalar gradient polynomial zP_i(z).", "mu_i": "Finite weighted complex mean E phi_i.", "a_i(z,v)": "Complex divided difference[phi_i(z)-phi_i(v)]/(z-v).", "rho_coup": "Nonnegative amplitude of the explicit added real PSD rank-one row.", "omega": "Unit complex number specifying the real projection of the difference of two spectral blocks.", "A_coup": "Explicit generally nonnormal real operator T diag(C(z1),C(z2))(T^T T)^(-1)T^T.", "Z_test": "First-block position projected perpendicular to the first-block last queried gradient; zero in other coordinates.", "v_test": "alpha times Z_test, a single all-final-step terminal test vector.", "G_i": "Initial-direction queried gradient projection <x0,g_i>.", "u_i": "Queried gradient transverse to the unit initial vector, g_i-G_i x0.", "S": "Sum of all retained executed steps; the final free step b is not included.", "K_row": "Uniform temporary residual bound16exp(2S) for the added real row."}}
{"id": "4091883df875c80f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:33:32.270790+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Amended complete joint-cycle evidence adds donor localization: even ALL complex donor arguments with |z|S<=(log m)/4, plus the stated full logarithmic intermediate class, force S X_fast->0 on the actual near-critical retained word.
