Every new estimate used\nbelow is proved here. Earlier unverified spectral findings are not premises.\n\n## 1. Actual word and a whole genuinely joint class\n\nLet m>=2^16, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nd_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1.\nParameters a,c remain fixed within the horizon. Remove the ORIGINAL last\nd_0 step of H_m to obtain the executed prefix H_m^-, of length q=n-1.\nPut S=sum H_m^-, D=d_(m-1), and P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),\n0<=i<=q. The original last displacement is x_q-d_0 g_q.\n\nAllow any finite probability distribution on the union of\n\n  Fast_m = {1-u/m +/- i sqrt(u-theta)/sqrt(m):\n                           5<=u<=6, 1/2<=theta<=1},\n  Intermediate_m = {z: r=|z|>0, t=Re(z)/r in (0,1/32],\n                                          r<=t/(1000d_0)},\n  Slow_m = {z: 0<r=|z|<=1/(8S), 1/2<=Re(z)/r<=3/4},\n\nand optionally z=0. Both imaginary signs are allowed. For probability\nweights w_z use actual vectors in the orthogonal sum of real planes:\n\n  x_i=(sqrt(w_z) Re P_i(z), sqrt(w_z) Im P_i(z))_z,\n  g_i=(sqrt(w_z) Re[zP_i(z)], sqrt(w_z) Im[zP_i(z)])_z.       (1)\n\nThus ||x_0||=1. Allow arbitrary JOINT values f_i satisfying EVERY ordered\nprefix constraint Q_ij<=0, including the optimum jet (0,0,0).\nIndividual component legality and separability of values are not assumed.\nSet tau_m=m/2^28 and C_m=200/49+2/tau_m. For every legal such prefix,\nevery convex 1-smooth interpolant F with F(0)=min F=0, and every b in [0,2],\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q) <= max{\n      (2+129 C_m/128) 3/(32S),\n      (2+6 C_m/5)/(512D)\n  }.                                                       (2)\n\nThis includes the original b=d_0 and the maximal completion with a free\nterminal gradient. Uniformly over all these choices,\n\n  limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n                        <=14379/(6272e)<7/8.                (3)\n\nThis bounds the displayed union, not all normal spectra or unrestricted\nW_n. Sections 7--8 give a definite finite mixture with ALL weights positive\nand one fixed interpolant satisfying\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(64S) for EVERY real b.                    (4)\n\nThe new result closes simultaneous repair by the stated intermediate\nmodes, rather than merely uniting earlier separate class exclusions.\n\n## 2. Uniform physical schedule estimates\n\nFor m>=128,\n\n  12/5<c<5/2, 1/3<a<5/12, 0<a/(c-2)<1,\n  11/8<=d_0<=3/2, 31/16<=d_1<=2.\n\nHere lambda(lambda-2)=1 gives a/(c-2)<1. The upper c bound follows from\nlambda(129/128)<5/2, or sqrt(2)<191/129 after squaring. The a bounds\nfollow directly; d_0=1+a and d_1=2-1/m give the step bounds.\nCounting dyadic multiplicities gives the full-block sum S_j and squared\nsum T_j:\n\n  S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),\n  T_j=2^j-1+2a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2)\n                         +a^2(c^(2j)-2^j)/(c^2-2).\n\nConsequently S_j>=[d_j-(c-1)]/(c-2) and S_j<6d_j. For the second bound\nuse 2^j<=c^j, 1/a<3 and 1/(c-2)<5/2. Whenever d_j>=2000,\n\n  S_j>=(19/10)d_j,\n  T_j<=d_j^2/(c^2-2)+2S_j\n       <=[25/94+12/2000]d_j^2<(7/25)d_j^2.                 (5)\n\nThe T_j bound follows also by subtracting 2S_j from its exact formula.\nHere D>2000.\n\n## 3. One actual cycle and its exact recursive defect\n\nFor z=alpha+i beta put sigma=|z|^2, kappa=alpha-sigma, and\neta=sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2. Let p_j be the polynomial of full H_j\nand R_j=p_j(1-d_jz), so p_(j+1)=R_j p_j. Starting locally at 1, set\nL_j=sum_(i in H_j)h_i|P_i|^2 and\nE_j=sum_(i in H_j)h_i^2 sigma|P_i|^2.\n\nConsider the cycle from the optimum to the end of H_j, backward along\nits queries to its start, then to the optimum. Its exact weight is\n\n  Dcal_j=beta^2 L_j-(kappa/2)(1+|p_j|^2+E_j).              (6)\n\nIndeed the backward path area is beta L_j, the optimum edges have zero\narea, and the energy is 1+|p_j|^2+E_j. Apply the accepted dfde cycle\nidentity beta*Area-kappa*Energy/2. Telescoping norms gives\n1-|p_j|^2=2alpha L_j-E_j. Thus when alpha>0,\n\n  Dcal_j=[eta/(2alpha)](1+E_j)\n                       -[sigma(1-alpha)/(2alpha)]|p_j|^2.\n\nSubstitute E_(j+1)=(1+|R_j|^2)E_j+d_j^2 sigma|p_j|^2 and\np_(j+1)=R_jp_j; use\nd_j^2 sigma|p_j|^2-|R_j|^2=(2d_j alpha-1)|p_j|^2. This gives\n\n  Dcal_(j+1)=(1+|R_j|^2)Dcal_j\n                                +|p_j|^2(kappa+d_j eta).  (7)\n\nThe empty-block value is Dcal_0=-kappa. With p_j^-,Dcal_j^- denoting\nthe same objects after removing the actual last d_0,\n\n  Dcal_j-Dcal_j^-\n       =d_0 sigma[1-alpha-d_0 kappa]|p_j^-|^2.             (8)\n\nTo check (8), the removed reverse edge has weight\nd_0 sigma(1-d_0 alpha+d_0 sigma/2)|p_j^-|^2; the change in the\noptimum-to-end edge is (sigma/2)(|p_j|^2-|p_j^-|^2). Their sum is (8).\n\nUse the LAST H_(m-1)^- copy inside H_m^-. Its starting polynomial is\nR_(m-1) and its endpoint is P_q=R_(m-1)p_(m-1)^-. Hence JOINT legality\nof the SAME indexed, oriented cycle, including the actual optimum, gives\n\n  sum_z w_z |R_(m-1)(z)|^2 Dcal_(m-1)^-(z)<=0.             (9)\n\nAll joint values cancel. No cycle or index is chosen separately by mode.\n\n## 4. Repetition amplifies the FAST positive coefficient\n\nFor Fast_m, direct expansion gives\n\n  kappa=theta/m-u^2/m^2>0,\n  eta=(u-2theta)/m+(u^2+u theta)/m^2-u^3/m^3>=3/m.\n\nAlso alpha>=1-6/m and sigma=alpha-kappa<=1. The first block has\n\n  Dcal_1=d_0 eta-kappa|1-d_0z|^2>=3/m,                    (10)\n\nsince d_0>=11/8, kappa<=1/m, and |1-d_0z|<3/5. For the last estimate\nuse |beta|<=1/20, d_0 alpha>1, and\n|1-d_0z|<=1/2+3/40<3/5. Thus each fast mode is already individually\nillegal on the initial d_0 step.\n\nWrite v=z-1 and A_j=1+1/(a c^j). We have A_j<4, Re v<0 and\n|v|^2<=6/m, whence |1+A_jv|^2<=1+96/m and\n|1+A_jv|<=exp(48/m). The exact product INCLUDING the bridge is\n\n  |R_j|=a(ac)^(2^j-1)\n          product_(ell<j)|1+A_ell v|^(2^(j-ell-1))\n          |1+A_jv|.\n\nIts last product has total exponent 2^j. Since ac=1-1/m<1, if\n2^j<=m/1024, then\n\n  |R_j|<(5/12)exp(3/64)\n        <=(5/12)/(1-3/64)=80/183<1/2.                   (11)\n\nThe exponential inequality follows by comparing positive power series.\n\nAll factors are nonzero since beta!=0. Define Acal_j=Dcal_j/|p_j|^2.\nEquation (7) becomes, for j>=1,\n\n  Acal_(j+1)=(1+|R_j|^(-2))Acal_j\n                          +(kappa+d_j eta)/|R_j|^2.\n\nIt stays positive and nondecreasing at every later level, and grows by\nat least 5 at each level satisfying (11). Let\nk=floor(log_2(m/1024)). Then k>=1, k<m-1 and 2^k>m/2048.\nSince |p_1|<1,\n\n  Acal_(m-1)>=Acal_k>=3*5^(k-1)/m\n               >=3*4^(k-1)/m>3m/2^24>=m/2^23.           (12)\n\nThe removed last factor obeys |1-d_0z|^2>=1/9, since\nd_0 alpha-1>1/3. The subtracted coefficient in (8) is at most 9/m:\nsigma<=1, kappa>0, d_0<=3/2 and 1-alpha<=6/m. Therefore\n\n  Dcal_(m-1)^-/|p_(m-1)^-|^2\n      >=m/(9*2^23)-9/m>=m/(18*2^23)>=tau_m.              (13)\n\nThe middle inequality needs m^2>=162*2^23, which holds for m>=2^16.\nIts contribution to (9) is at least tau_m|P_q|^2. This includes every\nchanged small-step and truncation constant, uniformly in the horizon.\n\n## 5. Intermediate modes and ALL negative credit on the same cycle\n\nFor an intermediate mode put t=alpha/r and h_c=2t/r. Then kappa>0,\neta>=sigma(1-2t^2)>0 and h_c>=2000d_0. Call it reached if D>=h_c,\nand choose its first d_k>=h_c. All steps of H_k contract, d_k>=2000,\nand v=d_kr is in [2t,5t]. By (5) and log(1+x)<=x,\n\n  log|p_k|^2<=-2alpha S_k+sigma T_k\n       <=-(19/5)t v+(7/25)v^2<=-6t^2.                  (14)\n\nFor the last inequality 19u/5-7u^2/25 is concave on [2,5] with endpoint\nvalues 162/25 and 12, both >=6. Nonzero imaginary parts avoid roots.\n\nA reverse path edge has exact weight\n\n  h sigma(1-h alpha+h sigma/2)|P_i|^2\n   =[sigma/(2alpha)](|P_i|^2-|P_(i+1)|^2)\n                      +[h^2 sigma eta/(2alpha)]|P_i|^2.\n\nAdd the two optimum edges and discard nonnegative terms to obtain\n\n  Dcal_k>=[sigma/(2alpha)](1-|p_k|^2)-alpha\n    >=alpha[(1-exp(-6t^2))/(2t^2)-1]>alpha/2.            (15)\n\nThe ratio (1-exp(-6s))/s decreases. Even at t^2=1/4 the bracket is\n2(1-exp(-3/2))-1>1/2, since\nexp(3/2)>1+3/2+9/8+27/48>4. Our t<=1/32 is inside that range.\nSince |p_k|<=1 and kappa+d_jeta>0, recursion (7) propagates\nDcal_(m-1)>=(alpha/2)|p_(m-1)|^2.\n\nFurthermore d_0alpha<=t^2/1000<=1/4000 and d_0sigma<=alpha/1000,\nso |1-d_0z|^2>=1999/2000. Subtracting (8), with its coefficient at\nmost d_0sigma, gives\n\n  Dcal_(m-1)^-\n    >=alpha(1999/4000-1/1000)|p_(m-1)^-|^2\n    >=(49alpha/100)|p_(m-1)^-|^2.                       (16)\n\nFor an unreached mode, all factors of the full word contract and\nalpha<2t^2/D<=1/(512D). Also\nE_(m-1)^-<=sigma T_(m-1)<=(28/25)t^2<=7/25.\nIn (6), beta^2 L>=0, kappa<=alpha, both endpoint moduli <=1, and the\nstarting factor |R_(m-1)|<=1. Thus its total negative credit in (9)\nis at most\n\n  (6/5)alpha.                                           (17)\n\nFor a slow mode every factor contracts because h r<=S r<=1/8<2t.\nThe internal energy is <=sigma sum_(H_(m-1)^-)h^2<=sigma S^2<=1/64.\nThe same calculation bounds its negative credit by\n\n  (129/128)alpha,       alpha<=3/(32S).                  (18)\n\nThe zero mode contributes zero. All these are contributions to ONE\ncycle, with no individual interpolation assumption.\n\n## 6. Joint elimination, actual last step, and exact benchmark\n\nSet X_fast=sum_fast w_z|P_q|^2,\nY_reached=sum_reached w_z alpha|P_q|^2,\nA_slow=sum_slow w_z alpha, A_unreached=sum_unreached w_z alpha.\nFrom (9), (13), and (16)--(18),\n\n  tau_m X_fast+(49/100)Y_reached\n      <=(129/128)A_slow+(6/5)A_unreached=:Credit.         (19)\n\nFor any legal interpolant, optimum interpolation at q and smoothness\nalong the actual terminal displacement imply, for b in [0,2],\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)\n   <=sum_z w_z[2alpha-(1+b(2-b))sigma]|P_q|^2\n   <=2X_fast+2Y_reached+2A_slow+2A_unreached.             (20)\n\nWe used alpha<=1 on fast modes and contraction on slow/unreached\nmodes. No terminal polynomial gradient is imposed. The right side is\nat most (2+129 C_m/128)A_slow+(2+6 C_m/5)A_unreached by (19).\nThe probabilities sum to one; their respective alpha bounds prove (2).\n\nFor the exact comparison, the accepted benchmark has mass joins\n\n  Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n  G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nLet rho=log_2lambda and V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. With t=exp(s),\n\n  d^2/ds^2 log V(exp(s))\n   =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive by integrating rho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0.\nConvexity at s and -s gives V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=2. For\nt=(A/B)^(1/rho), L=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho this reads\n(L-A)(L-B)>=2AB, hence Omega(A,B)<=L. Induction in the primitive\nrecursion yields C_j=S_circle(j)+1<=(j+1)^rho. Since G(A,B)<=4A+B\nand rho>1, the dominant recursion then gives B_j<=1+4j^rho.\n\nFrom the exact sums and D=1+(1-1/m)c^(m-2),\n\n  S/lambda^m -> e,     D/lambda^m -> e/lambda^2.\n\nIndeed a/(c-2)->1, (1+1/m)^m->e, and (2/lambda)^m->0.\nMultiplying (2) by B_n<=1+4n^rho and using tau_m->infinity gives\nthe maximum of\n\n  (4/e)(3/32)[2+(200/49)(129/128)]=14379/(6272e),\n  (4lambda^2/e)(1/512)[2+(200/49)(6/5)]\n                                         =169lambda^2/(3136e).\n\nThe second is smaller since lambda^2<6. The first is <7/8 because\ne>8/3 and 43137/50176<43904/50176. This proves (3) uniformly.\n\nIn particular the prior proposed three-type assignment with fixed\nintermediate r=t/(1000d_0) and slow alpha=3/(32S) cannot have\nX_fast=eta_fixed/S for a fixed eta_fixed>0. Its intermediate threshold\nis reached, so (19) forces\n\n  eta_fixed<=387*2^16/m.\n\nThis rejects that proposed endpoint scale regardless of the proposed\nintermediate amplitude or joint values, within its exact scope.\n\n## 7. Explicit positive weights and EVERY ordered constraint\n\nChoose two fast modes with (u,theta)=(5,1),(6,1), 2m intermediate\nmodes at t_1=1/32,t_2=1/64, r_(j,ell)=t_ell/(1000d_j), and 2m slow\nmodes at t_1=3/4,t_2=1/2, r_j=d_0/(8S d_j), 0<=j<m. Set\nz=r[t+i sqrt(1-t^2)]. These 4m+2 planes give R^(8m+4) in (1).\nThe slow (j,ell)=(0,1) block is the base:\n\n  r=1/(8S), alpha=3/(32S), sigma=1/(64S^2), t=3/4.\n\nWrite p_i=P_i(z_base). All factors contract and 7/8<=|p_i|<=1,\nsince product_(t<i)(1-h_t r)>=1-r sum_(t<i)h_t>=7/8. Define\n\n  f_0^base=3alpha/4,\n  f_(i+1)^base=f_i^base-h_i sigma(1-alpha h_i/2)|p_i|^2.\n\nThe nonnegative decrements total at most sigma S=alpha/6, so\n7alpha/12<=f_i^base<=3alpha/4.\n\nFor EVERY finite pair i<j put T=sum_(i<=s<j)h_s. Telescoping gives\n|p_s-p_i|<=r(sum_(i<=t<s)h_t)|p_i| and hence\n\n  |p_j-p_i|>=rT|p_i|(1-rT/2)>=(15/16)rT|p_i|.            (21)\n\nThe closed chronological polygon i,...,j,i has signed area\nA_ij=sigma sum_(i<=s<t<j)h_s h_t Im(conj(p_s)p_t).\nTelescoping a subproduct minus its conjugate, whose remaining factors\nall contract, gives |Im(conj(p_s)p_t)|<=|p_i|^2|Im z_base|T.\nTherefore\n\n  |(Im z_base)A_ij|<=sigma^2 T^3|p_i|^2/2.               (22)\n\nThe base decrements are trapezoidal work along each segment. Subtract\nthe direct edge identity from the sum of those works: their norm\ndifference terms telescope, leaving beta times the closed polygon\narea. Thus the two ordered pair slacks are exactly\n(kappa/2)|p_j-p_i|^2 +/- beta A_ij.\nSince kappa>=5alpha/6 and sigma T<=alpha/6, (21)--(22) bound them below by\n\n  [1125/3072-1/12]alpha sigma T^2|p_i|^2\n                                 >(alpha sigma/4)T^2|p_i|^2.\n\nEvery step exceeds one, so T>1. All ordered finite residuals are\n<=-delta for delta=alpha sigma/8. The optimum lower slack is\n>=7alpha/12-sigma/2>alpha/2; the upper slack is\n\n  >=(alpha-sigma/2)(49/64)-3alpha/4\n   =alpha[1/64-49/(768S)]>alpha/100>delta,\n\nusing S>=100. Every base pair, including the optimum, is checked.\n\nAssign temporary zero values to the other modes, which need not be\nindividually legal. All chosen |z|<=1, so |P_i|,|zP_i|<=M=exp(S);\nevery ordered residual for temporary zero values is <=K=4M^2.\nChoose\n\n  epsilon=delta/[2(K+delta)], w_base=1-epsilon,\n  w_z=epsilon/(4m+1) for every other block,\n  f_i=(1-epsilon)f_i^base, f_star=0.\n\nAll weights are positive and sum to one. Orthogonality makes each\nactual joint residual the weighted sum of its component residuals:\n\n  Q_ij<=-(1-epsilon)delta+epsilon K=-delta/2<0.             (23)\n\nThis holds for EVERY distinct finite/optimum ordered pair, hence for\nevery cycle on these same data. In particular the illegal fast\ncomponents are repaired jointly, with a formula rather than numerics.\n\nAccepted 1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the concrete function on R^(8m+4):\n\n  H(v)=max_(i in {star,0,...,q})\n      {<g_i,x_i>-f_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2},\n  F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                                  (24)\n\nIt is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, interpolates every\nqueried jet, and has F(0)=min F=0. The chosen initial distance is one.\n\n## 8. One terminal vector checks EVERY anchor for EVERY real b\n\nLet Z be the orthogonal projection of the BASE BLOCK of x_q onto the\nline perpendicular to its base gradient, zero in every other block.\nThen <Z,g_q>=0 and, since epsilon<1/8,\n\n  ||Z||^2=(1-epsilon)|p_q|^2(1-t^2)\n     >=(7/8)(49/64)(7/16)=2401/8192>2/7.\n\nSet v_test=alpha Z, so ||v_test||<=alpha. The optimum anchor in (24)\ngives (2alpha-alpha^2)||Z||^2>=alpha/2.\n\nFor any finite anchor put y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nWe have ||g_i||^2<=sigma+epsilon M^2<=sigma+delta/8<=2sigma.\nThe base block of x_q-y_i has norm <=rS+r<=101/800. Therefore\n\n  2delta_i>=49alpha/48-2sigma,\n  2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,v_test>-||v_test||^2\n    >=alpha[49/48-1/300-101/400-3/3200]>alpha/2.            (25)\n\nWe used S>=100, 2sigma/alpha=1/(3S), alpha<=3/3200, and the fact\nthat other blocks do not contribute to this inner product.\nSince <v_test,g_q>=0, all these anchor inequalities imply for every real b\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)\n   >=min_i{2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,v_test>-||v_test||^2}\n   >=alpha/2=3/(64S),\n\nwith y_star=delta_star=0. This proves (4) for the SAME fixed F and\nthe genuinely free terminal gradient.\n\nBalanced primitive splits give C_(2^(m-1)-1)>=lambda^(m-1), and\nOmega(C_(j-1),1) makes C_j increasing. The dominant split with\nright length zero yields B_n>=2C_(n-1)>=2lambda^(m-1). Hence\n\n  liminf_m B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(32lambda e)>0,\n\nuniformly for every real b. The chosen minimizer is zero and the\ninitial distance to it is one; no closest-minimizer assertion is used.\n\n## 9. Scope left open\n\nThe single joint cycle (19) forces fast endpoint mass O(1/(mS));\nthe specified intermediate donors cannot create a leading fast payoff.\nThis does not close arbitrary normal spectra. Intermediate angles or\nradii outside the stated set, notably moderate-angle frequencies with\nrS of order one or larger, can change the sign and size of the cycle\ncoefficient. Nonnormal coupling and the original all-schedule/all-horizon\nexact-profile target remain unresolved.\n\n\n## 10. A broader donor-localization consequence on the same cycle\n\nThis additional scoped conclusion uses no individual legality. Replace\nSlow_m by the ENTIRE complex disk |z|<=R/S, with no sign or angle\nrestriction, where R>=1/8 may depend on m. Retain Fast_m and the same\nIntermediate_m. Classify each mode only once, with priority Fast_m,\nthen the disk, then Intermediate_m. Let X_fast include all fast modes;\nlet Y_reached include only reached modes in the last category. Then\n\n  tau_m X_fast+(49/100)Y_reached\n   <=max{\n       exp(2R) R(1+R^2/2)/S,\n       3/(1280D)\n     }.                                                   (26)\n\nThe cycle and the fast/reached estimates are exactly the fully derived\n(6)--(16). Here is the complete new credit estimate. For a disk mode,\nevery chronological product has modulus <=exp(r times its executed\nmass). Let R_start be the polynomial at the start of the selected last\ncopy. The two optimum-edge squared moduli are each <=exp(2R).\nFor the internal path energy, each physically scaled prefix product is\nalso <=exp(R), so\n\n  |R_start|^2 E_copy\n      <=sigma exp(2R) sum_copy h_i^2\n      <=R^2 exp(2R).\n\nIn (6), beta^2 L>=0 and max(kappa,0)<=max(alpha,0)<=r. Therefore its\ntotal negative credit is <=exp(2R)r(1+R^2/2), proving the first entry\nof (26). This is valid even when alpha is negative or kappa is negative.\nFor an unreached intermediate mode, (17) and alpha<=1/(512D) give\ncredit <=3/(1280D). Multiplying by the respective initial probabilities,\nwhose sum is <=1, proves (26). The zero mode again contributes zero.\n\nIn particular take R_m=(log m)/4 for sufficiently large m. Then\nexp(2R_m)=sqrt(m) and S/D tends to lambda^2. Consequently\n\n  S X_fast\n   <=2^28 [ R_m(1+R_m^2/2)/sqrt(m) + 3S/(1280mD)]\n   ->0.                                                    (27)\n\nThus the positive fast part of the terminal upper functional (20) is\no(1/S), uniformly for ALL b in [0,2], even when donors have arbitrary\ncomplex arguments and scaled moduli growing as (log m)/4, together with\nthe full specified intermediate class. This is stronger than a list of\nbounded scaled-frequency exclusions.\n\nThis does NOT mean that the projected non-fast data remain legal after\ndiscarding the fast blocks, or that their optimal objective is unchanged.\nEquation (27) only controls their fast endpoint moment in the SAME joint\nlegal data and hence its explicitly bounded contribution to (20). It is\nnot an upper bound on the entire enlarged class or on W_n.\n\nThe all-positive construction of Sections 7--8 is already contained in\nthis enlarged disk class once R_m>=1/8. If finitely many additional disk\nparameters are prescribed, all with |z|<=1, the very same proof of\n(21)--(25) includes them by changing the equal non-base denominator from\n4m+1 to the actual number of non-base blocks. K=4exp(2S) is unchanged;\nthe bound on the SUM of their gradient squared norms uses epsilon M^2,\nnot their count. Thus every ordered pair and the fixed all-real-b payoff\nremain checked, even for added individually illegal disk modes.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["041069a3df853d6c", "e5b2279554b01471"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/joint_fast_intermediate_slow_obstruction.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "verification_status": "unverified_no_routine_allocation"}, "glossary": {"R_start": "Actual polynomial at the beginning of the common last recursive copy.", "R_m": "Donor scaled-radius(log m)/4 in the added consequence.", "X_fast": "Weighted fast squared endpoint mass, counting every fast parameter before assigning remaining modes to disk or intermediate categories."}}
{"id": "03d773db330bf6cf", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:39:16.246414+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "New all-initial-point steep-ramp obstruction on the ACTUAL near-critical ruler: g=mu x inside radius R, g=x-(1-mu)R sign(x) outside. For every 0<mu<=3/d_(m-1), all R and all starts, limsup B*payoff<=12/[e(1+sqrt2)^2]<1, with an explicit finite bound. Separately, at high core curvature mu=1/d_0 the exact entire-word map is -sign(x) U_m(|x|-R T_m)_+, so the exact supremum payoff is U_m^2. Proof also establishes full-prefix invariance for unequal quadratic cores. Intermediate/high nonresonant ramp curvatures remain open; candidate only.", "evidence": "# Unequal quadratic rays on the actual critical ruler\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No solver, trajectory sweep or\nfact_submit is allocated. All new claims are proved together here. The only\naccepted input is the benchmark recurrence in b98bb807d514e80d. This result\ndoes not bound the unrestricted worst-case objective or all scalar functions.\n\n## 1. Whole-family statement\n\nFor an integer m>=128 keep the following parameters fixed during the run:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    r=c-2, d_j=1+a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1,\n    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=S_j+1, K=1/(2S_m+1).\n\nThe H_j here are schedules, not the conjugate quadratics in the project\nglossary. For ANY two curvatures u,v in[0,1], define one fixed derivative\nand objective by\n\n    g(x)=u x for x>=0, g(x)=v x for x<=0,\n    f(x)=u x^2/2 for x>=0, f(x)=v x^2/2 for x<=0.       (1)\n\nThe derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz; f is convex,\ndifferentiable and1-smooth, with minimizer0. For positive curvatures the\nminimizer is unique. Both curvatures may depend arbitrarily on m. For every\nnonzero starting point, exact GD with H_m satisfies\n\n  2f(x_n)/x_0^2 <= E_m := max{\n       1/(2e S_m),\n       d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),\n       r^2/(2e S_(m-1))\n  } < (3/4)K.                                         (2)\n\nFor the exact benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1,\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) E_m <= 2/e^2 <1.     (3)\n\nThe same upper bound holds for arbitrary finite orthogonal direct sums\nof (1), with positive coordinate curvatures, translations of their unique\nminimizer and arbitrary orthogonal changes of basis. Thus independent\none-sided quadratic curvatures, including horizon-dependent unequal cores\nthat extend over the entire rays, cannot supply the target witness here.\n\n## 2. Parameter estimates and the scalar product\n\nDirectly from the ruler recurrence,\n\n    M_j=(a/r)c^j+(1-a/r)2^j.                           (4)\n\nFor m>=128 the following deliberately loose rational bounds hold:\n\n    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,\n    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,\n    ac=1-1/m>7/8, 0<a<r<1/2, cr<5/4.                 (5)\n\nFor example lambda<17/7 gives c<2193/896<49/20.\nThen a>(127/128)/(49/20)>3/8; also\na=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2<r.\nEquation(4) implies\n\n    S_j >= a c^j/r-1,\n    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j.                         (6)\n\nLet\n\n    P_j(t)=product_(l=0)^(j-1)(1-d_l t)^(2^(j-l-1)),\n    P_0(t)=1.                                        (7)\n\nBefore the first level crossing for curvature t all these factors are\npositive and\n\n    0<P_j(t)<=exp(-S_j t).                            (8)\n\nWe first prove (2) when u>=v>0 and neither d_j u nor d_j v equals1.\nReflection deals with v>u. Positive homogeneity reduces initial points\nto+1 and-1. Finally, finite compositions of the continuous maps\nx -> x-d_j g(x), and the objective itself, are continuous in u,v.\nThis extends the proved inequalities to zero factors and zero curvatures.\n\nDefine p and q as the first nonnegative levels with d_p u>1 and d_q v>1,\nrespectively. They exist because a>0,c>1,u,v>0, and p<=q.\n\n## 3. Same crossing level: two swapping maps\n\nSuppose p=q<m. H_p preserves both signs, and H_(p+1) swaps them.\nWrite A_j for the magnitude at the end of H_j starting at+1, and B_j\nfor that starting at-1. At j=p+1,\n\n    A_(p+1)=(d_p u-1)P_p(u)P_p(v),\n    B_(p+1)=(d_p v-1)P_p(u)P_p(v).                    (9)\n\nAll subsequent H_j also swap signs: the three maps H_j, the central\nd_j step and H_j each swap. Consequently, for j>=p+1,\n\n    A_(j+1)=(d_j v-1)A_j^2,\n    B_(j+1)=(d_j u-1)B_j^2.                          (10)\n\nFor either of these recurrences, let t be the output-ray curvature\n(v for A, u for B), let Z_j be its magnitude, and put\n\n    Y_j=c a c^j t Z_j.\n\nAn exact substitution in(10) gives\n\n    Y_(j+1)=[1-(1-t)/(a c^j t)]Y_j^2 <= Y_j^2.        (11)\n\nThe bracket lies in[0,1] because all these levels cross and t<=1.\n\nIt remains to control the actual entry value, including both curvatures.\nIf p=0, (9) gives\n\n    c a c v A_1=c^2 a v(d_0 u-1)<=(ac)^2 v<=1,\n\nand the other starting sign has the symmetric bound.\nIf p>=1, then u,v<1/d_(p-1)<=1/d_0<8/11. Put\nU=a c^p u and V=a c^p v. From(6),(8),(9),\n\n    c a c^(p+1) v A_(p+1)\n       <=c^2 U V exp(-(U+V)/r+u+v)\n       <=(cr)^2 exp(u+v-2)<1.                        (12)\n\nThe middle inequality maximizes z exp(-z/r) at z=r. The last uses\ncr<5/4 and exp(6/11)>1+6/11+18/121=205/121>25/16.\nThe bound for B is identical with u,v exchanged. Iterating(11) proves\nY_m<=1 for both initial signs. Since the terminal curvature t crosses\nat a level<=m-1, t>1/d_(m-1), and hence\n\n    2f(x_n)=t Z_m^2\n       <=1/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t)\n       <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)).                  (13)\n\n## 4. Different crossing levels: an exact sign reset\n\nSuppose p<q and p<m. The level-p step maps the high-curvature ray to\nthe low-curvature ray and preserves the low ray. Thus H_(p+1) maps\nBOTH signs to the low ray. Every subsequent H_j still has this\nconstant sign map, because the final H_j in H_(j+1) returns to that\nray regardless of what the central step does.\n\nFor p+1<=j<=q, low-ray initial data have not yet crossed, so\n\n    B_j=P_j(v),\n    A_j=D_p P_j(v)/[P_p(v)(1-d_p v)],\n    D_p=(d_p u-1)P_p(u).                             (14)\n\nOne can also obtain this identity by splitting the actual run into its\ninitial high-ray H_p and d_p step, followed by low-ray steps of total\nmass S_j-S_p-d_p. No queries or spatial pieces are chosen independently.\n\nFor j>=q the central step crosses from low to high, after which H_j\nreturns to low. The exact magnitude recurrences are\n\n    A_(j+1)=(d_j v-1)A_j^2,\n    B_(j+1)=(d_j v-1)A_j B_j.                        (15)\n\nIn particular, with Z_j=max(A_j,B_j),\nZ_(j+1)<=(d_j v-1)Z_j^2. Therefore the scaled Y_j=c a c^j v Z_j\nagain satisfies(11), with <= in place of its first equality.\n\nAssume first q<m. We prove the needed entry Y_q<=1.\nFor B_q=P_q(v), q>=1 and v<1/d_(q-1)<=1/d_0<8/11. With\nV=a c^q v, (6),(8) give\n\n    c V B_q <= c V exp(-V/r+v)\n               <=cr exp(v-1)<1,                    (16)\n\nwhere exp(3/11)>1+3/11>5/4 was used.\n\nFor A_q observe that S_q-S_p-d_p>=S_(q-1), because p<=q-1 and\nS_p+d_p<=S_(q-1)+d_(q-1). Equation(14) therefore gives\n\n    A_q<=D_p exp(-v S_(q-1)).                        (17)\n\nIf p>=1, setting U=a c^p u and using(6),(8) yields\n\n    D_p<=U exp(-U/r+u)<=r exp(u-1).\n\nSince u,v<8/11, (17) implies\n\n    c V A_q<=c r exp(u-1)V exp(-V/(cr)+v)\n                <=(cr)^2 exp(u+v-2)<1.              (18)\n\nIf p=0,q=1, the entry is exact:\nA_1=d_0u-1<=a, and c a c v A_1<=(ac)^2v<=1.\nIf p=0,q>=2, use D_0<=a and v<1/d_0<8/11 in(17) to get\n\n    c V A_q<=a c^2 r exp(v-1)\n                <=cr exp(v-1)<1,                    (19)\n\nbecause ac<1. Equations(16),(18),(19) cover every entry regime. Now\n(15),(11) give Y_m<=1. The terminal ray is low, whose curvature\nv>1/d_(m-1), so (13) applies to BOTH starting signs.\n\nAssume instead q>=m. Starting on the low ray never crosses and gives\nv P_m(v)^2<=1/(2e S_m) by(8). Starting on the high ray crosses once\nat level p, after which every actual step preserves the low ray. The\nexact decomposition underlying(14) and the proved bound D_p<=r give\n\n    A_m<=r exp[-v(S_m-S_p-d_p)]\n         <=r exp(-v S_(m-1)).\n\nConsequently\n\n    2f(x_n)=v A_m^2<=r^2/(2e S_(m-1)).               (20)\n\nFinally, if p>=m neither ray crosses: (8) gives the first term in(2)\nfor each initial sign. This completes all generic cases, and continuity\nas explained above completes (2)'s non-strict upper bound.\n\n## 5. Uniform finite and benchmark constants\n\nEach of the three entries in E_m is less than(3/4)K.\nFor the first, S_m>=1 and e>2 give\n\n    (2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4.\n\nFor the second, M_m<=c^m and ac>7/8 imply\n\n (2S_m+1)d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))\n  <= 2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]\n  <= 16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                      (21)\n\nHere c>2,m>=3 bound the first summand, and c>12/5 bounds the second.\nFor the third, put S=S_(m-1)>=1. Since S_m<=c(S+1)-1, c<5/2,r<1/2,\n\n    (2S_m+1)r^2/(2e S)\n       <=r^2(4c-1)/(2e)<9/(8e)<3/4.                 (22)\n\nFor completeness, derive the benchmark bound directly from the accepted\nrecurrence rather than use another unverified candidate as a premise.\nWrite rho=log_2(lambda), C_N=S_circle(N)+1. Its mass join is\nOmega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2. The dominant join is\nG(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nFor t>0 set F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. With t=exp(z), differentiation gives\n\n (d^2/dz^2)log F(exp(z))\n =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive because rho>1. Convexity at z,-z therefore\ngives F(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and D=(1+t)^rho B, this\nsays (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. It follows that\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho. Induction in the accepted\nprimitive recurrence yields C_N<=(N+1)^rho. Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,\ninduction in the accepted dominant recurrence gives\n\n    B_N<=1+4N^rho.                                  (23)\n\nAt a split N=k+l+1 the induction uses\n(k+1)^rho+l^rho<=(k+l+1)^rho; this holds for nonnegative arguments\nbecause rho>1. The base N=0 is B_0=1.\n\nEquation(4) and the actual horizon-dependent parameters give\n\n    c^m/lambda^m -> e,\n    S_m/c^m ->1, S_(m-1)/c^(m-1)->1,\n    a->1/lambda, r->1/lambda.\n\nMultiplying the three entries in E_m by the upper bound(23), at\nN=2^m-1, gives limits at most, respectively,\n\n    2/e^2, 4/(e lambda^2), 2/(e^2 lambda).           (24)\n\nThe first is the largest: lambda>1 and 2e<lambda^2. One elementary\ncheck is e<49/18<11/4 while lambda^2>29/5>11/2. The e bound follows\nby bounding the factorial tail from3 onward by a geometric series\nwith first term1/6 and ratio1/4. This proves(3), uniformly over all\nhorizon-dependent choices of curvatures and starting signs.\n\nFor finite orthogonal direct sums, the GD recursions separate by\ncoordinate and the normalized terminal objective is a weighted average\nof coordinate normalized objectives, with weights proportional to their\nsquared initial displacements. This proves the stated extension.\n\n## 6. Scope and audit\n\nThe crucial new mechanism is the exact constant-sign map when the two\ncurvatures have different crossing levels. It is not an assertion that\nall scalar maps are controlled by their largest secant. Spatially varying\nderivatives can give amplitude-dependent crossing levels, so their\nrecursive maps need not be sign-constant and equations(14)-(15) fail.\n\nNo saturated initial jet, equal-ray curvature, fixed-m-independent\ncurvature or small-core-curvature assumption was made. Zero factors\nand zero curvatures are included by an actual continuity argument.\nThe objective in every case is the complete area in(1), with true\ninitial distance to the unique minimizer for u,v>0. This is a precisely\nscoped obstruction, not the original all-schedule theorem.\n\nThe literature check used Matlas for the sign-switching model; its hits\nwere not applicable. The exact local copy of Altschuler--Parrilo,\narXiv:2309.16530, Section2 and the opening of Section3, was read for\nthe ruler recursion and the importance of full trajectory consistency.\nNo external theorem is a premise of this proof; the altered ruler's\nmass formula and all estimates were rederived above.\n\n## 7. Every actual prefix stays inside the initial radius\n\nThe same proof also gives the following statement for the entire\ntwo-ray family(1): every prefix of H_m has |x_t|<=|x_0|. This is stronger\nthan a bound only at the complete ruler endpoints.\n\nFor clarity define J_j as the maximum magnitude of the output of the\nword(H_j,d_j), over the two starting points+1,-1. Define L_j as the\nmaximum magnitude over all prefixes of H_j and those starting points.\nPositive homogeneity implies\n\n    L_(j+1)<=L_j max(1,J_j), L_0=1.                  (25)\n\nHere the first H_j accounts for L_j. The central step produces an\ninput of magnitude at most J_j for the second H_j, whose every prefix\nhas magnitude at most J_j L_j.\n\nIn the notation p<=q of Sections3-4, every J_j<=1, as follows.\nFor j<p both multipliers preserve sign and have magnitude at most1.\nAt j=p the crossing-ray magnitude is D_p=(d_p u-1)P_p(u)<=r<1\nif p>=1, and D_0<=a<1 if p=0. The other ray either crosses with the\nsame bound (p=q), or stays on its ray with magnitude<=1.\nFor p<j<q, equation(14) has B_j=P_j(v)<=1 and A_(p+1)<=r<1;\nthe recurrence A_(j+1)=A_j B_j(1-d_jv) keeps A_j<=1. The central\nlow-ray multiplier has magnitude<=1, so J_j<=1 in this regime.\nFor j>=q in the different-threshold case, (16)-(19) and (11) give\n\n    J_j=(d_jv-1)max(A_j,B_j)<=Y_j/c<=1/c.\n\nFor j>=p+1 in the matched-threshold case, (11)-(12) give the same\nbound separately for both rays, using their respective output curvature.\nThese cases cover all j. Equation(25), followed by the earlier continuity\nargument for boundary curvatures, proves L_m<=1.\n\nConsequently, let a fixed globally convex1-smooth scalar function have\nderivative u x on[0,R] and v x on[-R,0], with any legal extension outside\nthis interval. Every run starting inside[-R,R] stays there and equals\nthe corresponding two-ray quadratic run at EVERY query and endpoint.\nIts final normalized objective is therefore bounded by(2), since its\nfunction-value difference from f(0) is the same integral inside the core.\nThis assertion does not apply to a suffix started at an arbitrary time.\n\n## 8. A steep outer-ramp family, all starting points\n\nThis section adds genuine spatial variation and controls ALL starting\npoints, without assuming that entering the core means permanent capture.\nFor any radius R>0 and curvature0<mu<=1 define the ONE FIXED derivative\n\n g_mu,R(x) = mu x,                           |x|<=R,\n            x-(1-mu)R sign(x),              |x|>=R,     (26)\n\nand let f_mu,R(x)=integral_0^x g_mu,R(t)dt. It is convex and1-smooth,\nits unique minimizer is0, and its derivative slopes are mu and1.\nThe outer radial elasticity is greater than1 when mu<1, so the\nsubpower hypotheses of the earlier scalar-clock candidates do not apply.\nThe radius, curvature and starting point may depend on the horizon.\n\nPut\n\n    U_m=product_(h in H_m)(h-1)=(ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m),\n    Qbar_m=max{1/(2e S_m),d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))}.\n\nFor 0<mu<1/d_0 define the explicit first-step shadow radius\n\n    R_sh(mu)=(2+a-2d_0 mu)/a>1.                       (27)\n\nThen, uniformly over every R>0 and every x_0!=0,\n\n  2f_mu,R(x_n)/x_0^2\n   <=max{mu P_m(mu)^2, U_m^2+mu/R_sh(mu)^2}\n   <=max{Qbar_m, U_m^2+mu/R_sh(mu)^2}.                (28)\n\nIn particular, for the WHOLE definite family\n\n    0<mu<=3/d_(m-1), R>0, x_0!=0, m>=128,           (29)\n\nlet C_sh,m=(2+a-6d_0/d_(m-1))/a. Then C_sh,m>1 and\n\n  2f_mu,R(x_n)/x_0^2\n     <=E_ramp,m:=max{Qbar_m,U_m^2+3/[d_(m-1)C_sh,m^2]},\n\n  limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) E_ramp,m\n     <=12/[e(1+sqrt(2))^2]<1.                       (30)\n\nThus horizon-dependent critical-scale inner curvature, coupled to a\nslope1 outer ramp, is another entire fixed-function ansatz that cannot\nsupply the target payoff. Queries in its outer regions are not required\nto have constant gradients. No initial gradient saturation is imposed.\n\n### 8.1. Exact suffix products and a global clipped-radius estimate\n\nScale space by R, so the core radius is1, and set tau=1-mu. Write\nz(x)=max{mu,|x|-tau}=mu+(|x|-1)_+. For any step h=d_j>1 and its\nactual GD output x^+=x-h g_mu,1(x), direct substitution gives\n\n    z(x^+)<=max{mu,(h-1)z(x)-2tau}\n            <=max{mu,(h-1)z(x)}.                    (31)\n\nIndeed, outside the core |g(x)|=|x|-tau=z(x), and\n|x^+|=|tau-(h-1)z(x)|. Therefore z(x^+) equals the first maximum\nin(31), because the other absolute-value branch contributes\n-(h-1)z(x)<=0. Inside the core, |x^+|<=|1-hmu|. If this bound\nexceeds1, then hmu>2 and z(x^+)<=hmu-2+mu\n=(h-1)mu-2tau; otherwise z(x^+)=mu. This proves(31) in both regions.\n\nEvery suffix of H_m has product of(h-1) at most1, including the empty\nsuffix. To see this without any orbit assumption, let V_j be the maximum\nof these products for H_j. Products are nonnegative and\n\n    V_(j+1)=V_j max{1,(d_j-1)U_j}=V_j,\n\nbecause (d_j-1)U_j=a(ac)^(2^j-1)<=a<1. With V_0=1 this gives V_m=1.\nUnrolling the second inequality in(31) therefore proves the uniform\nterminal estimate\n\n    z(x_n)<=max{mu,U_m z(x_0)}.                      (32)\n\nThis argument includes every crossing and every ordinary-block query;\nit makes no permanent-core-capture assumption.\n\nFor |x_0|=r_0>=1, z(x_0)=r_0-tau. If |x_n|<=1, then\n2f_mu,1(x_n)<=mu. If |x_n|>1, the exact area is\n\n    2f_mu,1(x_n)=z(x_n)^2+mu tau.\n\nIn this latter case z(x_n)>mu, so(32) forces\nz(x_n)<=U_m(r_0-tau). Thus in either case\n\n    2f_mu,1(x_n)/r_0^2<=U_m^2+mu/r_0^2.              (33)\n\n### 8.2. An exact first-step shadow for the remaining starts\n\nAssume0<mu<1/d_0, so k_0=1-d_0mu>0. On the core the first step\nmaps[-1,1] onto[-k_0,k_0]. For a positive outer initial point r_0>=1,\nits actual first iterate is\n\n    x_1=d_0(1-mu)-a r_0.\n\nIt belongs to[-k_0,k_0] precisely when1<=r_0<=R_sh(mu). For such\nr_0 choose z_0=x_1/k_0, so |z_0|<=1. The two runs have the same\npoint after their first step. Section7 proves that the run from z_0\nremains in the core forever on the FULL known schedule. Hence all\nsubsequent points of the run from r_0 equal its quadratic-core points,\nand the actual normalized objective is\n\n    mu P_m(mu)^2 (z_0/r_0)^2<=mu P_m(mu)^2.          (34)\n\nNegative starts follow by oddness. Starts inside the core are already\ncovered by Section7. For |x_0|>R_sh(mu), (33) gives the second term\nof(28). Equations(33)-(34) prove the first inequality in(28).\n\nFor the second inequality in(28), specialize the direct proof in\nSections2-3 to u=v=mu. With no crossing, (8) gives\nmu P_m(mu)^2<=1/(2eS_m); with a crossing, (13) gives its other term.\nNo different-threshold term is needed for a single quadratic curvature.\n\n### 8.3. Exact-benchmark comparison and scope\n\nFor m>=128, d_(m-1)>=d_3>1+(3/8)(12/5)^3>3d_0, since d_0<3/2.\nTherefore every mu in(29) is less than1/d_0, as required in(27).\nThe function mu/R_sh(mu)^2 is increasing on this interval (its\nnumerator increases and its positive denominator decreases). This\nproves the finite E_ramp,m bound in(30).\n\nFrom the actual parameters,\n\n    C_sh,m -> 1+2lambda=lambda^2,\n    d_(m-1)/c^m ->1/lambda^2,\n    c^m/lambda^m ->e.\n\nThe benchmark estimate(23) gives limsup B_n Qbar_m<=2/e^2 and\nB_n U_m^2->0, the latter already following from U_m<=c^(-m).\nThe remaining term has limit superior at most12/(e lambda^2), which\nis larger than2/e^2 and strictly less than1. For explicit inequalities,\nlambda^2<6, e>1 imply6e>lambda^2, while e>8/3 and lambda^2>29/5\ngive e lambda^2>232/15>12. This completes(30).\n\nMore generally the same displayed proof, with3 replaced by any fixed\npositive kappa, gives limit-superior bound\nmax{2/e^2,4kappa/(e lambda^2)}. Its below-benchmark consequence holds\nwhen kappa<e lambda^2/4. This is a quantitative range statement, not\na claim about the larger-curvature steep-ramp family.\n\nAt mu=0 the unique-minimizer hypothesis changes: f is half the squared\ndistance to[-R,R]. If y=(|x|-R)_+, every step h>1 obeys exactly\ny^+=[(h-1)y-2R]_+, until it reaches zero and stays there. Thus even\nwith normalization by the true closest initial distance y_0>0, the\nnormalized final objective is at most U_m^2. This boundary does not\ncreate an additional witness.\n\n## 9. A core entry can be followed by a genuine re-exit\n\nDo not generalize Section7 to a suffix beginning at an arbitrary\ncapture time. Here is an exact countertest on the actual first four\nsteps d_0,d_1,d_0,d_2, all part of H_m for m>=128.\n\nUse the fixed ramp(26) with R=1, mu=3/4, tau=1/4, and define\n\n    z=(4/5+d_0/4)/a,\n    y=(z+d_1/4)/(d_1-1),\n    X=(y+d_0/4)/a, x_0=-X.\n\nThese three numbers X,y,z exceed1: a<1/2 and0<d_1-1<1 suffice.\nThe actual iterates, computed on the same two ramp pieces, are\n\n    x_1=y>1, x_2=-z<-1, x_3=4/5 in(-1,1),\n    x_4=(4/5)(1-3d_2/4)<-1.\n\nFor the last inequality, d_2=1+(1-1/m)c>541/160, so\n(4/5)(3d_2/4-1)>983/800>1. The first three equalities follow\nrespectively from aX-d_0/4=y, -(d_1-1)y+d_1/4=-z,\nand az-d_0/4=4/5. All spatial slopes and areas are those of(26),\nand0 is the unique minimizer.\n\nThis refutes permanent capture at the FIRST arbitrary core entry for\nthe steep-ramp family. It does not refute the all-initial-point estimate\n(28), which never uses that assumption, and mu=3/4 is outside the\ncritical-scale range(29) for large m. No full-tail benchmark payoff is\nclaimed for this countertest.\n\n## 10. Exact high-curvature steep-ramp calibration\n\nThere is a second, disjoint regime where the entire nonlinear dynamics\ncan be written explicitly. In(26), fix mu=1/d_0=1/(1+a), so\ntau=1-mu=a/(1+a). The first-step core curvature is reciprocal, but\nthe outer slope remains1 and the initial point is unrestricted.\n\nFor j>=1 define T_1=1 and, with beta_j=d_j-1=a c^j,\n\n    T_(j+1)=T_j+(T_j+d_j tau)/(beta_j U_j).           (35)\n\nThen the EXACT map of the full word H_j on the fixed function is\n\n    Phi_j(x)=-sign(x) U_j (|x|-R T_j)_+.             (36)\n\nIn particular, its worst normalized final objective over every nonzero\ninitial point and every R>0 is exactly\n\n    sup 2f_mu,R(Phi_m(x_0))/x_0^2=U_m^2,\n    B_(2^m-1) U_m^2 ->0.                            (37)\n\nTo prove this, scale R=1. The first map is exactly\nPhi_1(x)=-a sign(x)(|x|-1)_+, proving the base. Suppose(36) holds\nat level j. We first ensure that the central step's core output is\ninside the interval killed by the final Phi_j. Since\n\n    beta_j U_j=a(ac)^(2^j-1)<=a,\n\nequation(35) implies T_(j+1)>=T_j(1+1/a)>c T_j.\nThus T_j>=c^(j-1) for every j>=1. Also\n\n    d_j mu-1=a(c^j-1)/(1+a)<c^(j-1)<=T_j,           (38)\n\nwhere ac<1 was used. Write z=U_j(|x|-T_j)_+, the magnitude after\nthe first Phi_j. If z<=1, the central step has magnitude\n(d_j mu-1)z<=T_j, so the final Phi_j kills it. If z>=1, the\ncentral step reverses sign and has magnitude beta_j z-d_j tau;\nthis is nonnegative because its value at z=1 is d_j mu-1>0.\nIts output exceeds T_j precisely when\n\n    z>(T_j+d_j tau)/beta_j>=1,\n\nthe last inequality being(38). The final Phi_j therefore gives\n\n -sign(x) U_j[beta_j U_j(|x|-T_j)-d_j tau-T_j]_+\n =-sign(x) U_(j+1)(|x|-T_(j+1))_+,\n\nbecause U_(j+1)=beta_j U_j^2. This proves the induction and(36).\n\nFor the payoff, f_mu,R(x)<=x^2/2 and(36) give the upper bound U_m^2.\nAs |x_0| tends to infinity at fixed m,R, the ratio\n|Phi_m(x_0)|/|x_0| tends to U_m, and the exact outer quadratic\narea gives 2f_mu,R(Phi_m(x_0))/Phi_m(x_0)^2 ->1.\nThus the supremum is precisely U_m^2. Its benchmark-scaled limit\nvanishes by(23) and U_m<=c^(-m).\n\nThis calibration is an exact renormalization of one steep-ramp core\ncurvature, not a theorem for all curvatures between the range(29)\nand mu=1/d_0. The critical-scale class(29), the exact calibration(36)\nand the re-exit counterexample in Section9 have separate scopes.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["f07e30eaf6cc2f95", "63a60cbd357b04c2"], "local_artifact": "research/max_unequal_ray_quadratic_closure.md"}, "glossary": {"g_mu,R": "Fixed scalar derivative with core curvature mu on[-R,R] and outer slope1, equation(26).", "f_mu,R": "Integral of g_mu,R from0; unique minimizer0 for mu>0.", "R_sh(mu)": "Explicit outer-start shadow radius (2+a-2d_0 mu)/a for mu<1/d_0, after scaling core radius to1.", "U_m": "Product of(h-1) over the actual H_m word: (ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m).", "Qbar_m": "Maximum of the two quadratic objective caps defined in Section8.", "C_sh,m": "Minimum first-step shadow radius over0<mu<=3/d_(m-1).", "E_ramp,m": "Finite all-initial-point upper bound max(Qbar_m,U_m^2+3/(d_(m-1)C_sh,m^2)).", "T_j_ramp": "Threshold in the exact resonant map, written T_j in Section10.", "z_clip(x)": "Clipped gradient radius mu+(|x|-1)_+, written z(x) after scaling R=1."}}

This closes one full sign-reversing two-block model, not arbitrary rank-one correlations.", "evidence": "# Joint covariance and an explicit nonnormal rank-one coupling\n\nStatus: complete UNVERIFIED candidate; no routine fact_submit allocation,\nnumerical solver, or new worker round. Accepted inputs are\n1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and free completion), dfde2fa326c908bf\n(polynomial notation), and b98bb807d514e80d (benchmark recursions).\nAll new geometry, inequalities, and construction checks appear below.\n\n## 1. Exact whole-class bounds on any retained schedule\n\nLet h_0,...,h_(q-1)>=0 have sum S>0. Let\nP_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), 0<=i<=q, and phi_i(z)=zP_i(z).\nFor ANY finite probability weights w_z at complex parameters satisfying\n\n  Re z>=0,             S|z|<=1/3,                       (1)\n\nform the real orthogonal-block vectors\nx_i=(sqrt(w_z)Re P_i(z),sqrt(w_z)Im P_i(z))_z and\ng_i=(sqrt(w_z)Re phi_i(z),sqrt(w_z)Im phi_i(z))_z.\nThus x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i and ||x_0||=1.\n\nAssume arbitrary JOINT legal values f_i, including the optimum (0,0,0).\nFor every interpolating convex 1-smooth F with minimum zero, and every\ngenuinely free terminal step b in [0,2],\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)<=1/[2S+1+b(2-b)].                        (2)\n\nThe statement is about all weights and all joint values; it does not infer\nlegality of the separate modes. A reflected complex block has the same\nGram data and fixes the initial direction. Thus we may take Im z>=0\nindependently in each normal block when proving this theorem.\n\nThere is also a specific nonnormal extension. Take TWO different parameters\nz_1,z_2 with Re z_l,Im z_l>=0 and S|z_l|<=1/3, and weights w_1,w_2>0\nwith sum one. Take\na complex number omega with |omega|=1, and rho_coup>=0. Append the coordinate\n\n  x_i^(5)=sqrt(rho_coup) Re[omega(P_i(z_1)-P_i(z_2))],\n  g_i^(5)=sqrt(rho_coup) Re[omega(phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2))]   (3)\n\nto the four normal coordinates. It starts at zero, so ||x_0||=1 exactly.\nThe same bound (2) holds for EVERY jointly legal prefix of these actual\nfive-dimensional vectors under either of the following conditions:\n\n  (a) omega(z_1-z_2)>0 is real; rho_coup is arbitrary, or\n  (b) rho_coup<=w_1 w_2/144; omega is arbitrary.             (4)\n\nThese are whole coupling classes, with no bound on the conditioning in (a).\nAt the smaller radius S|z_l|<=1/4, condition (b) improves to\n\n  rho_coup<=(449/256)w_1w_2.                              (5)\n\nOnly this explicit finite-dimensional rank-one coupling is claimed;\narbitrary cross-mode covariance is not being reparameterized.\n\n## 2. Divided differences and a finite covariance identity\n\nWork in the convex quarter disk Re z,Im z>=0, |z|S<=R. For every factor,\n\n  |1-hz|^2=1-2h Re z+h^2|z|^2<=exp(h^2|z|^2).\n\nAll prefix products and products omitting any factors therefore have\nmodulus <=exp(R^2/2), since sum h^2<=S^2. Telescoping P_i-1 and\ndifferentiating its product give\n\n  |P_i-1|<=|z|S exp(R^2/2),\n  |zP_i'|<=|z|S exp(R^2/2),\n  |phi_i'-1|<=2R exp(R^2/2).                             (6)\n\nFor R=1/3, exp(1/18)<=18/17 by positive power series comparison.\nThus all derivatives lie in the disk centered at 1 of radius\nd=12/17<1/sqrt(2). For R=1/4 one may instead take d=16/31.\n\nFor any two distinct z,v in the quarter disk, define\na_i(z,v)=[phi_i(z)-phi_i(v)]/(z-v). Integrating phi_i' along the straight\nsegment between them shows |a_i-1|<=d. A complex number a in this disk\nhas positive real part and |arg a|<=arcsin d. Indeed\n2Re a>=|a|^2+1-d^2>=2|a|sqrt(1-d^2).\nConsequently, for every i,j and every z,v,\n\n  Re[(phi_i(z)-phi_i(v))conj(phi_j(z)-phi_j(v))]\n   >=(1-2d^2)|z-v|^2 |a_i(z,v)||a_j(z,v)|>=0.             (7)\n\nThe coincident-parameter case is zero. At R=1/3, 1-2d^2=1/289.\n\nWe also need the signs of the means, not just covariance. Write nonzero\nz=r exp(i theta), 0<=theta<=pi/2. Since hr<=R<=1/3, each factor\nhas positive real part. Its negative argument is\natan(h r sin theta/(1-h r cos theta)). The sum of these arguments is at most\n\n  rS sin theta/(1-rS cos theta)\n      <=[R/(1-R)]theta<=theta/2.\n\nThus arg phi_i(z) lies in [theta/2,theta]; both Re phi_i(z) and\nIm phi_i(z) are nonnegative. The zero parameter is immediate.\n\nLet expectation denote the finite weighted sum and put mu_i=E phi_i.\nA direct expansion, with z,v independent under the same weights, gives\n\n  Re[E(phi_i conj(phi_j))-mu_i conj(mu_j)]\n   =(1/2)E_(z,v) Re[(phi_i(z)-phi_i(v))\n                                conj(phi_j(z)-phi_j(v))]>=0.  (8)\n\nSet e=x_0, G_i=<e,g_i>=Re mu_i, u_i=g_i-G_i e. Then G_i>=0 and\n\n  <u_i,u_j>\n    =Re[E(phi_i conj(phi_j))-mu_i conj(mu_j)]\n                                  +(Im mu_i)(Im mu_j)>=0. (9)\n\nThis establishes one joint cone directly from the probability law. It is\nnot the union of separate single-mode legality statements.\n\n## 3. The explicit PSD rank-one coupling\n\nIn real coordinates y=(y_1,y_2,y_3,y_4), let\nv=(Re omega,-Im omega,-Re omega,Im omega). The map T:R^4->R^5 is\n\n  T y=(sqrt(w_1)y_1,sqrt(w_1)y_2,\n       sqrt(w_2)y_3,sqrt(w_2)y_4,sqrt(rho_coup)<v,y>).\n\nIts Gram metric is exactly\n\n  T^T T=diag(w_1,w_1,w_2,w_2)+rho_coup vv^T,\n\nwhich is real positive definite for w_1,w_2>0. Formula (3) is precisely\nT applied to the two polynomial blocks, so it gives actual vectors and\na fixed linear realization. The fifth coordinate of e=x_0 is zero.\nIt does not change G_i; it adds rho_coup U_i U_j to (9), where\n\n  U_i=Re[omega(phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2))].\n\nUnder (4a), U_i=|z_1-z_2| Re a_i(z_1,z_2)>0, so the cone is preserved\nfor every rho_coup>=0.\n\nFor arbitrary orientation, put A=phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2) and\nB=phi_j(z_1)-phi_j(z_2). The elementary identity\n\n  Re(omega A) Re(omega B)\n    =(Re(A conj B)+Re(omega^2 A B))/2\n    >=(Re(A conj B)-|A||B|)/2\n    >=-d^2 |A||B|\n\nfollows from (7). The two-point covariance term in (8) equals\nw_1w_2 Re(A conj B), which is at least\nw_1w_2(1-2d^2)|A||B|. The imaginary-mean term is nonnegative.\nThus every transverse pair remains nonnegative whenever\n\n  rho_coup <= [(1-2d^2)/d^2] w_1w_2.                    (10)\n\nAt d=12/17 the ratio is 1/144; at d=16/31 it is 449/256.\nThis proves (4b)--(5).\n\nFor clarity these vectors really permit a nonnormal operator. Let\nZ=diag(C(z_1),C(z_2)), where\nC(alpha+i beta)=[[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]].\nOn R^5 set A_coup=T Z (T^T T)^(-1)T^T, zero on the orthogonal complement\nof im T. Then A_coup x_i=g_i. If rho_coup>0 and, for example, omega=1\nand z_1,z_2 have the same positive imaginary part but different real\nparts, its complex eigenvectors T(e_1-i e_2) and T(e_3-i e_4) have\ndistinct eigenvalues and inner product -rho_coup!=0. Hence A_coup is\nnot normal. To justify that implication directly, normality gives\n||(A-lambda I)u||=||(A^*-conj(lambda)I)u||; an eigenvector for lambda\nis then also an adjoint eigenvector, forcing eigenvectors of distinct\neigenvalues to be orthogonal. No generic covariance kernel is used.\n\n## 4. Exact cone bound with all queried anchors retained\n\nHere is the entire cone argument, so no earlier unverified result is a\nproof premise. It applies to any legal queried data with ||x_0||=1 and\n\n  g_i=G_i e+u_i,  G_i>=0, <e,u_i>=0, <u_i,u_j>>=0.\n\nPut X_i=1-sum_(t<i)h_t G_t, W_i=sum_(t<i)h_t u_t, so\nx_i=X_i e-W_i and X_i>=X_q. Add the two actual inequalities\nQ_(q,i)<=0 and Q_(i,star)<=0 from accepted 1252b8823bb5a290:\n\n  f_q<=<g_q,x_q>+<g_i-g_q,x_i>\n                         -||g_i||^2/2-||g_i-g_q||^2/2.   (11)\n\nFor i=q the optimum inequality alone gives the same formula.\nThe transverse part of the first two terms is exactly\n\n  -<u_q,W_q-W_i>-<u_i,W_i><=0.\n\nThe transverse contributions of the two squared norms are nonpositive\nas well. Choose an actual index i minimizing G_i and write p=G_i>=0.\nThen G_q>=p, X_i>=X_q, and X_q<=1-Sp, whence\n\n  2f_q<=2p-(2S+1)p^2-(G_q-p)^2.                          (12)\n\nFor b in [0,2], smoothness subtracts b(2-b)||g_q||^2 from (12).\nSince G_q>=p>=0, this subtracts at least b(2-b)p^2. Completing the\nsquare in p proves (2), for every interpolant and its free terminal\ngradient. No interpolation constraints within the prefix were deleted.\n\n## 5. A concrete legal, nonnormal, positively coupled family\n\nLet m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nd_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), and n=2^m-1.\nKeep a,c fixed within the horizon, remove the ORIGINAL last d_0 step\nfrom H_m, and retain all queries through q=n-1. Its sum S exceeds 100.\nPut\n\n  z_1=(6+2i sqrt(7))/(64S),   z_2=(3+2i sqrt(7))/(64S),\n  omega=1.\n\nBoth lie in (1), with |z_1|=1/(8S), |z_2|=sqrt(37)/(64S), and their\ndifference is real positive. Thus the whole coupling class (4a) applies.\nThe following choice proves it contains actually legal examples with\nrho_coup>0, not just formal vectors.\n\nFor the base z_1 write alpha=3/(32S), sigma=1/(64S^2), p_i=P_i(z_1).\nAll factors contract and 7/8<=|p_i|<=1. Define\nf_0^base=3alpha/4 and\nf_(i+1)^base=f_i^base-h_i sigma(1-alpha h_i/2)|p_i|^2.\nThen 7alpha/12<=f_i^base<=3alpha/4, because the decrements total at\nmost sigma S=alpha/6.\n\nEvery base ordered residual, including the optimum, is <=-delta with\ndelta=alpha sigma/8. Here are the full estimates. For i<j set\nT_seg=sum_(i<=s<j)h_s. Telescoping contracting factors gives\n\n  |p_j-p_i|>=(15/16)|z_1|T_seg |p_i|.\n\nThe signed area of the closed polygon i,...,j,i is\nsigma sum_(i<=s<t<j)h_s h_t Im(conj(p_s)p_t).\nTelescoping the subproduct minus its conjugate bounds\n|Im(conj(p_s)p_t)|<=|p_i|^2 |Im z_1| T_seg.\nThus |beta Area|<=sigma^2 T_seg^3 |p_i|^2/2.\nSubtracting direct-edge work from summed trapezoidal work shows that\nthe two ordered slacks are\n(kappa/2)|p_j-p_i|^2 +/- beta Area. With\nkappa>=5alpha/6 and sigma T_seg<=alpha/6, both are at least\n\n  [1125/3072-1/12]alpha sigma T_seg^2|p_i|^2\n                              >(alpha sigma/4)T_seg^2|p_i|^2>delta.\n\nThe last inequality uses every actual step >1 and |p_i|>=7/8.\nThe optimum lower slack is >=7alpha/12-sigma/2>alpha/2.\nThe optimum upper slack is >=alpha[1/64-49/(768S)]>alpha/100>delta.\n\nPut M=exp(S), K=4M^2, K_row=16M^2, and choose\n\n  epsilon=delta/[8(K+delta)], w_1=1-epsilon, w_2=epsilon,\n  rho_coup=delta/(8K_row)>0, f_i=(1-epsilon)f_i^base.\n\nGive the second normal block temporary zero values. Since all relevant\n|z|<=1, its ordered residuals are <=K. The two normal blocks therefore\nhave joint residual at most -delta+epsilon(K+delta)=-7delta/8.\nFor the added unscaled row with position Re(P_i(z_1)-P_i(z_2))\nand gradient Re(phi_i(z_1)-phi_i(z_2)), each position\nand gradient has magnitude <=2M. With zero temporary values, its\nordered residual is at most K_row. Adding the PSD row contributes at\nmost rho_coup K_row=delta/8, so EVERY distinct ordered residual obeys\n\n  Q_ij<=-3delta/4<0,                                    (13)\n\nincluding every optimum pair. All cycles hold on the SAME coupled data.\n\nThe actual interpolant in R^5 is explicitly\n\n  H(v)=max_(i in {star,0,...,q})\n       {<g_i,x_i>-f_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2},\n  F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.\n\nAccepted 1252b8823bb5a290 makes it finite, convex, differentiable and\n1-smooth, with F(0)=min F=0 and every queried jet retained.\nThe chosen initial distance remains exactly one.\n\n## 6. Fixed-function payoff for every real final step\n\nLet Z_test be the orthogonal projection of the first normal block of x_q\nonto the line perpendicular to its corresponding queried gradient,\nwith zero coordinates in the other normal block and in the coupled row.\nThen <Z_test,g_q>=0,\n||Z_test||^2>=(7/8)(49/64)(7/16)>2/7. Set v_test=alpha Z_test.\n\nFor the optimum anchor,\n2<x_q,v_test>-||v_test||^2=(2alpha-alpha^2)||Z_test||^2>=alpha/2.\nFor every finite anchor put y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nHere\n\n  ||g_i||^2<=sigma+epsilon M^2+4rho_coup M^2\n              <=sigma+delta/16<=2sigma,\n\nand the first-block norm of x_q-y_i is <=(1+1/S)/8<=101/800.\nThus 2delta_i>=49alpha/48-2sigma and\n\n  2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,v_test>-||v_test||^2\n    >=alpha[49/48-1/300-101/400-3/3200]>alpha/2.\n\nBoth the extra block and the coupled row have zero inner product with\nv_test. Evaluating the same conjugate F at v_test proves\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)>=alpha/2=3/(64S)\n\nfor EVERY real b, since v_test is orthogonal to g_q. Every actual\nanchor, including the optimum, was checked. The true terminal gradient\nis unrestricted.\n\n## 7. Exact benchmark normalization and scope\n\nFor the actual near-critical word, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nd_j=1+a c^j, n=2^m-1, and\nS=2^m-1+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)-d_0. Thus S/lambda^m->e.\nThe bound (2) includes the original final b=d_0 in [0,2].\n\nThe exact recursions of b98bb807d514e80d give C_j=S_circle(j)+1 and\nB_j via Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2 and\nG(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2. With rho=log_2lambda,\nV(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 has\n\n  d^2/ds^2 log V(exp(s))\n   =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2>0,\n  t=exp(s).\n\nConvexity at s,-s gives V(t)V(1/t)>=2, which yields\nOmega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho after substituting\nt=(A/B)^(1/rho) and expanding (L-A)(L-B)>=2AB.\nInduction gives C_j<=(j+1)^rho; G(A,B)<=4A+B then gives\nB_j<=1+4j^rho. Balanced primitive splits and a dominant split with\nright length zero also give B_(2^m-1)>=2lambda^(m-1).\nConsequently, uniformly over the whole normal and specified coupled\nclasses and every b in [0,2],\n\n  limsup B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=2/e<1.\n\nThe explicit nonnormal R^5 construction, for every real b, satisfies\n\n  liminf B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)>=3/(32lambda e)>0.\n\nThese are exact normalization losses, not a sharp unrestricted profile\nclaim. No all-b sharpness of the upper bound is asserted.\n\nThe unrestricted rank-one coupling is still open: a sufficiently large\nunaligned row can have opposite signs at different queries, and the\njoint transverse-cone argument then requires additional analysis.\nThe original all-schedule/all-horizon target remains unproved.\n\n\n## 8. A second complete model: sign reversal with arbitrary coupling strength\n\nThe alignment hypothesis cannot be diagnosed by looking only at the sign\nof the added coordinate. Here is a different whole two-block model, whose\nadded queried gradients DO change sign when rho_coup>0, but whose full joint legality\nstill implies (2) for every coupling amplitude.\n\nLet all retained h_i be positive, their sum S>=100, and their last entry\nh_(q-1)<=2. Put\n\n  z_1=(1+i)/(10S), z_2=(2+i)/(10S), w_1=w_2=1/2,\n  Omega_proj=-1/10+i.\n\nAppend the coordinate sqrt(rho_coup) Re[Omega_proj(P_i(z_1)-P_i(z_2))]\nand its corresponding gradient coordinate, for arbitrary rho_coup>=0.\nThis is (3) with a nonunit projection; equivalently replace omega by\nOmega_proj/|Omega_proj| and multiply the amplitude by |Omega_proj|^2.\nThe explicit PSD realization of Section 3 applies unchanged.\n\nFor EVERY jointly legal prefix of these vectors, and every b in [0,2],\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)<=1/[2S+1+b(2-b)].                        (14)\n\nThere is no amplitude or conditioning bound. We prove (14) by deriving\nthe joint cone from the actual adjacent interpolation constraints.\n\nWrite zeta_1=(1+i)/10, zeta_2=(2+i)/10 and tau_i=sum_(t<i)h_t/S.\nLet e_k(i) be the k-th elementary symmetric polynomial of the nonnegative\nnumbers h_t/S, t<i, with e_0=1 and e_k=0 above their count.\nThen e_1=tau_i and 0<=e_k<=tau_i^k/k!<=1/k!.\nDefine Hom_k=sum_(l=0)^k zeta_1^(k-l)zeta_2^l and\nq_k=Re(Omega_proj Hom_k). Direct multiplication gives\n\n  q_0=-1/10, q_1=-23/100, q_2=-47/500, q_3=-237/10000.    (15)\n\nIf U_i is the unscaled added gradient and B_i is the unscaled added\ncoordinate of A_coup g_i, polynomial subtraction gives EXACTLY\n\n  U_i=-(1/(10S)) sum_(k>=0)(-1)^k e_k(i)q_k,\n  B_i=-(1/(10S^2)) sum_(k>=0)(-1)^k e_k(i)q_(k+1),\n  U_(i+1)=U_i-h_i B_i.                                   (16)\n\nThese sums are finite. Since |zeta_l|<1/4 and |Omega_proj|<101/100,\n|q_k|<=(101/100)(k+1)4^(-k).\n\nIn the second sum of (16), the tail after k=2 has absolute value <1/250.\nIndeed its first majorant is (101/100)*5/1536, and each subsequent\nterm is at most 3/40 times its predecessor. Together with (15) and\n0<=tau_i<=1, e_2<=1/2, this proves\n\n  -1/4 < sum_(k>=0)(-1)^k e_k q_(k+1) < -13/100,\n  13/(1000S^2)<B_i<1/(40S^2).                            (17)\n\nFor the first sum of (16), the tail after k=3 has absolute value <1/1000:\nits first majorant is (101/100)*5/6144 and later ratios are <=3/50.\nThe k=3 term is nonnegative. If tau_i>=49/50, (15) gives\n\n  sum_(k>=0)(-1)^k e_k q_k\n    >=-1/10+(23/100)(49/50)-(47/500)/2-1/1000\n    >3/40,\n  U_i<-3/(400S).                                        (18)\n\nThus U_i decreases strictly from U_0=1/(100S) and is negative at q-1\nand q, since tau_(q-1)>=1-2/S>=49/50. This is an actual sign reversal,\nfor every prescribed retained schedule in the stated class.\n\nWe next bound the transverse covariance of the two NORMAL blocks alone.\nTheir factors contract, since h|z_l|<=|zeta_l|<1/4<2Re(zeta_l)/|zeta_l|.\nThe finite expansion\nphi_i(z_l)=S^(-1)sum_k(-1)^k e_k zeta_l^(k+1) shows\n\n  Im phi_i(z_1)>=(1/S)(1/10-1/50-1/1440),\n  Im phi_i(z_2)>=(1/S)(1/10-1/25-1/1440).\n\nHere the k=2 terms have nonnegative imaginary parts; the remaining\ntail is bounded by (1/1536)/(1-1/16)=1/1440.\nTheir mean imaginary part is consequently >=69/(1000S).\n\nAt radius 1/4, Section 2 gives d=16/31, Re(a_i conj(a_j))>=\n(449/961)|a_i||a_j| and |a_i|>=15/31 for divided differences.\nSince |z_1-z_2|=1/(10S), the two-point covariance in (8) is at least\n\n  [449*225/(4*961^2*100)]/S^2 > 1/(4000S^2).\n\nAdding the imaginary means as in (9), the two normal blocks therefore\nhave, for EVERY i,j, transverse inner product\n\n  >[(69/1000)^2+1/4000]/S^2>1/(200S^2).                  (19)\n\nNow apply the ACTUAL ordered pair constraints between i and i+1,\nfor each i<q. Their sum gives\n<g_i,A_coup g_i>>=||A_coup g_i||^2>=0, because h_i>0.\nThe normal contribution is at most\n\n  (1/2)sum_l Re(z_l)|z_l|^2|P_i(z_l)|^2<=3/(500S^3).\n\nConsequently if U_i<0, the positive B_i from (17) satisfies\n\n  rho_coup |U_i| B_i <=3/(500S^3).                      (20)\n\nThis is joint legality, including the added row; no scalar row is assumed\nlegal by itself.\n\nIf U_i U_j>=0, the coupled row cannot hurt (19). Otherwise take i<j,\nso 0<U_i<=1/(100S) and U_j<0. If j<q, (17),(20) give\n\n  rho_coup U_i|U_j| <=3/(650S^2).\n\nFor j=q use (20) at q-1. Equations (17)--(18) and h_(q-1)<=2 give\n\n  |U_q|/|U_(q-1)|\n      <=1+[2/(40S^2)]/[3/(400S)]\n      <=16/15.\n\nThus in EVERY case the magnitude of a negative added pair contribution\nis <=8/(1625S^2). Combining with (19),\n\n  <u_i,u_j> >= [1/200-8/1625]/S^2=1/(13000S^2)>0.         (21)\n\nThe initial-direction projections remain nonnegative by Section 2.\nThe fully reproduced cone proof in Section 4 now proves (14).\nThe sign reversal by itself does not evade the common interpolation\nconstraints, even at arbitrarily large proposed amplitude.\n\n## 9. A legal realization of the sign-reversing model and its payoff\n\nApply Section 8 to the actual retained near-critical word. Its last\nexecuted step is d_1=2-1/m<2, and every retained step exceeds one.\nHere is an explicit positive rho_coup and one fixed interpolant.\n\nFor each of the two NORMAL modes of Section 8 define alpha_l=Re z_l,\nsigma_l=|z_l|^2, p_i,l=P_i(z_l), and values\n\n  f_0,l=alpha_l/2,\n  f_(i+1),l=f_i,l-h_i sigma_l(1-alpha_l h_i/2)|p_i,l|^2.\n\nTheir factors contract and |p_i,l|>=3/4. Since\nsigma_l S<=alpha_l/4,\nalpha_l/4<=f_i,l<=alpha_l/2.\nEach has every ordered residual <=-delta, for\ndelta=1/(4000S^3). We provide the checks instead of imposing legality.\n\nFor an interval of mass T_seg, telescoping gives the chord lower bound\n(7/8)|z_l|T_seg|p_i,l|. The same polygon identity used in Section 5\ngives |beta_l Area|<=sigma_l^2 T_seg^3|p_i,l|^2/2.\nHere kappa_l>=99alpha_l/100 and sigma_l T_seg<=alpha_l/4.\nThus either ordered pair slack is at least\n\n  [(99/200)(49/64)-1/8]alpha_l sigma_l T_seg^2|p_i,l|^2\n       >(alpha_l sigma_l/4)T_seg^2|p_i,l|^2\n       >=alpha_l sigma_l/8>=delta.\n\nThe last step uses T_seg>1 and |p_i,l|>=3/4.\nThe optimum lower slack is >=alpha_l/4-sigma_l/2>alpha_l/5.\nThe upper slack is >=\n(alpha_l-sigma_l/2)(9/16)-alpha_l/2>alpha_l/20>delta.\n\nUse their equal weighted values f_i=(f_i,1+f_i,2)/2.\nLet M=exp(S) and choose rho_coup=delta/(128M^2)>0.\nFor the unscaled added row, each position and gradient has magnitude\nat most 4M, so every ordered zero-value residual is at most64M^2.\nAdding it to the two normal blocks yields Q_ij<=-delta/2 for EVERY\ndistinct finite or optimum ordered pair. The explicit conjugate\ninterpolant from Section 5 is therefore legal on R^5.\nIt is nonnormal by the distinct-eigenvector argument in Section 3:\nfor this projection their inner product is -rho_coup|Omega_proj|^2!=0.\nIts added gradient actually changes sign by (16)--(18).\n\nFor an all-final-step payoff let Z_test be the projection of the first\nnormal block of x_q perpendicular to its first-block gradient, with\nzero coordinates in the second block and in the coupled row. Then\n\n  <Z_test,g_q>=0, ||Z_test||^2>=(1/2)(9/16)(1/2)=9/64.\n\nTake v_test=Z_test/(20S). The optimum anchor yields\n2<x_q,v_test>-||v_test||^2>=(39/(400S))(9/64)>1/(100S).\n\nFor every finite anchor y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\nwe have f_i>=3/(80S), ||g_i||^2<=1/(20S^2), and the first-block norm\nof x_q-y_i is <=(1+1/S)/10<=101/1000. Hence\n\n  2delta_i+2<x_q-y_i,v_test>-||v_test||^2\n    >=3/(40S)-1/(20S^2)-101/(10000S)-1/(400S^2)\n    >1/(100S).\n\nThese inequalities check every actual anchor, including the optimum.\nThe same conjugate evaluation as Section 6 proves for the SAME fixed F\n\n  2F(x_q-b g_q)>=1/(100S) for EVERY real b.                (22)\n\nThus the whole arbitrary-amplitude sign-reversing model has the exact\nupper cap (14), while it has a fully legal positive-coupling construction\nof benchmark order. On the actual near-critical word its limsup ratio\nis <=2/e for b in [0,2], and the constructed all-real-b liminf ratio is\n>=1/(50lambda e)>0. No conclusion about all unaligned couplings follows.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["f42b4c43d2ea0c3a", "0576f736952b6e97"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/joint_covariance_rank_one_coupling.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "verification_status": "unverified_no_routine_allocation", "scope": "Sections8-9 add the arbitrary-amplitude unaligned model; complete previous proof retained"}, "glossary": {"Omega_proj": "Fixed nonunit complex projection -1/10+i for the sign-reversing real coupling row.", "zeta_l": "Dimensionless complex parameter S z_l, equal to(1+i)/10 or(2+i)/10 in the second model.", "Hom_k": "Complete homogeneous sum sum_(l=0)^k zeta_1^(k-l)zeta_2^l.", "e_k(i)": "Elementary symmetric polynomial of degree k in the normalized retained steps h_t/S,t<i.", "q_k": "Real coefficient Re(Omega_proj Hom_k) in the sign-reversal calculation.", "U_i": "Unscaled real coupled-row gradient Re[Omega_proj(phi_i(z1)-phi_i(z2))].", "B_i": "Unscaled coupled-row coordinate of A_coup g_i.", "rho_coup": "Arbitrary nonnegative coefficient of the specified real PSD rank-one addition.", "tau_i": "Retained partial stepsize mass divided by S.", "S": "Sum of the retained executed prefix, before the genuinely free final b."}}
{"id": "b20258ab0af30cea", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T18:47:37.794333+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C49 covers arbitrary nonnegative forward-incidence graphs with a sparse set of positive older-history correlations: no rank, memory, degree or adjacent-correlation restriction. For m>=512,N>=128, if at most Jbad<=N/128 high phases have <X_i,p_(i+1)><0, EVERY legal member and full terminal completion has score_free/K<7 exp(-N/32)<7/16 and limsup B_n score_free<=7/(8e)<1. Every Jbad=o(N) family has vanishing benchmark payoff as N grows. A firm-projection bound charges each exceptional phase by at most(9/8)c^ell, including Q<1. Unverified candidate, not unrestricted W.", "evidence": "# C49. Sparse high-history violations cannot rescue any positive flow\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint candidate. No verifier or numerical computation.\nEvery argument is included internally; earlier candidates are motivation\nonly, not correctness premises.\n\nFor the near-critical ruler, m>=512 and N>=128, let Jbad count high phases\nwhere the next low gradient is positively correlated with the entire\nstrictly older weighted gradient sum. For arbitrary nonnegative forward\nincidence, every legal member and every full terminal completion satisfy\n\n    score_free/K<7 exp(-N/32)<7/16 when Jbad<=N/128.\n\nConsequently limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=7/(8e)<1 in that range.\nIf N grows and Jbad=o(N), the normalized benchmark payoff tends to zero.\nThere is no rank, memory, degree, or adjacent-correlation restriction.\n\n## 1. Real geometry, all normal tests, and the history count\n\nSet lambda=1+sqrt(F2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Fix 0<=r<=m-7, let\nk=m-r, N=2^k, M=1+sum H_r, q=a c^r/M, A=1+q, and put\n\n q_i=q c^(nu_2(i+1)), tau_i=1+q_i, T_i=M tau_i, i<N-1,\n tau_(N-1)=t>=1, T_(N-1)=Mt.\n\nThe symbol nu_2 denotes binary valuation. The N ordinary phases are exactly\nthe physical H_r copies, with mass M-1; each nonfinal phase is followed by\nthe actual checkpoint1+Mq_i.\n\nChoose arbitrary transfers a_ij>=0 for i<j and masses\n\n    R_0>0, R_j=sum_(i<j)a_ij>0 for j>=1, E_i=R_i^2.\n\nIn the real space R^N with standard orthonormal basis define\n\n p_i=(R_i e_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij e_j)/T_i,\n C=conv(0,p_0,...,p_(N-1)),\n F_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2).\n\nIts gradient is Euclidean projection onto C, by the projection variational\ninequality and maximizing-difference inequalities. That projection is\nnonexpansive; thus F_C is convex and1-smooth.\n\nFor i>=0 and j>i put r_j^(i)=sum_(l<i)a_lj, an actual inherited coordinate\nmass. The exact phase state is\n\n    X_i=R_i e_i+sum_(j>i)r_j^(i)e_j,\n\nwhere the root sum vanishes. Subtracting T_i p_i exhausts coordinate i and\ncreates X_(i+1); every individual physical step is retained.\n\nDefine\n\n V_i=E_i-sum_(j>i)a_ij r_j^(i),\n C_i=sum_(j>i)a_ij[r_j^(i)+a_ij],\n Delta_ij=a_ij R_j-sum_(l>j)a_il a_jl, i<j.               (F1)\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\n    Delta_ij=-T_i T_j <p_i,p_j>.                         (F2)\n\nFor the complete normal test put z=(ordinary partial mass+1)/M in[1/M,1].\nMultiplying all normal values by M, the own value is\n\n    [(tau_i-z)V_i-z C_i]/tau_i^2,                        (F3)\n\nand a future-j value is\n\n    [sum_(l<i)Delta_lj+(z/tau_i)Delta_ij]/tau_j.           (F4)\n\nPast normal values are nonpositive since their positive coordinates have\nbeen exhausted and all remaining proximal coordinates are nonnegative.\nComparing(F3) with zero and EVERY(F4) at BOTH z=1/M and z=1 is necessary\nand sufficient for all actual queries. All differences are affine. At the\nlast phase t>=1 suffices because there is no outgoing transfer or future\nnormal. This keeps the full old-query and future-query test explicit.\n\nEvery legal nonterminal phase has\n\n    q_i V_i>=C_i>=0.                                    (F5)\n\nIn fact V_i>0: if C_i>0 use(F5); if C_i=0 all outgoing transfers vanish\nand V_i=E_i>0. At the terminal phase V_(N-1)=E_(N-1)>0.\n\nFor every high phase i (odd i with1<=i<=N-3), define\n\n F_i=sum_(l<i)Delta_(l,i+1), G_i=Delta_(i,i+1).\n Call the phase exceptional when F_i<0. Let Jbad be their number\n and let Lbad=sum_(exceptional i)nu_2(i+1).              (F6)\n\nNo correlation-sign condition is imposed. At nonexceptional high phases\nF_i>=0; all adjacent correlations and all low-phase correlations remain\narbitrary. By(F2), exceptionality means positive correlation of the next\nlow gradient with the entire strictly older weighted gradient sum.\n\nThe class is nonempty. For example take only root-to-later transfers\na_0j=R_j and no outgoing transfers from j>=1. Set E_(N-1)=1, then choose\nE_i backward large enough that\nq_i E_i/tau_i^2>=max_(j>i) E_j/tau_j. Finally choose\nqE_0>=sum_(j>=1)E_j+A max_(j>=1)E_j/tau_j.\nThese give every endpoint normal above, and they have no exceptional high phase.\n\nEvery original queried value is <x,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The polar inequalities\n<p_i,z><=0 imply backward that every coordinate of z is nonpositive.\nConsequently0 is the actual closest minimizer to R_0e_0, with distance R_0.\nWriting htot=sum H_m, the original objective and primitive defect are\n\n score_C=(2Mt-2M+1)E_(N-1)/(M^2t^2E_0),\n V/E_0=1-[(Mt-(M-1)+htot)/(Mt)]^2 E_(N-1)/E_0.            (F7)\n\nHere V without a subscript is the project primitive defect; V_i in(F1)\nis the phase energy numerator. Formula(F7) follows from the terminal state\nand the exact identity\n2hF+h(1-h)||g||^2=||x||^2-||x-hg||^2 along the original trajectory.\n\n## 2. Low phases and nonexceptional high phases\n\nSet Z_i=V_i/tau_i>0. At the final phase Z_(N-1)=E_(N-1)/t.\nBy direct summation in(F1),\n\n    F_i+G_i=V_(i+1).                                    (F8)\n\nThe current squared-transfer cost also satisfies\n\n C_i>=a_(i,i+1)R_(i+1)>=Delta_(i,i+1)=G_i,                (F9)\n\nbecause R_(i+1)=r_(i+1)^(i)+a_(i,i+1) and all products omitted from\nC_i are nonnegative; the second inequality discards the shared-tail sum\nsubtracted in Delta.\n\nThe endpoint future-i+1 comparison(F3)-(F4), together with\nC_i>=max(0,G_i), gives\n\n q_i V_i >=[tau_i^2 V_(i+1)-tau_i q_i G_i\n                              +tau_(i+1)max(0,G_i)]/tau_(i+1).   (F9a)\n\nIf G_i<=0, the two extra terms are nonnegative. Thus\n\n    Z_(i+1)/Z_i<=q_i/tau_i.                             (F10a)\n\nIf G_i>=0, the extra coefficient of G_i in(F9a) is\nDelta_cap=tau_(i+1)-tau_i q_i.\nAt a LOW phase tau_i=A and Delta_cap>=1-Aq>0, so(F10a)\nholds for arbitrary F_i and G_i, without ANY correlation-sign condition.\n\nAt a HIGH phase tau_i=B=1+Q and tau_(i+1)=A, one has\nDelta_cap=A-BQ<0. If this high phase is nonexceptional, definition(F6) gives\nG_i<=F_i+G_i=V_(i+1). Multiplication by the negative Delta_cap\ntherefore bounds the right side of(F9a) below by\n[B^2+A-BQ]V_(i+1)/A=(A+B)V_(i+1)/A. Consequently\n\n    Z_(i+1)/Z_i<=q_i tau_i/(tau_i+tau_(i+1)).            (F10b)\n\nThe elementary parameter signs Aq<1 and BQ>A are established below.\nWhen G_i<=0 at a high phase, (F10a) is stronger since BQ>A.\nThus at every low phase and every NONEXCEPTIONAL high phase,\n\n Z_(i+1)/Z_i\n <=max(q_i/tau_i, q_i tau_i/(tau_i+tau_(i+1))).           (F10)\n\nNo adjacent-correlation sign is required, and no low-phase sign is required.\n\nAt the root, V_1=Delta_01>0, while\nC_0=sum_(j>0)a_0j^2>=R_1^2>=Delta_01.\nIts endpoint comparison with phase1 therefore gives\n\n    Z_1/E_0<=q/(A+tau_1).                                (F11)\n\nNo factorization, finite memory, or scalar common direction was used.\nThis argument is used only at the nonexceptional high phases. We next\nbound every exceptional phase without any sign assumption.\n\n## 3. A universal bound for every exceptional high phase\n\nFor every phase let ||p_i|| be the actual Euclidean gradient norm.\nThe definitions in Section1 give\n\n M^2||p_i||^2=(V_i+C_i)/tau_i^2.\n\nAt a nonterminal phase(F5) gives C_i<=q_i V_i; nonnegative transfers\ngive V_i<=E_i<=M^2 tau_i^2||p_i||^2. At the terminal phase these estimates\nfollow directly from its one-coordinate vector. Hence\n\n    M^2||p_i||^2<=Z_i<=tau_i M^2||p_i||^2.              (P1)\n\nCompare the endpoints of two adjacent ordinary blocks in the ORIGINAL\nfixed function F_C. They are\n\n y_i=X_i-(M-1)p_i,\n y_(i+1)=X_i-M tau_i p_i-(M-1)p_(i+1).\n\nTheir gradients are p_i,p_(i+1), including the prescribed old terminal\ngradient if this is the last block. Projection variational inequalities\ngive firm nonexpansiveness directly: for p=P_C(x),r=P_C(y),\n<x-p,r-p><=0 and <y-r,p-r><=0 imply\n<x-y,p-r>>=||p-r||^2.\nApply this to y_i,y_(i+1). Their difference is\n(Mq_i+1)p_i+(M-1)p_(i+1). Expansion and division by M gives\n\n ||p_(i+1)||^2\n <=q_i||p_i||^2+(1-q_i)<p_i,p_(i+1)>.                   (P2)\n\nIf q_i>=1, Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the right side by\nq_i||p_i||^2+(q_i-1)||p_i||||p_(i+1)||. Setting\nr=||p_(i+1)||/||p_i|| yields (r-q_i)(r+1)<=0.\nIf q_i<=1 the corresponding inequality is (r-1)(r+q_i)<=0.\nThus universally\n\n    ||p_(i+1)||<=max(1,q_i)||p_i||.                     (P3)\n\nThe normals are nonzero by their positive diagonal.\nAt every high phase Q=q_i>=99/100 one must be careful: Q may be\nslightly BELOW1. The universal bound is therefore max(1,Q), not Q.\nTo account for this, put Qhat=max(1,Q). Since Q>=1-1/m,\nQhat/Q<=m/(m-1)<=512/511 for m>=512.\n\nUsing(P1)-(P3) and tau_(i+1)=A gives\n\n    Z_(i+1)<=A Qhat^2 Z_i.                              (P4)\n\nRelative to the nonexceptional factor QB/(A+B), B=1+Q, this costs at most\n\n A Qhat^2(A+B)/(QB)\n  =Aq(1+A/B)(Qhat/Q)^2 c^ell\n  <=kappa c^ell,  kappa=9/8, ell=nu_2(i+1).             (P5)\n\nFor an explicit rational check use\nAq(1+A/B)<220731/203776, as q<7/16,A<23/16,B>=199/100.\nMultiplying by(512/511)^2 still gives a number below9/8:\n8*220731*512^2 <9*203776*511^2.\nThis is an elementary fixed integer inequality, not a numerical sweep.\nThe small Q<1 case is thus included.\n\nLet Ebad=(kappa c)^Lbad, with Ebad=1 for no exceptional phase.\nSince every ell>=1, multiplying(P5) over exceptional phases bounds\ntheir total extra cost by Ebad. No restriction on any graph edge or\ncorrelation has been used.\n\n## 4. Multiply the exact ruler factors\n\nThe mass recursion gives, with b=a/(c-2) in(0,1),\n\n    M_j=1+sum H_j=b c^j+(1-b)2^j.\n\nConsequently M_m<=Mc^k and a<=q<c-2. For m>=512 use\n\n 2/5<q<7/16, 12/5<c<39/16,\n cq>=1-1/m>=99/100,\n qc<=c(c-2)<1+8/m<=65/64.                                (10)\n\nFor example lambda<169/70 and a>=127/(129lambda)>2540/6321>2/5\ngive the first lower bound.
