The expansion\nc(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))/m+(3+2sqrt(2))/m^2 gives the last one.\n\nAt a LOW phase tau_i=A and tau_(i+1)>=1. Since A q<1,\n\n    A^2<A+tau_(i+1),\n\nso the first alternative in(F10) is larger and the factor is at most q/A.\n\nAt a NONEXCEPTIONAL HIGH phase tau_i=B=1+Q with Q>=99/100,\nits next phase is low, tau_(i+1)=A. The bounds imply BQ>A, so B^2>B+A\nand the second alternative in(F10) is larger. Its factor is at most\nQB/(B+A); exceptional high phases pay the additional factor(P5).\n\nWrite B_1=1+cq=tau_1 (not the benchmark with the same letter).\nThere are N/2-1 high phases, with levels summing to N-k-1, and the\nsame number of low phases after the root. Combining(F10)-(F11) with(P4)-(P5) at exceptional phases gives\n\n Z_(N-1)/E_0\n <=Ebad*(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)]\n               *product_(high i) tau_i/(tau_i+A)\n <=Ebad*(qc)^(N-1)c^(-k)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-1)].                  (11)\n\nThe count follows directly from 2^(k-ell-1) high checkpoints at level\nell=1,...,k-1. No asymptotic replacement of the physical word is made.\n\n## 5. Full terminal completion and the actual distance\n\nLet f be ANY convex1-smooth completion retaining all queried jets and\nthe optimum(0,0,0), including the maximal completion in accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nIf r>=1, every p_i is actually queried. Interpolation to any new\nminimizer z gives <p_i,z><=0, since the retained value is\n<x_i,p_i>-||p_i||^2/2. The triangular inequalities force its coordinates\nnonpositive, so0 remains the true closest minimizer at distance R_0.\nAdditional orthogonal dimensions cannot lower the distance.\n\nThe last physical step is h_last=1+a. Its actual retained query gives\n\n 2f(x_n)/E_0\n <=[2Mt-(2M-1)+h_last^2]E_(N-1)/(M^2 t^2 E_0)\n <2E_(N-1)/(MtE_0),\n\nbecause M>=2+a yields 2M-1-h_last^2>=2-a^2>0.\nPut K=1/(2M_m-1). Use M_m<=Mc^k and(11):\n\n score_free/K\n <Ebad*[4A/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <2*Ebad*(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (12)\n\nThe last inequality uses B_1>A.\n\nIf r=0, the old terminal gradient is UNQUERIED. Use instead the\nLipschitz-gradient lower bound\n\n    dist(x_0,argmin f)^2>=||p_0||^2>=E_0/A^2.             (13)\n\nAt the final query i=N-2, put\nv=r_(i+1)^(i)/R_(i+1) in[0,1]. Its position is\nR_i e_i+v R_(i+1)e_(i+1), and its gradient is\n[R_i e_i-(1-v)R_(i+1)e_(i+1)]/A. One smoothness step of length A gives\n\n 2f(x_n)<=(1-A^(-2))E_i\n +[-2v(1-v)/A+(1-2/A-1/A^2)(1-v)^2]E_(i+1)\n <=(1-A^(-2))E_i.                                       (14)\n\nThe bracket is nonpositive for A<23/16.\n\nWrite b=a_(i,i+1) and d=r_(i+1)^(i). The final low phase's\nown zero comparison says qE_i>=A b d+b^2. Since Z_i=(E_i-bd)/A,\nit follows that\n\n    Z_i>=E_i/A^2.                                       (15)\n\nIndeed bd<=qE_i/A, and A-q=1 then proves the displayed bound.\n\nRepeat the same product, including every exceptional-high penalty, but\nomit the final low factor q/A. It yields\n\n Z_(N-2)/E_0\n <=Ebad*q^(N-2)c^(N-k-1)/[(A+B_1)A^(N/2-2)].\n\nCombine(13)-(15), multiply by K^(-1)<2c^k, and use A^2-1=q(2+q):\n\n score_free/K\n <Ebad*[2(2+q)A^4/(A+B_1)](qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2)\n <7*Ebad*(qc)^(N-1)/A^(N/2).                                 (16)\n\nFor the numerical rational constant, A+B_1>3 and\n2(39/16)(23/16)^4/3=21827598/3145728<7.\n\nNo unqueried normal has been used to constrain the new minimizer.\nThe terminal bound follows only from retained queries and their actual\nphysical last displacement.\n\n## 6. Count the exceptional levels and retain the exact benchmark\n\nThe number of high phases with level at least s is at most N/2^s.\nConsequently\n\n    Lbad<=sum_(s>=1)min(Jbad,N/2^s).\n\nIf Jbad<=N/128, split the sum after s=7:\n\n    Lbad<=7 Jbad+N/128<=N/16.                            (P6)\n\nLet z0=65/64>1. Using(qc)^(N-1)<=z0^N includes qc<1.\nThe terminal bounds in Section5 imply\n\n score_free/K\n <7 exp[N log z0-(N/2)log A+Lbad log(kappa c)].\n\nThe elementary bounds are\nlog z0<=1/64, log A>=log(7/5)>=2/7, and log(kappa c)<9/8.\nThe last follows from kappa c<351/128 and\nexp(9/8)>1+9/8+(9/8)^2/2+(9/8)^3/6>351/128.\nBy(P6) the exponent is at most\n\n    N[1/64-1/7+9/128] < -N/32.\n\nThus for EVERY legal positive forward flow in the stated range,\n\n    score_free/K<7 exp(-N/32) whenever Jbad<=N/128.       (P7)\n\nFor N>=128, exp(N/32)>=exp(4)>16, so the ratio is below7/16.\nIf N grows and Jbad=o(N), the condition in(P7) holds eventually and\nthe ratio tends uniformly to zero. This covers arbitrary graph rank,\nmemory, parent counts, edge weights, and exceptional correlation sizes.\n\nFor the exact benchmark use accepted b98bb807d514e80d and set\nrho_s=log_2(lambda). Put F(z)=(1+z)^rho_s-1. The second derivative\nof log F(exp w) equals\n\n rho_s*z*(1+z)^(rho_s-2)*\n [(1+z)^rho_s-1-rho_s*z]/F(z)^2>0.\n\nThus F(z)F(1/z)>=F(1)^2=2 by midpoint convexity in w. Comparing the\npositive root of t^2-(X+Y)t-XY proves\nOmega(X,Y)<=(X^(1/rho_s)+Y^(1/rho_s))^rho_s.\nInduction in the accepted primitive recursion gives\n1+S_circle(n)<=(n+1)^rho_s. Since the accepted dominant join obeys\nGamma(X,D)<=4X+D, its recursion gives B_n<=1+4n^rho_s.\nThe explicit mass formula in Section4 gives M_m/lambda^m->exp(1), hence\n\n    limsup K B_(2^m-1)<=2/exp(1).\n\nTherefore limsup B_(2^m-1)*score_free<=7/[8exp(1)]<1 whenever\nm>=512,N>=128,Jbad<=N/128 along the family. The limit superior is zero\nwhen N grows and Jbad=o(N).\n\n## 7. Scope\n\nEvery graph edge is nonnegative, but no gradient-correlation sign is\nassumed globally. Every old/future normal and every original physical\nstep is retained. The bounds cover full terminal completion and its true\nclosest-minimizer distance, including the unqueried-terminal-gradient\ncase r=0. The gradient bound(P2) was used only for the original realized\nfunction; the completion's last gradient was never fixed.\n\nAn escaping positive-flow family with N tending to infinity therefore\nrequires exceptional older-history correlation at a positive density\nof high phases. A finite or sublinear set of unusually strong merges\ncannot suffice. A dense set is only a necessary condition, not a useful\nwitness or a sufficiency claim.\n\nThe statement still concerns the defined positive triangular incidence\ngeometry. Dense exceptional histories, signed transfers, reactivated\ncoordinates and varying gradients within an ordinary block remain open.\nIt is not an unrestricted W upper bound and does not settle the project's\noriginal exact-profile target.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["88b640ae31a56475", "849a06cf1649008b", "fa31a0416d170f02"]}, "glossary": {"Jbad": "Number of high phases i with F_i=sum_(l<i)Delta_(l,i+1)<0, equivalently <X_i,p_(i+1)><0.", "Lbad": "Sum of ruler levels nu_2(i+1) over exceptional high phases.", "Ebad": "Variation penalty ((9/8)c)^Lbad.", "V_i": "R_i^2-sum_(j>i)a_ij r_j^(i), the positive phase energy numerator.", "Z_i": "V_i/tau_i, bounded between M^2||p_i||^2 and tau_i M^2||p_i||^2.", "C_i": "sum_(j>i)a_ij(r_j^(i)+a_ij), the full transfer cost.", "Delta_ij": "a_ij R_j-sum_(k>j)a_ik a_jk=-T_iT_j<p_i,p_j>.", "Qhat": "max(1,Q), necessary because a high excess Q=cq can be slightly below1.", "K": "Canonical Huber score1/(2M_m-1).", "score_free": "Actual normalized objective of any convex1-smooth completion retaining all original queried jets and the optimum."}}
{"id": "047c257795dc59ba", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:22:58.694496+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C51 COMPLETE EXACT CANDIDATE: actual H_3 has a full all-pair 9x9 certificate at its Huber value9/(139+52sqrt2), with strictly positive quotient pivots. Also an explicit LOSSLESS Huber certificate coordinate system eliminates all star/terminal flow variables for every nonnegative schedule of positive total sum. This is finite evidence for max's upper attempt, not a growing-horizon result; no verifier allocated.", "evidence": "# C51: a full exact H3 certificate and explicit Huber certificate coordinates\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, **not verifier-accepted**. No new verifier\nallocation and no additional optimization call. The only mathematical\npredecessor is accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`. The numerical solve guided the\nchoice of rational coefficients; its values, eigenvalues and inferred\nmultipliers are not proof premises.\n\nThis supplies a concrete full-objective certificate for the actual m=3\nschedule. It does not prove a growing-horizon certificate or resolve the\noriginal exact-profile problem.\n\n## 1. A lossless parameterization at the Huber value\n\nFix n>=1 and any nonnegative schedule h=(h0,...,h_(n-1)) with sum S>0.\nSet P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for 0<=i<=n, h_n=0, and tau=1/(2S+1).\nUse the independent formal vectors (x0,g0,...,gn), the GD identities\nxi=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t gt, and all ordered interpolation residuals\n\n    Qij=fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2,\n\nwith the optimum jet equal to zero. Every legal trajectory satisfies Qij<=0\nby fact1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nChoose an arbitrary array r_ij>=0 for 0<=i<n and 0<=j<=n, j!=i, and set\nr_ii=0. These are all finite multipliers whose source is a queried prefix\nsample; there is no support restriction among them. Define\n\n    mu_i = [2h_i+sum_(j=0)^n r_ij(P_i-P_j)]/(2S-P_i),  i<n,             (1)\n    mu_n = 2-sum_(i<n)mu_i,\n    beta_i=mu_i+sum_(j=0)^n r_ij-sum_(k<n)r_ki,          i<n.            (2)\n\nThe denominators in (1) are positive, since 2S-P_i>=S>0. Define the\ncomplete multiplier array by\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=0,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i,                 0<=i<=n,\n    lambda_ij=r_ij,                      0<=i<n, j finite, j!=i,\n    lambda_(n,i)=beta_i,                 0<=i<n.                       (3)\n\nFor the moment mu and beta may have either sign. Their eventual\nnonnegativity will be checked explicitly.\n\nLet e_i be the standard basis of the real space of dimension n+1, indexed 0,...,n. Put\n\n    p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t e_t,\n    v_ij=p_i-p_j,\n    A_i*= e_i e_i^T/2+(e_i p_i^T+p_i e_i^T)/2,\n    A_ij=(e_i v_ij^T+v_ij e_i^T)/2\n                           +(e_i-e_j)(e_i-e_j)^T/2,\n    A=sum_i mu_i A_i*+sum_(i!=j finite)lambda_ij A_ij,\n    M=[[tau,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]],\n    Z_hub=A-mu mu^T/(4tau),\n    K_hub=Z_hub restricted to rows/columns 0,...,n-1.                          (4)\n\nAll matrices in (4) are full formal coefficient matrices, not restrictions\nto a recovered primal trajectory. The exact identity is\n\n    tau||x0||^2-2fn+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Qij\n                       =(x0,g0,...,gn)^T M (x0,g0,...,gn).             (5)\n\nHere vector-valued quadratic notation means the corresponding sum of\ninner products in any finite-dimensional real Euclidean space.\n\nThe sufficient and necessary certificate tests in these coordinates are\n\n         r_ij>=0,  mu_i>=0,  beta_i>=0,  K_hub>=0.                        (6)\n\nMore precisely, (6) is equivalent to existence of this full certificate,\nand every full certificate at level tau is represented by some r.\n\n### Proof of the identity and the nullspace condition\n\nAt a prefix finite node i<n, equation (2) makes outgoing minus incoming\nflow zero. At n it is\n\n    mu_n+sum_i beta_i-sum_i r_(i,n)\n      =sum_i mu_i=2.\n\nThus every fi coefficient in (5) cancels. Expanding each Q gives exactly\nthe matrices in (4), including the initial-vector cross coefficient\n-mu_i/2. This proves (5).\n\nLet 1 denote the all-ones vector of length n+1, and define the finite drift\nd_i=sum_(j finite)lambda_ij(P_i-P_j). Directly from (4) and the value flow,\n\n              2(A1)_i=2h_i+(1+P_i)mu_i+d_i.                          (7)\n\nFor a detailed derivation, the term e_i v_ij^T+v_ij e_i^T contributes\nlambda_ij(P_i-P_j) to coordinate i and\nh_k lambda_ij(1_(k<i)-1_(k<j)) to coordinate k. The difference-square\nterm annihilates 1. For k<n, summing the latter cut term over all finite\nedges gives h_k[2-sum_(i>k)mu_i] by finite value flow. The star terms\ncontribute (1+P_k)mu_k+h_k sum_(i>k)mu_i. The two tail sums cancel.\nFor k=n both cut terms vanish and h_n=0. This establishes (7) for all k.\n\nFor i<n, equations (1) and (7) imply\n\n              (A1)_i=mu_i/(2tau).                                    (8)\n\nAlso sum_i mu_i=2, so the x0 row of M annihilates\nv_H=(1,tau,...,tau).\n\nTo establish the last gradient row without assuming it, use the exact\nHuber jets\n\n    x0=1, xi=1-tau P_i, gi=tau,\n    fi=tau xi-tau^2/2, f_star=g_star=x_star=0.                         (9)\n\nThey are the GD trajectory of the fixed scalar function that is x^2/2\non |x|<=tau and tau|x|-tau^2/2 elsewhere. Since P_i<=S, every finite\nxi>=tau. Every finite-source Qij in (3) is zero, and 2fn=tau.\nEvaluate the polynomial identity (5) at (9): v_H^T M v_H=0, without any\npositivity assumption on the coefficients. All rows except possibly the\nlast already annihilate v_H, and its last coordinate tau is positive.\nConsequently the last row also annihilates it. We have\n\n              M v_H=0,  A1=mu/(2tau),  Z_hub 1=0.                    (10)\n\nCompleting the x0 square in (5) gives a positive initial square plus the\ngradient form with matrix Z_hub. Since Z_hub 1=0, for every scalar coordinate vector\nz=(z0,...,zn),\n\n    z^T Z_hub z=(z0-zn,...,z_(n-1)-zn)^T K_hub\n                                      (z0-zn,...,z_(n-1)-zn).         (11)\n\nHence M>=0 iff K_hub>=0. If the signs in (6) hold, (5) proves\n2fn<=tau||x0||^2 on every legal trajectory in every dimension. The exact\nHuber function (9) attains equality. Normalization from the predecessor\nthen gives W_n(h)=tau.\n\n### Why these coordinates cover every tight full certificate\n\nLet any nonnegative full certificate at level tau be given. Evaluate it\non (9). The only strict interpolation residuals are those leaving star:\n\n    Q_(star,i)=-tau[1-tau(P_i+1)]<0,\n\nbecause P_i<=S and 1-tau(S+1)=S/(2S+1)>0. The objective gap is zero.\nFull PSD and nonnegative multipliers therefore force lambda_(star,i)=0\nand Mv_H=0. The value flow implies sum mu_i=2. Applying (7) to the\nprefix rows gives (1), and the flow at each prefix node gives (2).\nTake r to be the actual finite prefix-source multipliers of the original\ncertificate. This reconstructs every coefficient, proving the asserted\nlosslessness. This argument does not delete incoming terminal edges.\n\n## 2. The actual seven-step word and rational coefficients\n\nWrite t=sqrt(2), a=(1+t)/2 and b=1+8(1+t)/9. For the assigned m=3\nparameters c_3=(4/3)(1+t), a_3=1/[2(1+t)], the actual word is exactly\n\n    h=(a,5/3,a,b,a,5/3,a),\n    S=(65+26t)/9,  tau=9/(139+52t).                                  (12)\n\nNo steps have been contracted or replaced. Use r=R_int/10^6, where R_int has\nrows i=0,...,6 and columns j=0,...,7:\n\n       0  12935   7196    7116   3081  2983  2929  3123\n   54548      0   9076   29849  11139  7899  5596  5450\n   30421  27844      0  227407   8150  7203  5823  5265\n   20167 182039 356887       0   3112  3600  4498  4434\n    9541  13214  13036   70218      0 16219  9699  7434\n   11466  13010  10230  174850  98657     0  9221  9067\n    6981   9672  10333  207590 167381 32994     0 13402\n\nEquations (1)--(4) specify every remaining multiplier and every entry of\nthe exact 9x9 residual in Q(sqrt(2)). All finite off-diagonal r are\nstrictly positive. The following rational enclosures verify the other\nsigns; all displayed integer intervals have common denominator 10^6:\n\n    mu_0 [98895,98896]       beta_0 [5134,5135]\n    mu_1 [143370,143371]     beta_1 [8213,8214]\n    mu_2 [106957,106958]     beta_2 [12312,12313]\n    mu_3 [390815,390816]     beta_3 [248522,248523]\n    mu_4 [179520,179521]     beta_4 [27361,27362]\n    mu_5 [311655,311656]     beta_5 [567258,567259]\n    mu_6 [352772,352773]     beta_6 [763359,763360]\n    mu_7 [416014,416015]\n\nThe zero star-source multipliers here are an explicit algebraic choice\nrequired by exact Huber complementarity. They are not a silent\ntruncation of the solver's small positive coefficients.\n\n## 3. Full PSD, certified with a fixed rational interval calculation\n\nHere is the precise arithmetic specification behind all the enclosures,\nso no floating eigenvalue or optimization output is a premise. Take\n\n    D=10^18,\n    l=1414213562373095048, u=l+1.\n\nThe integer inequalities l^2<2D^2<u^2 put t in [l/D,u/D]. Represent\neach intermediate interval by two integers [L,U] denoting [L/D,U/D].\nAddition and negation use their exact endpoint rules. For multiplication,\ntake the minimum and maximum of the four endpoint integer products,\ndivide by D, and round the lower endpoint down and upper endpoint up.\nFor division by an interval not containing zero, take the minimum and\nmaximum of the four rational quotients (numerator endpoint times D,\ndivided by denominator endpoint) and round outward to integers.\nAll input rationals are enclosed by the same outward rule. These rules\npreserve inclusion by monotonicity and the interval endpoint extrema.\n\nEvaluate (1)--(4) with these rules, then apply the following explicitly\nordered L_ldl D_ldl L_ldl^T recurrence to the leading 7x7 K_hub:\n\n    (L_ldl)_ii=1, (L_ldl)_ij=0 when j>i,\n    d_k=(K_hub)_kk-sum_(j<k)(L_ldl)_kj^2 d_j,\n    (L_ldl)_ik=[(K_hub)_ik-sum_(j<k)(L_ldl)_ij (L_ldl)_kj d_j]/d_k,\n                                      i>k, k=0,...,6.                (13)\n\nHere D_ldl denotes the diagonal matrix with entries d_0,...,d_6.\n\nThe resulting pivot enclosures, again with denominator 10^6, are\n\n    d_0 [33001,33002]\n    d_1 [18414,18415]\n    d_2 [32189,32190]\n    d_3 [6594,6595]\n    d_4 [55347,55348]\n    d_5 [17763,17764]\n    d_6 [13887,13888].                                                (14)\n\nFor additional intermediate audit data, the strictly lower triangular\nentries of L_ldl are enclosed by the following integer intervals /10^6:\n\n    row1: [-606615,-606614]\n    row2: [-301481,-301480], [-733972,-733971]\n    row3: [-388850,-388849], [126723,126724], [-290764,-290763]\n    row4: [-74869,-74868], [-461577,-461576], [-373558,-373557],\n          [-1923862,-1923861]\n    row5: [23910,23911], [-155759,-155758], [-235331,-235330],\n          [389447,389448], [-488882,-488881]\n    row6: [130893,130894], [51682,51683], [-87399,-87398],\n          [296037,296038], [-143294,-143293], [-1378617,-1378616].\n\nAll denominators in (13) are positive by the sequentially established\nlower endpoints in (14). Thus the exact K_hub has an L_ldl D_ldl L_ldl^T factorization\nwith all seven diagonal pivots positive. This proves K_hub positive definite.\nEquations (10)--(11) prove full 9x9 PSD and an exactly one-dimensional\nkernel generated by (1,tau,...,tau). In particular this is not PSD checked\nonly on the returned primal span.\n\nThe tiny reference calculation `c51_exact_h3_audit.py` implements exactly\nthese rational interval rules once on this fixed 7x7 matrix; it runs no\nsolver, search or numerical sweep. Its JSON gives every full M and Z_hub\nentry as an outward rational enclosure, as well as the finer pivot bounds.\nThe proof is the explicit coefficient formulas, the rational inequalities\nabove and the LDL recurrence, rather than any repaired numerical spectrum.\n\n## 4. Consequences and limits\n\nTogether (3), (5), (6), (12) and (14) constitute a full exact candidate\ncertificate with objective level 9/(139+52sqrt(2)). The fixed Huber\nfunction (9) attains it with initial distance exactly one. Every ordered\ninterpolation inequality and every formal gradient, including terminal,\nbelongs to the model. Returning to arbitrary L and initial distance uses\nthe normalization in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290.\n\nThe strict sign and pivot margins imply, by continuity of the explicit\nformulas, that the SAME fixed rational r gives a Huber-tight certificate\nfor every h in some open neighborhood of this seven-step word. This is\na local finite-horizon statement, not a claim that Huber tightness or the\npivot margins persist as m grows.\n\nThe numerical benchmark comparison is tau/U_7^ZJ approximately1.296454,\nwith the exact benchmark still defined by the full two-sequence DP.\nConsequently this finite word is not evidence of improvement over ZJ.\nThe operationally useful output for the growing-horizon upper attempt is\nan explicit lossless coordinate system in which the exact remaining tasks\nare mu,beta nonnegativity and the full K_hub PSD. No recurring n-dependent\nchoice of r, or uniform positive pivot bound, has been proved here.\n\nAll mathematical conclusions in this file remain candidate statements\nuntil accepted by the verifier; this file is not a fact-graph node.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["016cae39337b285e"], "artifact": "workers/max3/research/c51_exact_h3_full_certificate.md", "arithmetic_specification": "workers/max3/research/c51_exact_h3_audit.py", "full_rational_enclosures": "workers/max3/research/c51_exact_h3_audit.json", "scope": "full exact finite certificate and general coordinate elimination; no asymptotic extrapolation"}, "glossary": {"r_ij": "Chosen finite interpolation multiplier with source i<n and finite target j, r_ii=0.", "mu_i": "Multiplier lambda_(i,star), determined by the explicit Huber kernel equation.", "beta_i": "Multiplier lambda_(n,i), determined by prefix value flow.", "R_int": "The displayed seven-by-eight nonnegative integer coefficient array; r=R_int/10^6.", "Z_hub": "Full gradient Schur complement A-mu mu^T/(4tau).", "K_hub": "Leading n-by-n principal matrix of Z_hub; because Z_hub annihilates the all-ones vector, its PSD is equivalent to full certificate PSD.", "L_ldl": "Unit lower triangular factor in the explicitly specified exact LDL transpose recurrence.", "D_ldl": "Diagonal matrix of the positive LDL pivots.", "v_H": "Full formal Huber kernel vector (1,tau,...,tau)."}}
{"id": "dc51c9b098da4e10", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:27:15.493421+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Actual legal-witness filter for ALL allowed fixed-constant deformations and ALL prescribed final steps: if c_m=lambda(1+tau/m),a_m=(1-sigma/m)/c_m and H_m is the fixed-parameter ruler, then liminf B_(2^m-1) W_(2^m-1)(H_m with any final b_m>=0) >= sqrt(2)(1+lambda^(-1/2))/(2 exp(tau)). Hence fixed asymptotic improvement is impossible when tau<=log[sqrt(2)(1+lambda^(-1/2))/2]. In particular reducing tau to zero cannot repair the new full-array obstruction. The assigned tau1 is NOT excluded.", "evidence": "# Direct objective gluing on the actual critical words\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint calculation and certificate-space obstruction.\nNo solver and no fact_submit. The negative formal vectors below are NOT legal\nsmooth-convex trajectories. They refute a specified full multiplier array and\nits stated repairs, not the schedule or the original profile conjecture.\n\nThe only mathematical predecessor used is accepted fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290` for interpolation, and `b98bb807d514e80d` for the\nHuber/cut witnesses and the exact benchmark recursions. The recursive pattern\nwas suggested by Altschuler--Parrilo, arXiv:2309.16530, Theorem 3.4; all\nidentities needed here are derived below rather than imported from it.\n\n## 1. Normalization and a fully specified multiplier array\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For every sample let\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,\n    C_ij=-2 Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.\n\nEvery position is substituted using the actual GD steps. In the full formal\nquadratic, x_0,g_0,...,g_n are independent; they are not restricted to any\nchosen primal family.\n\nFix positive numbers a,c, with c>2. Write\n\n    d_j=a c^j,  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThroughout this calculation require d_j<M_j at the levels being used.\nDefine the following exact scalar recurrence:\n\n    Z_0=1, t_0=1,\n    z_j=(M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    r_j=1+2z_j=(3M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    Z_(j+1)=r_j Z_j,\n    t_(j+1)=(1+sqrt(4 Z_(j+1)-3))/2,\n    delta_j=Z_j-r_j d_j^2.                              (1)\n\nThus Z_j=t_j^2-t_j+1. All r_j>1, so the square roots are real and\nt_j>=1. This fixes every parameter; there is no numerical optimization.\n\nHere is the actual nonnegative array lambda^(j) on C_ij. Its base has only\nlambda^0_(star,0)=1. Suppose its block has length n=n_j, sum S=S_j,\nand put p=n, q=2n+1, b=1+d_j, z=z_j, r=r_j, t=t_j, t'=t_(j+1).\n\n1. Embed the old array on the left sample indices {0,...,p,star}.\n2. Add r times the old array on {p+1,...,q,star}.\n3. For each right queried index i=p+1,...,q-1, whose physical step is h_i,\n   add z h_i to entries (p,i) and (q,i), and subtract 2z h_i from (star,i).\n4. Add the following six sparse corrections:\n\n    (p,q): z,                 (q,p): z d_j,\n    (p,star): -S,             (q,star): 2S+b-rS,\n    (star,p): b-t,            (star,q): t'-rt.           (2)\n\nThe corrections individually may be signed, but the FINAL array is\nnonnegative. In fact its optimum entries are exactly\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=h_i for i<q,\n    lambda_(star,q)=t',\n    lambda_(i,star)=0 for i<q,\n    lambda_(q,star)=2S+b.                                (3)\n\nTo verify this inductively, left interior entries are unchanged, the old\nleft terminal entry t becomes b, right interior entries become\n(r-2z)h_i=h_i, and the right terminal entry becomes t'. Incoming optimum\nentries at the left terminal cancel, and the right terminal entry becomes\n2S+b. Every other update either embeds a nonnegative old coefficient or\nadds z h_i, z, or z d_j. This proves actual coefficient signs, including\nall optimum edges, rather than just signs of an auxiliary matrix.\n\n## 2. Complete identity and calibration\n\nThere is an exact gradient-only remainder E_j, defined on all the formal\ngradients of the block, such that\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-t_j g_end||^2-2t_j f_end\n       =sum lambda^(j)_ik C_ik + E_j.                  (4)\n\nIts recursion is\n\n    E_0=0,\n    E_(j+1)=E_j(left)+r_j E_j(right)\n                         +delta_j ||g_p||^2.            (5)\n\nHere each child is evaluated at its actual chronological sample indices.\nIn particular E_j contains no terminal-gradient term of its own: its\nvariables are only the midpoint gradients of all internal recursion nodes.\n\nWe verify (4)--(5) without omitting any function coefficient. Denote the\nleft endpoint and gradient by x,g and the right endpoint and gradient by\ny,v. Their values are f,F. The weighted right displacement is\n\n    w=sum_(p<i<q) h_i g_i=x-bg-y.\n\nThe rank-one correction in step 3 sums to\n\n    2zS(f+F)-zS(||g||^2+||v||^2)\n                         -2z<w,x+y-g-v>.               (6)\n\nCombine the two child identities, the correction (6), and (2). The\ncoefficient of f is\n\n    2[(z-1)S-b+z-zd_j]=0,\n\nbecause z(S+2-b)=S+b. The coefficient of F is -2t', and every\ninterior value has already canceled within its child and (6). The\noptimum value is zero under normalization; before normalization the\ntotal value flow cancels it against the terminal coefficient.\n\nThe x^2, <x,g>, <x,v>, <y,g>, and <g,v> coefficients all cancel.\nThe remaining expression is\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||y-t'v||^2-2t'F\n       +(r d_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2\n       +(Z_(j+1)-r Z_j)||v||^2\n       -E_j(left)-r E_j(right).\n\nThe last gradient coefficient is zero by (1). This is exactly (4)--(5).\nThis calculation also follows by direct expansion of (6); the cancellations\nuse only z(S+2-b)=S+b and r=1+2z.\n\nThe calibration is exact. Set lambda=1+sqrt(2), a=1/lambda, c=lambda.\nThen M_j=lambda^j, d_j=lambda^(j-1), z_j=lambda, r_j=lambda^2,\nZ_j=M_j^2, and delta_j=0 at every level. Thus E_j=0 and (4) is a\nvalid full initial-distance certificate at the undeformed silver word.\nIt is the canonical endpoint-square guarantee, not a claim that this\nparticular certificate attains the sharper Huber constant.\n\nFor an arbitrary real q_cert, the SAME nonnegative array supplies the\nfollowing fully value-canceled candidate objective matrix:\n\n    q_cert ||x_0||^2-2 f_n\n       +sum_(i!=k) [2 lambda^(m)_ki/t_m] Q_ik\n     =(q_cert-1/t_m)||x_0||^2\n       +[||x_n-t_m g_n||^2+E_m]/t_m.                 (7)\n\nThis explicitly includes the true initial vector and the optimum index.\nIts validity as an upper bound depends on FULL PSD of the right side.\n\n## 3. An exact negative midpoint for the assigned parameters\n\nNow set m>=128, epsilon=1/m, and use the assigned family\n\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c.\n\nFor these parameters 2<c<3 and 0<a<c-2<1. The exact mass formula is\n\n    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2),                 (8)\n\nso 0<A<1 and M_j-d_j=A(3-c)c^j+(1-A)2^j>0.\nThus every coefficient in the proposed array is defined and nonnegative.\n\nNevertheless delta_2<0. Here is a finite exact proof with an explicit\nparameter interval, not a limiting differential equation. Put s=sqrt(2).\nFor j=0,1,2, write r_j=N_j/D_j, where\n\n    N_0=(2+s)+(4-s)epsilon,\n    D_0=(2-s)+s epsilon,\n    N_1=(4+3s)+(9-3s)epsilon-epsilon^2,\n    D_1=s+(3-s)epsilon+epsilon^2,\n    N_2=(10+7s)+(19-5s)epsilon-(4+s)epsilon^2\n                                                 -(1+s)epsilon^3,\n    D_2=(2+s)+(5-3s)epsilon+s epsilon^2\n                                                 +(1+s)epsilon^3.\n\nAll denominator coefficients are positive. Since d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\n\n    delta_2=P(epsilon)/(D_0 D_1 D_2),\n    P=N_0 N_1 D_2-lambda^2(1-epsilon^2)^2 N_2 D_0 D_1.\n\nThe coefficients of P, in increasing degree 0 through 10, are\n\n    0,\n    -112-96s,\n    -96-84s,\n    640+12s,\n    528+368s,\n    88+92s,\n    -320-167s,\n    -202-158s,\n    -2-4s,\n    42+30s,\n    10+7s.                                           (9)\n\nThese coefficients follow by multiplying the displayed degree-at-most-three\npolynomials. A tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) polynomial multiplication independently\nchecked the list; the displayed identity is the mathematical evidence.\nUsing s<3/2, the sum of absolute upper bounds for coefficients of degree\nat least two is 3311. Consequently, for 0<epsilon<=1/128,\n\n    P(epsilon)/epsilon\n       <=-112-96s+3311 epsilon\n       <-112+3311/128<0.                             (10)\n\nFor comparison, the first derivative is\n\n    delta_2=-(48+28sqrt(2))epsilon+O(epsilon^2).\n\nThe same negative coefficient occurs at the finite diagnostic m=3:\n\n    P(1/3)=(-1059632-2141552sqrt(2))/59049<0.\n\nThe three relevant denominators are positive there as well. This agrees\nwith failure of the ARRAY even though max3's unrestricted numerical H_3\ndiagnostic reported a Huber match. That diagnostic is not used as a premise.\n\n## 4. Negative FULL formal directions, and limits of repairs\n\nEvery occurrence of a level-two midpoint contributes\n\n    omega_i delta_2 ||g_i||^2\n\nto E_m, with omega_i>0, a product of right-child weights r_j from its\nancestors. There are K=2^(m-3) such indices. Their actual step is the same\nnumber b_2=1+d_2. No other term of E_m uses any of those gradient\ncoordinates, by the diagonal recursion (5).\n\nFirst choose one such index i, set x_0=0, g_i=v!=0, all other queried\ngradients zero, and g_n=-(b_2/t_m)v. Then\n\n    x_n=-b_2 v, x_n-t_m g_n=0, E_m=omega_i delta_2||v||^2<0.\n\nThus (7) is not PSD for ANY q_cert. Merely increasing the initial-distance\ncoefficient does not repair the proposed array.\n\nThere is a larger obstruction when m>=4. Work first with scalar formal\ngradients, put x_0=g_n=0, allow arbitrary g_i at the K selected indices\nsubject to sum_i g_i=0, and set every other gradient to zero. This is a\nsubspace D of dimension K-1 on which\n\n    x_n=0, g_(n-1)=0,\n    E_m=delta_2 sum_i omega_i g_i^2<0\n\nfor every nonzero vector. The weights need not coincide. It follows that:\n\n* Any added quadratic depending only on x_0,x_n,g_n,g_(n-1) vanishes on D.\n* Replacing only the final physical step leaves every position unchanged\n  on D, because g_(n-1)=0. With the same array, the negative evaluation\n  persists for every prescribed final-step replacement and every q_cert.\n* Any PSD matrix addition of rank less than K-1 has a nonzero kernel vector\n  in D, by dimension counting, and therefore cannot repair the full matrix.\n\nThe last statement is about additions to this fixed matrix. Changing\nrelative interpolation multipliers may have an indefinite difference and\nis NOT excluded. Likewise a correction of growing rank is not excluded.\nThe selected vectors deliberately do not satisfy the interpolation\nconstraints; no legal lower witness, no W upper bound, and no impossibility\ntheorem for H_m is inferred.\n\n## 5. Fixed-constant deformations: the same ansatz always fails for tau>0\n\nConsider the allowed neighboring family\n\n    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=(1-sigma/m)/c,                (11)\n\nwith fixed real tau,sigma, for all sufficiently large m. Positivity and\nd_j<M_j hold uniformly over the word once 0<a<1 and 2<c<3: indeed\n\n    M_j/d_j=1/(c-2)\n               +[1/a-1/(c-2)](2/c)^j\n\nlies between 1/a and 1/(c-2), both greater than one.\n\nIf tau>0, the specified multiplier recursion always develops a negative\ncoefficient at a FIXED level as m tends to infinity. If sigma+tau<0,\nthen d_0>1/lambda and delta_0=1-r_0 d_0^2<0, since\nd maps to d^2(3+d)/(1-d) is strictly increasing for 0<d<1 and equals\none at d=1/lambda.\n\nFor sigma+tau>=0 the fixed level j=4 suffices. To show this, temporarily\nregard epsilon as a real variable tending to zero and set\n\n    u=2/lambda, D=sigma+(1+lambda^2)tau,\n    T_j=lambda^2 tau-D u^j.\n\nDifferentiation at epsilon=0 of the explicit finite formulas gives\n\n    M_j(0)=lambda^j,\n    (d/depsilon)log M_j |0=j tau-D(1-u^j),\n    (d/depsilon)(d_j/M_j)|0=(1/lambda)T_j.\n\nFor r(x)=(3+x)/(1-x), (d/dx)log r at x=1/lambda equals 2.\nUsing 1-u=1/lambda^2 and\n2(1/lambda-1/lambda^2)/(1-u)=2sqrt(2), equation (1) therefore gives\n\n    (d/depsilon)[delta_j/M_j^2]|0\n       =2sqrt(2)[tau(j-lambda^2)-D(1-2u^j)].          (12)\n\nOne can also verify (12) by summing the finite geometric series for T_j.\nAt j=4, 4-lambda^2<0 and 1-2u^4>0. The latter follows from\nu^4=272-192sqrt(2)<1/2; squaring 543<384sqrt(2) verifies it with\npositive sides. Moreover D>=lambda^2 tau>0. Thus (12) is strictly\nnegative. The expression is an analytic finite rational function near\nzero, so delta_4<0 for every sufficiently large m. This uses a derivative\nat one fixed finite depth, not an unproved continuum recurrence.\n\nThe same full-vector argument as in Section 4 then excludes the array and\nits stated endpoint or fixed-rank patches. This generalization does not\nexclude a different full certificate for any of the deformed schedules.\n\n## 6. Rechecking the exact comparison and an actual deformation filter\n\nSet rho=log_2(lambda). From the accepted exact recursions, primitive shifted\nmass C_n=S_circle(n)+1 has join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n\nand the benchmark B_n has join\n\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nHere is a proof of C_n<=(n+1)^rho and B_n<=1+4n^rho, retained rather\nthan assuming the earlier draft comparison. Define F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1.\nFor t=exp(z), direct differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp(z))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n        [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe numerator bracket is positive since rho>1 and its derivative is\nrho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0. Convexity at z,-z gives\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. With A=t^rho B and\nE=(1+t)^rho B, this implies (E-A)(E-B)>=2AB, so\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\n\nInduction in the primitive recursion gives its claimed bound. Also\nG(A,D)<=4A+D. If k+l+1=n, the dominant induction now gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho.\n\nThe last inequality follows from superadditivity of t^rho on nonnegative\narguments, which follows by integrating its increasing derivative.\n\nFor (11), the exact mass formula (8) gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+tau/m)^m [A+(1-A)(2/c)^m] -> exp(tau).       (13)\n\nThus the actual benchmark satisfies\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)/(2M_m-1)<=2 exp(-tau).             (14)\n\nIn particular tau=1 recovers 2/e. This comparison alone is not an\nobjective upper bound; the failed matrix in Section 4 cannot supply one.\n\nThere is also a useful LOWER bound for the exact benchmark. Balanced\nprimitive joins give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j. Put b_0=1 and\n\n    b_(j+1)=G(1,b_j)/lambda.\n\nBalanced dominant joins imply B_(2^j-1)>=b_j lambda^j. The map in this\nrecursion is strictly increasing. Its fixed point above one is\n\n    b_infty=sqrt(2)(1+lambda^(-1/2))>2.                (15)\n\nTo verify the fixed point, squaring the equation G(1,b)=lambda b\ngives lambda(lambda-1)b^2-4lambda b+4=0; the positive root (15)\nsatisfies the original equation, whereas the smaller algebraic root does\nnot. Alternatively uniqueness above one follows because\nG(1,b)/b=2/b+1/2+sqrt(1+8/b)/2 is strictly decreasing. Since\n1<b_1=4/lambda<b_infty, monotonicity shows b_j increases to b_infty.\n\nThis yields an ACTUAL legal-witness filter even when the final step is\nreplaced arbitrarily. Let P_m=S_m-(1+d_0) be the sum of the queried prefix\nbefore that final step, and prescribe any b_m>=0. For P_m>=1, the two\naccepted Huber/cut witnesses give\n\n    W_(n_m)(H_m with final step b_m)\n       >=max{1/(2P_m+2b_m+1),\n                    (b_m-1)_+^2/(P_m+1)^2}\n       >=1/(2P_m+3+2sqrt(P_m)).                       (16)\n\nFor b_m<=1+sqrt(P_m), the first term proves (16). For larger b_m, the\nsecond is at least P_m/(P_m+1)^2, which is at least its right side\nbecause P_m^2+P_m+2P_m sqrt(P_m)-1>=0 for P_m>=1.\nEquations (13), (15), and (16) prove the uniform statement\n\n    liminf_m B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(modified word)\n                     >=b_infty/(2 exp(tau)).           (17)\n\nConsequently no fixed asymptotic separation below the benchmark is possible\nwithin these fixed-constant deformations, even with an arbitrary final step,\nwhen tau<=log(b_infty/2). This is a legal lower-bound conclusion for that\nparameter region. It is distinct from the formal-matrix obstruction for\ntau>0, and does not settle the assigned tau=1 family.\n\n## 7. A richer exact join, with the unresolved full-matrix issue exposed\n\nThe following calculation records the next concrete repair interface. It\ndoes not assert that a scalar local condition closes the target.\n\nReplace the canonical child storage by\n\n    I=||x_start||^2-||x_end-u0 g_end||^2-2v0 f_end\n       =sum lambda C+E,\n    Z=(u0-1)^2+v0,\n    k=S+u0-v0>=0.\n\nAssume its optimum entries are h_i on (star,i), u0 on the terminal\n(star,end), zero on other (i,star), and k on (end,star); retain the\nENTIRE gradient remainder E. These properties hold for the calibrated\ncanonical family. They are assumptions of this interface, not all full\ncertificates.\n\nUse two nonnegative correction weights A0,B0. Scale the right child by\nr=1+A0+B0. For each right queried i add A0 h_i on (p,i), B0 h_i on\n(q,i), and -(A0+B0)h_i on (star,i). Choose cross coefficients p0,q0>=0\nwith\n\n    p0-q0=b+(1-A0)S.\n\nAdjust optimum entries to physical h_i, terminal u1, and\nk1=2S+b+u1-v1>=0 exactly as in (2), with child k in place of S.\nThus the actual final array is nonnegative provided the named scalar\ncoefficients and u1,k1 are nonnegative. Put\n\n    d=b-1, M=S+1, t=A0-B0,\n    H=M+d-B0(M-d)-t M,\n    ell=p0-B0-t,\n    Z1=(u1-1)^2+v1,\n    V=x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q.\n\nFull coefficient expansion, including all function values, gives\n\n    E_new=E_left+r E_right\n       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g_p+ell g_q>\n       +(Z-rd^2)||g_p||^2+(rZ-Z1)||g_q||^2.           (18)\n\nFor example the canonical construction sets A0=B0=z, p0=z,\nq0=zd, H=ell=t=0 and Z1=rZ, recovering (5).\nTo check (18), first use D=x_p-x_q. The local coefficients of\nD^2, D g_p, D g_q are t,-2(ell+t-H),2(ell+t).\nSubstituting D=V+g_p-g_q makes the g_p g_q coefficient zero and gives\nexactly the displayed diagonal and cross terms. The value coefficient\nat p is 2[(A0-1)S-b+p0-q0]=0 and the terminal coefficient is -2v1.\n\nDemanding the isolated three-vector matrix in (18) be PSD is only a\nSUFFICIENT restriction. It forces Z>=rd^2 and Z1<=rZ, and risks\nrepeating the already recorded scalar-deficit failure. The useful open\nquestion is whether the FULL child remainders can pay for an indefinite\nlocal term.\n\nThere is a finite exact way to retain that information for this array\nclass. Define the gradient moment z_block=sum h_i g_i+g_end. If a child\nremainder is decomposed into nonnegative internal squares plus the sharp\ntwo-variable form\n\n    a0||z_block||^2+2b0<z_block,g_end>+c0||g_end||^2,\n\nthen the parent remainder before elimination is the explicit four-variable\nform, in L=z_left, g=g_p, R=z_right, e=g_q,\n\n    a0||L||^2+2b0<L,g>+c0||g||^2\n      +r[a0||R||^2+2b0<R,e>+c0||e||^2]\n      +t||d g+R||^2+2<d g+R,(H-ell)g+ell e>\n      +(Z-rd^2)||g||^2+(rZ-Z1)||e||^2.                (19)\n\nThe parent moment is exactly z_parent=L+d g+R. Substituting\nL=z_parent-dg-R and completing squares in g,R gives its next\ntwo-variable form when the corresponding eliminated block is PSD and\nthe cross columns lie in its range. Negative eliminated directions are\nfull-matrix obstructions and must not be dropped. This is an explicit\nrecursively generated multiplier family and exact remainder interface;\nno choice of its free parameters or uniform constant establishing the\nrequested asymptotic separation has yet been proved.\n\nThe original target remains open. The finished result of this attempt is\nthe fully exhibited obstruction to (1)--(7), the exact parameter filter\n(17), and the identified need to change the full recursive array rather\nthan inflate its endpoint bound.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max/research/max_direct_full_matrix_gluing.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "status": "unverified_joint_derivation_no_submission", "scope": "Actual normalized lower witnesses, fixed-constant deformation region; not unrestricted target", "related_finding": "a1661dacd3b561f7"}, "glossary": {"b_infty": "The limit sqrt(2)(1+lambda^(-1/2)) of the balanced dominant comparison recurrence b_0=1,b_(j+1)=G(1,b_j)/lambda.", "P_m": "Sum of all queried steps of H_m except its final one, before prescribing the replacement b_m.", "tau": "Fixed growth deformation in c_m=lambda(1+tau/m), as allowed in the assignment.", "sigma": "Fixed amplitude deformation in a_m=(1-sigma/m)/c_m, as allowed in the assignment."}}
{"id": "661034034c273cb4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T19:42:51.951977+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Stronger FULL-matrix zero-mode obstruction for the new direct-objective joins: if a PSD child remainder has an attained boundary zero z_block=kappa g_end with kappa²=Z, then EVERY general join (18), with arbitrary mixed H/ell and all child matrices retained, requires Z_next<=Z²/d². Equality propagates the same type of zero, kappa_next=-Z/d. The constructed exact H3 repair has this zero mode. Thus any continuation saturating storage at every level still collapses double-exponentially on the ACTUAL critical word; changing mixed coefficients alone does not repair it. Nonsaturating matrix choices and other multiplier families remain open.", "evidence": "# Direct objective gluing on the actual critical words\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint calculation and certificate-space obstruction.\nNo solver and no fact_submit. The negative formal vectors below are NOT legal\nsmooth-convex trajectories. They refute a specified full multiplier array and\nits stated repairs, not the schedule or the original profile conjecture.\n\nThe only mathematical predecessor used is accepted fact\n`1252b8823bb5a290` for interpolation, and `b98bb807d514e80d` for the\nHuber/cut witnesses and the exact benchmark recursions. The recursive pattern\nwas suggested by Altschuler--Parrilo, arXiv:2309.16530, Theorem 3.4; all\nidentities needed here are derived below rather than imported from it.\n\n## 1. Normalization and a fully specified multiplier array\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For every sample let\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,\n    C_ij=-2 Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.\n\nEvery position is substituted using the actual GD steps. In the full formal\nquadratic, x_0,g_0,...,g_n are independent; they are not restricted to any\nchosen primal family.\n\nFix positive numbers a,c, with c>2. Write\n\n    d_j=a c^j,  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThroughout this calculation require d_j<M_j at the levels being used.\nDefine the following exact scalar recurrence:\n\n    Z_0=1, t_0=1,\n    z_j=(M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    r_j=1+2z_j=(3M_j+d_j)/(M_j-d_j),\n    Z_(j+1)=r_j Z_j,\n    t_(j+1)=(1+sqrt(4 Z_(j+1)-3))/2,\n    delta_j=Z_j-r_j d_j^2.                              (1)\n\nThus Z_j=t_j^2-t_j+1. All r_j>1, so the square roots are real and\nt_j>=1. This fixes every parameter; there is no numerical optimization.\n\nHere is the actual nonnegative array lambda^(j) on C_ij. Its base has only\nlambda^0_(star,0)=1. Suppose its block has length n=n_j, sum S=S_j,\nand put p=n, q=2n+1, b=1+d_j, z=z_j, r=r_j, t=t_j, t'=t_(j+1).\n\n1. Embed the old array on the left sample indices {0,...,p,star}.\n2. Add r times the old array on {p+1,...,q,star}.\n3. For each right queried index i=p+1,...,q-1, whose physical step is h_i,\n   add z h_i to entries (p,i) and (q,i), and subtract 2z h_i from (star,i).\n4. Add the following six sparse corrections:\n\n    (p,q): z,                 (q,p): z d_j,\n    (p,star): -S,             (q,star): 2S+b-rS,\n    (star,p): b-t,            (star,q): t'-rt.           (2)\n\nThe corrections individually may be signed, but the FINAL array is\nnonnegative. In fact its optimum entries are exactly\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=h_i for i<q,\n    lambda_(star,q)=t',\n    lambda_(i,star)=0 for i<q,\n    lambda_(q,star)=2S+b.                                (3)\n\nTo verify this inductively, left interior entries are unchanged, the old\nleft terminal entry t becomes b, right interior entries become\n(r-2z)h_i=h_i, and the right terminal entry becomes t'. Incoming optimum\nentries at the left terminal cancel, and the right terminal entry becomes\n2S+b. Every other update either embeds a nonnegative old coefficient or\nadds z h_i, z, or z d_j. This proves actual coefficient signs, including\nall optimum edges, rather than just signs of an auxiliary matrix.\n\n## 2. Complete identity and calibration\n\nThere is an exact gradient-only remainder E_j, defined on all the formal\ngradients of the block, such that\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-t_j g_end||^2-2t_j f_end\n       =sum lambda^(j)_ik C_ik + E_j.                  (4)\n\nIts recursion is\n\n    E_0=0,\n    E_(j+1)=E_j(left)+r_j E_j(right)\n                         +delta_j ||g_p||^2.            (5)\n\nHere each child is evaluated at its actual chronological sample indices.\nIn particular E_j contains no terminal-gradient term of its own: its\nvariables are only the midpoint gradients of all internal recursion nodes.\n\nWe verify (4)--(5) without omitting any function coefficient. Denote the\nleft endpoint and gradient by x,g and the right endpoint and gradient by\ny,v. Their values are f,F. The weighted right displacement is\n\n    w=sum_(p<i<q) h_i g_i=x-bg-y.\n\nThe rank-one correction in step 3 sums to\n\n    2zS(f+F)-zS(||g||^2+||v||^2)\n                         -2z<w,x+y-g-v>.               (6)\n\nCombine the two child identities, the correction (6), and (2). The\ncoefficient of f is\n\n    2[(z-1)S-b+z-zd_j]=0,\n\nbecause z(S+2-b)=S+b. The coefficient of F is -2t', and every\ninterior value has already canceled within its child and (6). The\noptimum value is zero under normalization; before normalization the\ntotal value flow cancels it against the terminal coefficient.\n\nThe x^2, <x,g>, <x,v>, <y,g>, and <g,v> coefficients all cancel.\nThe remaining expression is\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||y-t'v||^2-2t'F\n       +(r d_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2\n       +(Z_(j+1)-r Z_j)||v||^2\n       -E_j(left)-r E_j(right).\n\nThe last gradient coefficient is zero by (1). This is exactly (4)--(5).\nThis calculation also follows by direct expansion of (6); the cancellations\nuse only z(S+2-b)=S+b and r=1+2z.\n\nThe calibration is exact. Set lambda=1+sqrt(2), a=1/lambda, c=lambda.\nThen M_j=lambda^j, d_j=lambda^(j-1), z_j=lambda, r_j=lambda^2,\nZ_j=M_j^2, and delta_j=0 at every level. Thus E_j=0 and (4) is a\nvalid full initial-distance certificate at the undeformed silver word.\nIt is the canonical endpoint-square guarantee, not a claim that this\nparticular certificate attains the sharper Huber constant.\n\nFor an arbitrary real q_cert, the SAME nonnegative array supplies the\nfollowing fully value-canceled candidate objective matrix:\n\n    q_cert ||x_0||^2-2 f_n\n       +sum_(i!=k) [2 lambda^(m)_ki/t_m] Q_ik\n     =(q_cert-1/t_m)||x_0||^2\n       +[||x_n-t_m g_n||^2+E_m]/t_m.                 (7)\n\nThis explicitly includes the true initial vector and the optimum index.\nIts validity as an upper bound depends on FULL PSD of the right side.\n\n## 3. An exact negative midpoint for the assigned parameters\n\nNow set m>=128, epsilon=1/m, and use the assigned family\n\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c.\n\nFor these parameters 2<c<3 and 0<a<c-2<1. The exact mass formula is\n\n    M_j=A c^j+(1-A)2^j,  A=a/(c-2),                 (8)\n\nso 0<A<1 and M_j-d_j=A(3-c)c^j+(1-A)2^j>0.\nThus every coefficient in the proposed array is defined and nonnegative.\n\nNevertheless delta_2<0. Here is a finite exact proof with an explicit\nparameter interval, not a limiting differential equation. Put s=sqrt(2).\nFor j=0,1,2, write r_j=N_j/D_j, where\n\n    N_0=(2+s)+(4-s)epsilon,\n    D_0=(2-s)+s epsilon,\n    N_1=(4+3s)+(9-3s)epsilon-epsilon^2,\n    D_1=s+(3-s)epsilon+epsilon^2,\n    N_2=(10+7s)+(19-5s)epsilon-(4+s)epsilon^2\n                                                 -(1+s)epsilon^3,\n    D_2=(2+s)+(5-3s)epsilon+s epsilon^2\n                                                 +(1+s)epsilon^3.\n\nAll denominator coefficients are positive. Since d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\n\n    delta_2=P(epsilon)/(D_0 D_1 D_2),\n    P=N_0 N_1 D_2-lambda^2(1-epsilon^2)^2 N_2 D_0 D_1.\n\nThe coefficients of P, in increasing degree 0 through 10, are\n\n    0,\n    -112-96s,\n    -96-84s,\n    640+12s,\n    528+368s,\n    88+92s,\n    -320-167s,\n    -202-158s,\n    -2-4s,\n    42+30s,\n    10+7s.                                           (9)\n\nThese coefficients follow by multiplying the displayed degree-at-most-three\npolynomials. A tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) polynomial multiplication independently\nchecked the list; the displayed identity is the mathematical evidence.\nUsing s<3/2, the sum of absolute upper bounds for coefficients of degree\nat least two is 3311. Consequently, for 0<epsilon<=1/128,\n\n    P(epsilon)/epsilon\n       <=-112-96s+3311 epsilon\n       <-112+3311/128<0.                             (10)\n\nFor comparison, the first derivative is\n\n    delta_2=-(48+28sqrt(2))epsilon+O(epsilon^2).\n\nThe same negative coefficient occurs at the finite diagnostic m=3:\n\n    P(1/3)=(-1059632-2141552sqrt(2))/59049<0.\n\nThe three relevant denominators are positive there as well. This agrees\nwith failure of the ARRAY even though max3's unrestricted numerical H_3\ndiagnostic reported a Huber match. That diagnostic is not used as a premise.\n\n## 4. Negative FULL formal directions, and limits of repairs\n\nEvery occurrence of a level-two midpoint contributes\n\n    omega_i delta_2 ||g_i||^2\n\nto E_m, with omega_i>0, a product of right-child weights r_j from its\nancestors. There are K=2^(m-3) such indices. Their actual step is the same\nnumber b_2=1+d_2. No other term of E_m uses any of those gradient\ncoordinates, by the diagonal recursion (5).\n\nFirst choose one such index i, set x_0=0, g_i=v!=0, all other queried\ngradients zero, and g_n=-(b_2/t_m)v. Then\n\n    x_n=-b_2 v, x_n-t_m g_n=0, E_m=omega_i delta_2||v||^2<0.\n\nThus (7) is not PSD for ANY q_cert. Merely increasing the initial-distance\ncoefficient does not repair the proposed array.\n\nThere is a larger obstruction when m>=4. Work first with scalar formal\ngradients, put x_0=g_n=0, allow arbitrary g_i at the K selected indices\nsubject to sum_i g_i=0, and set every other gradient to zero. This is a\nsubspace D of dimension K-1 on which\n\n    x_n=0, g_(n-1)=0,\n    E_m=delta_2 sum_i omega_i g_i^2<0\n\nfor every nonzero vector. The weights need not coincide. It follows that:\n\n* Any added quadratic depending only on x_0,x_n,g_n,g_(n-1) vanishes on D.\n* Replacing only the final physical step leaves every position unchanged\n  on D, because g_(n-1)=0. With the same array, the negative evaluation\n  persists for every prescribed final-step replacement and every q_cert.\n* Any PSD matrix addition of rank less than K-1 has a nonzero kernel vector\n  in D, by dimension counting, and therefore cannot repair the full matrix.\n\nThe last statement is about additions to this fixed matrix. Changing\nrelative interpolation multipliers may have an indefinite difference and\nis NOT excluded. Likewise a correction of growing rank is not excluded.\nThe selected vectors deliberately do not satisfy the interpolation\nconstraints; no legal lower witness, no W upper bound, and no impossibility\ntheorem for H_m is inferred.\n\n## 5. Fixed-constant deformations: the same ansatz always fails for tau>0\n\nConsider the allowed neighboring family\n\n    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=(1-sigma/m)/c,                (11)\n\nwith fixed real tau,sigma, for all sufficiently large m. Positivity and\nd_j<M_j hold uniformly over the word once 0<a<1 and 2<c<3: indeed\n\n    M_j/d_j=1/(c-2)\n               +[1/a-1/(c-2)](2/c)^j\n\nlies between 1/a and 1/(c-2), both greater than one.\n\nIf tau>0, the specified multiplier recursion always develops a negative\ncoefficient at a FIXED level as m tends to infinity. If sigma+tau<0,\nthen d_0>1/lambda and delta_0=1-r_0 d_0^2<0, since\nd maps to d^2(3+d)/(1-d) is strictly increasing for 0<d<1 and equals\none at d=1/lambda.\n\nFor sigma+tau>=0 the fixed level j=4 suffices. To show this, temporarily\nregard epsilon as a real variable tending to zero and set\n\n    u=2/lambda, D=sigma+(1+lambda^2)tau,\n    T_j=lambda^2 tau-D u^j.\n\nDifferentiation at epsilon=0 of the explicit finite formulas gives\n\n    M_j(0)=lambda^j,\n    (d/depsilon)log M_j |0=j tau-D(1-u^j),\n    (d/depsilon)(d_j/M_j)|0=(1/lambda)T_j.\n\nFor r(x)=(3+x)/(1-x), (d/dx)log r at x=1/lambda equals 2.\nUsing 1-u=1/lambda^2 and\n2(1/lambda-1/lambda^2)/(1-u)=2sqrt(2), equation (1) therefore gives\n\n    (d/depsilon)[delta_j/M_j^2]|0\n       =2sqrt(2)[tau(j-lambda^2)-D(1-2u^j)].          (12)\n\nOne can also verify (12) by summing the finite geometric series for T_j.\nAt j=4, 4-lambda^2<0 and 1-2u^4>0. The latter follows from\nu^4=272-192sqrt(2)<1/2; squaring 543<384sqrt(2) verifies it with\npositive sides. Moreover D>=lambda^2 tau>0. Thus (12) is strictly\nnegative. The expression is an analytic finite rational function near\nzero, so delta_4<0 for every sufficiently large m. This uses a derivative\nat one fixed finite depth, not an unproved continuum recurrence.\n\nThe same full-vector argument as in Section 4 then excludes the array and\nits stated endpoint or fixed-rank patches. This generalization does not\nexclude a different full certificate for any of the deformed schedules.\n\n## 6. Rechecking the exact comparison and an actual deformation filter\n\nSet rho=log_2(lambda). From the accepted exact recursions, primitive shifted\nmass C_n=S_circle(n)+1 has join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n\nand the benchmark B_n has join\n\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nHere is a proof of C_n<=(n+1)^rho and B_n<=1+4n^rho, retained rather\nthan assuming the earlier draft comparison. Define F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1.\nFor t=exp(z), direct differentiation gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp(z))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n        [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.\n\nThe numerator bracket is positive since rho>1 and its derivative is\nrho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0. Convexity at z,-z gives\nF(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. With A=t^rho B and\nE=(1+t)^rho B, this implies (E-A)(E-B)>=2AB, so\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.\n\nInduction in the primitive recursion gives its claimed bound. Also\nG(A,D)<=4A+D. If k+l+1=n, the dominant induction now gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho.\n\nThe last inequality follows from superadditivity of t^rho on nonnegative\narguments, which follows by integrating its increasing derivative.\n\nFor (11), the exact mass formula (8) gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+tau/m)^m [A+(1-A)(2/c)^m] -> exp(tau).       (13)\n\nThus the actual benchmark satisfies\n\n    limsup B_(2^m-1)/(2M_m-1)<=2 exp(-tau).             (14)\n\nIn particular tau=1 recovers 2/e. This comparison alone is not an\nobjective upper bound; the failed matrix in Section 4 cannot supply one.\n\nThere is also a useful LOWER bound for the exact benchmark. Balanced\nprimitive joins give C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j. Put b_0=1 and\n\n    b_(j+1)=G(1,b_j)/lambda.\n\nBalanced dominant joins imply B_(2^j-1)>=b_j lambda^j. The map in this\nrecursion is strictly increasing. Its fixed point above one is\n\n    b_infty=sqrt(2)(1+lambda^(-1/2))>2.                (15)\n\nTo verify the fixed point, squaring the equation G(1,b)=lambda b\ngives lambda(lambda-1)b^2-4lambda b+4=0; the positive root (15)\nsatisfies the original equation, whereas the smaller algebraic root does\nnot. Alternatively uniqueness above one follows because\nG(1,b)/b=2/b+1/2+sqrt(1+8/b)/2 is strictly decreasing. Since\n1<b_1=4/lambda<b_infty, monotonicity shows b_j increases to b_infty.\n\nThis yields an ACTUAL legal-witness filter even when the final step is\nreplaced arbitrarily. Let P_m=S_m-(1+d_0) be the sum of the queried prefix\nbefore that final step, and prescribe any b_m>=0. For P_m>=1, the two\naccepted Huber/cut witnesses give\n\n    W_(n_m)(H_m with final step b_m)\n       >=max{1/(2P_m+2b_m+1),\n                    (b_m-1)_+^2/(P_m+1)^2}\n       >=1/(2P_m+3+2sqrt(P_m)).                       (16)\n\nFor b_m<=1+sqrt(P_m), the first term proves (16). For larger b_m, the\nsecond is at least P_m/(P_m+1)^2, which is at least its right side\nbecause P_m^2+P_m+2P_m sqrt(P_m)-1>=0 for P_m>=1.\nEquations (13), (15), and (16) prove the uniform statement\n\n    liminf_m B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(modified word)\n                     >=b_infty/(2 exp(tau)).           (17)\n\nConsequently no fixed asymptotic separation below the benchmark is possible\nwithin these fixed-constant deformations, even with an arbitrary final step,\nwhen tau<=log(b_infty/2). This is a legal lower-bound conclusion for that\nparameter region. It is distinct from the formal-matrix obstruction for\ntau>0, and does not settle the assigned tau=1 family.\n\n## 7. A richer exact join, with the unresolved full-matrix issue exposed\n\nThe following calculation records the next concrete repair interface. It\ndoes not assert that a scalar local condition closes the target.\n\nReplace the canonical child storage by\n\n    I=||x_start||^2-||x_end-u0 g_end||^2-2v0 f_end\n       =sum lambda C+E,\n    Z=(u0-1)^2+v0,\n    k=S+u0-v0>=0.\n\nAssume its optimum entries are h_i on (star,i), u0 on the terminal\n(star,end), zero on other (i,star), and k on (end,star); retain the\nENTIRE gradient remainder E. These properties hold for the calibrated\ncanonical family. They are assumptions of this interface, not all full\ncertificates.\n\nUse two nonnegative correction weights A0,B0. Scale the right child by\nr=1+A0+B0. For each right queried i add A0 h_i on (p,i), B0 h_i on\n(q,i), and -(A0+B0)h_i on (star,i). Choose cross coefficients p0,q0>=0\nwith\n\n    p0-q0=b+(1-A0)S.\n\nAdjust optimum entries to physical h_i, terminal u1, and\nk1=2S+b+u1-v1>=0 exactly as in (2), with child k in place of S.\nThus the actual final array is nonnegative provided the named scalar\ncoefficients and u1,k1 are nonnegative. Put\n\n    d=b-1, M=S+1, t=A0-B0,\n    H=M+d-B0(M-d)-t M,\n    ell=p0-B0-t,\n    Z1=(u1-1)^2+v1,\n    V=x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q.\n\nFull coefficient expansion, including all function values, gives\n\n    E_new=E_left+r E_right\n       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g_p+ell g_q>\n       +(Z-rd^2)||g_p||^2+(rZ-Z1)||g_q||^2.           (18)\n\nFor example the canonical construction sets A0=B0=z, p0=z,\nq0=zd, H=ell=t=0 and Z1=rZ, recovering (5).\nTo check (18), first use D=x_p-x_q. The local coefficients of\nD^2, D g_p, D g_q are t,-2(ell+t-H),2(ell+t).\nSubstituting D=V+g_p-g_q makes the g_p g_q coefficient zero and gives\nexactly the displayed diagonal and cross terms. The value coefficient\nat p is 2[(A0-1)S-b+p0-q0]=0 and the terminal coefficient is -2v1.\n\nDemanding the isolated three-vector matrix in (18) be PSD is only a\nSUFFICIENT restriction. It forces Z>=rd^2 and Z1<=rZ, and risks\nrepeating the already recorded scalar-deficit failure. The useful open\nquestion is whether the FULL child remainders can pay for an indefinite\nlocal term.\n\nThere is a finite exact way to retain that information for this array\nclass. Define the gradient moment z_block=sum h_i g_i+g_end. If a child\nremainder is decomposed into nonnegative internal squares plus the sharp\ntwo-variable form\n\n    a0||z_block||^2+2b0<z_block,g_end>+c0||g_end||^2,\n\nthen the parent remainder before elimination is the explicit four-variable\nform, in L=z_left, g=g_p, R=z_right, e=g_q,\n\n    a0||L||^2+2b0<L,g>+c0||g||^2\n      +r[a0||R||^2+2b0<R,e>+c0||e||^2]\n      +t||d g+R||^2+2<d g+R,(H-ell)g+ell e>\n      +(Z-rd^2)||g||^2+(rZ-Z1)||e||^2.                (19)\n\nThe parent moment is exactly z_parent=L+d g+R. Substituting\nL=z_parent-dg-R and completing squares in g,R gives its next\ntwo-variable form when the corresponding eliminated block is PSD and\nthe cross columns lie in its range. Negative eliminated directions are\nfull-matrix obstructions and must not be dropped. This is an explicit\nrecursively generated multiplier family and exact remainder interface;\nno choice of its free parameters or uniform constant establishing the\nrequested asymptotic separation has yet been proved.\n\nThe original target remains open. The finished result of this attempt is\nthe fully exhibited obstruction to (1)--(7), the exact parameter filter\n(17), and the identified need to change the full recursive array rather\nthan inflate its endpoint bound.\n\n## 8. A constructive cross-block repair, and its exact finite-depth limit\n\nThere is an actual positive repair of the first bad join in Section 3.\nIt is useful to record it before diagnosing its later failure. Everything\nin this section remains part of the same unverified joint calculation.\n\nFirst an elementary seed, including its full initial-distance certificate.\nFor one physical step h with 0<h<=sqrt(2), put M=1+h. Direct expansion\nof the interpolation expressions gives\n\n    V_h=2h(f_0-f_1)+h(1-h)||g_0||^2-hM||g_1||^2\n       =M C_(0,1)+C_(1,0)+(2-h^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nAdding h C_(star,0)+M C_(star,1) gives\n\n    ||x_0||^2-||x_1-Mg_1||^2-2(2M-1)f_1\n       =sum lambda C+(2-h^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.             (20)\n\nAll four displayed multipliers are nonnegative. Its parameters in Section\n7 are u0=M, v0=2M-1, Z=M^2, k=0. Its exact remainder is\na0||z_block-Mg_1||^2 with a0=(2-h^2)/h^2.\n\nHere is an explicit subcritical join that builds the H_2 child needed next.\nSuppose a block of mass M has u0=M,v0=2M-1,Z=M^2,k=0 and a full PSD\nremainder E. Put x=d/M, assume 0<x<sqrt(2)-1, and set\n\n    A0=1/x, B0=2+x, r=3+x+1/x,\n    t=1/x-2-x>0,\n    eta=x^2+2x-1<0,\n    ell=M eta/x, p0=(1+M eta)/x, q0=M,\n    u1=2M+d, v1=2(2M+d)-1, Z1=(2M+d)^2.             (21)\n\nRequire p0>=0. These parameters satisfy the value-flow and optimum-edge\nconditions of Section 7. If the child remainder is at least\na0||z_block-Mg_end||^2, then the FULL new remainder is at least\n\n    a0||U||^2+(r a0+t)||V||^2\n                    +M^2(-eta)||g_p-g_q||^2,          (22)\n\nwhere U=z_left-Mg_p and V=z_right-Mg_q. All omitted terms here are\nthe actual nonnegative child remainders after their stated lower forms\nare removed. Formula (22) follows by substituting (21) into (18);\nthe mixed coefficient between V and g_p-g_q is A0 d-M=0.\nThe parent moment obeys\n\n    z_parent-(2M+d)g_q=U+V+(M+d)(g_p-g_q).\n\nWeighted completion of squares therefore also gives the sharp condensed\nlower form a1||z_parent-(2M+d)g_q||^2, where\n\n    1/a1=1/a0+1/(r a0+t)+(1+x)^2/(-eta).             (23)\n\nThere is no discarded negative direction in this completion: the\ndifference between the three squares in (22) and (23) is a sum of\nnonnegative squares, or follows by expanding weighted Cauchy--Schwarz.\n\nApply (20) with h=1+d_0 for the assigned epsilon=1/m<=1/128.\nHere 1<h<sqrt(2). At the next bridge d_1=1-epsilon,\n\n    d_1-(sqrt(2)-1)M_1\n      =-epsilon[1-2(sqrt(2)-1)^2/(1+epsilon)]<0.\n\nThus (21) is subcritical. Also 3/8<x<sqrt(2)-1 and M_1<5/2, so\neta>=-7/64 and 1+M_1 eta>=1-35/128>0. All actual multipliers in\n(21) are therefore nonnegative. This proves a full H_2 certificate with\nZ_2=M_2^2 and a positive condensed coefficient a1, directly from (20)--(23).\n\nThe next bridge is genuinely supercritical. With r0=sqrt(2)-1,\n\n    d_2-r0 M_2\n      =epsilon[(4-7r0)-2epsilon-lambda epsilon^2]\n                                                   /(1+epsilon)>0. (24)\n\nThe numerator bracket is positive since 4-7r0>1 and epsilon<=1/128.\nAlso x=d_2/M_2<1/2: the ratios d_j/M_j increase to c-2<1/2.\n\nFor ANY primitive child with mass M and any bridge ratio\n\n    sqrt(2)-1 < x=d/M <= (sqrt(5)-1)/2,\n\nchoose instead\n\n    r=1/x^2,\n    A0=1/x, B0=1/x^2-1-1/x>=0,\n    t=A0-B0=(x^2+2x-1)/x^2>0,\n    ell=H=0, p0=A0, q0=B0 d,\n    Z1=M^2/x^2.                                     (25)\n\nChoose u1>=0 solving u1^2-u1+(2M+d)=Z1, and put\nv1=2M+d-1+u1, whenever that root is real and v1>0. At the present\nH_2-to-H_3 join this holds: x<1/2 and M=M_2>4 give\nZ1>4M^2>2M+d. Thus u1=(1+sqrt(1+4(Z1-2M-d)))/2>1,\nv1>0, and k1=0.\n\nThe relation p0-q0=b+(1-A0)S is exact. All actual multipliers are\nnonnegative, and (18) becomes\n\n    E_new=E_left+r E_right\n                   +t||x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q||^2 >=0.       (26)\n\nBoth endpoint diagonal residuals in (18) vanish because Z=M^2,\nr=1/x^2, and Z1=rZ. This is a FULL valid initial-distance objective\ncertificate, with q_cert=1/v1 and multipliers 2lambda_ki/v1 on Q_ik.\nIts matrix is\n\n    [||x_n-u1 g_n||^2+E_new]/v1,\n\non the full original formal vectors. It repairs the first failed midpoint\nof the canonical array. The claim is for the finite H_3 subblock with the\nparameters of the assigned m, not an asymptotic bound for H_m.\n\nThe same explicit construction ALSO applies to the original finite m=3\ndiagnostic, without the small-epsilon estimates. In that case\n\n    d_0=(sqrt(2)-1)/2, d_1=2/3, d_2=8lambda/9,\n    M_1=2+(sqrt(2)-1)/2,\n    M_2=11/3+sqrt(2), M_3=(74+26sqrt(2))/9,\n    Z_3=(10707-1022sqrt(2))/64.\n\nFor the subcritical join, 8/27<d_1/M_1<sqrt(2)-1 and M_1<9/4.\nThus 1+M_1 eta>1-(9/4)(233/729)>0. At the next join\nd_2-(sqrt(2)-1)M_2=[7-16(sqrt(2)-1)]/9>0 and d_2/M_2<1/2.\nEvery sign condition in (20)--(26) therefore holds. An exact upper level is\n\n    q_3=1/[M_3-1+(1+sqrt(1+4(Z_3-M_3)))/2].          (26a)\n\nThis level exceeds the Huber value because Z_3<M_3^2. It is an exact\nfinite full certificate, not a reconstruction or validation of max3's\nfloating optimal dual, and not a benchmark improvement.\n\nFor completeness, (26) retains more information than an endpoint scalar.\nIf both children have sharp remainder a1||z-Mg||^2, its condensed\nremainder is\n\n    a2||z_parent+(M/x)g_q||^2,\n    1/a2=2/a1+(1+x)^2/(x^2+2x-1).                   (27)\n\nTo verify this, set U=z_left-Mg_p, V=z_right-Mg_q,\nT=x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q=d g_p+z_right. Then\nz_parent+(M/x)g_q=U-V/x+(1+1/x)T, while (26) is bounded below by\na1||U||^2+(a1/x^2)||V||^2+[(x^2+2x-1)/x^2]||T||^2.\nWeighted completion gives (27). The positive sign of the terminal term\nin (27) matters; replacing it by z_parent-M_new g_q loses the state.\n\nOne natural continuation of this repair has a precise limit. Keep H=ell=0\nin (18), and certify the new local term separately by its three\nnonnegative square coefficients. For arbitrary child storage Z, the\nconditions are\n\n    t>=0, Z>=r d^2, Z1<=rZ,\n    A0=(M+rd)/(M+d),\n    B0=[rM-2M-d]/(M+d)>=0.                           (28)\n\nConsequently r>=2+d/M and Z1<=Z^2/d^2. Choosing the largest\nadmissible r=Z/d^2 and Z1=rZ gives an actual recursive full certificate\nas long as B0,t>=0 and the endpoint root defining u1 remains admissible.\nIt preserves the full child matrices in (26); the following failure is\nnot caused by deleting them.\n\nAt the first supercritical input j=2, Z_2=M_2^2. Under the maximal rule\nput Y_j=Z_j/d_j^2. Because d_(j+1)=c d_j, its exact recurrence is\n\n    Y_(j+1)=Y_j^2/c^2,\n    Y_j=c^2 T^(2^(j-2)),\n    T=M_2^2/(c^2 d_2^2).                             (29)\n\nFor epsilon<=1/128 one has T<=1-epsilon/2. An explicit check is as\nfollows. The ratio M_2/(c d_2) has numerator\n\n    lambda^2+(8-2lambda)epsilon-epsilon^2\n\nand denominator lambda^2(1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^3. The denominator\ntimes (1-epsilon/2), minus the numerator, is\n\n    epsilon[(5lambda-13/2)+(1-lambda^2)epsilon\n                    -2lambda^2 epsilon^2+(lambda^2/2)epsilon^4]>0.\n\nIndeed 5lambda-13/2>5, lambda^2<6, and the negative terms have\nmagnitude less than 5/128+12/128^2. Squaring the resulting positive\nratio bound gives T<=(1-epsilon/2)^2<=1-epsilon/2.\n\nUsing c<5/2, equation (29) implies\n\n    Y_j <= (25/4) exp[-2^(j-2)/(2m)].                 (30)\n\nFor j=ceil(log_2 m)+4, the right side is at most (25/4)exp(-2)<2,\nbecause exp(2)>1+2+2^2/2=5>25/8. But (28) requires\nY_j>=r>=2+d_j/M_j>2. Hence this continuation must fail no later\nthan that level; for m>=128 the level is strictly below m.\n\nThe same upper recurrence bounds ANY continuation within the isolated\nPSD-local-square restriction (28) that starts at Z_2<=M_2^2: smaller\nZ or smaller r cannot improve the later bound. Such a continuation can\nfail earlier, including from an endpoint root or a different coefficient\nsign, but cannot survive to the required horizon. This is a limitation\nof (28), not of the general full recurrence (18). Allowing H or ell to\nbe nonzero and using the entire indefinite local/positive child matrix\ninteraction remains outside this obstruction.\n\nAn independent tiny rational coefficient audit in\n`research/direct_gluing_exact_audit.py` checked the one-step seed, a\nsubcritical three-step join, and a supercritical seven-step join. It matched\nEVERY function and FULL Gram coefficient exactly and checked every final\nmultiplier sign using Fraction arithmetic. The audit is an algebra check\non the displayed construction, not verifier acceptance or a substitute\nfor the written derivation.\n\n## 9. The sharp-storage zero mode also obstructs general mixed joins\n\nThere is a stronger, precise restriction on attempts to repair (29) merely\nby choosing nonzero H or ell. It does not require the local matrix to be\nPSD separately.\n\nSuppose the FULL child remainder E is PSD and, for every endpoint vector\ng, there is a choice of all its internal formal gradients with\n\n    z_block=kappa g, E=0, kappa^2=Z.                  (31)\n\nThe H_3 remainder constructed above has this property with\nkappa=-M_2/(d_2/M_2)=-M_2^2/d_2 and Z=Z_3. Attainment follows\ndirectly from the square decompositions in (22), (26), and (27): all\nzero-square conditions specify actual formal gradients, not only an\ninfimum. A formal block may be embedded twice independently while sharing\nonly the prescribed starting position; its gradient remainder is\ntranslation invariant.\n\nConsider ANY join of the full family (18), with arbitrary nonnegative\nactual multipliers and arbitrary H,ell, and suppose its WHOLE remainder\nis PSD. Fix a nonzero terminal vector e and select the right child at\nits zero mode z_right=kappa e. Select the left child with endpoint\n\n    g_p=-(kappa/d)e, z_left=kappa g_p, E_left=0.\n\nThen E_right=0 and V=d g_p+z_right=0. Every cross-block term in\n(18) therefore vanishes, leaving exactly\n\n    E_new=[Z^2/d^2-Z1]||e||^2.                       (32)\n\nConsequently full PSD NECESSARILY requires Z1<=Z^2/d^2. This argument\nuses actual full formal directions of both children; it is not a local\ndiagonal test.\n\nIf equality is imposed in (32), the same nonzero vector has zero parent\nremainder and\n\n    z_parent=z_left+V=-(Z/d)e.\n\nThus property (31) propagates with kappa1=-Z/d and kappa1^2=Z1.\nInductively, a continuation that saturates this storage bound at every\njoin obeys (29) even with arbitrary H and ell and with every child matrix\nretained. It cannot escape the storage collapse by changing those mixed\ncoefficients alone.\n\nMore explicitly, starting at Z_2=M_2^2 and saturating from level two,\n\n    Z_m=d_m^2 c^2 T^(2^(m-2)),\n    log Z_m <= 2log a+(2m+2)log c-2^(m-2)/(2m) -> -infinity.\n\nHere d_m=a c^m is just the next level's scalar, and the bound on T was\nproved in Section 8. For a positive endpoint objective weight v_m one\nalways has v_m<=Z_m, since Z_m=(u_m-1)^2+v_m. Hence the resulting\nobjective level q_m=1/v_m is at least 1/Z_m and cannot establish the\nrequired decreasing error, much less the exact benchmark separation.\n\nThis is a conditional obstruction to repeated SHARP storage selection.\nA strict sacrifice of storage together with changed relative multipliers\nmay remove the zero mode (31), and a different full certificate family\nneed not have it. Those possibilities remain open. The next meaningful\ntest must therefore preserve the full two-variable remainder and allow\ngenuinely nonsaturating intermediate choices; it cannot simply perturb\nthe mixed coefficients while keeping the same sharp endpoint recursion.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["c7f10ed5dc99275b", "a1661dacd3b561f7"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "workers/max/research/max_direct_full_matrix_gluing.md", "scope": "General join family (18) under the explicit attained child zero mode; recursive collapse additionally requires saturation at every level.", "status": "candidate_only_no_verifier_allocation", "next": "Do not force saturation; retain the full two-variable remainder and allow altered relative interpolation weights and an indefinite local contribution.", "new_solver_calls": 0, "new_verifier_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"z_block": "sum h_i g_i+g_end on a block; a gradient moment retaining its actual steps.", "kappa": "Signed boundary zero-mode parameter: z_block=kappa g_end and E=0, with kappa²=Z.", "Z_next": "Next block's combined endpoint storage, denoted Z1 in the full join identity."}}
