No routine fact submission is\nallocated. The only correctness inputs used are facts 1252b8823bb5a290\n(necessary interpolation inequalities and smoothness) and b98bb807d514e80d\n(the exact benchmark recursions). Every new estimate is given below. Earlier\nunverified spectral and covariance findings are not premises.\n\n## 1. Actual schedule, full added space, and claim\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2). For an integer m>=128 put\nc=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, d_j=1+a c^j. Within this horizon these\nparameters are fixed. Set H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1,\nq=n-1, and let h be H_m with ONLY its original last step d_0 removed.\nWrite S=sum h_t. For 0<=i<=q put P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z).\nFix zeta_1=(1+i)/2, zeta_2=(4+i)/2, and z_l=zeta_l/S.\n\nFor a pair of complex numbers define the real column\n\n J(v_1,v_2)=(Re(v_1-v_2), Im v_1, Im v_2)^T.\n\nLet w be arbitrary in [0,1] and T be ANY real matrix with three columns,\nwith no upper bound on its entries or number of rows. Define Euclidean data\n\n x_i=(sqrt(w)Re P_i(z_1),sqrt(w)Im P_i(z_1),\n      sqrt(1-w)Re P_i(z_2),sqrt(1-w)Im P_i(z_2),\n      T J(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2))),\n g_i=(sqrt(w)Re[z_1 P_i(z_1)],sqrt(w)Im[z_1 P_i(z_1)],\n      sqrt(1-w)Re[z_2 P_i(z_2)],sqrt(1-w)Im[z_2 P_i(z_2)],\n      T J(z_1 P_i(z_1),z_2 P_i(z_2))).                    (1)\n\nThen ||x_0||=1 and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. Every real linear functional\non (v_1,v_2) that vanishes at (1,1) is a linear combination of the three\ncomponents of J: its two real coefficients must sum to zero, and its two\nimaginary coefficients are unrestricted. Thus (1) includes EVERY PSD\naddition annihilating the initial vector, added orthogonally to the two\nnormal blocks. Write M=T^T T; it is an arbitrary PSD 3 by 3 matrix.\n\nSuppose arbitrary JOINT queried values are interpolated by a convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth function F, with F(0)=0=min F and grad F(x_i)=g_i.\nNo separate modal values or modal legality are assumed. Then, for every\nsufficiently large m, uniformly over w,T,F and b in [0,2],\n\n                 2F(x_q-b g_q) <= 2/(3S).               (2)\n\nFor B_n=1/U_n^ZJ=2S_bullet(n)+1 this implies\n\n limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(w,T,F,b) B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n                       <= 8/(3e) < 64/65 < 1.           (3)\n\nThe actual final step b=d_0 lies in [0,2]. The statement concerns exactly\nthe displayed spectral family. It does not bound unrestricted W_n(H_m),\narbitrary spectra, or every possible metric on the two-mode cyclic space.\n\n## 2. Two exact shared interpolation inequalities\n\nUse Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0 from the accepted\nfact 1252b8823bb5a290. First sum Q_(0,star) and Q_(t+1,t) for every\n0<=t<q. The values telescope, and GD gives\n\n f_q <= <g_0,x_0>-sum_(t<q) h_t<g_(t+1),g_t>\n                -||g_0||^2/2-sum_(t<q)||g_(t+1)-g_t||^2/2.\n\nDropping only the explicitly nonnegative squared terms yields\n\n f_q <= <g_0,x_0>-sum_(t<q)h_t<g_(t+1),g_t>.             (4)\n\nSecond, for any actual queried r, sum Q_(q,r) and Q_(r,star), and drop\ntheir two squared terms. This gives\n\n f_q <= <g_q,x_q>+<g_r-g_q,x_r>.                         (5)\n\nChoose r=2^(m-1), the query immediately AFTER the largest bridge. Both\ninequalities concern the same joint transcript and the same function.\nTheir convex combination uses the fixed coefficients\n\n                theta=6/25 on (4), 1-theta=19/25 on (5). (6)\n\nFor scaled gradients U_i=J(zeta_1 P_i(z_1),zeta_2 P_i(z_2)), and\nY_i=J(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2)), let sym(u,v)=(uv^T+vu^T)/2. Define\n\n E_m=sum_(t<q) (h_t/S) sym(U_(t+1),U_t),\n Kplus_m=sym(U_q,Y_q)+sym(U_r-U_q,Y_r),\n K_m=(19/25)Kplus_m-(6/25)E_m.                          (7)\n\nFor l=1,2 let e_t=h_t/S and p_i,l=P_i(z_l), and define\n\n C_l,m=Re zeta_l-|zeta_l|^2 sum_(t<q)\n                   e_t |p_t,l|^2(1-e_t Re zeta_l),\n Dplus_l,m=Re[zeta_l p_q,l conj(p_q,l)\n                     +zeta_l(p_r,l-p_q,l)conj(p_r,l)],\n D_l,m=(6/25)C_l,m+(19/25)Dplus_l,m.                    (8)\n\nThe initial added coordinates are zero. Substituting (1) into (4)--(6)\ntherefore gives the EXACT finite-word bound\n\n             S f_q <= w D_1,m+(1-w)D_2,m+trace(M K_m).  (9)\n\nThe proof will show K_m is negative definite and D_l,m<1/3 for all\nsufficiently large m. Crucially these properties will hold at each such\nFINITE m before allowing M to vary or grow without bound.\n\n## 3. Limit word and continuity of its chronological work\n\nDirectly summing the multiplicity of every step gives\n\n S=2^m-2-a+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),      S/lambda^m -> e,\n d_(m-1-j)/S -> delta lambda^(-j),\n delta=1-2/lambda=3-2sqrt(2).                            (10)\n\nHere a->1/lambda, (1+1/m)^m->e, and lambda(lambda-2)=1. The largest\nnormalized step has limit delta. The normalized limiting word consists\nof a central step delta, two copies with central step delta/lambda,\nfour copies with central step delta/lambda^2, and so on, with their\nrecursive chronology retained. The total mass at level j is\ndelta(2/lambda)^j; the total mass is one.\n\nIts truncation after N largest levels is the finite word obtained from\nH <- (H,d,H), starting empty and using, in ascending order,\n\n d=delta/lambda^(N-1), ..., delta/lambda, delta.\n\nThe omitted mass is exactly epsilon_N=(2/lambda)^N. This definition\ndoes not introduce any new physical terminal step.\n\nFor the four modal numbers\n\n zeta_1, conjugate(zeta_1), zeta_2, conjugate(zeta_2),\n\nevery normalized factor satisfies |1-d zeta|<=1 whenever 0<=d<=1/5:\nexpanding the square reduces this to d<=2Re(zeta)/|zeta|^2, whose\nminimum here is 16/17. All limiting steps and, eventually, all actual\nnormalized steps satisfy the stronger bound d<1/5.\n\nDeleting steps of total mass tau from such a chronological word changes\nany modal prefix product by at most |zeta|tau. To see this, telescope\nthe deletion one factor at a time; all other factors have modulus<=1,\nand the deleted factor differs from one by d zeta.\n\nFor any two of the four modal numbers A,B define the scalar work moment\n\n W_word(A,B)=sum_t d_t P_t(A)P_t(B)[1-d_t(A+B)/2].        (11)\n\nThe bracket is the average of 1-d_t A and 1-d_t B, so its modulus is\nat most one. Deleted terms have total modulus<=tau. At retained terms,\nthe change in the two-product is at most (|A|+|B|)tau. Since the total\nstep mass is at most one,\n\n |W_word-W_deleted| <= (1+|A|+|B|)tau.                  (12)\n\nAfter multiplying by AB this is <23tau, because |A|,|B|<=sqrt(17)/2\n<21/10 and |AB|<=17/4. Expressing the three real coordinates J in these\nfour complex modes uses rows with absolute coefficient sums 2,1,1.\nConsequently deleting mass tau changes any entry of the real work\nmatrix by less than 92tau. We use the simpler uniform bound 100tau.\nThe normal work terms have error less than 23tau.\n\nThese inequalities prove existence of the infinite-word moments. They\nalso prove convergence from the ACTUAL words in (10). Indeed, retain\nonly their N largest levels. The resulting finite word converges to\nthe displayed N-level truncation, including its chronological order.\nFor fixed N the deleted normalized mass converges to epsilon_N:\nthe normalized total mass is exactly one and the retained masses\nconverge individually. Apply (12), first let m tend to infinity, and\nthen let N tend to infinity. The removed original d_0 is among the\ndeleted levels when m>N, so this argument also handles it explicitly.\nWe obtain E_m->E and convergence of the two normal work coefficients.\n\nThe left-half product and bridge/endpoint products are\n\n L(zeta)=product_(j>=1)(1-delta lambda^(-j)zeta)^(2^(j-1)),\n p_l=L(zeta_l),   v_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l,\n t_l=p_l v_l.                                          (13)\n\nFor the actual words, the corresponding products are exactly\nv_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m and\nt_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m^2/(1-d_0 z_l).\nThe last denominator tends to one. Deletion of a small total mass and\nthe same product bound prove convergence to (13). Thus K_m and D_l,m\nconverge entrywise to the quantities K,D_l given by (7)--(8) and the\nlimiting work moments.\n\n## 4. Finite rational enclosures of the fixed limiting coefficients\n\nEverything in this section is bounded arithmetic for one fixed\ncoefficient matrix; no PEP or optimization evaluation is used.\n\nFor the product (13) define mu_s=delta^s/(lambda^s-2). Absolutely\nconvergent logarithmic expansion gives\n\n log L(zeta)=-sum_(s>=1)mu_s zeta^s/s.\n\nLet A_10 be the sum through s=10. Since mu_2<1/125,\n|zeta|^2<=17/4, and eta=|zeta|delta/lambda<3/20, the omitted tail is\n\n <=mu_2 |zeta|^2 eta^9/[11(1-eta)] < 1/10^9.           (14)\n\nThe bound on mu_s used here is mu_s<=mu_2(delta/lambda)^(s-2), obtained\ndirectly by summing the positive powers of the left-half step sizes.\nCompute exp(A_10) through degree18. The bounds Re A_10<0 and |A_10|<1\ngive exponential tail <3/19!. Perturbing its argument by (14) changes\nthe exponential by <(10^-9)/(1-10^-9). Hence error 2/10^9 in each real\ncomponent safely covers both tails.\n\nFor the moment (11), its exact concatenation recurrence is short.\nIf a word has products p_A,p_B and moment W, appending its central\nstep d and another copy gives\n\n p_A,new=p_A^2(1-dA),  p_B,new=p_B^2(1-dB),\n W_new=W[1+p_A p_B(1-dA)(1-dB)]\n                     +d p_A p_B[1-d(A+B)/2].           (15)\n\nThe first copy contributes W, the central step gives the last term,\nand the second copy is multiplied by p_A p_B(1-dA)(1-dB), proving\nthe identity. Use N=112 levels in (15). The rational bound\n\n                 100(2/lambda)^112 < 1/10^7             (16)\n\nand (12) supply the error for every resulting real work coefficient.\n\nFor a completely specified finite arithmetic procedure, use the interval\n\n r_- = 1.41421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694,\n r_+ = r_-+10^-50\n\nfor sqrt(2); its squared endpoints straddle 2. Set lambda=1+[r_-,r_+]\nand delta=1-2/lambda. Interval additions, products, and positive\nreciprocals enclose their exact values. After every intermediate\noperation round its lower endpoint down and upper endpoint up to a\nmultiple of 10^-60. This only enlarges an enclosure and prevents\ndenominator growth. Evaluate A_10, then its degree18 exponential using\nt_0=1, t_j=t_(j-1)A_10/j. Add the componentwise error in (14). For\nthe work moments, use (15) on all sixteen pairs of the four modes.\n\nThe coefficients expressing J in these four modes, in that order, are\n\n c_1=(1/2,1/2,-1/2,-1/2),\n c_2=(-i/2,i/2,0,0), c_3=(0,0,-i/2,i/2).\n\nThe real work matrix entries are\nE_kl=Re sum_(r,s)c_kr c_ls zeta_r zeta_s W(zeta_r,zeta_s).\nThe two normal chain coefficients are\nC_1=1/2-(1/2)W(zeta_1,conjugate(zeta_1)) and\nC_2=2-(17/4)W(zeta_2,conjugate(zeta_2)).\nAdd error interval [-10^-7,10^-7] to these expressions. This includes\n(16); all preceding rounding errors are already in the intervals.\n\nThe following outward rational enclosures result. Finite decimals are\nexact rational numbers; the margins are deliberately larger than the\nrounding errors.\n\n| quantity | enclosing interval |\n|---|---|\n| Re p_1 | [.7952608488,.7952608529] |\n| Im p_1 | [-.1687257189,-.1687257148] |\n| Re p_2 | [.4200940450,.4200940491] |\n| Im p_2 | [-.0920602833,-.0920602792] |\n| E_11 | [.3738957,.3738960] |\n| E_12 | [-.1629610,-.1629607] |\n| E_13 | [-.0946327,-.0946324] |\n| E_22 | [.0952537,.0952540] |\n| E_23 | [.0317060,.0317063] |\n| E_33 | [.0282053,.0282056] |\n| C_1 | [.1841427,.1841430] |\n| C_2 | [.9918514,.9918517] |\n\nSubstituting the directly computed intervals in (7)--(8), at theta=6/25,\ngives\n\n 19/100 < D_1 < 193/1000,       329/1000 < D_2 < 33/100,\n -132/1000 < K_11 < -131/1000,\n 21/10000 < K_11 K_22-K_12^2 < 22/10000,\n -18/10^8 < det K < -15/10^8.                           (17)\n\nThese are finite rational inequalities obtained by the displayed\nrecurrences, without eigenvalue computation. The three successive\ndiagonal pivots in completing the square are\nK_11, (K_11 K_22-K_12^2)/K_11, det K/(K_11 K_22-K_12^2).\nThey are all negative by (17). Thus K is negative definite, and both\nnormal coefficients are strictly below 1/3.\n\nThe companion chain_anchor_interval_check.py implements these exact\ninterval operations and asserts (17). Its first version was stopped\nbecause an unrounded exponential expression caused denominator growth.\nThe corrected version rounds the intermediate log/exponential terms\nand the fixed-size moment recurrence; it exits zero. An earlier\nordinary floating-point exploration was not used as proof: repeated\nsquaring of factors extremely close to one loses significant small-step\nmass in floating arithmetic. The rational calculation and its explicit\ntail estimates are the evidence for (17).\n\n## 5. Finite-word uniformity, free terminal step, and exact benchmark\n\nEntrywise convergence and the strict inequalities (17) imply that for\nall sufficiently large m, the EXACT K_m is negative definite and\nD_l,m<1/3. None of those coefficients depends on w or T. Since\ntrace(T^T T K_m) is a sum of nonpositive row quadratic forms, (9) gives\nF(x_q)<=1/(3S), uniformly even for coupling matrices diverging with m.\n\nIntegration of the 1-Lipschitz gradient along the final segment gives\n\n F(x_q-b g_q)<=F(x_q)-b(1-b/2)||g_q||^2<=F(x_q),\n\nfor all b in [0,2]. The terminal gradient and value are free; this\nproves (2) for the same interpolating F.\n\nHere is the comparison with the exact project benchmark, proved rather\nthan imported from an unverified finding. Let rho=log_2 lambda,\nC_k=S_circle(k)+1, and B_k=2S_bullet(k)+1. The accepted recursions give\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nFor V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, differentiation gives, with t=exp u,\n\n d^2/du^2 log V(exp u)\n =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2 > 0.\n\nThe bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative for t>0.\nConvexity at u and -u implies V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=2. If A,B>0 and\nM=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho, this means (M-A)(M-B)>=2AB;\nthe positive root of this quadratic is Omega(A,B). Thus\nOmega(A,B)<=M and induction gives C_k<=(k+1)^rho.\nThe inequality sqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A yields G(A,B)<=B+4A.\nUsing u^rho+v^rho<=(u+v)^rho for nonnegative u,v proves by induction\nB_n<=1+4n^rho. The latter inequality follows by differentiating\n(1+t)^rho-t^rho, whose minimum for t>=0 is one.\n\nAt n=2^m-1, n^rho/lambda^m->1 and S/lambda^m->e by (10).\nMultiplying (2) by B_n proves (3). Finally e>sum_(j=0)^4 1/j!=65/24,\nso 8/(3e)<64/65.\n\n## 6. Why the chronology is necessary in this certificate\n\nDefine chi(v_1,v_2)=Re(v_1-v_2)/4+Im v_1/5+Im v_2.\nIt vanishes at (1,1). Apply it to the left, right, and endpoint modal\npairs in (13), obtaining y_-,y_+,y_q and, after multiplication by\nthe modal zeta values, u_-,u_+,u_q. The coefficients of its squared\namplitude in the two individual bridge-anchor bounds are\n\n k_-=u_q y_q+(u_--u_q)y_-,\n k_+=u_q y_q+(u_+-u_q)y_+.\n\nThe same rational product enclosures give\n\n        23/100000 < k_- < 24/100000,\n       103/100000 < k_+ < 104/100000.                    (18)\n\nEvery convex combination of just these two anchor matrices therefore\nhas a strictly positive direction. This refutes a purely algebraic\nextension of the two-anchor method to the full three-dimensional\nspace. It is NOT a legal high-payoff construction. Adding the actual\nchronological chain supplies a different constraint and yields (17).\n\n## 7. Scope that remains\n\nThe proof covers arbitrary PSD zero-initial additions to the displayed\ntwo normal blocks, arbitrary joint values, and the free terminal step.\nIt does not cover arbitrary inner products that couple an added\ncoordinate to the initial direction, nor other scaled spectra. A\ngeneral PSD metric on the whole four-dimensional cyclic space has\nadditional initial-direction cross terms. Extending a coefficient\ncertificate to that space requires controlling those terms as well;\nnegative definiteness on J alone is insufficient. The certificate family, including all its available parameters\n\nUse smoothness one and optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For every ordered\npair of distinct samples put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    C_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nThe accepted fact implies C_ij>=0 on every legal trajectory. All identities\nbelow, however, are identities in independent formal vectors after the GD\nsubstitution; no interpolation constraints are imposed on test vectors.\n\nFix a>0,c>0 and define\n\n    d_j=a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThe initial block in the certificate recursion is H_1=(1+a), not H_0.\nFor each block choose real u_j,v_j, with final v_m>0, and a multiplier array\nlambda^(j) on its samples and star. Require the following star pattern:\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=h_i             for every queried i<n_j,\n    lambda_(star,n_j)=u_j,\n    lambda_(i,star)=0               for i<n_j,\n    lambda_(n_j,star)=s_j,\n    s_j=S_j+u_j-v_j.                                      (1)\n\nHere s_j is only an incoming optimum multiplier, not the stepsize sum S_j.\nAll final actual multipliers must be nonnegative if the array is to certify\nan objective bound. We will prove a necessary condition even without using\nthose signs. Assume exact cancellation of every function-value coefficient\nin the identity defining the gradient-only remainder E_j:\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-u_j g_end||^2-2v_j f_end\n            =sum lambda^(j)_ik C_ik+E_j.                  (2)\n\nPut\n\n    Z_j=(u_j-1)^2+v_j,\n    z_j=sum_(i<n_j) h_i g_i+g_end.                         (3)\n\nThe seed may be ANY one-step array satisfying (1)--(2). Its remainder need\nnot be PSD. In particular this includes the primitive one-step seed in the\nshared construction, with u_1=M_1,v_1=2M_1-1 and\nE_1=(2-(1+a)^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nHere is the permitted join. Write n=n_j,p=n,q=2n+1,S=S_j,\nd=d_j,b=1+d,M=M_j. Choose parameters A,B,P,Q with\n\n    r=1+A+B,\n    P-Q=b+(1-A)S.                                       (4)\n\nEmbed one copy of the old array on the left block and r times the same\narray on the right block. For every right queried index i=p+1,...,q-1 add\n\n    A h_i to (p,i), B h_i to (q,i),\n    -(A+B)h_i to (star,i).                               (5)\n\nAdd the following six sparse corrections:\n\n    (p,q): P,                    (q,p): Q,\n    (p,star): -s_j,              (q,star): s_(j+1)-r s_j,\n    (star,p): b-u_j,             (star,q): u_(j+1)-r u_j.  (6)\n\nNo restriction to H=ell=0, greedy choices of Z, PSD local terms, rank-one\nchild remainders, or PSD child remainders is imposed. The final objective\nmatrix must still be PSD on the full formal space. Nonnegative actual\ncoefficients, when they hold, include all the star coefficients in (1);\nrequiring A,B,P,Q>=0 is a sufficient way to preserve the other signs.\n\nThe desired result is a necessary condition for ANY such recursion. It is\ntherefore valid for every choice of its parameters that passes all actual\nsign tests, including choices depending on the target horizon.\n\n## 2. The complete local identity\n\nDefine\n\n    t=A-B,\n    H=M+d-B(M-d)-t M,\n    ell=P-A,\n    V=x_p-x_q-g_p+g_q.\n\nThe exact recursion for the entire gradient remainder is\n\n    E_(j+1)=E_j(left)+r E_j(right)\n       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g_p+ell g_q>\n       +(Z_j-rd^2)||g_p||^2+(rZ_j-Z_(j+1))||g_q||^2.    (7)\n\nWe verify both the value and quadratic coefficients. Write x=x_p,g=g_p,\ny=x_q,e=g_q, and w=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i=x-bg-y. Summing (5) gives\n\n    2AS f_p+2BS f_q-AS||g||^2-BS||e||^2\n                      -2<w,A(x-g)+B(y-e)>.             (8)\n\nThis follows by expanding a summand: the coefficient of each f_i and of\neach ||g_i||^2 cancels, leaving the displayed linear and bilinear terms.\nThe value coefficient at p in the two children plus (5)--(6) is\n\n    -2v_j+2AS+2(P-Q)-2s_j-2(b-u_j)\n       =2[(A-1)S+P-Q-b]=0.\n\nThe coefficient at q is\n\n    -2r v_j+2BS-2(P-Q)+2(s_(j+1)-r s_j)\n                         -2(u_(j+1)-r u_j)=-2v_(j+1).\n\nThis uses (1),(4) and r=1+A+B. All other values have already canceled.\n\nFor completeness, the quadratic parts of the six expressions used in (6)\nare respectively\n\n    C_pq: 2<e,y-x>-||g-e||^2,\n    C_qp: 2<g,x-y>-||g-e||^2,\n    C_pstar: -||g||^2,       C_qstar: -||e||^2,\n    C_starp: 2<g,x>-||g||^2,\n    C_starq: 2<e,y>-||e||^2.\n\nSubtract their weighted sum and (8) from the new potential minus the two\nchild potentials. With D=x-y, all dependence on the separate x,y cancels.\nThe resulting coefficients, using the convention that cross coefficients\nmultiply twice the stated inner product, are\n\n    ||D||^2: t,\n    <D,g>: H-ell-t,               <D,e>: ell+t,\n    ||g||^2: Z_j-rd^2+t-2H+2ell,\n    ||e||^2: rZ_j-Z_(j+1)+t+2ell,\n    <g,e>: H-2ell-t.\n\nSubstituting V=D-g+e gives (7). Thus (7) concerns the full original\ngradient space, not only a condensed boundary form.\n\n## 3. A formal template on which all mixed terms vanish\n\nDefine nonzero signed scalars\n\n    k_1=2+a=M_1,\n    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j,       j>=1.                       (9)\n\nFor each block H_j and each vector e, there is an explicit formal gradient\nassignment, linear in e, with terminal gradient e such that\n\n    z_j=k_j e,\n    E_j=(k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2.                              (10)\n\nCrucially this is true for EVERY parameter choice in (4)--(6). The same\ntemplate (9) works after a change of the storage variables, multiplier\nweights, or mixed boundary terms.\n\nFirst check the seed carefully. From (1)--(2), subtraction of the star\nterms gives the intrinsic identity\n\n    E_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_end)\n          +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n          +(M_j-Z_j)||g_end||^2\n          -sum_(i!=k finite)lambda^(j)_ik C_ik.          (11)\n\nTo obtain (11), use\n||x_start||^2-||x_end||^2\n =2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2\nand s_j=S_j+u_j-v_j. This also verifies that no hypothesis s_j=0 is needed.\n\nAssign both seed gradients equal to e, and set the formal values\nf_i=<e,x_i>-||e||^2/2. Every finite C_ik is then zero. More generally,\nfor a constant-gradient block with prefix sums P_i,\nf_i-f_end=(S_j-P_i)||e||^2 and\n\n    2sum h_i(S_j-P_i)+sum h_i(1-h_i)=S_j(S_j+1).\n\nThe latter equality follows from\nsum_i h_i sum_(t>=i)h_t=(S_j^2+sum_i h_i^2)/2.\nEquation (11) therefore evaluates to (M_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2.\nFor H_1, z_1=(2+a)e=k_1 e, proving (10). The auxiliary values merely\nevaluate a polynomial identity; they are not claimed to be feasible jets.\n\nSuppose (10) holds at level j. In the right child use its template with\nterminal vector e. In the left child use its template with terminal vector\n\n    g=-k_j e/d_j.\n\nThe block displacement gives exactly\n\n    V=d_j g+z_right=0,\n    z_parent=z_left+d_j g+z_right=z_left=k_j g=k_(j+1)e.  (12)\n\nEvaluating the ENTIRE right side of (7), rather than discarding any child\npart, now gives\n\n    E_(j+1)\n      =(k_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2+r(k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2\n           +(Z_j-rd_j^2)||g||^2+(rZ_j-Z_(j+1))||e||^2\n      =[(k_j^2-rd_j^2)k_j^2/d_j^2+r k_j^2-Z_(j+1)]||e||^2\n      =(k_j^4/d_j^2-Z_(j+1))||e||^2.\n\nThis is (10) at the parent. Neither E_j>=0 nor Z_j=k_j^2 was used.\nIn particular reducing Z at an earlier join does not remove the test:\nits resulting positive template energy is carried exactly into the next\ncalculation and cancels with that join's storage terms.\n\n## 4. Even changing the final initial-distance coefficient cannot repair it\n\nWrite u=u_m,v=v_m>0,k=k_m,Z=(u-1)^2+v. The full objective certificate\ngenerated by the final array, at any proposed level q_cert, is\n\n    M_cert=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n\n                         +sum_(i!=j) (2lambda^(m)_ji/v) Q_ij\n       =(q_cert-1/v)||x_0||^2\n                         +[||x_n-u g_n||^2+E_m]/v.       (13)\n\nThe whole formula follows from (2) and C_ij=-2Q_ji. Its actual\ninterpolation multipliers are nonnegative when the lambda array is.\n\nApply (10), keep the initial formal vector x_0 independent, and write e\nfor the terminal template vector. Since z_m=k e,\n\n    x_n-u g_n=x_0-(k+u-1)e.\n\nSet beta=k+u-1. The restriction of v M_cert to the two independent\nscalar coordinates in x_0,e has matrix\n\n                    [ q_cert v      -beta       ]\n                    [   -beta       2k beta-v   ].       (14)\n\nIndeed its second diagonal is\nbeta^2+k^2-Z=2k(k+u-1)-v. Full PSD requires (14) PSD.\nIts lower diagonal cannot be zero: PSD would then force beta=0, making\nthat diagonal -v<0. Consequently 2k beta-v>0, and its determinant gives\n\n    q_cert >= beta^2/[v(2k beta-v)] >= 1/k^2.             (15)\n\nFor the last inequality use the exact identity\n\n    k^2 beta^2-v(2k beta-v)=(v-k beta)^2>=0.\n\nBoth denominators are positive. Thus (15) holds whether E_m is PSD or\nindefinite, whether q_cert equals 1/v or is increased, and whether any\nchild storage was selected greedily. All nonlinear mixing parameters in\n(7) have vanished from the necessary bound.\n\nThis is a full-space certificate obstruction. Passing the test on legal\nsampled trajectories alone would not suffice, since the formal gradient\nassignment used in (10) is generally not a legal transcript.\n\n## 5. Quantitative growing-horizon consequence for the assigned words\n\nFix an integer m>=128, set epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2), and use the\nactual assignment\n\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    d_j=(1-epsilon)c^(j-1), 1<=j<=m.\n\nThe symbol d_m is used only to express the recurrence solution; H_m uses\nthe bridges d_0,...,d_(m-1). Put\n\n    R=(2+a)/[c(1-epsilon)].\n\nSolving (9) in absolute value gives\n\n    |k_j|=(1-epsilon)c^j R^(2^(j-1)),   1<=j<=m.          (16)\n\nTo check (16), divide |k_j| by c d_j. This ratio is R at j=1 and\nis squared at every next step, since d_(j+1)=c d_j.\n\nThere is a strict gap below one in R, with an explicit uniform constant:\n\n    1-R = epsilon[2-lambda^2 epsilon(1+epsilon)]\n                  /[lambda^2(1+epsilon)^2(1-epsilon)]\n          > epsilon/4.                                  (17)\n\nFor the equality multiply by c^2(1-epsilon). Its numerator is\nlambda^2(1+epsilon)^2(1-epsilon)\n -[lambda^2+(2lambda-1)epsilon]\n =2epsilon-lambda^2 epsilon^2(1+epsilon),\nusing lambda^2=2lambda+1. For the inequality, lambda^2<6 and\nepsilon<=1/128 imply\n\n    2-lambda^2 epsilon(1+epsilon)>7/4,\n    lambda^2(1+epsilon)^2(1-epsilon)<7.\n\nAlso R>0 from its definition. The bound 1-x<=exp(-x), proved for\n0<=x<1 by integrating the derivative of log(1-x), now yields\n\n    |k_m| < c^m exp[-2^(m-3)/m].                         (18)\n\nHere c<5/2: sqrt(2)<10/7 gives\nc<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<5/2. Combining (15),(18) gives\n\n    q_cert > (2/5)^(2m) exp[2^(m-2)/m] -> infinity.       (19)\n\nThe limit follows because 2^m/m^2 tends to infinity: its successive ratio\nis 2m^2/(m+1)^2>3/2 for all m>=7. In particular the exponential term in\nthe logarithm of (19) dominates the linear term 2m log(5/2).\n\nAlready for every m>=128, (18) gives |k_m|<1 and hence q_cert>1.\nFor an explicit elementary comparison, exp(1)>1+1+1/2+1/6>5/2, so\nlog(5/2)<1, while 2^(m-3)>m^2 for every m>=16 by checking m=16\nand noting that 2m^2/(m+1)^2>1 thereafter. Thus the exponent in (18),\nafter bounding c^m by exp(m), is negative.\n\nNo upper coefficient from this family can therefore approach the small\nobjective levels needed for a comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang\nprofile. This is stronger than failure of the previously chosen H=ell=0\nrule: all the mixed terms, all retained child matrices, all earlier\nstorage choices, and arbitrary q_cert were included in the derivation.\n\n## 6. Scope and the surviving repair directions\n\nThe obstruction covers exactly the repeated-copy array (4)--(6), started\nfrom any H_1 seed with the star pattern (1). The identity requires neither\nPSD child remainders nor local PSD checks, and the final test includes x_0\nand every original gradient coordinate. It also permits nonzero incoming\nterminal optimum weights s_j and arbitrary u_j,v_j consistent with (1).\n\nIt does NOT exclude another complete interpolation array for H_m. In\nparticular C51's finite H_3 certificate uses a different unrestricted\narray, and is consistent with this result. The obstruction also does not\nautomatically cover independent internal coefficient changes at a parent,\nadditional interpolation edges or corrections outside (5)--(6), a\ndifferent star pattern, or a different seed size allowed to grow with m.\n\nThe concrete repair requirement is now an algebraic one: a useful changed\narray must act nontrivially on the template (10)--(12). Altering only the\nlocal V-dependent terms cannot do that. Nor can changing Z alone, since\nthe precise earlier template energy is propagated in (10).\n\nFor literature context, the gluing-plus-rank-one-plus-sparse architecture\nis present in Altschuler--Parrilo, arXiv:2309.16530v1, Theorem 3.4 and its\nproof in Sections 3.1--3.2. The exact paper was downloaded and read. No\nstatement from that paper is imported into this proof, and its certificate\nfor the undeformed silver schedule is not claimed to apply after the\npresent parameter deformation.\n\nAll new claims here remain candidates until verifier acceptance. The\noriginal unrestricted target remains unresolved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "C53_full_boundary_repair", "finding_ids": ["694fb2dacb4f7389", "c7f10ed5dc99275b", "09f0b530f2583c54"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "local_artifact": "research/c53_full_mixed_join_obstruction.md", "verification_status": "not submitted; no allocation", "validation": "All full Gram coefficients, all function flows, actual signs, and template equality checked at two fixed rational joins by a trivial Fraction audit; proof does not rely on audit."}, "glossary": {"k_j": "C53 signed formal-template moment; k_1=2+a and k_(j+1)=-k_j²/d_j.", "kappa_m": "C53 |k_m|.", "z_j": "C53 weighted queried-gradient sum plus terminal gradient.", "u_j": "C53 terminal vector coefficient of the displayed block potential.", "v_j": "C53 positive final objective weight in the displayed block potential.", "Z_j": "C53 endpoint storage (u_j-1)²+v_j.", "s_j": "C53 incoming optimum coefficient S_j+u_j-v_j; distinct from stepsize sum S_j.", "C_ij": "C53 nonnegative interpolation form -2Q_ji.", "R": "C53 scalar (2+a)/(c(1-epsilon))."}}
{"id": "e3c8501149a5c47e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:07:57.240625+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Priority scalar comparator SOLVED as a self-contained candidate: G(x,lambda*y)<=lambda F(x,y) for all x,y>=0, with lambda=1+sqrt2 SHARP for this pointwise inequality. Exact DP induction gives B_n<=lambda P_n; P_n<=(n+1)^rho and P_(2^m-1)=lambda^m, rho=log_2lambda. On actual critical H_m, limsup B_(2^m-1) K_Hub,m<=lambda/(2e), so q_m/K_Hub,m<2e/lambda asymptotically suffices. In particular q_m<=1/M_m gives limsup q_m B<=lambda/e<1. No certificate existence or full target is claimed; no verifier allocated.", "evidence": "Definitions from accepted b98bb807d514e80d: P_n=S_circle(n)+1, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, P0=B0=1. Their exact recursions are P_n=max_(k+l=n-1)F(P_k,P_l), B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(P_k,B_l), with F(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nSCALAR COMPARISON. Set alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda)>0, L=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Its coefficients exceed1, so L>=x+y. Exact expansion gives alpha^2[L^2-(x+y)L-xy]=(2x/lambda-sqrt2 lambda y)^2>=0. Thus L>=F(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy=0. At z=lambda F(x,y), the polynomial defining G(x,lambda y) evaluates to z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2=x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha F(x,y)]>=0. Also z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it lies above the larger root G. Equality holds for x=0 and for x/y=lambda^2/sqrt2 with y>0. Homogeneity gives G(x,c y)/c=G(x/c,y), strictly decreasing in c for x>0, so any c<lambda fails at that equality point. No squaring-sign condition was omitted.\n\nDP INDUCTION. G is nondecreasing in its second argument. If B_l<=lambda P_l, then G(P_k,B_l)<=G(P_k,lambda P_l)<=lambda F(P_k,P_l). Taking maxima proves B_n<=lambda P_n from the base.\n\nDYADIC PRIMITIVE BOUND, NO PROFILE LIMIT. Let rho=log_2lambda>1. For t>0 put D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 and g(z)=log D(exp z). With t=exp z, differentiation gives g''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/D(t)^2>0. The bracket is positive by its derivative rho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1]>0 and its zero value at0. Consequently D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. For x,y>0 put A=x^(1/rho),B=y^(1/rho),T=(A+B)^rho. This inequality gives (T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, so T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0; T>=x+y by superadditivity of the power, and hence F(x,y)<=T. Zero arguments follow directly. Induction now gives P_n<=(n+1)^rho. Balanced joins give P_(2^m-1)>=lambda^m, hence equality. Therefore B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1) at every m.\n\nACTUAL NORMALIZATION. For c_m=lambda(1+1/m),a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j), M_j=1+sum H_j satisfies M_(j+1)=2M_j+a_m c_m^j. Thus M_m=A_m c_m^m+(1-A_m)2^m, A_m=a_m/(c_m-2)->1, and M_m/lambda^m->e. Define explicitly K_Hub,m=1/(2M_m-1), without presuming that this equals every earlier K notation. Then limsup B K_Hub<=lambda/(2e). If a genuine full certificate supplies q_m<=C K_Hub,m+o(K_Hub,m) for any C<2e/lambda, then limsup q_m B<=C lambda/(2e)<1. Equivalently q_m M_m has limsup<e/lambda as a sufficient condition. In particular q_m<=1/M_m would give limsup q_m B<=lambda/e<1; lambda<5/2<e. This is a sufficient scalar threshold only, not an upper certificate. The same proof yields thresholds 2exp(tau)/lambda and exp(tau)/lambda for the fixed deformation c_m=lambda(1+tau/m),a_m=(1-sigma/m)/c_m. All new statements remain unverified candidates; accepted b98 provides definitions, and all new inequalities are proved here rather than using unverified findings as premises.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "C54_scalar_benchmark", "guidance_ids": ["6f0f42aa11644848", "84f7ef64640db7b2"], "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "status": "complete proof candidate, no verifier allocation"}, "glossary": {"F_join": "The scalar operation (x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2, denoted F only in this scalar proof.", "G_join": "The scalar operation 2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2, denoted G only in this scalar proof.", "P_n": "Shifted primitive DP value S_circle(n)+1.", "rho": "log_2(1+sqrt2), only in the scalar primitive envelope.", "M_m": "1+sum of the actual critical word H_m.", "K_Hub_m": "The explicitly defined global Huber level 1/(2M_m-1), independent of other K notation."}}

{"id": "4bcbedc36b522e70", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:12:11.228450+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Stronger calibrated four-fan obstruction: allow SIGNED within-child/endpoint corrections and require only FINAL actual multipliers nonnegative. The full interface now recovers EVERY undeformed Silver word exactly. Yet if the left Huber certificate has nonproportional queried proof weights, common-gradient kernel forces u/gamma<=S/(3M+2d-1), while its full first-gradient diagonal forces u/gamma>=a/(1+a). Correction to2105aae5913d003a claim wording: the necessary lower inequality is >=, not >; the strict contradiction remains proved.", "evidence": "# Independent proof weights: an exact join and a full-matrix obstruction\n\nStatus: joint, self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No solver and no\nfact_submit. This is a failure of explicitly specified multiplier families,\nnot a lower witness for the schedules and not a resolution of the target.\n\nThe accepted mathematical input is the interpolation inequality from\nfact `1252b8823bb5a290`. The weighted-history approach was informed by\nGrimmer--Shu--Wang, *Composing Optimized Stepsize Schedules for Gradient\nDescent*, arXiv:2410.16249, Proposition 4 and its proof, and by the public\nfinite candidate C51 (047c257795dc59ba). Neither unverified C51 nor an\nexternal composability theorem is used as a proof premise. In particular,\nno quadratic-product equality or primitive hypothesis is imposed here.\n\n## 1. Full objective certificate with independent proof weights\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For distinct sampled indices use\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,\n    C_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.\n\nSet C_ii=0. All positions are the actual GD substitutions. The formal\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n remain independent.\n\nFor a block h of length n, write S=sum h_i, P=x_start-x_end=sum h_i g_i.\nChoose nonnegative proof weights w_0,...,w_n with D=sum w_i>0, and define\n\n    W=sum_i w_i g_i,\n    J_w=2sum_i w_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i w_i||g_i||^2\n          +2sum_i w_i<g_i,x_start-x_i>-||W||^2.\n\nThe exact identity, retaining the initial distance, is\n\n    ||x_start||^2-2D f_end+2sum_i w_i Q_(i,star)\n        =||x_start-W||^2+J_w.                         (1)\n\nSuppose a finite-edge array lambda_ij>=0 gives\n\n    J_w=sum_(finite i!=j) lambda_ij C_ij+R.            (2)\n\nIts necessary and sufficient value cancellation is\n\n    out_i-in_i=w_i-D 1_(i=end),                       (3)\n\nwhere out_i=sum_j lambda_ij and in_i=sum_j lambda_ji.\nFor every real q the resulting complete full objective identity is\n\n    q||x_start||^2-2f_end\n      +2/D sum_i w_i Q_(i,star)\n      +2/D sum_(finite i!=j) lambda_ij Q_ji\n       =(q-1/D)||x_start||^2\n          +[||x_start-W||^2+R]/D.                    (4)\n\nEvery multiplier in (4) is nonnegative. At q=1/D, its full PSD condition\nis exactly R>=0, since x_start can be set equal to W in the formal space.\nThis formulation places no equality between the proof weights and h.\n\nWhen D=2S+1, all finite gradients equal to one give J_w=0 after using\nf_i-f_end=S-P_i, where P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. These are legal Huber jets\nwith x_start=D and x_i=D-P_i. Every finite C_ij is zero. Consequently\nR(1,...,1)=0, and if R is PSD then R annihilates the all-ones vector.\n\n## 2. A first-gradient condition for EVERY such full certificate\n\nAssume n>=1 and h_0=1+a with a>0. Let e_0 mean the formal assignment\ng_0=1 and every other block gradient zero. All finite positions after\nthe first step have the same displacement h_0 from the start. Directly\nfrom (2)--(3),\n\n    R(e_0)=w_0-w_0^2+out_0-(2h_0-1)in_0\n          =2w_0-w_0^2-2a in_0.                      (5)\n\nThus the g_0 diagonal of the full unscaled matrix\n||x_start-W||^2+R at x_start=0 is\n\n    2w_0-2a in_0.                                   (6)\n\nThis calculation uses all finite edges, without a support assumption.\nIn particular any PSD certificate needs in_0<=w_0/a.\n\nAlso every PSD certificate in this form has w_0>0. Indeed, if w_0=0,\n(5) and PSD force in_0=out_0=0. Hence no finite edge touches index 0.\nEvery remaining finite position difference cancels the term h_0 g_0.\nIn J_w, however, the g_0 cross coefficient with g_i is 2h_0 w_i for\ni>0. At least one such w_i is positive because D>0. R would then have\na zero g_0 diagonal and a nonzero g_0 row, impossible for a PSD matrix:\nthe two-variable restriction 2bxy+cy^2 with b!=0 takes negative values.\n\n## 3. The complete four-fan join, with both moments retained\n\nTake two certificates (2) on the same physical block h, with proof\nweights w^L,w^R, each of mass D=2S+1; the two arrays and remainders\nmay differ. Let p=n be the left endpoint and q=2n+1 the parent endpoint.\nInsert the actual bridge b=1+d, d>=0, and denote\n\n    M=S+1, T=M+d, M'=2M+d, S'=M'-1, D'=2M'-1,\n    g=g_p, e=g_q,\n    P_L=sum_(left queried i) h_i g_i,\n    P_R=sum_(right queried i) h_i g_i,\n    W_L=sum_(left samples i) w^L_i g_i,\n    W_R=sum_(right samples i) w^R_i g_i.\n\nChoose nonnegative u,r,A,E and real B,C,k,l,p0,q0. The proposed parent weights\nare u w^L on the left, r w^R on the right, with k added at p and l at q.\nRequire every FINAL parent weight to be nonnegative and\n\n    (u+r)D+k+l=D'.                                  (7)\n\nSet gamma_L=u+C+E and gamma_R=r+A+B. Embed gamma_L times the entire\nleft finite array and gamma_R times the entire right finite array.\nAdd the following endpoint fans, omitting self edges:\n\n    C w^L_i on (p,i),    E w^L_i on (q,i),   i in left samples,\n    A w^R_i on (p,i),    B w^R_i on (q,i),   i in right samples.\n\nFinally add p0 on (p,q) and q0 on (q,p), where\n\n    p0-q0=(u-A+E)D+k.                               (8)\n\nRequire gamma_L,gamma_R>=0 and EVERY FINAL finite coefficient to be\nnonnegative. The corrections B,C,p0,q0 may themselves be negative when\nalready present coefficients compensate them. Positivity is checked\nafter addition, rather than separately on each correction. A,E are\ncross-copy fan coefficients whose edges to queried interiors are absent\nfrom the child arrays; they must be nonnegative when those weights are\npositive. No earlier interpolation constraint is silently removed; the\nfinal array is the explicitly stated sum.\nDefine W'=u W_L+r W_R+k g+l e. The exact\nparent identity has remainder\n\n    R'=gamma_L R_L+gamma_R R_R+F,                    (9)\n\nwhere the COMPLETE local quadratic is\n\n    F=gamma_L||W_L||^2+gamma_R||W_R||^2-||W'||^2\n       +2r<W_R,P_L+b g>\n       +k||g||^2+2k<g,P_L>\n       +l||e||^2+2l<e,P_L+b g+P_R>\n       -2(C+E)<W_L,P_L>-2C<W_L,g>\n       -2E<W_L,b g+P_R+e>\n       +(A+C)D||g||^2+(B+E)D||e||^2\n       +2A d<g,W_R>-2B<W_R,P_R+e>\n       +p0[||g||^2+||e||^2+2d<g,e>+2<e,P_R>]\n       +q0[-(2b-1)||g||^2+||e||^2-2<g,e>-2<g,P_R>]. (10)\n\nHere P_L and W_L are genuinely distinct; none of their cross terms has\nbeen discarded. To verify (9)--(10), the four fan identities are\n\n    sum_L w_i C_(p,i)\n       =-J_L-||W_L||^2+2<W_L,P_L+g>-D||g||^2,\n    sum_L w_i C_(q,i)\n       =-J_L-||W_L||^2+2<W_L,P_L+b g+P_R+e>\n                                            -D||e||^2-2D(f_p-f_q),\n    sum_R w_i C_(p,i)\n       =2D(f_p-f_q)-J_R-||W_R||^2\n                                            -2d<g,W_R>-D||g||^2,\n    sum_R w_i C_(q,i)\n       =-J_R-||W_R||^2+2<W_R,P_R+e>-D||e||^2.       (11)\n\nExpanding J of the concatenated weights and subtracting (11) leaves\nthe value coefficient 2[(u-A+E)D+k-p0+q0](f_p-f_q), which is zero\nby (8), and exactly (10). This proves every value and Gram coefficient.\n\n### Full undeformed Silver calibration of this interface\n\nFor a block whose proof weights are (h_0,...,h_(n-1),M), one has\nD=2M-1 and W=P+M g_end. Substitution in J gives\n\n    V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-SM||g_n||^2.\n\nIn (9)--(10) choose, for 0<=d<M,\n\n    z=(M+d)/(M-d), u=r=1, A=B=z, C=E=0,\n    k=1+d-M, l=M+d,\n    p0=z(1-M), q0=z d.                              (11a)\n\nAlthough p0 can be negative, its FINAL (p,q) coefficient is zM+p0=z,\nand the (q,p) coefficient is z d. All other new entries are nonnegative;\nthe midpoint proof weight becomes 1+d and the terminal weight M'.\nThe child scales are 1 and 1+2z. Substituting W_L=P_L+Mg and W_R=P_R+Me\ninto the FULL quadratic (10), without falsely treating dependent\nmoments as independent, yields exactly\n\n    F=[M^2-(1+2z)d^2]||g||^2\n            +[(1+2z)M^2-(2M+d)^2]||e||^2.          (11b)\n\nSet lambda=1+sqrt(2), M=lambda^j and d=lambda^(j-1).\nThen z=lambda, 1+2z=lambda^2 and M'=lambda M, so both\ncoefficients in (11b) vanish. Starting with the empty block, weight 1\nand zero finite array, induction gives a full Huber-level certificate\nfor every undeformed Silver word, with zero remainder. At the first\nstep its finite entries are lambda and 1. This is a proved algebraic\ncalibration; no primitive or composability theorem is imported.\n\nThe independence assumption below is essential: these calibrated\nSilver certificates have W=P+Mg_end and do not satisfy it.\n\n## 4. A whole-family obstruction when the left moments are independent\n\nAssume the two child remainders are PSD. Suppose that the left queried\nweight vector (w^L_0,...,w^L_(n-1)) is not proportional to the physical\nstep vector (h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Then the three formal linear forms\n(P_L,W_L,g) are independent. The terminal gradient is an independent\ncoordinate, and the two queried coefficient rows are linearly independent.\n\nIf (9) were PSD, it would annihilate the all-ones gradient vector, as\nwould both child remainders. Hence F must be stationary at\n\n    P_L=P_R=S, W_L=W_R=D, g=e=1.\n\nThe P_L derivative in (10), divided by 2, is\n\n    rD+k+l-(C+E)D=D'-gamma_L D.\n\nThe W_L derivative, divided by 2, is\n\n    gamma_L D-uD'-CM-EM'.\n\nIndependence of P_L,W_L,g therefore forces\n\n    gamma_L=gamma:=D'/D,\n    CM+EM'=D'(1-u),\n    E=[gamma S-u(D'-M)]/(M+d).                      (12)\n\nSince E>=0, every member of this four-fan family satisfying full PSD\nat the Huber value necessarily obeys\n\n    u/gamma<=S/(D'-M)= (M-1)/(3M+2d-1).             (13)\n\nOn the other hand, evaluate the FULL parent matrix (4) at x_start=0,\ng_0=1 and every other parent gradient zero. The additional incoming\nfinite mass at index 0 is (C+E)w^L_0. Formula (6) gives its unscaled\nvalue exactly as\n\n    2w^L_0[(1+a)u-a gamma]-2a gamma in^L_0.          (14)\n\nThe child has w^L_0>0 by Section 2. Thus the parent matrix is strictly\nnegative whenever\n\n    (M-1)/(3M+2d-1) < a/(1+a).                     (15)\n\nEquivalently, (15) says (1-2a)M<1+2ad. A simple sufficient domain is\n\n    a>=2/5, d/M>=a.                                (16)\n\nIndeed 2a^2+2a-1>=3/25>0 there, so\n(1-2a)M-2ad <=(1-2a-2a^2)M<0<1.\n\nConsequently NO such Huber-tight four-fan join is possible in (16)\nwhen the left child has independent physical and proof moments.\nThe conclusion allows signed corrections B,C,p0,q0 subject to actual\nfinal multiplier positivity. Its proof only needs E>=0. The child\narrays may be dense and contain all ordered finite pairs;\nthe obstruction does not result from deleting their earlier edges.\n\nThe proportional queried-weight case is expressly outside this argument.\nSo is a different cross-block edge pattern, a nonsaturated objective\nconstruction that changes the weights and multipliers, or a general\nunrestricted certificate. This is not a nonexistence theorem for H_m.\n\n## 5. An explicit recursively specified candidate, rejected at every q\n\nThere is a particularly simple fully specified instance of (9). For\nevery join put V=D'-M=3M+2d-1 and\n\n    gamma=D'/D, z=gamma(M+d)/V,\n    u=gamma-2z=gamma S/V,\n    r=gamma-z=gamma S'/V,\n    k=(3z-gamma)D=bD'/V,\n    l=0, A=z, B=0, C=2z, E=0, p0=q0=0.             (17)\n\nFor S>0 and d>=0 all of gamma,z,u,r,k are positive. Equations (7)\nand (8) hold exactly: u+r=2gamma-3z and k=(3z-gamma)D.\nThus the rule is:\n\n* scale both complete old finite arrays by gamma;\n* add 2z w^L_i on (p,i) throughout the left block;\n* add z w^R_i on (p,i) throughout the right block;\n* use proof weights (u w^L, r w^R), adding k at p.\n\nAll FINAL finite coefficients and optimum coefficients in (4) are\nnonnegative. Every value cancels; the full remainder is (9)--(10).\nUnlike Section 4, this is an explicit array at every depth, even after\nits PSD test fails, and does not require any moment-rank hypothesis.\n\nFor a one-step seed h=1+a with 0<a<=1/2, take\n\n    w=(1+2a,2), D=3+2a,\n    lambda_(0,1)=2, lambda_(1,0)=1-2a,\n    R=(1-2a)||g_0-g_1||^2.                          (18)\n\nDirect expansion gives (2) with these coefficients. This is a full\nHuber-level objective certificate. At the undeformed Silver one-step\nbenchmark a=sqrt(2)-1, all coefficients are nonnegative and\nR=(3-2sqrt(2))||g_0-g_1||^2. Thus the seed is calibrated exactly.\n\nNevertheless, (17) already fails at the undeformed three-step Silver\njoin. Its M=1+sqrt(2), d=1 and a=sqrt(2)-1 satisfy (16), so (14)\nis negative. This calibration failure is not suppressed by extending\nthe formula to deformed words.\n\nMore strongly, the recursively specified array (17)--(18) has a\nnegative full g_0 diagonal at EVERY join satisfying (16), even if the\nchild remainder has already become indefinite. To see this, (14)\nuses only the child value flow, the nonnegative finite coefficients,\nand w^L_0>0. All of those hold inductively for the actual array; PSD\nof the child is not needed. Its left weight is multiplied by u>0.\nEquations (13), (15), and (17) therefore make (14) strictly negative.\n\nIn particular q can be arbitrarily large in (4): x_start=0 makes the\nq term vanish, so changing the bound without changing the actual\nmultiplier array cannot repair it. The negative assignment is a formal\nfull-matrix test, NOT a legal interpolated function trajectory.\n\n## 6. Application to the actual horizon-dependent critical word\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), m>=64,\n\n    c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j).\n\nWithin this fixed horizon, use (18) on H_1 and (17) at every later join.\nLet M_j=1+sum H_j. Directly from the word,\n\n    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2M_j+d_j,\n    M_j=[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j.              (19)\n\nWe have 0<a<c-2, hence d_j/M_j>=a for every j: write\nM_j/c^j=A+(1-A)(2/c)^j<=1 with A=a/(c-2) in (0,1).\nAlso a>=2/5 for m>=64. The function (m-1)/(m+1) increases, and at\nm=64 this is equivalent to 63sqrt(2)>=89, whose square is the strict\ninteger inequality 7938>7921. The seed satisfies a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2.\n\nThus every join from H_1 onward satisfies (16). For every m>=64 the\nfull final matrix generated by this explicit array is negative on\nx_0=0, g_0 nonzero, all other gradients zero, for EVERY q. This is an\nactual full-matrix obstruction for the stated critical parameters,\nnot just an asymptotic scalar calculation or a matrix tested on a\nselected primal family.\n\nReplacing only the final physical step by any prescribed nonnegative\nnumber leaves this negative evaluation unchanged: its gradient is zero,\nso every substituted position is unchanged. The same array remains\nvalue-canceling. This observation excludes that repair to this array,\nnot independently chosen certificates for a modified schedule.\n\n## 7. What remains open\n\nThe new family genuinely permits proof weights different from the\nphysical steps and retains both moments in every full coefficient.\nIts failure is caused by the exact incompatibility between the\nHuber common-gradient kernel and the first-gradient diagonal.\n\nThe target requires some q_m B_(2^m-1)<1 asymptotically, not necessarily\nHuber equality. Neither the original schedules nor this weaker upper\ntarget are ruled out. Unrestricted cross-block interior edges, a\nnonsaturated rule with a different multiplier array, and proportional\nqueried-weight certificates remain possible. None has been proved to\ngive the required growing-horizon upper bound here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["2105aae5913d003a", "3153862b814bfe16", "047c257795dc59ba"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "branch_id": "max_independent_history_four_fan", "status": "unverified full-matrix obstruction only; original target open", "artifact": "research/max_independent_history_fan_obstruction.md", "exact_audit": "research/independent_history_exact_audit.py", "new_solver_calls": 0, "new_verifier_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"C_ij": "Nonnegative interpolation slack -2 Q_ji, with C_ii=0.", "J_w": "Independent proof-weight intrinsic objective energy of Section1.", "P_L": "Physical gradient displacement in the left block.", "P_R": "Physical gradient displacement in the right block.", "W_L": "Left proof-weighted gradient moment.", "W_R": "Right proof-weighted gradient moment.", "gamma_L": "Left child finite-array scale u+C+E.", "gamma_R": "Right child finite-array scale r+A+B.", "D'": "Parent Huber denominator 2M'-1.", "M'": "Parent shifted physical mass 2M+d.", "p0": "Possibly signed correction on C_(p,q); final coefficient must be nonnegative.", "q0": "Possibly signed correction on C_(q,p); final coefficient must be nonnegative.", "V_h": "Intrinsic physical-weight energy defined in the calibration subsection."}}
{"id": "4cf747fea7fe2e00", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:13:28.746486+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Every positive physical step must retain an incident interpolation edge in EVERY nonnegative full-objective certificate, at every finite q: an isolated queried positive-step vertex forces an explicit scalar formal direction with remainder unbounded below. Thus the successful terminal/zero-step selector cannot be extended to deleting positive-step vertices by ANY choice of redistributed remaining coefficients.", "evidence": "Let n>=1, h>=0, and let lambda>=0 cancel every value in q||x0||^2-2fn+sum lambda_ik Q_ik for a finite real q, with all original gradients independent and xi=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t gt. The necessary finite value flow is out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n). Suppose h_j>0 and every interpolation coefficient incident to j is0. Put T_cut={j+1,...,n}, and let C_out,C_in be the actual multiplier sums crossing this cut in the two directions, optimum included in its complement. Summing the value flow over T_cut gives C_out-C_in=2, hence C_out>=2. Set scalar formal x0=0, g_i=0 for i<j, g_j=t, g_i=1 for i>j. No incident edge uses the gradient g_j, so every norm-difference term is independent of t. All positions inside T_cut acquire the common term -h_j t, while earlier/optimum positions do not. Thus the only t terms are from edges sourced inside T_cut and targeting its complement, each contributing +lambda_ik h_j t. Every other edge either has equal position shifts or source gradient0. The FULL polynomial is M(t)=M(0)+h_j C_out t. Choosing t=-(|M(0)|+1)/(h_j C_out) gives M(t)<=-1. Therefore no such full PSD certificate exists. This is a formal certificate-support obstruction, not a legal GD lower witness. It permits arbitrary q>Huber and arbitrary optimum-source multipliers. It does not require tightness or a positive-definite remainder, and it gives no obstruction to preserving some incident edges during a bulk re-selection.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["8d60e800f95e6026", "039d8b5e7f9ce6df"], "justification_type": "calculation", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "scope": "subgoal", "status": "candidate_not_verified"}, "glossary": {"T_cut": "Tail query index set {j+1,...,n} following the positive-step query j.", "C_out": "Total actual multiplier mass from T_cut to its complement, including the optimum.", "C_in": "Total actual multiplier mass entering T_cut from its complement."}}
{"id": "cebf601250c82dc2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:17:05.190686+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Full extension now complete as a candidate: EVERY normalized real linear image of the two complex modes z1=(1+i)/(2S),z2=(4+i)/(2S), hence EVERY common PSD metric including unrestricted initial-direction correlations, has2F(x_q-bg_q)<=21/(20S) eventually on the actual H_m^- for ALLb[0,2]. A three-inequality coefficient square completion gives full-metric constant<.5205<21/40. A self-contained weaker scalar comparison B_n<=(5/2)C_n<=(5/2)(n+1)^rho then gives full-class limsup benchmark ratio<=21/(8e)<63/65. Original unrestricted target remains open.", "evidence": "# Chronological and optimum anchors control every metric of two complex modes\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, UNVERIFIED. No routine fact submission is\nallocated. The only correctness inputs used are facts 1252b8823bb5a290\n(necessary interpolation inequalities and smoothness) and b98bb807d514e80d\n(the exact benchmark recursions). Every new estimate is given below. Earlier\nunverified spectral and covariance findings are not premises.\n\n## 1. Actual schedule, full added space, and claim\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2). For an integer m>=128 put\nc=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, d_j=1+a c^j. Within this horizon these\nparameters are fixed. Set H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), n=2^m-1,\nq=n-1, and let h be H_m with ONLY its original last step d_0 removed.\nWrite S=sum h_t. For 0<=i<=q put P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z).\nFix zeta_1=(1+i)/2, zeta_2=(4+i)/2, and z_l=zeta_l/S.\n\nFor a pair of complex numbers define the real column\n\n J(v_1,v_2)=(Re(v_1-v_2), Im v_1, Im v_2)^T.\n\nLet w be arbitrary in [0,1] and T be ANY real matrix with three columns,\nwith no upper bound on its entries or number of rows. Define Euclidean data\n\n x_i=(sqrt(w)Re P_i(z_1),sqrt(w)Im P_i(z_1),\n      sqrt(1-w)Re P_i(z_2),sqrt(1-w)Im P_i(z_2),\n      T J(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2))),\n g_i=(sqrt(w)Re[z_1 P_i(z_1)],sqrt(w)Im[z_1 P_i(z_1)],\n      sqrt(1-w)Re[z_2 P_i(z_2)],sqrt(1-w)Im[z_2 P_i(z_2)],\n      T J(z_1 P_i(z_1),z_2 P_i(z_2))).                    (1)\n\nThen ||x_0||=1 and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. Every real linear functional\non (v_1,v_2) that vanishes at (1,1) is a linear combination of the three\ncomponents of J: its two real coefficients must sum to zero, and its two\nimaginary coefficients are unrestricted. Thus (1) includes EVERY PSD\naddition annihilating the initial vector, added orthogonally to the two\nnormal blocks. Write M=T^T T; it is an arbitrary PSD 3 by 3 matrix.\n\nSuppose arbitrary JOINT queried values are interpolated by a convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth function F, with F(0)=0=min F and grad F(x_i)=g_i.\nNo separate modal values or modal legality are assumed. Then, for every\nsufficiently large m, uniformly over w,T,F and b in [0,2],\n\n                 2F(x_q-b g_q) <= 2/(3S).               (2)\n\nFor B_n=1/U_n^ZJ=2S_bullet(n)+1 this implies\n\n limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(w,T,F,b) B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n                       <= 8/(3e) < 64/65 < 1.           (3)\n\nThe actual final step b=d_0 lies in [0,2]. The statement concerns exactly\nthe displayed spectral family. Sections 8--9 prove a broader result: for\nANY real linear map A from C^2, viewed as R^4, to a finite-dimensional\nEuclidean space, with ||A(1,1)||=1, set\n\n x_i=A(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2)),\n g_i=A(z_1 P_i(z_1),z_2 P_i(z_2)).\n\nFor arbitrary jointly legal values and all b in [0,2], eventually,\nuniformly over these maps and their conditioning,\n\n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=21/(20S),\n limsup sup B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=21/(8e)<63/65<1.          (19)\n\nThus the broader result includes every PSD metric on the complete\nfour-dimensional two-mode evaluation space, including initial-direction\ncross terms. Neither result bounds unrestricted W_n(H_m) or arbitrary\nspectra. The number of modes is fixed at the two specified parameters.\n\n## 2. Two exact shared interpolation inequalities\n\nUse Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0 from the accepted\nfact 1252b8823bb5a290. First sum Q_(0,star) and Q_(t+1,t) for every\n0<=t<q. The values telescope, and GD gives\n\n f_q <= <g_0,x_0>-sum_(t<q) h_t<g_(t+1),g_t>\n                -||g_0||^2/2-sum_(t<q)||g_(t+1)-g_t||^2/2.\n\nDropping only the explicitly nonnegative squared terms yields\n\n f_q <= <g_0,x_0>-sum_(t<q)h_t<g_(t+1),g_t>.             (4)\n\nSecond, for any actual queried r, sum Q_(q,r) and Q_(r,star), and drop\ntheir two squared terms. This gives\n\n f_q <= <g_q,x_q>+<g_r-g_q,x_r>.                         (5)\n\nChoose r=2^(m-1), the query immediately AFTER the largest bridge. Both\ninequalities concern the same joint transcript and the same function.\nTheir convex combination uses the fixed coefficients\n\n                theta=6/25 on (4), 1-theta=19/25 on (5). (6)\n\nFor scaled gradients U_i=J(zeta_1 P_i(z_1),zeta_2 P_i(z_2)), and\nY_i=J(P_i(z_1),P_i(z_2)), let sym(u,v)=(uv^T+vu^T)/2. Define\n\n E_m=sum_(t<q) (h_t/S) sym(U_(t+1),U_t),\n Kplus_m=sym(U_q,Y_q)+sym(U_r-U_q,Y_r),\n K_m=(19/25)Kplus_m-(6/25)E_m.                          (7)\n\nFor l=1,2 let e_t=h_t/S and p_i,l=P_i(z_l), and define\n\n C_l,m=Re zeta_l-|zeta_l|^2 sum_(t<q)\n                   e_t |p_t,l|^2(1-e_t Re zeta_l),\n Dplus_l,m=Re[zeta_l p_q,l conj(p_q,l)\n                     +zeta_l(p_r,l-p_q,l)conj(p_r,l)],\n D_l,m=(6/25)C_l,m+(19/25)Dplus_l,m.                    (8)\n\nThe initial added coordinates are zero. Substituting (1) into (4)--(6)\ntherefore gives the EXACT finite-word bound\n\n             S f_q <= w D_1,m+(1-w)D_2,m+trace(M K_m).  (9)\n\nThe proof will show K_m is negative definite and D_l,m<1/3 for all\nsufficiently large m. Crucially these properties will hold at each such\nFINITE m before allowing M to vary or grow without bound.\n\n## 3. Limit word and continuity of its chronological work\n\nDirectly summing the multiplicity of every step gives\n\n S=2^m-2-a+a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),      S/lambda^m -> e,\n d_(m-1-j)/S -> delta lambda^(-j),\n delta=1-2/lambda=3-2sqrt(2).                            (10)\n\nHere a->1/lambda, (1+1/m)^m->e, and lambda(lambda-2)=1. The largest\nnormalized step has limit delta. The normalized limiting word consists\nof a central step delta, two copies with central step delta/lambda,\nfour copies with central step delta/lambda^2, and so on, with their\nrecursive chronology retained. The total mass at level j is\ndelta(2/lambda)^j; the total mass is one.\n\nIts truncation after N largest levels is the finite word obtained from\nH <- (H,d,H), starting empty and using, in ascending order,\n\n d=delta/lambda^(N-1), ..., delta/lambda, delta.\n\nThe omitted mass is exactly epsilon_N=(2/lambda)^N. This definition\ndoes not introduce any new physical terminal step.\n\nFor the four modal numbers\n\n zeta_1, conjugate(zeta_1), zeta_2, conjugate(zeta_2),\n\nevery normalized factor satisfies |1-d zeta|<=1 whenever 0<=d<=1/5:\nexpanding the square reduces this to d<=2Re(zeta)/|zeta|^2, whose\nminimum here is 16/17. All limiting steps and, eventually, all actual\nnormalized steps satisfy the stronger bound d<1/5.\n\nDeleting steps of total mass tau from such a chronological word changes\nany modal prefix product by at most |zeta|tau. To see this, telescope\nthe deletion one factor at a time; all other factors have modulus<=1,\nand the deleted factor differs from one by d zeta.\n\nFor any two of the four modal numbers A,B define the scalar work moment\n\n W_word(A,B)=sum_t d_t P_t(A)P_t(B)[1-d_t(A+B)/2].        (11)\n\nThe bracket is the average of 1-d_t A and 1-d_t B, so its modulus is\nat most one. Deleted terms have total modulus<=tau. At retained terms,\nthe change in the two-product is at most (|A|+|B|)tau. Since the total\nstep mass is at most one,\n\n |W_word-W_deleted| <= (1+|A|+|B|)tau.                  (12)\n\nAfter multiplying by AB this is <23tau, because |A|,|B|<=sqrt(17)/2\n<21/10 and |AB|<=17/4. Expressing the three real coordinates J in these\nfour complex modes uses rows with absolute coefficient sums 2,1,1.\nConsequently deleting mass tau changes any entry of the real work\nmatrix by less than 92tau. We use the simpler uniform bound 100tau.\nThe normal work terms have error less than 23tau.\n\nThese inequalities prove existence of the infinite-word moments. They\nalso prove convergence from the ACTUAL words in (10). Indeed, retain\nonly their N largest levels. The resulting finite word converges to\nthe displayed N-level truncation, including its chronological order.\nFor fixed N the deleted normalized mass converges to epsilon_N:\nthe normalized total mass is exactly one and the retained masses\nconverge individually. Apply (12), first let m tend to infinity, and\nthen let N tend to infinity. The removed original d_0 is among the\ndeleted levels when m>N, so this argument also handles it explicitly.\nWe obtain E_m->E and convergence of the two normal work coefficients.\n\nThe left-half product and bridge/endpoint products are\n\n L(zeta)=product_(j>=1)(1-delta lambda^(-j)zeta)^(2^(j-1)),\n p_l=L(zeta_l),   v_l=(1-delta zeta_l)p_l,\n t_l=p_l v_l.                                          (13)\n\nFor the actual words, the corresponding products are exactly\nv_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m and\nt_l,m=(1-d_(m-1)z_l)p_l,m^2/(1-d_0 z_l).\nThe last denominator tends to one. Deletion of a small total mass and\nthe same product bound prove convergence to (13). Thus K_m and D_l,m\nconverge entrywise to the quantities K,D_l given by (7)--(8) and the\nlimiting work moments.\n\n## 4. Finite rational enclosures of the fixed limiting coefficients\n\nEverything in this section is bounded arithmetic for one fixed\ncoefficient matrix; no PEP or optimization evaluation is used.\n\nFor the product (13) define mu_s=delta^s/(lambda^s-2). Absolutely\nconvergent logarithmic expansion gives\n\n log L(zeta)=-sum_(s>=1)mu_s zeta^s/s.\n\nLet A_10 be the sum through s=10. Since mu_2<1/125,\n|zeta|^2<=17/4, and eta=|zeta|delta/lambda<3/20, the omitted tail is\n\n <=mu_2 |zeta|^2 eta^9/[11(1-eta)] < 1/10^9.           (14)\n\nThe bound on mu_s used here is mu_s<=mu_2(delta/lambda)^(s-2), obtained\ndirectly by summing the positive powers of the left-half step sizes.\nCompute exp(A_10) through degree18. The bounds Re A_10<0 and |A_10|<1\ngive exponential tail <3/19!. Perturbing its argument by (14) changes\nthe exponential by <(10^-9)/(1-10^-9). Hence error 2/10^9 in each real\ncomponent safely covers both tails.\n\nFor the moment (11), its exact concatenation recurrence is short.\nIf a word has products p_A,p_B and moment W, appending its central\nstep d and another copy gives\n\n p_A,new=p_A^2(1-dA),  p_B,new=p_B^2(1-dB),\n W_new=W[1+p_A p_B(1-dA)(1-dB)]\n                     +d p_A p_B[1-d(A+B)/2].           (15)\n\nThe first copy contributes W, the central step gives the last term,\nand the second copy is multiplied by p_A p_B(1-dA)(1-dB), proving\nthe identity. Use N=112 levels in (15). The rational bound\n\n                 100(2/lambda)^112 < 1/10^7             (16)\n\nand (12) supply the error for every resulting real work coefficient.\n\nFor a completely specified finite arithmetic procedure, use the interval\n\n r_- = 1.41421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694,\n r_+ = r_-+10^-50\n\nfor sqrt(2); its squared endpoints straddle 2. Set lambda=1+[r_-,r_+]\nand delta=1-2/lambda. Interval additions, products, and positive\nreciprocals enclose their exact values. At each stored log-series,\nexponential-series, or word-recursion update, round the lower endpoint\ndown and upper endpoint up to a\nmultiple of 10^-60. This only enlarges an enclosure and prevents\ndenominator growth. Evaluate A_10, then its degree18 exponential using\nt_0=1, t_j=t_(j-1)A_10/j. Add the componentwise error in (14). For\nthe work moments, use (15) on all sixteen pairs of the four modes.\n\nThe coefficients expressing J in these four modes, in that order, are\n\n c_1=(1/2,1/2,-1/2,-1/2),\n c_2=(-i/2,i/2,0,0), c_3=(0,0,-i/2,i/2).\n\nThe real work matrix entries are\nE_kl=Re sum_(r,s)c_kr c_ls zeta_r zeta_s W(zeta_r,zeta_s).\nThe two normal chain coefficients are\nC_1=1/2-(1/2)W(zeta_1,conjugate(zeta_1)) and\nC_2=2-(17/4)W(zeta_2,conjugate(zeta_2)).\nAdd error interval [-10^-7,10^-7] to these expressions. This includes\n(16); all preceding rounding errors are already in the intervals.\n\nThe following outward rational enclosures result. Finite decimals are\nexact rational numbers; the margins are deliberately larger than the\nrounding errors.\n\n| quantity | enclosing interval |\n|---|---|\n| Re p_1 | [.7952608488,.7952608529] |\n| Im p_1 | [-.1687257189,-.1687257148] |\n| Re p_2 | [.4200940450,.4200940491] |\n| Im p_2 | [-.0920602833,-.0920602792] |\n| E_11 | [.3738957,.3738960] |\n| E_12 | [-.1629610,-.1629607] |\n| E_13 | [-.0946327,-.0946324] |\n| E_22 | [.0952537,.0952540] |\n| E_23 | [.0317060,.0317063] |\n| E_33 | [.0282053,.0282056] |\n| C_1 | [.1841427,.1841430] |\n| C_2 | [.9918514,.9918517] |\n\nSubstituting the directly computed intervals in (7)--(8), at theta=6/25,\ngives\n\n 19/100 < D_1 < 193/1000,       329/1000 < D_2 < 33/100,\n -132/1000 < K_11 < -131/1000,\n 21/10000 < K_11 K_22-K_12^2 < 22/10000,\n -18/10^8 < det K < -15/10^8.                           (17)\n\nThese are finite rational inequalities obtained by the displayed\nrecurrences, without eigenvalue computation. The three successive\ndiagonal pivots in completing the square are\nK_11, (K_11 K_22-K_12^2)/K_11, det K/(K_11 K_22-K_12^2).\nThey are all negative by (17). Thus K is negative definite, and both\nnormal coefficients are strictly below 1/3.\n\nThe companion chain_anchor_interval_check.py implements these exact\ninterval operations and asserts (17). Its first version was stopped\nbecause an unrounded exponential expression caused denominator growth.\nThe corrected version rounds the intermediate log/exponential terms\nand the fixed-size moment recurrence; it exits zero. An earlier\nordinary floating-point exploration was not used as proof: repeated\nsquaring of factors extremely close to one loses significant small-step\nmass in floating arithmetic. The rational calculation and its explicit\ntail estimates are the evidence for (17).\n\n## 5. Finite-word uniformity, free terminal step, and exact benchmark\n\nEntrywise convergence and the strict inequalities (17) imply that for\nall sufficiently large m, the EXACT K_m is negative definite and\nD_l,m<1/3. None of those coefficients depends on w or T. Since\ntrace(T^T T K_m) is a sum of nonpositive row quadratic forms, (9) gives\nF(x_q)<=1/(3S), uniformly even for coupling matrices diverging with m.\n\nIntegration of the 1-Lipschitz gradient along the final segment gives\n\n F(x_q-b g_q)<=F(x_q)-b(1-b/2)||g_q||^2<=F(x_q),\n\nfor all b in [0,2]. The terminal gradient and value are free; this\nproves (2) for the same interpolating F.\n\nHere is the comparison with the exact project benchmark, proved rather\nthan imported from an unverified finding. Let rho=log_2 lambda,\nC_k=S_circle(k)+1, and B_k=2S_bullet(k)+1. The accepted recursions give\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,\n G(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.\n\nFor V(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, differentiation gives, with t=exp u,\n\n d^2/du^2 log V(exp u)\n =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/V(t)^2 > 0.\n\nThe bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative for t>0.\nConvexity at u and -u implies V(t)V(1/t)>=V(1)^2=2. If A,B>0 and\nM=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho, this means (M-A)(M-B)>=2AB;\nthe positive root of this quadratic is Omega(A,B). Thus\nOmega(A,B)<=M and induction gives C_k<=(k+1)^rho.\nThe inequality sqrt(B^2+8AB)<=B+4A yields G(A,B)<=B+4A.\nUsing u^rho+v^rho<=(u+v)^rho for nonnegative u,v proves by induction\nB_n<=1+4n^rho. The latter inequality follows by differentiating\n(1+t)^rho-t^rho, whose minimum for t>=0 is one.\n\nAt n=2^m-1, n^rho/lambda^m->1 and S/lambda^m->e by (10).\nMultiplying (2) by B_n proves (3). Finally e>sum_(j=0)^4 1/j!=65/24,\nso 8/(3e)<64/65.\n\n## 6. Why the chronology is necessary in this certificate\n\nDefine chi(v_1,v_2)=Re(v_1-v_2)/4+Im v_1/5+Im v_2.\nIt vanishes at (1,1). Apply it to the left, right, and endpoint modal\npairs in (13), obtaining y_-,y_+,y_q and, after multiplication by\nthe modal zeta values, u_-,u_+,u_q. The coefficients of its squared\namplitude in the two individual bridge-anchor bounds are\n\n k_-=u_q y_q+(u_--u_q)y_-,\n k_+=u_q y_q+(u_+-u_q)y_+.\n\nThe same rational product enclosures give\n\n        23/100000 < k_- < 24/100000,\n       103/100000 < k_+ < 104/100000.                    (18)\n\nEvery convex combination of just these two anchor matrices therefore\nhas a strictly positive direction. This refutes a purely algebraic\nextension of the two-anchor method to the full three-dimensional\nspace. It is NOT a legal high-payoff construction. Adding the actual\nchronological chain supplies a different constraint and yields (17).\n\n## 7. Scope of the first coefficient bound\n\nThe bound (2) covers arbitrary PSD zero-initial additions to the two\nnormal blocks. Negative definiteness on J alone does not establish (2)\nfor arbitrary metrics: the initial-direction cross terms must also be\ncontrolled. The next section does this with an additional actual\ninterpolation bound and a larger, explicitly paid coefficient. It uses\nno unverified result as a premise: the complete coefficient derivation\nand all estimates are part of this same proof.\n\n## 8. Full initial-direction correlations and the third inequality\n\nLet A be any map in the broader claim (19), and put e=A(1,1), so ||e||=1.\nWrite a(v_1,v_2)=Re v_1. The scalar map <e,A(v_1,v_2)> has value one\nat (1,1), and therefore equals a+c^T J for some real column c in R^3.\nThe orthogonal projection of A onto e-perpendicular annihilates (1,1),\nso it has the form T J, where T has range perpendicular to e. Hence\n\n A(v)=e[a(v)+c^T J(v)]+T J(v).                          (20)\n\nThis is an exact decomposition of EVERY normalized map A, not a\nrestriction to a chosen initial direction. The matrix M=T^T T is PSD\nand c is unrestricted. Both may depend arbitrarily on m.\n\nApply the combination (6) to (20). At finite m it gives\n\n S f_q <= k00_m+2 k0_m^T c+c^T K_m c+trace(M K_m).      (21)\n\nHere K_m is still (7). To specify the new coefficients, set\na_i=Re P_i(z_1), u_i=Re[zeta_1 P_i(z_1)],\nu_init=(-3/2,1/2,1/2)^T. Define the scalar and vector work moments\n\n E00_m=sum_t e_t u_(t+1)u_t,\n E0_m=sum_t e_t[u_(t+1)U_t+u_t U_(t+1)]/2.\n\nThe coefficient formulas, with r the post-bridge query, are\n\n k00_m=(19/25)[u_q a_q+(u_r-u_q)a_r]+(6/25)[1/2-E00_m],\n k0_m=(19/50)[u_q Y_q+U_q a_q+(u_r-u_q)Y_r+(U_r-U_q)a_r]\n                                  +(6/25)[u_init/2-E0_m]. (22)\n\nAll these moments converge by the same deletion proof (12). They use\nthe same sixteen scalar moments (15). In that formula the coefficient\nrow for a is c_0=(1/2,1/2,0,0). Thus E00 and E0_j are the real parts\nof the contractions with c_0,c_0 and c_0,c_j respectively, with the\nsame error bound 10^-7. Alternatively E00=1/2-C_1-E_22, since the\nfirst normal block is the sum of its real and imaginary coordinates.\n\nFor precision, direct outward interval calculation in the procedure\nof Section 4 gives\n\n k00 in [.26264513,.26264525],\n k0_1 in [-.10147100,-.10147092],\n k0_2 in [.03390797,.03390804],\n k0_3 in [.03049014,.03049021].                          (23)\n\nThe coefficient from (21) alone is too large after unrestricted square\ncompletion. This is a limitation of that bound, not a legal adversary.\nThe needed additional inequality is simply the ACTUAL Q_(q,star)<=0:\n\n                   S f_q <= <S g_q,x_q>.               (24)\n\nIts omitted squared gradient term is nonnegative. Add tau=2/3 times\n(24) to (21), and divide by 1+tau=5/3. The actual weights on the\nchronological chain, post-bridge anchor and optimum anchor are\n18/125,57/125,2/5, respectively. They are nonnegative and sum to one.\n\nBefore dividing, define\n\n Kbar_m=K_m+tau sym(U_q,Y_q),\n kbar_m=k0_m+(tau/2)(u_q Y_q+U_q a_q),\n kbar00_m=k00_m+tau u_q a_q.                            (25)\n\nThen\n\n (1+tau) S f_q <= kbar00_m+2 kbar_m^T c\n                            +c^T Kbar_m c+trace(M Kbar_m). (26)\n\nThe limiting coefficients from the same rational procedure satisfy\n\n -.082622 < Kbar_11 < -.082621,\n .0007105 < Kbar_11 Kbar_22-Kbar_12^2 < .0007106,\n -81/10^9 < det Kbar < -79/10^9.                        (27)\n\nIn particular Kbar is negative definite. For an entirely specified\narithmetic check of the remaining scalar, let cof_ji(Kbar) denote its\ncofactor obtained by deleting row j and column i, with sign (-1)^(i+j).\nEvaluate\n\n R_full = (3/5)[kbar00\n           -sum_(i,j)kbar_i cof_ji(Kbar) kbar_j/det Kbar].\n\nThe direct enclosing interval from (15), (22), (25) is\n\n            .5173 < R_full < .5205 < 21/40.             (28)\n\nThese cofactor formulas use only products, sums, and division by the\nstrictly negative interval in (27). No eigenvalue or optimization\nroutine is needed. The companion initial_cross_coefficient_check.py\nimplements this same fixed coefficient arithmetic. Its finer interval\nis [.5173336917833667,.5204116728479283]. These displayed endpoints\nreport the outward rational result; the exact coarse inequalities\n(27)--(28) are the claimed bounds.\n\nFor finite m sufficiently large, Kbar_m is negative definite and\nR_full,m<21/40 by convergence and the strict margins. Completing the\nsquare gives\n\n kbar00_m+2 kbar_m^T c+c^T Kbar_m c\n =kbar00_m-kbar_m^T Kbar_m^(-1)kbar_m\n  +(c+Kbar_m^(-1)kbar_m)^T Kbar_m(c+Kbar_m^(-1)kbar_m)\n <=(5/3) R_full,m.\n\nAlso trace(M Kbar_m)<=0. Equation (26) gives F(x_q)<=21/(40S), uniformly\nover ALL normalized maps A, including arbitrarily growing initial\ncorrelations and transverse metrics. The same final descent inequality\nas in Section 5 proves the first assertion of (19).\n\nOne scope audit can be stated explicitly. The scalar row a+5chi, with\nchi from Section 6, has unit initial value. Its coefficient in the\noriginal combination (21) lies in (.374,.375), exceeding 1/3. Thus\none cannot extend the OLD1/3 coefficient just by ignoring the initial\ncross terms. This row is not a legal counterexample: if its scaled\nterminal gradient is U and scaled derivative is V, the exact limiting\ncoefficient UV is in (-.084,-.082). At the actual last retained\nadjacent pair, cocoercivity implies UV>=V^2/S, so this scalar row\nfails legality for sufficiently large m. The third inequality and\nsquare completion prove (19) without assuming that this row, or any\nother unsupported coefficient direction, is realizable.\n\n## 9. A self-contained benchmark envelope sufficient for the full metric\n\nThis proof needs only the following weaker scalar comparison, which\nis established here directly:\n\n             G(x,(5/2)y) <= (5/2)Omega(x,y), x,y>=0.    (29)\n\nLet k=5/2 and p=Omega(x,y). The plane\nR=(16/15)x+(5/3)y bounds p from above. Indeed R>=max(x,y), and\n\n (R-x)(R-y)-2xy\n  =(16x^2-40xy+250y^2)/225\n  =[16(x-5y/4)^2+225y^2]/225 >=0.\n\nSince p is the larger root of (p-x)(p-y)=2xy, this proves p<=R.\nThe number G(x,ky) is the larger root of\nt^2-(4x+ky)t+4x^2=0. Substituting t=kp and using\np^2=(x+y)p+xy gives\n\n (kp)^2-(4x+ky)kp+4x^2\n        =x[4x+(25/4)y-(15/4)p] >=0.\n\nMoreover p>=x+y, so kp>=2x+(k/2)y, the vertex of the quadratic.\nIt follows that kp is at least its larger root, proving (29).\nThe reasoning includes x=0 or y=0; these cases also follow directly.\n\nThe function G is nondecreasing in its second nonnegative argument,\nas follows immediately from its formula. Starting from B_0=C_0=1,\ninduction in the accepted recursions and (29) gives\n\n B_n=max_j G(C_j,B_(n-j-1))\n     <=max_j G(C_j,(5/2)C_(n-j-1))\n     <=(5/2)max_j Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1))=(5/2)C_n.\n\nSection 5 already proved C_n<=(n+1)^rho directly from the recursions.\nTherefore B_(2^m-1)<=(5/2)lambda^m. Together with S/lambda^m->e and\nthe first bound in (19), this proves\n\n limsup sup B_n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=21/(8e)<63/65,\n\nwhere the strict inequality uses e>65/24. No numerical estimate for\nthe benchmark or presumed global profile limit enters this comparison.\nThe stronger coefficient 1+sqrt2 requested elsewhere is not assumed.\n\n## 10. Remaining target\n\nEvery normalized common PSD metric for THESE two complex modes is\ncovered, with full joint prefix legality, exact initial distance, and\nfree final value/gradient. The result is still an upper bound for a\nrestricted adversary family. It supplies neither an upper certificate\non the entire formal PEP space nor a matching legal lower witness for\nthe original problem. Additional spectral support, growing modal\ndimension, and nonlinear trajectory mechanisms remain outside the\nstatement.
