Full objective identity and a relaxed proof-weight choice\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,\n    C_ij=-2Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0,\n\nand put C_ii=0. All positions use the exact physical GD recursion.\nEvery formal vector x_0,g_0,...,g_n is independent.\n\nAt a block h of length n put S=sum h_i, M=S+1, P=sum h_i g_i, and fix\n\n    w_i=h_i for i<n, w_n=1, D=sum w_i=M,\n    W=P+g_n,\n    J_rel=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n                   +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.    (1)\n\nThe same quantity can be written\n\n    J_rel=2sum_i w_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_i w_i||g_i||^2\n       +2sum_i w_i<g_i,x_0-x_i>-||W||^2.\n\nTo check this, expand ||sum h_i g_i||^2 and use\n2sum_i h_i<g_i,sum_(t<i)h_t g_t>\n=||sum h_i g_i||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.\n\nSuppose finite nonnegative coefficients lambda_ij give the exact identity\n\n    J_rel=sum_(finite i!=j)lambda_ij C_ij+R.          (2)\n\nAll function coefficients cancel if and only if\n\n    out_i-in_i=w_i-M 1_(i=n).                       (3)\n\nFor EVERY real q the corresponding FULL objective polynomial is\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2f_n\n       +2/M sum_i w_i Q_(i,star)\n       +2/M sum_(finite i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ji\n      =(q-1/M)||x_0||^2+[||x_0-W||^2+R]/M.          (4)\n\nThis follows by expanding the optimum interpolation terms. All actual\nmultipliers are nonnegative. The array fixes Q_(star,i) multipliers to\nzero; general relaxed certificates need not have that property.\nIf R were PSD, (4) at q=1/M would be the requested weaker objective\nupper bound. This is genuinely above the Huber value when S>0.\n\n## 2. The actual four-fan recursion, with signed corrections allowed\n\nUse two copies of the same block. Let p be the left endpoint and q_end\nthe parent endpoint. Insert bridge b=1+d with d>0, and write\n\n    g=g_p, e=g_(q_end), M'=2M+d,\n    P_L=x_start-x_p, P_R=x_(p+1)-x_(q_end),\n    W_L=P_L+g, W_R=P_R+e,\n    V=d g+W_R, W_parent=W_L+V.\n\nChoose level-dependent A,E>=0 and real B,C,p0,q0. Set\n\n    gamma_L=1+C+E, gamma_R=1+A+B,\n    p0-q0=(1-A+E)M+d.                              (5)\n\nRequire gamma_L,gamma_R>=0. Scale the COMPLETE left and right child\nfinite arrays by gamma_L and gamma_R. Add the fans\n\n    C w_i on (p,i),       E w_i on (q_end,i),   i in left samples,\n    A w_i on (p,i),       B w_i on (q_end,i),   i in right samples,\n\nomitting self edges, and add p0 on (p,q_end) and q0 on (q_end,p).\nRequire EVERY FINAL finite multiplier nonnegative. In particular B,C,\np0,q0 can be signed when their actual resulting entries are nonnegative.\nThe new proof weights are the old physical weights in each queried\ncopy, with the left terminal weight changed from 1 to b, and the\nright terminal weight retained as 1. Thus D_parent=M', exactly as in (1).\n\nNo earlier interpolation constraints are deleted. The final array is\nthe explicit sum just given. Its value flow is (3); alternatively the\nfollowing expansion checks every value coefficient directly.\n\nThe four weighted fan identities, derived by expanding C, are\n\n    sum_L w_i C_(p,i)\n       =-J_L-||W_L||^2+2<W_L,P_L+g>-M||g||^2,\n    sum_L w_i C_(q_end,i)\n       =-J_L-||W_L||^2+2<W_L,P_L+b g+P_R+e>\n                                     -M||e||^2-2M(f_p-f_(q_end)),\n    sum_R w_i C_(p,i)\n       =2M(f_p-f_(q_end))-J_R-||W_R||^2\n                                     -2d<g,W_R>-M||g||^2,\n    sum_R w_i C_(q_end,i)\n       =-J_R-||W_R||^2+2<W_R,P_R+e>-M||e||^2.       (6)\n\nAfter adding the child identities and these fans, the only remaining\nfunction coefficient is\n2[(1-A+E)M+d-p0+q0](f_p-f_(q_end)), which is zero by (5).\nSubstitute P_L=W_L-g and P_R=W_R-e in the quadratic terms. The exact\nFULL gradient remainder is\n\n    R_parent=gamma_L R_L+gamma_R R_R+F_rel,\n\n    F_rel=-(C+E)||W_L||^2+(A-B)||W_R||^2\n       -2E<W_L,W_R+d g>\n       +2(Ad-q0)<g,W_R>+2p0<W_R,e>\n       +(gamma_L M-d^2-2dq0)||g||^2\n       +(gamma_R M-2M-d)||e||^2+2p0d<g,e>.          (7)\n\nThis retains both physical displacements through the displayed W_L,W_R\nand retains every child matrix. It is not a local-PSD assumption.\n\n## 3. Exact undeformed calibration\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2). For the undeformed Silver word, M=lambda^j and\nd=lambda^(j-1) at level j. Choose\n\n    A=B=lambda, C=E=0, p0=0, q0=lambda d,\n    gamma_L=1, gamma_R=lambda^2.                    (8)\n\nStart with the one-step finite array lambda_(0,1)=lambda,\nlambda_(1,0)=1, physical step sqrt(2). Direct expansion gives\nR=M(M-1)||g_end||^2 at this seed. Equation (5) holds in (8) because\n(1-lambda)M+d=-lambda d. All final coefficients are nonnegative.\n\nAssuming the same remainder formula for both children, (7) proves it\nfor the parent. The g coefficient from the left child cancels\nM-lambda^2 d^2=M-M^2. The e coefficient is\nlambda^2 M(M-1)+lambda^2 M-M'=M'^2-M'. All mixed coefficients are\nzero. Thus the relaxed identity passes the FULL undeformed benchmark\nat every depth, with its true initial distance and all values canceled.\n\n## 4. A formal template for EVERY allowed choice of the new fans\n\nFor the deformed word H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), let\nM_j=1+sum H_j. Define signed numbers\n\n    kappa_1=M_1=2+a,\n    kappa_(j+1)=-kappa_j^2/d_j.                     (9)\n\nEvery array produced by (5)--(7) has a recursively specified scalar\nformal-gradient template with terminal gradient e such that\n\n    W_j=kappa_j e,\n    R_j=(kappa_j^2-M_j)e^2.                         (10)\n\nAt the one-step seed, take both formal gradients equal to e. The two\nfinite C residuals are zero after setting f_0-f_1=h_0 e^2. Equation\n(1) gives R_1=M_1(M_1-1)e^2=(kappa_1^2-M_1)e^2 for ANY seed array\nsatisfying (3). PSD of the seed is not needed for this calculation.\n\nFor the induction use a right template with terminal e and a left\ntemplate with terminal g=-kappa_j e/d_j. Then\n\n    V=d_j g+W_R=0,\n    W_parent=W_L+V=kappa_j g=kappa_(j+1)e.\n\nOn V=0, the COMPLETE local expression (7) becomes\n\n    F_rel=-(gamma_L-1)||W_L||^2\n          +(gamma_L M_j-gamma_R d_j^2)||g||^2\n          +(gamma_R M_j-M_(j+1))||e||^2.            (11)\n\nIndeed W_R=-d_j g cancels both p0 mixed terms and the E term. Adding\ngamma_L R_L+gamma_R R_R and W_L=kappa_j g cancels gamma_L, gamma_R\nand M_j, leaving (kappa_j^4/d_j^2-M_(j+1))e^2. This proves (10).\nNo child PSD, attained zero, greedy storage selection, or saturation\nassumption occurs anywhere in this induction.\n\nNow insert (10) into the FULL initial-distance identity (4). After\nmultiplication by M_j its restriction to independent scalar x_0,e is\n\n             [ M_j q       -kappa_j     ]\n             [ -kappa_j  2kappa_j^2-M_j ].           (12)\n\nIf 2kappa_j^2<M_j this matrix is negative at x_0=0,e=1 for EVERY q.\nIf the lower diagonal is positive, PSD would force\n\n    q>=kappa_j^2/[M_j(2kappa_j^2-M_j)]>=1/kappa_j^2.\n\nThe last comparison is exactly (kappa_j^2-M_j)^2>=0. Thus merely\nincreasing q cannot repair the late negative diagonal.\n\n## 5. Explicit failure at the actual critical horizon\n\nPut m>=128, epsilon=1/m, c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c and\nd_j=a c^j=(1-epsilon)c^(j-1). All parameters are fixed within this\nwhole word. Solving (9) in absolute value gives\n\n    |kappa_m|=(1-epsilon)c^m Rcrit^(2^(m-1)),\n    Rcrit=(2+a)/[(1-epsilon)c].                     (13)\n\nThe formula holds at j=1 by definition, and squaring and dividing by\nd_j proves the next term. Direct subtraction gives\n\n    1-Rcrit=epsilon[2-lambda^2 epsilon(1+epsilon)]\n                    /[lambda^2(1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^2]\n             >=epsilon/4.                         (14)\n\nFor the inequality, lambda^2<6, the numerator bracket exceeds 7/4,\nand the denominator is less than 6(129/128)^2<25/4. Their ratio is\ntherefore greater than 7/25>1/4. Also 0<Rcrit<1.\nSince 1-t<=exp(-t) and c<3,\n\n    |kappa_m|<3^m exp[-2^(m-3)/m]\n               <=(3/exp(2))^m<2^(-m).              (15)\n\nHere 2^(m-4)>=m^2 holds at m=128 and propagates by induction because\n2m^2>=(m+1)^2 for m>=3. Also exp(2)>6 follows from the four positive\nseries terms 1+2+2+4/3. Since M_m>=1, (15) implies\n2kappa_m^2<M_m. Equation (12) is therefore a concrete negative FULL\nmatrix restriction at the actual target horizon, for every real q.\n\nThis excludes all arrays in the stated relaxed four-fan family,\nincluding arbitrary nonsaturating child remainders and all the signed\ncorrections permitted by actual coefficient positivity. It does not\nexclude unrestricted relaxed certificates. In particular independent\nnonsaturated history weights, different interior cross-copy edges,\nand nonzero optimum-source multipliers are not covered. The formal\ntemplate is not asserted to be a legal interpolated function.\n\nThe original uniform lower theorem and a rigorous fixed asymptotic\nseparation both remain unproved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["4bcbedc36b522e70", "694fb2dacb4f7389", "e3c8501149a5c47e"], "master_guidance": "84f7ef64640db7b2", "branch_id": "max_relaxed_four_fan", "artifact": "research/max_relaxed_four_fan_null_template.md", "exact_audit": "research/independent_history_exact_audit.py", "scope": "Four-fan arrays with optimum Q_i,star weights2(h,1)/M and zero Q_star,i; no arbitrary-q or schedule impossibility beyond that family", "calibration": "All-depth exact undeformed Silver; rational3/7-step full coefficients and null templates", "new_solver_calls": 0, "new_verifier_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"J_rel": "Relaxed physical-proof-weight energy in (1), with w=(h,1) and total mass M.", "F_rel": "Complete local four-fan gradient quadratic in (7).", "W_parent": "Actual parent moment P_parent+g_end=W_L+d g+W_R.", "kappa_j": "Signed scalar template moment, kappa_1=2+a and kappa_(j+1)=-kappa_j²/d_j.", "Rcrit": "Positive ratio (2+a)/[(1-epsilon)c] in the exact template recurrence solution.", "gamma_L": "Left child finite-array scale 1+C+E.", "gamma_R": "Right child finite-array scale 1+A+B."}}
{"id": "5f198236d1ff200e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:48:07.251588+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Growing temporal-span obstruction on ACTUAL H_m, uniform over ALL common real PSD metrics and joint legal values: exact reciprocal-level Newton roots annihilate with their actual occurrence multiplicities; near-unit roots |theta-1|<=1/(32m) with arbitrary collisions/Jordan depth r satisfy 2F<=3600 c^(-2m)(384m r^(3/2))^(2r) exp[-3(2^m-1)/(2m)] for every final b in[0,2]. This is o(1/B_n) for r=m, and even r<=n/(32m²). Includes the complete all-pair interpolation law and an actual full-rank NONQUADRATIC strongly convex realization with true initial distance1 and positive quantitative payoff. These are scoped unverified candidates, not the target.", "evidence": "# Growing Newton temporal spaces on the actual critical word\n\nStatus: complete draft candidates, UNVERIFIED. No solver, numerical evaluation,\ninstallation or fact_submit. Accepted facts 1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and\nnormalization) and b98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark and its elementary\nenvelope) are the only correctness inputs. Every new estimate is proved here.\nThese are obstructions for specified growing temporal spaces, not an upper\nbound for the unrestricted worst-case objective and not the original target.\n\n## 1. Actual word, queried prefix and a genuinely restricted growing space\n\nFix m>=128 and keep the following parameters fixed within that horizon:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, q=n-1.\n\nThe prescribed queried-prefix steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are H_m with its last\nentry d_0 removed. Queries are indexed 0,...,q; the last free step is b in\n[0,2], including the original b=d_0. Write\n\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), 0<=i<=q.\n\nChoose a REAL monic polynomial chi of degree r, with 1<=r<=q. For a\npolynomial p let [p] denote its remainder modulo chi. This is an r-dimensional\nreal vector space; use coefficients in 1,z,...,z^(r-1) as its real coordinates.\nLet T be ANY real linear map from this space into any finite-dimensional\nEuclidean space, with ||T[1]||=1. Put\n\n    x_i=T[P_i],       g_i=T[z P_i],     0<=i<=q.          (1)\n\nThe map, ambient dimension, metric, coefficients and polynomial can all depend\non m. Singular maps are allowed. Initial correlations are unrestricted. The\nidentity P_(i+1)=P_i-h_i zP_i proves the ACTUAL GD recursion for (1).\n\nAll queried values f_i remain joint variables. Assume that the data (1), those\nvalues and the optimum jet (0,0,0) belong to ONE convex differentiable function\nF with 1-Lipschitz gradient. Thus the initial distance to this designated\nminimizer is exactly one. All conclusions below hold for EVERY interpolant of\nthese jets; no terminal gradient identity is assumed after the last step b.\n\nThis is a fixed r-dimensional temporal space, even when the physical image\ndimension is larger. For r=m it retains m independent Newton coefficients.\nMore generally the theorem below permits a degree much larger than m but\nstrictly smaller than the full n+1-node representation of arbitrary data.\n\n## 2. The full computable all-pair law and function realization\n\nLet J be multiplication by z modulo chi, let v_0 be the coefficient vector of\n1, and define\n\n    v_(i+1)=(I-h_i J)v_i,    w_i=Jv_i,    M=T^T T.\n\nThen x_i=T v_i, g_i=T w_i, M is a REAL PSD matrix, and v_0^T M v_0=1.\nConversely every real PSD M has a real Euclidean factor T. With\nv_star=w_star=0 and f_star=0, the necessary and sufficient full interpolation\nlaw is, for EVERY distinct ordered pair i,j in {star,0,...,q},\n\n f_i-f_j+w_i^T M(v_j-v_i)\n                   +(w_i-w_j)^T M(w_i-w_j)/2 <=0.       (2)\n\nThis is exactly Q_ij<=0 in fact 1252b8823bb5a290, evaluated on the stated\nvectors. No pair or common value is replaced by a componentwise condition.\nFor feasible data that fact constructs an actual function explicitly:\n\n cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j,\n H(v)=max_j {cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2},\n F_max(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.\n\nThe maximum includes the optimum jet. The function is finite, convex and\n1-smooth, interpolates every queried value and gradient, and has minimum zero\nat zero. This provides a function realization, not just a Gram representation.\n\nFor a Newton basis with roots theta_1,...,theta_r, repetitions allowed,\nthe basis polynomials are N_k(z)=product_(ell=1)^k(z-theta_ell), 0<=k<r.\nMultiplication obeys zN_k=theta_(k+1)N_k+N_(k+1), with N_r=0 in the quotient.\nThus J is a bidiagonal Newton chain in that basis. For nonreal conjugate\nroots the original real monomial coordinates, or real and imaginary parts of\nthe Newton coordinates, give the same REAL law (2); a complex matrix is not\nbeing called a real PSD metric.\n\n## 3. Exact level resonance: a complete growing-span annihilation obstruction\n\nThe level d_j occurs 2^(m-j-1) times in H_m. In the retained prefix the\ncounts are\n\n    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1), 1<=j<m.              (3)\n\nLet mu_j=1/d_j. Suppose\n\n    chi(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(z-mu_j)^(r_j),\n    0<=r_j<=N_j,     1<=sum r_j<=q.                    (4)\n\nSince P_q(z)=product_j(1-d_j z)^(N_j), chi divides P_q. Thus x_q=g_q=0\nin (1), for EVERY map T and every common metric, regardless of its condition\nnumber or initial correlations. Every legal interpolant has\n\n                    2F(x_q-b g_q)=0                    (5)\n\nfor every real b. This includes one independent Newton direction at EVERY\nrecursive large-step level (r=m), as well as every growing multiplicity\npermitted by the actual counts (3). It also handles confluent derivatives:\nvanishing to order r_j, not merely vanishing once, is the exact requirement.\n\nOne can add a SINGLE zero root, chi=z product_j(z-mu_j)^(r_j), provided its\ndegree is <=q. Then chi divides zP_q, so g_q=0. The position need not be zero,\nbut legality says it is a minimizer of F; again (5) holds. Adding a second\nzero root is not covered: a nonzero constant terminal gradient may survive.\n\nFor the simple positive roots r_j=1, all factors have occurred by the first\ncentral top-level step (index 2^(m-1)-1). The model already reaches zero after\nthat step. This uses the actual chronology; (3) is the more general terminal\ncriterion for growing confluent blocks.\n\n## 4. Near-unit roots: quantitative obstruction with growing Jordan depth\n\nSuppose EVERY complex root theta of the real polynomial chi satisfies\n\n                         |theta-1|<=1/(32m).            (6)\n\nRoots may be arbitrarily close, repeated, nonreal, or depend on the horizon.\nThere is no bound on T or on a Jordan-chain condition number. For every legal\nprefix (1) and every b in [0,2],\n\n 2F(x_q-b g_q)\n <=3600 c^(-2m)(384m r^(3/2))^(2r)\n                              exp[-3n/(2m)].           (7)\n\nConsequently the ratio to the EXACT benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n tends uniformly\nto zero whenever\n\n             r log(384m r^(3/2))=o(n/m).                (8)\n\nIn particular r=m is covered. There is also an explicit larger range:\n\n 1<=r<=floor(n/(32m^2))  implies\n 2F(x_q-b g_q)<=3600 exp[-11n/(8m)].                    (9)\n\nThis closes an entire growing polynomial/Jordan space, not just a direct sum\nof individually legal modes. It does not cover a fixed-width disk about one,\nslow roots near zero, or arbitrary spectra.\n\n### 4.1 Elementary parameter and product estimates\n\nThe rational inequalities 12/5<lambda<17/7 and m>=128 give\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 3/8<a<1/2, 11/8<d_0<3/2.             (10)\n\nFor example c<2193/896<5/2 and\na>(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8. Also ac=1-1/m.\n\nLet mathcal_P_m(z) be the product for the COMPLETE word H_m. Its multiplicity\nformula gives\n\n mathcal_P_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)),\n |mathcal_P_m(1)|=(ac)^n c^(-m).                       (11)\n\nIndeed sum_j 2^(m-j-1)=n and sum_j j2^(m-j-1)=n-m, each proved by induction\nusing H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). For z=1+w with |w|=R=1/(16m),\n\n |1-d_j z| <= a c^j (1+4|w|),\n\nbecause d_j/(a c^j)=1+1/(a c^j)<11/3<4. Therefore 1+t<=exp(t) and\n1-1/m<=exp(-1/m) imply\n\n |mathcal_P_m(z)|<=c^(-m) exp[-3n/(4m)].               (12)\n\nOn that circle, |1-d_0 z|>=a-d_0R>1/3 by (10). Since the retained product\nis P_q=mathcal_P_m/(1-d_0z), put\n\n E_m=3 c^(-m) exp[-3n/(4m)].\n\nThen\n\n             |P_q(z)|<=E_m,  |zP_q(z)|<=2E_m          (13)\n\non the whole circle. The damping estimate is for complex z, not a real-axis\nestimate incorrectly differentiated across a growing Jordan block.\n\n### 4.2 Legal early queries bound every polynomial image, without conditioning\n\nWrite D_r=product_(t<r)(1+h_t). Fact 1252b8823bb5a290 gives\n||x_i||<=product_(t<i)(1+h_t)<=D_r for all 0<=i<r: this follows directly from\n||grad F(x)||<=||x|| and the actual recursion. All these queries are retained.\n\nThe polynomials P_0,...,P_(r-1) have successive degrees and nonzero leading\ncoefficients, so they form a basis of degree-<r polynomials. Moreover\n\n                zP_i=(P_i-P_(i+1))/h_i.                (14)\n\nEvery h_i>1. Thus multiplying a polynomial of degree <=r-2 by z increases\nthe l1 norm of its coefficients in the P-basis by at most a factor two.\nMultiplying instead by z-theta increases that norm by at most 2+|theta|<=4\nfor the roots in (6). Starting with P_0=1 proves\n\n                     ||T[N_k]||<=4^k D_r, k<r.        (15)\n\nIf N_k has complex coefficients, extend T complex linearly and use the usual\nHermitian norm. Its expansion in the REAL x_i still proves (15) by the\ntriangle inequality. No inverse Vandermonde or inverse root-separation factor\noccurs, and no bound on the common metric was imposed.\n\nIn the first r chronological steps, a level j occurs only if 2^j<=r. Since\nc<5/2<2^(3/2), h_t<=1+(1/2)r^(3/2) there. Hence\n\n                     D_r<=(3r^(3/2))^r.               (16)\n\nThis is deliberately a loose legal-trajectory bound. Its exponential cost\ndepends on the temporal degree r, not on the full horizon n.\n\n### 4.3 A separation-free Newton remainder bound, including repeated roots\n\nFor completeness the required contour identity is proved here. For any\nordered roots theta_1,...,theta_r inside the circle |z-1|=R, put\nN_k(t)=product_(ell<=k)(t-theta_ell). Direct telescoping gives\n\n 1/(z-t)=sum_(k=0)^(r-1) N_k(t)/N_(k+1)(z)\n                         +N_r(t)/[(z-t)N_r(z)].        (17)\n\nFor a polynomial p define\n\n C_k=(1/(2pi i)) integral_(|z-1|=R) p(z)/N_(k+1)(z) dz.\n\nMultiplying (17) by p(z) and integrating shows\np(t)-sum C_k N_k(t) is divisible by N_r(t)=chi(t): for t inside the circle,\nthe remainder term is chi(t) times an analytic function. Thus it and its\nrequired derivatives vanish at every root, with the specified multiplicities.\nA polynomial vanishes with those multiplicities iff it is divisible by chi,\nas follows by successive division by each linear factor. The polynomial sum\nhas degree<r, so it is exactly [p]. The integral identity for polynomials is\nalso obtainable by expanding in powers about the circle's center and\nintegrating each finite/geometric series; no node-separation hypothesis occurs.\n\nFor (6), each |z-theta_ell|>=1/(32m) on the circle. If |p(z)|<=E there,\n\n                       |C_k|<=2(32m)^k E.              (18)\n\nCombining (15), (18) and the geometric sum with 128m>=2 gives\n\n ||T[p]||<=2ED_r sum_(k<r)(128m)^k\n                        <=4ED_r(128m)^r.              (19)\n\nThis proof covers collisions of roots directly. The explicit root ordering\nneed not preserve real coefficients term by term, since the final remainder\nof a real polynomial by real chi is real.\n\nApply (19) to p=P_q and p=zP_q using (13) and (16). With\n\n K_mr=12 c^(-m)(384m r^(3/2))^r exp[-3n/(4m)],\n\nwe get ||x_q||<=K_mr and ||g_q||<=2K_mr. For 0<=b<=2 the actual final\nposition y=x_q-b g_q satisfies ||y||<=5K_mr. Since F has minimum zero at zero\nand is 1-smooth, 2F(y)<=||y||^2. This proves (7), including every possible\nterminal interpolation and the original last step d_0.\n\n### 4.4 Exact benchmark comparison and the explicit degree range\n\nWe retain the actual B_n from b98bb807d514e80d, rather than replacing the\ntarget by a guessed profile constant. The bound on the actual normalized\nratio is B_n times the right side of (7). The accepted elementary estimate\n\n             B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3),                 (20)\n\nis sufficient to show this exact ratio tends to zero under (8): its logarithm\ncontributes O(m), whereas n/m grows exponentially in m. No unverified C54\ncomparison is a premise, and no inference about a whole-schedule upper bound\nis made.\n\nFor r<=n/(32m^2), use r<n<2^m. The inequalities log384<6, logm<=m/16 for\nm>=128, 6<=m/16, and log2<3/4 give\n\n log(384m r^(3/2))<2m.\n\nFor logm<=m/16, the difference m/16-logm is positive at128 and has positive\nderivative for m>=128. Consequently the positive exponent in (7) is at most\n4mr<=n/(8m). Dropping c^(-2m)<=1 gives (9). Multiplying (9) by the exact B_n\nand then using (20) proves the asserted uniform negligible profile ratio.\n\n## 5. Actual nonquadratic, full-rank realizations in the growing class\n\nThe class in Section4 is not empty and is not restricted to quadratic data.\nHere is a finite, symbolic, quantitative construction for every m>=128 and\n2<=r<=q. This is a nonvacuity calibration, not a new strong lower witness.\n\nChoose a rational alpha in\n\n              (1-1/(64m), 1-1/(128m))                 (21)\n\nsuch that all P_i(alpha), 0<=i<=q, are nonzero and pairwise distinct. Such a\nchoice exists: each P_i is a nonzero polynomial, and P_i-P_j is nonzero for\ni!=j because their degrees differ. Only finitely many real roots are\nforbidden, whereas the interval contains infinitely many rational numbers.\nThis is a finite algebraic specification, not a request for numerical search.\n\nLet e_0,...,e_(r-1) be the orthonormal basis of R^r and let N_shift e_k=e_(k+1)\nfor k<r-1, N_shift e_(r-1)=0. For epsilon>0 set\n\n A_epsilon=alpha I+epsilon N_shift,\n X_i=P_i(A_epsilon)e_0, G_i=A_epsilon X_i,\n V_i=(alpha/2)||X_i||^2.                               (22)\n\nTake optimum data zero, and put nu=1/4. Define transformed jets\n\n G'_i=(G_i-nu X_i)/(1-nu),\n V'_i=(V_i-nu||X_i||^2/2)/(1-nu).                      (23)\n\nAt epsilon=0 these are the jets of the scalar quadratic of curvature\nalpha'=(alpha-nu)/(1-nu), lying strictly in (0,1), at points\nP_i(alpha)e_0. All points, including the optimum, are distinct. Thus EVERY\nordered residual is strictly negative:\n\n Q'_ij(0)=-alpha'(1-alpha')||X_i(0)-X_j(0)||^2/2<0.\n\nFor epsilon variable each Q'_ij(epsilon) is a real polynomial, since all\nquantities in (22)--(23) are polynomial. Define the exact finite constants\n\n delta=min_(i!=j)(-Q'_ij(0))>0,\n D=1+sum_(i!=j) sum_(k>=1) |coefficient_(epsilon^k) Q'_ij|,\n epsilon=min(1/2,delta/(2D)).                           (24)\n\nAll indices here include star and every query. The sums are finite and\nprovide a computable algebraic law, not a solver oracle. Since epsilon<=1,\n|Q'_ij(epsilon)-Q'_ij(0)|<=epsilon D<=delta/2. Thus all transformed ordered\ninequalities are strictly satisfied. Fact1252 constructs a convex 1-smooth\ninterpolant K of them. The single function\n\n                F(x)=(1-nu)K(x)+nu||x||^2/2           (25)\n\nis convex, 1-smooth and nu-strongly convex, has its UNIQUE minimizer at zero,\nand interpolates EVERY original (X_i,G_i,V_i). Its actual GD follows (22)\nwith the prescribed steps. X_0=e_0 has TRUE initial distance exactly one.\n\nThese data have the representation (1) with chi=(z-alpha)^r and\nT[p]=p(A_epsilon)e_0. The r vectors e_0,A_epsilon e_0,...,\nA_epsilon^(r-1)e_0 are linearly independent: in the standard basis their\ncoefficient matrix is triangular with nonzero diagonal epsilon^k. Thus T is\ninjective and its REAL common metric is positive definite. The first r\npositions X_0,...,X_(r-1) also form a basis, since their polynomials have\nsuccessive degrees and nonzero leading coefficients.\n\nNo quadratic function with a minimizer at zero can interpolate these\ngradients. Its Hessian would be symmetric and would have to equal\nA_epsilon on that basis, whereas A_epsilon is nonsymmetric for r>=2 and\nepsilon>0. Thus (25) is genuinely nonquadratic on the required jet data.\n\nThere is an exact positive terminal payoff, even uniformly over all real b.\nWrite p0=P_q(alpha)!=0 and p1=P'_q(alpha). The first two coordinates of X_q\nand G_q are respectively\n\n            (p0,epsilon p1),\n            (alpha p0,epsilon(alpha p1+p0)).\n\nTheir determinant is epsilon p0^2. Minimizing the squared distance from the\nfirst vector to the line spanned by the second gives, for every real b,\n\n 2F(X_q-b G_q)\n >=nu||X_q-b G_q||^2\n >=nu epsilon^2 p0^4 /\n                 [alpha^2 p0^2+epsilon^2(alpha p1+p0)^2] >0.  (26)\n\nThe line-distance identity follows by completing the scalar square in b;\nthe denominator is nonzero since alpha p0!=0. All quantities are fixed by\n(21)--(24) and the actual word. For degrees covered by Section4, (7) also\napplies, so this positive payoff is nevertheless asymptotically negligible\nagainst the exact B_n profile. It is not presented as an improved adversary.\n\n## 6. Source audit, scope and the remaining interface\n\nCarl de Boor, \"Divided Differences,\" Surveys in Approximation Theory1\n(2005),46--69, arXiv:math/0502036, Proposition7 identifies Newton truncation\nwith the Hermite remainder; Proposition25 gives the bidiagonal multiplication\nformula; equations(49)--(51) give its contour representation. The exact paper\nand these proofs were read. Polynomial, repeated-node and real-coordinate\ndefinitions match this application. Sections2 and4.3 re-prove the required\nfinite algebra rather than importing an unchecked numerical formula.\n\nDownloaded PDF: downloads/deboor_divided_differences.pdf.\nRelevant browser extraction: downloads/deboor_relevant_browser_extract.txt.\nThe local pdftotext command and existing Python PDF extractors were unavailable;\nno installation was attempted. Matlas returned no directly applicable theorem\nfor the two mechanism queries; browser search supplied the exact paper.\n\nThese drafts close two SPECIFIED growing-span classes. Exact reciprocal roots\nat every scale annihilate, while a near-unit cluster with growing Jordan depth\nis controlled uniformly over all common metrics by legal early positions.\nNeither proof extends by spectral radius alone to arbitrary metrics or to\nslow nodes of order1/S_m. In particular, adding a zero root of multiplicity\n>=2 to the resonant class leaves polynomial/constant-gradient suffix data and\nis genuinely outside the annihilation proof. A changed generator at each\nblock is also outside this common-quotient class.\n\nThe all-pair law (2) for unrestricted chi of degree n+1 would simply reproduce\nthe original finite interpolation problem. Nothing here treats that\ntheorem-strength interface as a solved mechanism. The exact all-schedule\nprofile target remains unresolved, and there is no new verified fact.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_growing_newton_reciprocal_zero_chain", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/growing_newton_temporal_obstructions.md", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified_no_routine_allocation", "source_context": {"authors": ["Carl de Boor"], "title": "Divided Differences", "arxiv": "math/0502036", "year": 2005, "locations": "Proposition7, Proposition25, equations49-51; exact polynomial identities reproved"}, "new_optimization_evaluations": 0, "new_verifier_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"chi_Newton": "Real monic degree-r polynomial defining the common temporal quotient R[z]/chi.", "r_Newton": "Dimension of the polynomial temporal quotient, allowed to grow with m.", "T_Newton": "Arbitrary real linear image of the quotient, normalized by ||T[1]||=1, with unrestricted common PSD metric.", "J_Newton": "Multiplication by z in the quotient; a bidiagonal matrix in a Newton basis.", "N_k_Newton": "Degree-k Newton basis polynomial product_(ell<=k)(z-theta_ell).", "E_m_Newton": "Contour bound 3 c^(-m) exp[-3(2^m-1)/(4m)] on the retained product.", "K_mr_Newton": "Vector bound12 c^(-m)(384m r^(3/2))^r exp[-3(2^m-1)/(4m)].", "D_r_Newton": "Elementary legal-trajectory growth bound product_(t<r)(1+h_t)."}}
{"id": "704f32e7f06cbf56", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:49:07.729914+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C55 enlarged-family obstruction (complete proof candidate, UNVERIFIED): the exact prescribed H2 three-boundary seed followed by arbitrary repeated-copy joins plus nonnegative balanced pairs (p,q-1) cannot certify a useful objective bound on the critical horizon-dependent word. Every full PSD objective certificate in this family, even with arbitrary relaxed q_cert, must satisfy q_cert>(2/5)^(2m) exp(2^(m-3)/m), hence q_cert>1 for m>=128 and q_cert tends to infinity. This is a certificate-family obstruction, not a legal adversary or a statement about unrestricted certificates/the actual schedule.", "evidence": "# C55: the prescribed three-boundary queried-pair recurrence is insufficient\n\nStatus: **complete scoped proof candidate, not verifier-accepted**. No\noptimization or verifier allocation is used. The predecessor\n`1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the interpolation convention and the requirement\nthat an objective certificate be positive semidefinite on all the original\nformal vectors. Everything specific to this recursive family is derived\nbelow. Prior C53/C55 findings are motivation, not mathematical premises.\n\nThe result covers precisely the H2 seed and the repeated-copy join with the\nadded balanced pair described below, including arbitrary horizon-dependent\njoin parameters and storage choices. It does not cover arbitrary seeds,\nindependent internal array corrections, or unrestricted certificates for the\nsame schedule. The test vectors are formal vectors, not a legal function.\n\n## 1. Statement and notation\n\nFix an integer m>=128 and define\n\n    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    d_j=a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nAll steps are predetermined and strictly positive; the bridges grow with\nthe recursion level. No upper bound on the steps is assumed. The claims\nbelow concern this explicit family, not arbitrary nonnegative schedules.\n\nFor formal gradients substitute the actual GD recursion with smoothness\none and optimum jet zero. For distinct samples define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    C_ij=-2 Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nThe accepted predecessor proves C_ij>=0 for legal smooth convex data.\nFormal vectors used to test a quadratic certificate need not be legal.\n\nAn array at level j has storage parameters u_j,v_j and star pattern\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=h_i              (i<n_j),\n    lambda_(star,n_j)=u_j,\n    lambda_(i,star)=0                (i<n_j),\n    lambda_(n_j,star)=s_j=S_j+u_j-v_j.                 (1)\n\nHere lambda_ik denotes a multiplier array on C_ik; the scalar lambda without\nindices denotes 1+sqrt(2). The full identity defines its remainder E_j:\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-u_j g_end||^2-2v_j f_end\n                   =sum lambda_ik C_ik+E_j,\n    Z_j=(u_j-1)^2+v_j,\n    z_j=sum_(i<n_j) h_i g_i+g_end.                    (2)\n\nStart from the exact H2 seed in Section 2, and for j=2,...,m-1 apply any\njoin in Section 3, with a balanced multiplier gamma_j>=0 on the queried\npair (p,q-1). The same child array is embedded on the left and multiplied\nby r_j>=0 on the right. All actual final multipliers must be nonnegative\nto give a certificate. One may use any real u_j,v_j, with v_m>0, and need\nnot require positive semidefinite child or local remainders.\n\nThen, if the complete objective matrix at level q_cert is PSD, necessarily\n\n    q_cert >= 1/k_m^2\n           > (2/5)^(2m) exp[2^(m-3)/m],              (3)\n\nwhere the signed numbers k_j are defined in Section 5. The last expression\ntends to infinity. In particular q_cert>1 for m>=128, so this family cannot\nsupply v_m>=M_m with E_m PSD, nor any useful bounded objective coefficient.\nThis is an obstruction for the enlarged C55 recurrence, not an upper or\nlower bound on the actual schedule's worst-case objective.\n\nThe proof retains every internal child square and every new pair term. Its\nkey change from C53 is that the last-query gradient is c times the terminal\ngradient, instead of equal to it.\n\n## 2. The exact seed, its signs, and its attained boundary form\n\nPut\n\n    b0=1+a, M=M_1=2+a, d=d_1=1-epsilon,\n    x=d/M, eta=x^2+2x-1, a0=(2-b0^2)/b0^2.\n\nThe one-step seed H1=(b0) has finite multipliers\n\n    lambda_01=M, lambda_10=1,\n\nstar multipliers lambda_(star,0)=b0, lambda_(star,1)=M, and no incoming\nstar multipliers. Set u_1=M, v_1=2M-1, hence Z_1=M^2. Direct expansion in\n(2) gives\n\n    E_1=(2-b0^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nTo obtain H2, use the join of Section 3 with gamma=0 and\n\n    A=1/x, B=2+x, r=3+x+1/x, t=1/x-2-x,\n    P=(1+M eta)/x, Q=M,\n    u_2=M_2=2M+d, v_2=2M_2-1.                        (4)\n\nHere 1<b0<sqrt(2), 3/8<x<sqrt(2)-1, and eta<0. For the upper bound on x,\nwith s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda, expansion gives\n\n    d-sM=-epsilon[1-2s^2/(1+epsilon)]<0.\n\nThe lower bound follows from d>=127/128 and M<5/2. Thus a0,t>0.\nMoreover eta>=-7/64, so 1+M eta>1-35/128>0. Consequently A,B,P,Q,r are\npositive. The physical star pattern has s_1=s_2=0, and the update described\nin Section 3 yields nonnegative actual star multipliers as well.\n\nFor the four gradients of H2 define\n\n    U=b0(g_0-g_1), V0=b0(g_2-g_3), D0=g_1-g_3.\n\nExpanding the complete join gives\n\n    E_2=a0||U||^2+(r a0+t)||V0||^2\n                         +M^2(-eta)||D0||^2.          (5)\n\nFor an explicit coefficient check, (4) gives H=-t(M+d), ell=-tM in (8)\nbelow. Substitute V=d g_1+b0 g_2+g_3, Z_1=M^2, Z_2=(2M+d)^2 into (8).\nThe mixed V0,D0 coefficient is A d-M=0; the D0 square coefficient is\nM^2(1-2x-x^2). The two seed remainders provide a0||U||^2+r a0||V0||^2.\nThe remaining term is t||V0||^2, establishing (5).\n\nChanging u_2,v_2 and only the two terminal star coefficients in (1) adds\n(M_2^2-Z_2)||g_3||^2 to (5). Thus the obstruction also permits these\nstorage changes at the seed. The finite seed coefficients remain fixed.\n\nWith (z,e,w)=(z_2,g_3,g_2), the boundary relation is\n\n    z-M_2 e=U+V0+(M+d)D0.\n\nAll three boundary vectors are independent. Set c0=M^2(-eta), l0=M+d.\nThe exact identity\n\n    a0||U||^2+c0||D0||^2\n      =a0 c0/(c0+a0 l0^2) ||U+l0 D0||^2\n           +||a0 l0 U-c0 D0||^2/(c0+a0 l0^2)\n\nhas its second square equal to zero for every prescribed value of\nU+l0 D0. Therefore the sharp, attained boundary form is\n\n    K_2(z,e,w)=A2||z-M_2 e-b0(w-e)||^2\n                         +B2||w-e||^2\n                         +(M_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2,\n    A2=[1/a0+(1+x)^2/(-eta)]^(-1),\n    B2=(r a0+t)b0^2.                                 (6)\n\nNo unattainability of boundary minimizers is assumed in what follows.\n\n## 3. The actual enlarged array and the full local identity\n\nAt a join write n=n_j, p=n, q=2n+1, S=S_j, M=M_j, d=d_j, b=1+d.\nChoose A,B,P,Q with\n\n    r=1+A+B, P-Q=b+(1-A)S.\n\nEmbed the old array on the left and r times that array on the right.\nFor each right queried index p<i<q add\n\n    A h_i to (p,i), B h_i to (q,i),\n    -(A+B)h_i to (star,i).\n\nAdd the six corrections\n\n    (p,q):P, (q,p):Q,\n    (p,star):-s_j, (q,star):s_(j+1)-r s_j,\n    (star,p):b-u_j, (star,q):u_(j+1)-r u_j.            (7)\n\nFinally add gamma>=0 to both (p,q-1) and (q-1,p). These are actual queried\nsamples. This balanced change preserves every function-value flow and\nevery star multiplier. It adds no incoming unqueried-terminal constraint.\n\nFor example A,B,P,Q>=0, u_j>=0 and s_j>=0 at the relevant levels suffice\nfor nonnegative actual arrays when the seed is nonnegative: right queried\nstar coefficients become (r-A-B)h_i=h_i; the two terminal star coefficients\nbecome b and u_(j+1); the two incoming terminal star coefficients become\nzero and s_(j+1). All other entries are copied or receive nonnegative\nadditions. The obstruction allows all real A,B,P,Q satisfying the flow\nidentity and r>=0, so it also covers choices whose signs are certified by\na more detailed inspection of the actual array.\n\nWrite g=g_p, e=g_q, w=g_(q-1), and define\n\n    t=A-B,\n    H=M+d-B(M-d)-tM,\n    ell=P-A,\n    V=x_p-x_q-g+e=d g+z_right.\n\nThe complete remainder identity is\n\n    E_next=E_left+r E_right\n       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g+ell e>\n       +(Z_j-rd^2)||g||^2+(rZ_j-Z_next)||e||^2\n       +2gamma<g-w,e+a w-V>.                          (8)\n\nHere a is the fixed parameter in b0=1+a, since every block ends with that\nsame step b0. To verify the new term, x_(q-1)=x_q+b0 w gives\n\n    -gamma(C_(p,q-1)+C_(q-1,p))\n       =2gamma[||g-w||^2-<g-w,x_p-x_(q-1)>]\n       =2gamma<g-w,e+a w-V>.\n\nFor completeness the old part of (8) can be checked before the pair is\nadded. Put D=x_p-x_q and W=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i=x_p-b g-x_q. The rank\ncorrection sums to\n\n    2AS f_p+2BS f_q-AS||g||^2-BS||e||^2\n                      -2<W,A(x_p-g)+B(x_q-e)>.\n\nThe value coefficient at p becomes\n\n    -2v_j+2AS+2(P-Q)-2s_j-2(b-u_j)\n            =2[(A-1)S+P-Q-b]=0.\n\nThe value coefficient at q becomes -2v_next using r=1+A+B and (1).\nAfter subtracting the correction from the new potential minus the two\nchild potentials, the quadratic coefficients are\n\n    D^2:t, (D,g):H-ell-t, (D,e):ell+t,\n    g^2:Z_j-rd^2+t-2H+2ell,\n    e^2:rZ_j-Z_next+t+2ell, (g,e):H-2ell-t,\n\nwhere a mixed coefficient multiplies twice the indicated inner product.\nSubstituting V=D-g+e proves the old part of (8). Thus (8) is an identity\non the full formal space, with every inherited remainder retained.\n\n## 4. Explicit eliminated matrix and every singular range condition\n\nThis section records the exact recurrence being obstructed, including\nits singular cases. Write an attained child boundary form as\n\n    K(z,e,w)=alpha||z||^2+2beta<z,e>+2chi<z,w>\n                        +delta||e||^2+2etaK<e,w>+phi||w||^2.\n\nIn (8) substitute E_left=K(L,g,u)+its internal remainder and\nE_right=K(R,e,w)+its internal remainder. The six-variable form has\nparent boundary z=L+d g+R. Eliminate X=(g,u,R) and retain Y=(z,e,w).\nAfter L=z-d g-R, its symmetric matrix is\n\n                         [ D_el  B_el ]\n                         [ B_el^T C_el].\n\nIts entries are exactly\n\n    D_el(1,1)=(alpha+t-r)d^2-2beta d+delta\n                                      +2d(H-ell-gamma)+Z_j,\n    D_el(1,2)=etaK-d chi,\n    D_el(1,3)=d(alpha+t)-beta+H-ell-gamma,\n    D_el(2,2)=phi,\n    D_el(2,3)=-chi,\n    D_el(3,3)=(1+r)alpha+t;\n\n    B_el=[ -d alpha+beta,  d ell+gamma, gamma(a+d);\n                 chi,          0,              0;\n               -alpha,     r beta+ell,       r chi+gamma ];\n\n    C_el=[ alpha,          0,                    0;\n              0, r delta+rZ_j-Z_next,  r etaK-gamma;\n              0,       r etaK-gamma,    r phi-2a gamma ].  (9)\n\nThe full six-variable matrix is PSD if and only if all of the following\nhold:\n\n    D_el is PSD;\n    B_el^T xi=0 for EVERY xi in ker(D_el);\n    C_el-B_el^T D_el^dagger B_el is PSD.                (10)\n\nThe middle condition is equivalently that every column of B_el belongs to\nthe range of D_el. The dagger is the inverse on its positive eigenspaces\nand zero on its kernel. To prove (10), first restrict to Y=0. A nonzero\ncross coefficient against a null vector of D_el makes the form unbounded\nbelow by scaling that null direction. Once these coefficients vanish,\ncomplete the square on the positive eigenspaces. This proves both\nnecessity and sufficiency, including every rank of D_el.\n\nWhen the children have exact attained decompositions with PSD internal\nremainders and r>=0, (10) is also equivalent to PSD of the entire parent\nremainder: the independent boundary data can be lifted while setting all\ninternal child squares to zero. The new sharp boundary form is the last\nSchur complement in (10). No such interpretation is needed for the\nobstruction below: it directly evaluates the original E_j and does not\nassume that any earlier elimination passed.\n\n## 5. A different formal seed survives every new queried pair\n\nDefine\n\n    k_2=M_2+6epsilon, theta=c.\n\nAt the H2 seed choose the following explicit four gradients, all multiples\nof an arbitrary vector e:\n\n    g_0=(2-c+6epsilon/b0)e,\n    g_1=e, g_2=c e, g_3=e.                            (11)\n\nThen the exact boundary is\n\n    z_2=k_2 e, g_end=e, g_last_query=c e.\n\nIndeed z_2=b0 g_0+(1+d_1)g_1+b0 g_2+g_3=(M_2+6epsilon)e.\nIn (5), D0=0, V0=b0(c-1)e, and U=[6epsilon-b0(c-1)]e.\nWe next prove the strict energy inequality\n\n    E_2 < (k_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2                         (12)\n\nfor e!=0, for every m>=128. This includes all internal seed squares.\n\nFirst, a>2/5. In fact sqrt(2)<10/7 gives lambda<17/7, so\n\n    a >= (127/129)(7/17)=889/2193 >2/5.\n\nAlso a<s=sqrt(2)-1, b0<sqrt(2), M>12/5 and c<5/2. To bound a0, use\n\n    b0=sqrt(2)-2s epsilon/(1+epsilon),\n    2-b0^2<4sqrt(2)s epsilon=4(2-sqrt(2))epsilon\n                                          <(12/5)epsilon,\n    b0^2>49/25.\n\nTherefore a0<(60/49)epsilon<(5/4)epsilon. Since 3/8<x<s<1/2,\n\n    r=3+x+1/x<37/6<25/4.\n\nFurthermore\n\n    s-x=epsilon[1-2s^2/(1+epsilon)]/M<(5/12)epsilon,\n    t=(s-x)(1+1/(sx))<(5/12)(23/3)epsilon<4epsilon.\n\nThe estimate on 1/(sx) uses s>2/5 and x>3/8. We have\n0<6epsilon<b0(c-1), because 6epsilon<=3/64 and b0,c-1>7/5. Hence\n\n    ||U||^2 <= b0^2(c-1)^2||e||^2,\n    b0^2(c-1)^2<2(3/2)^2=9/2.\n\nSubstitution into the FULL seed formula (5) gives\n\n    E_2-(M_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2\n       <=[(r+1)a0+t] b0^2(c-1)^2||e||^2\n       <[(29/4)(5/4)+4](9/2)epsilon||e||^2\n       =(1881/32)epsilon||e||^2\n       <60epsilon||e||^2.\n\nFinally M_2=5-epsilon+2a>5, so\n\n    k_2^2-M_2^2=12M_2 epsilon+36epsilon^2>60epsilon.\n\nThis proves (12). In particular the argument has not replaced the full\nseed by an unattainable boundary minimum or dropped a positive square.\n\n## 6. A uniform subcritical gap and its exact propagation\n\nSet\n\n    R0=k_2/(c d_2)=(M_2+6epsilon)/[(1-epsilon)c^2].\n\nWe have the explicit bound\n\n    0<R0<1-epsilon/4.                                (13)\n\nFor its proof expand the available gap using lambda^2=2lambda+1:\n\n    (c d_2-M_2)/epsilon\n      =[6sqrt(2)+epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2\n                                  -lambda^2 epsilon^3]/(1+epsilon).\n\nThis is greater than 8. Indeed after subtracting 8 and multiplying by\n1+epsilon, the numerator is\n\n    6sqrt(2)-8-7epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^3\n      >2/5-7/128-12/128^2-6/128^3>0.\n\nConsequently c d_2-k_2>2epsilon. Since c d_2=(1-epsilon)c^2<25/4,\n\n    1-R0>8epsilon/25>epsilon/4,\n\nas claimed.\n\nNow define signed numbers recursively by\n\n    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j, j>=2.                         (14)\n\nFor every block H_j there is a formal gradient assignment linear in its\nterminal vector e such that\n\n    z_j=k_j e, g_end=e, g_last_query=c e,\n    E_j <= (k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2.                        (15)\n\nThis holds simultaneously for every permitted choice of the join and\nstorage parameters, including choices depending on the target horizon.\nThe seed is (11)-(12). For the induction step put the right child on its\ntemplate with terminal e, and the left child on its template with terminal\n\n    g=-k_j e/d_j.\n\nThen V=d_j g+z_right=0, and the parent moment is\n\n    z_next=z_left+d_j g+z_right=k_j g=k_(j+1)e.\n\nIts last-query gradient remains c e. The crucial NEW pair term in (8) is\n\n    2gamma(-k_j/d_j-c)(1+a c)||e||^2.                 (16)\n\nIt is nonpositive at every level. At j=2, k_2>0 proves this directly. For\nj>=3, k_j<0; solving (14) gives\n\n    |k_j|=(1-epsilon)c^j R0^(2^(j-2)),\n    |k_j|/d_j=c R0^(2^(j-2))<c.                     (17)\n\nThus (16) is negative for gamma>0 and e!=0, since 1+a c=2-epsilon>0.\n\nThe other mixed local terms vanish because V=0. Since r>=0, the two\ncomplete child estimates in (15) retain their direction under addition.\nKeeping their full values and the nonpositive new pair term gives\n\n    E_next <= (k_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2\n                 +r(k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2\n                 +(Z_j-rd_j^2)||g||^2+(rZ_j-Z_next)||e||^2\n             = (k_j^4/d_j^2-Z_next)||e||^2,\n\nwhich is exactly (15) at the parent. No local PSD, nonsingularity, greedy\nstorage, or rank assumption was used.\n\nIn co-coercivity language the pair has\n\n    C_(p,q-1)+C_(q-1,p)\n          =2(2-epsilon)(c+k_j/d_j)||e||^2>=0.\n\nThis explains why a nonnegative weight on this actual pair cannot repair\nthis new formal template. The old C53 template had the opposite sign on\nsome such pairs; those are different formal vectors.\n\n## 7. The original initial-vector objective conversion\n\nLet u=u_m, v=v_m>0, Z=(u-1)^2+v and k=k_m. For the final array the full\nobjective certificate at any level q_cert is\n\n    M_cert=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n\n                     +sum_(i!=j) (2lambda_ji/v)Q_ij\n           =(q_cert-1/v)||x_0||^2\n                     +[||x_n-u g_n||^2+E_m]/v.        (18)\n\nThis follows directly from (2) and C_ij=-2Q_ji. In particular E_m PSD\nand nonnegative actual multipliers yield the bound q_cert=1/v. The\nnormalization is the project's 2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), by the\nscaling in predecessor `1252b8823bb5a290`.\n\nKeep x_0 independent of the formal template in (15), and set\nbeta0=k+u-1. Since z_m=k e,\n\n    x_n-u g_n=x_0-beta0 e.\n\nThe restriction of v M_cert is bounded ABOVE by the two-variable form\nwith matrix\n\n                  [ q_cert v    -beta0       ]\n                  [  -beta0    2k beta0-v    ].        (19)\n\nThe difference is a nonnegative multiple of ||e||^2, by (15). If the\noriginal M_cert is PSD, this upper matrix must therefore also be PSD.\nIts second diagonal is positive: if it were zero, PSD would force\nbeta0=0, making that diagonal -v<0. Its determinant now implies\n\n    q_cert >= beta0^2/[v(2k beta0-v)] >=1/k^2,\n\nwhere the last step is exactly\n\n    k^2 beta0^2-v(2k beta0-v)=(v-k beta0)^2>=0.\n\nThis includes arbitrary increases of q_cert and indefinite child or final\nremainders. It tests the original x_0 variable, not only the condensed\ngradient state.\n\nFrom (13),(17) and 1-x<=exp(-x),\n\n    |k_m|<c^m exp[-2^(m-4)/m].                        (20)\n\nThus (3) follows from c<5/2. The logarithm of its right side is\n2^(m-3)/m-2m log(5/2), which tends to infinity because 2^m/m^2 tends to\ninfinity. Its successive ratio is 2m^2/(m+1)^2>3/2 for m>=7.\nFor m>=128, (20) also gives |k_m|<1: log(5/2)<1 follows from\nexp(1)>1+1+1/2+1/6>5/2, and 2^(m-4)>m^2 for m>=128. The latter inequality\nholds at 128 and is preserved since 2m^2/(m+1)^2>1. Hence q_cert>1.\n\n## 8. Undeformed calibration and scope checks\n\nSet epsilon=0, so a=1/lambda, c=lambda. Then b0=sqrt(2), M_j=lambda^j,\nd_j=lambda^(j-1), a0=t=eta=0, and the physical seed E_2 is identically\nzero. At each higher join choose\n\n    A=B=lambda, r=lambda^2, t=H=ell=0,\n    P=lambda, Q=M_j,\n    u_j=(1+sqrt(4M_j^2-3))/2, v_j=u_j, Z_j=M_j^2.\n\nThe same storage choice can be made at H2 by its permitted terminal-star\nchange, preserving E_2=0. It has s_j=S_j>=0. The flow equation follows\nfrom M_j=lambda d_j, and (8) with gamma=0 gives E_next=0. Every actual\nmultiplier is nonnegative by Section 3. This is an exact valid undeformed\ncalibration of the full array and the objective conversion (18).\n\nIf one retains the physical H2 storage u_2=M_2,v_2=2M_2-1 instead, the\nseed still has Z_2=M_2^2; switching to the displayed u_j,v_j at the next\nlevel likewise changes only the two terminal star coefficients and is\nallowed. No calibration identity is being carried from a differently\nnormalized performance measure.\n\nAt epsilon=0 the obstruction template has k_2=M_2 and R0=1, so it yields\nonly q_cert>=lambda^(-2m), entirely compatible with the valid bound\n1/u_m of order lambda^(-m). The strict horizon-dependent gap in (13)\nis essential.\n\nThe hypothesis r>=0 is exactly the scaling restriction in the C55 state\nrecurrence, not an extra PSD assumption on its children. It is also\nforced by nonnegative final arrays in this prescribed construction. The\nseed has the positive coefficient lambda_01=M. At every larger level,\nthe first two queried samples retain this coefficient unchanged, because\nthey lie in the left child and none of the join corrections touches their\nordered pair. In a right child the corresponding ordered pair has\ncoefficient rM and is untouched by the parent's fan, sparse, and new-pair\ncorrections. In the final leftmost occurrence of any such join it remains\nuntouched by all higher joins. Thus a negative r at any level would leave\na negative actual coefficient in the final array. This also shows that\nthe proof covers arrays required to be nonnegative only at the final\nlevel. The case r=0 is already included in the energy induction.\n\nThe seed restriction matters: the result does not assert that every H2\narray has (5), or that every three-boundary seed is covered. A different\nseed has to be tested against the seed-energy and last-query conditions\n(12),(15). Arbitrary additional queried pairs can change (16); no claim\nabout those corrections is made here. Likewise the formal template need\nnot satisfy omitted interpolation constraints, so it is not an actual\nlower witness for H_m. Accepted terminal completion removes no queried\nprefix constraints and cannot justify dropping them.\n\nThis analysis makes no lower-bound claim for schedules with zero steps.\nThe accepted predecessor handles those schedules without identifying\nduplicated formal gradients. Any future unrestricted conclusion would\nstill have to retain both zero-step and arbitrarily-large-step cases.\n\nAll new mathematics in this file remains a proof candidate. The exact\nminimax/profile target remains unresolved, and the fact graph is unchanged.\n\n## 9. Reproducibility of the algebraic audit\n\nOne fixed rational substitution in the six-variable identity (9) was\nchecked by polarization of its 21 coefficients, and the four rational\nconstant margins in Sections 5-6 were checked exactly. These were tiny\narithmetic checks, not optimization or a numerical search. The displayed\nexpansions and inequalities are the proof; the checks are not premises.\n\nFor architecture context, the exact local text of Altschuler--Parrilo,\narXiv:2309.16530, Theorem 3.4 and its helper-lemma proof was reread. No\ntheorem from that paper is imported; its undeformed recursion motivated\nthe array, whose modified full identity is proved in Section 3.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "C55_three_boundary_balanced_pair", "plan_id": "C55_three_boundary_balanced_pair", "finding_ids": ["1f81b116bd1128ab", "2bb9e9dc51c6dcc6"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "research_path": "research/c55_three_boundary_pair_obstruction.md", "verification_status": "complete draft only; {"id": "367cf22cf1bd54cc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:50:43.134096+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Quantitative arbitrary-q queried-cut candidate: every nonnegative FULL objective certificate obeys an exact 3x3 PSD capacity inequality in h_j, actual incident/cross-cut flow, initial-distance coupling and target-position first moments. A full three-interval/two-cut matrix and exact singular rules are derived. An explicit h=(1,1),q=1/5 certificate refutes dropping the moments, while a supplied long-step family refutes nonnegative-flow closure under direct Schur elimination. The original exact all-schedule profile remains unresolved.", "evidence": "# Quantitative queried cuts of full objective certificates\n\nStatus: complete self-contained candidate calculations, NOT verifier-accepted.\nCurrent TASK/master_guidance closes routine verification and numerical search;\nneither a solver nor fact_submit is used. Every new calculation below belongs\nto this single proof package, not to a chain of unverified premises. The only\naccepted mathematical dependency is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for the exact\nnormalized problem, interpolation necessity, and attained full certificates.\n\n## 1. The unchanged target and the full certificate hypothesis\n\nFor a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), W_n(h) is the\nsupremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\ndimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer\nx_star, and x_0 != x_star, using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).\nThe exact benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, with the original\nphi/psi recursions in accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d`. The affirmative\ntarget remains: for every epsilon>0, all sufficiently large n and EVERY\nnonnegative length-n schedule have W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n. A negative\nanswer requires a fixed positive relative improvement along unbounded\nhorizons by actual full-objective upper bounds. Neither outcome is claimed.\n\nWork at smoothness one and let x_0,g_0,...,g_n be independent formal vectors.\nSet x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All finite f_i\nare independent formal scalars. Put I={star,0,...,n}, lambda_ii=0, and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nA supplied full certificate consists of a finite real q_cert and ACTUAL\nlambda_ij>=0 for which\n\n M=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij                 (1)\n\nhas every value coefficient zero and is PSD on the full original formal\nspace. In particular the finite-node flows obey\n\n out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n).                                         (2)\n\nNo optimum-source edge is forbidden. No support, symmetrizability,\npositive-definiteness, h_i<2, or Huber equality assumption is made.\nZero-step gradients remain independent.\n\nNecessity of Q_ij<=0 on legal instances (accepted1252b8823bb5a290) gives\nW_n(h)<=q_cert for any such certificate. Conversely, that SAME accepted\nfact supplies a full certificate at q_cert=W_n(h) for every nonnegative\nh, including empty/all-zero schedules, by positive-step attainment and\nexplicit zero-step lifting. Thus any universal necessary inequality proved\nbelow can be applied to at least one attained certificate at the actual W.\nAn inequality involving the multipliers of a particular supplied certificate\nis not by itself a multiplier-free lower bound on W.\n\nEvery feasible q_cert is positive. Setting all gradients to zero first\ngives q_cert>=0. Summing (2) yields\nsum_i lambda_(i,star)=2+sum_i lambda_(star,i)>=2. If q_cert=0, set all\nfinite gradients to the same scalar1. The coefficient of scalar x_0 in\nM is then -sum_i lambda_(i,star)<0, so M is unbounded below. This excludes0.\n\n## 2. A nontrivial queried-cut capacity inequality\n\nFix a queried index j<n, write b_cut=h_j, and let T_cut={j+1,...,n}.\nIts complement in I includes the optimum and indices0,...,j. Define\n\n s_tail=sum_(j<t<n)h_t,\n ell_tail(k)=sum_(j<t<k)h_t for k in T_cut,\n C_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)lambda_ik,\n C_out=sum_(i in T_cut,k outside T_cut)lambda_ik,\n F_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(j,k),\n B_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(k,j),\n I_cut=sum_(i!=j)lambda_(i,j),\n J_forward=sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_(j,k),\n J_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_ik,\n mu_query=lambda_(j,star),\n mu_tail=sum_(i in T_cut)lambda_(i,star).\n\nThese are actual array sums, including every remote edge and optimum edge.\nSumming (2) over T_cut gives C_out-C_in=2. Set\n\n A_cut=I_cut-b_cut F_cut,\n D_cut=1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in,\n K_cut=b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward.                       (3)\n\nThe following matrix is necessarily PSD:\n\n              [ q_cert       -mu_query/2       -mu_tail/2 ]\n T_query   =  [ -mu_query/2    A_cut             K_cut/2   ].    (4)\n              [ -mu_tail/2    K_cut/2            D_cut     ]\n\nProof. Evaluate (1) on the formal subspace\n\n x_0=z,  g_i=0 (i<j),  g_j=u,  g_i=v (i>j).\n\nAll value coefficients have already canceled, so they may be set to0.\nOnly finite-source edges to star contribute z; their total is\n-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>. The coefficient of ||u||^2 in the norm\nterms is (out_j+in_j)/2=I_cut, since j<n. The only additional diagonal\nterms are -h_j lambda_(j,k)||u||^2 for k>j. This gives A_cut.\n\nFor the ||v||^2 coefficient, set u=z=0. A tail point k then has\nx_k=-ell_tail(k)v, and a non-tail finite point has position0. Within-tail\nposition terms sum to\n\n sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k)(out_k^internal-in_k^internal).\n\nUse (2) to replace the internal divergence by\n2*1_(k=n)-out_k^cross+in_k^cross. The outgoing cross position terms\nadd back sum_k ell_tail(k)out_k^cross. This leaves\n2s_tail+J_in. The cross norm terms are\n(C_out+C_in)/2=1+C_in; internal norm terms are zero. Hence D_cut.\n\nFor the coefficient of <u,v>, norm terms give -(F_cut+B_cut).\nAn edge j->k with k in T_cut contributes -ell_tail(k)<u,v>, totaling\n-J_forward. An edge sourced in T_cut and targeting its complement\nhas the position shift +b_cut*u, totaling b_cut C_out<u,v>.\nAll other position terms have either zero source gradient or cancel\nthe common tail shift. These are exactly K_cut. Thus the restriction is\n\n q_cert||z||^2-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>\n                   +A_cut||u||^2+K_cut<u,v>+D_cut||v||^2,\n\nwhose scalar coefficient matrix is (4). Since the original polynomial\nis PSD on every formal vector, its restriction is PSD. This restriction\nis a necessary test, not an assertion that its formal vectors are legal\ntrajectories, and it deletes no edge from the original certificate.\n\nComplete the independent z square, using q_cert>0. Put\n\n a_cut=A_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert),\n d_cut=D_cut-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert),\n k_cut=K_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert).\n\nThen (4) is equivalent to\n\n a_cut>=0, d_cut>=0, k_cut^2<=4a_cut*d_cut.                     (5)\n\nIn particular, when d_cut>0 the quantitative incidence cost is\n\n I_cut >= b_cut F_cut + mu_query^2/(4q_cert)\n              + [b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward\n                       -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)]^2/(4d_cut). (6)\n\nWhen d_cut=0 the square numerator in (6) must be zero and the remaining\ncondition is I_cut>=b_cut F_cut+mu_query^2/(4q_cert). There is no division\nby a vanished residual and no strict-PSD assumption. Equivalently,\n\n |b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward\n                     -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)|\n       <=2 sqrt((I_cut-b_cut F_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert))\n                 *(1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert))).   (7)\n\nIf every edge incident to j vanishes, I_cut=F_cut=B_cut=J_forward=\nmu_query=0. Then (5) forces b_cut C_out=0. Since C_out>=2, b_cut=0.\nThis recovers the qualitative incidence obstruction, now as the zero-cost\ncase of an exact quantitative condition at every finite certificate level.\nIf b_cut=0 there is no contradiction; the zero-step case is included.\n\nFor an alternative exact q cost define\n\n Z_cut=[ A_cut K_cut/2 ; K_cut/2 D_cut ],\n m_cut=(mu_query,mu_tail)^T.\n\nCondition (4) is equivalent to Z_cut>=0, m_cut in Range(Z_cut), and\n\n q_cert >= (1/4) m_cut^T Z_cut^+ m_cut.                         (8)\n\nHere Z_cut^+ inverts its positive eigenvalues and vanishes on its kernel.\nFor proof, diagonalize the real symmetric Z_cut. A component of m_cut\non a zero eigenvector would give an unbounded linear polynomial in that\nfree direction. Once these components vanish, complete one square for\neach positive eigenvalue. This proves necessity and sufficiency, including\nall singular cases. If 4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2>0, the right side is explicitly\n\n (D_cut*mu_query^2-K_cut*mu_query*mu_tail+A_cut*mu_tail^2)\n                         /(4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2).             (9)\n\n## 3. Why total cut flow cannot replace its position moment\n\nHere is ONE exact supplied certificate. Take n=2, h=(1,1), q_cert=1/5,\nwith the following nonzero multipliers and every other multiplier zero:\n\n lambda_02=1, lambda_10=1, lambda_21=2,\n lambda_(1,star)=1, lambda_(2,star)=1.                          (10)\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative. The finite flows are respectively0,0,2.\nWith x_1=x_0-g_0 and x_2=x_0-g_0-g_1, direct expansion of (1) gives\n\n M=(1/5)||x_0-(5/2)(g_1+g_2)||^2\n                                      +(3/4)||g_1-g_2||^2.   (11)\n\nFor an explicit coefficient check, the sum of the position terms in (10)\nis -<x_0,g_1+g_2>-||g_0||^2+<g_0,g_1>+<g_0,g_2>\n+3<g_1,g_2>. The norm terms add ||g_0||^2+2||g_1||^2+2||g_2||^2\n-<g_0,g_1>-<g_0,g_2>-2<g_1,g_2>. Their sum is exactly the expansion\nof (11) after adding q_cert||x_0||^2. This is full PSD with the\noriginal independent g_0 present as a kernel direction.\n\nFor j=0, T_cut={1,2}, the exact data are\n\n b_cut=1, s_tail=1, C_in=1, C_out=3,\n F_cut=B_cut=I_cut=1, J_forward=J_in=1,\n mu_query=0, mu_tail=2,\n A_cut=0, D_cut=5, K_cut=0.\n\nThus (4) is the singular rank-one square (z-5v)^2/5, and (8) gives\nthe exact cost1/5. Dropping the target-position moment J_in would assert\n\n mu_tail^2 <= 4q_cert(1+2s_tail+C_in),                          (12)\n\nwhich here reads4<=16/5, false. Equivalently, that proposed scalar cost\nwould falsely require q_cert>=1/4. Dropping J_forward from K_cut likewise\nproduces K_cut=1 while A_cut=0, falsely rejecting the same full certificate.\nTherefore unweighted cross-cut flow cannot be substituted in these exact\ncapacity tests. The actual remote edge0->2, whose target lies one unit\nof step mass into the tail, supplies the missing moment. This refutes\n(12), not every conceivable scalar invariant or an existential re-selection.\n\n## 4. A general interval restriction and an explicit two-cut test\n\nPartition {0,...,n} into m nonempty chronological interval blocks\nB_0,...,B_(m-1), including n in the final block. Let\n\n H_r=sum_(i in B_r,i<n)h_i,\n ell_r(i)=sum_(t in B_r,t<i)h_t for i in B_r,\n Lambda_rs=sum_(i in B_r,j in B_s)lambda_ij for r!=s,\n Lambda_(r,star)=sum_(i in B_r)lambda_(i,star)=mu_r,\n Lambda_(star,r)=sum_(i in B_r)lambda_(star,i),\n E_rs=sum_(i in B_r,j in B_s)ell_s(j)lambda_ij for r!=s,\n E_(star,r)=sum_(j in B_r)ell_r(j)lambda_(star,j),\n in_r^cross=sum_(a!=r)Lambda_ar,\n\nwhere the last sum includes a=star. E is a target-position FIRST MOMENT,\nnot an arbitrary error term. Only off-diagonal block data are needed.\nTemporarily write E_rr for the analogous internal sum during the proof.\nSet every g_i=u_r on B_r, while leaving x_0=z independent.\n\nThe complete restricted polynomial is\n\n q_cert||z||^2-sum_r mu_r<z,u_r>\n             +sum_r D_r^blk||u_r||^2+sum_(r<s)K_rs^blk<u_r,u_s>,  (13)\n\nwhere\n\n D_r^blk=(1+2H_r)*1_(r=m-1)\n       +sum_(a!=r)(Lambda_ar+E_ar)-H_r sum_(s>r)Lambda_rs,     (14)\n\n K_rs^blk=H_r(mu_s+sum_(t<=r)Lambda_st)\n       -H_s sum_(t>s)Lambda_rt\n       -Lambda_rs-Lambda_sr-E_rs-E_sr       (r<s).             (15)\n\nThe sums in (15) have only finite block indices; all appearances of the\noptimum in its position term are in mu_s. These identities hold even\nfor arbitrary flow arrays before imposing PSD, and every original edge\nis included through these exact sums.\n\nProof. Write p_i=sum_(a<r)H_a u_a+ell_r(i)u_r for i in B_r, so\nx_i=z-p_i. Position terms equal\n\n -sum_r mu_r<z,u_r>\n +sum_r sum_(i in B_r)out_i<u_r,p_i>\n -sum_(r,s finite)sum_(i in B_r,j in B_s)lambda_ij<u_r,p_j>.\n\nFor ||u_r||^2 the local part of the first sum is\nsum_(i in B_r)ell_r(i)out_i. By node flow (2) this equals\nsum_a E_ar+2H_r*1_(r=m-1), with a including r and star. The second\nsum subtracts E_rr and H_r sum_(s>r)Lambda_rs. The cross norm coefficient\nis (out_r^cross+in_r^cross)/2=in_r^cross+1_(r=m-1). Combining proves(14).\n\nFor r<s, sources in B_r contribute\n-H_s sum_(t>s)Lambda_rt-E_rs to the coefficient of <u_r,u_s>.\nSources in B_s contribute\nH_r(out_s-sum_(t>r)Lambda_st)-E_sr\n=H_r(mu_s+sum_(t<=r)Lambda_st)-E_sr.\nThe norm terms contribute -Lambda_rs-Lambda_sr. Their sum is(15).\n\nLet Z_blk be the m by m matrix with diagonal D_r^blk and off-diagonal\nK_rs^blk/2, and let mu_blk=(mu_0,...,mu_(m-1))^T. Necessarily\n\n Z_blk>=0, mu_blk in Range(Z_blk),\n q_cert >= (1/4)mu_blk^T Z_blk^+ mu_blk.                       (16)\n\nThis follows from the same elementary square completion proved for(8).\nIt is EXACT for the chosen restricted subspace, hence only necessary for\nthe full certificate unless every block is a singleton.\n\nFor a concrete TWO-CUT statement take B_0={0,...,k-1},\nB_1={k,...,l-1}, B_2={l,...,n}, where1<=k<l<=n. Then\n\n D_0^blk=sum_(a!=0)(Lambda_a0+E_a0)-H_0(Lambda_01+Lambda_02),\n D_1^blk=sum_(a!=1)(Lambda_a1+E_a1)-H_1 Lambda_12,\n D_2^blk=1+2H_2+sum_(a!=2)(Lambda_a2+E_a2),\n K_01^blk=H_0(mu_1+Lambda_10)-H_1 Lambda_02\n                              -Lambda_01-Lambda_10-E_01-E_10,\n K_02^blk=H_0(mu_2+Lambda_20)-Lambda_02-Lambda_20-E_02-E_20,\n K_12^blk=H_1(mu_2+Lambda_20+Lambda_21)\n                              -Lambda_12-Lambda_21-E_12-E_21. (17)\n\nThe diagonal sums include star. Define\nd_r=D_r^blk-mu_r^2/(4q_cert) and\nc_rs=K_rs^blk/2-mu_r*mu_s/(4q_cert).\nFull PSD implies all d_r>=0, c_rs^2<=d_r*d_s, and the additional\ncoupled two-cut condition\n\n d_0 d_1 d_2+2c_01 c_02 c_12\n              -d_0 c_12^2-d_1 c_02^2-d_2 c_01^2>=0.          (18)\n\nConversely these principal-minor conditions make the restricted3 by3\nSchur matrix PSD. To justify the singular cases without a theorem black\nbox: if some d_r=0, its two pair tests force both adjacent c entries0\nand the remaining2 by2 criterion suffices. If all d_r>0, eliminate the\nfirst diagonal; the remaining2 by2 matrix has nonnegative diagonal from\nthe pair tests and determinant det(original)/d_0>=0. Square completion\nthen proves PSD. Thus(18) retains the simultaneous two-cut correlation,\nincluding degenerate kernels, rather than checking two separate cuts.\n\nAs a normalization check, the ONE-BLOCK case of(14) has S=sum h_i,\nnu_i=lambda_(star,i), nu=sum_i nu_i, mu=2+nu, and\n\n D=1+2S+sum_i(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)nu_i\n                  <=1+2S+(1+S)nu.\n\nConsequently q_cert>=mu^2/(4D)>=1/(1+2S). The last inequality is exactly\n\n (1+2S)(2+nu)^2-4(1+2S+(1+S)nu)\n                      =(1+2S)nu^2+4S nu>=0.\n\nThis recovers the familiar Huber bound without incorrectly suppressing\noptimum-source edges at q_cert>Huber. The empty horizon is m=1,H_0=0.\n\n## 5. What does and does not close under another cut\n\nThere IS an exact matrix update with explicitly retained boundary data.\nMerge neighboring blocks r,s=r+1 into B. For an outside source block a,\n\n H_B=H_r+H_s, mu_B=mu_r+mu_s,\n Lambda_aB=Lambda_ar+Lambda_as,\n E_aB=E_ar+E_as+H_r Lambda_as,\n Lambda_Ba=Lambda_ra+Lambda_sa,\n E_Ba=E_ra+E_sa.                                              (19)\n\nThe formulas for a=star use the same target moment. They follow by\nshifting every target in B_s by H_r in its new local elapsed coordinate.\nAlgebraically merging means setting u_r=u_s in(13), so\nD_B^blk=D_r^blk+D_s^blk+K_rs^blk and\nK_Ba^blk=K_ra^blk+K_sa^blk, with symmetric interpretation of K.\nThe matrix is a congruence of the finer matrix. A further split requires\nthe actual finer Lambda and E data; it cannot be recovered from(19) by\nknowing only aggregate totals. Conditions become no weaker under refinement\nbecause the tested formal subspace increases.\n\nOne may instead eliminate a block variable by minimizing(13). If D_r^blk>0,\nthe exact Schur update on the other blocks is\n\n q_new=q_cert-mu_r^2/(4D_r^blk),\n mu_s,new=mu_s-mu_r K_rs^blk/(2D_r^blk),\n D_s,new=D_s^blk-(K_rs^blk)^2/(4D_r^blk),\n K_st,new=K_st^blk-K_rs^blk K_rt^blk/(2D_r^blk).               (20)\n\nIf D_r^blk=0, PSD requires mu_r=0 and every K_rs^blk=0, so this is a\nzero row and can be removed without division. These claims follow by\ncompleting D_r^blk||u_r-[mu_r z-sum_(s!=r)K_rs^blk u_s]/(2D_r^blk)||^2.\nThey preserve PSD as matrix statements. They need not preserve nonnegative\noptimum flow or the normalized full-objective certificate class.\n\nHere is an exact supplied FAMILY showing that failure. For any b_long>2,\ntake n=1,h_0=b_long,\n\n q_cert=b_long^2-2b_long+2,\n lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(star,0)=2b_long^2,\n lambda_(1,star)=2,\n\nand every other coefficient0. The flows are0,2 and every coefficient is\nnonnegative. Its complete full-space identity is\n\n M=2b_long^2||g_0-x_0/2+g_1/(2b_long)||^2\n                              +(1/2)||g_1+(b_long-2)x_0||^2. (21)\n\nExpanding gives q_cert||x_0||^2-2b_long^2<x_0,g_0>-2<x_0,g_1>\n+2b_long^2||g_0||^2+2b_long<g_0,g_1>+||g_1||^2, which is exactly(1)\nfor the stated multipliers. Thus there is no feasibility or duality\nassumption hidden in this example. Eliminating g_0 yields the last square\nin(21), whose effective optimum coefficient is\n\n mu_1,new=2-b_long<0.                                         (22)\n\nIn a genuine full certificate on the remaining vectors, the coefficient\nof -<x_0,g_1> must be the nonnegative actual lambda_(1,star).\nThus this Schur elimination does NOT close in that cone. It also changes\nthe initial-distance coefficient to (b_long-2)^2/2. A purported exact\nconstant-preserving step cannot discard these changes. The example refutes\nautomatic direct Schur heredity, not the possibility of another nonnegative\nre-selection at the same original q_cert.\n\nThe interval state (H,Lambda,E,mu,Z_blk) therefore closes algebraically\nunder coarsening or refinement WHEN the actual finer data are retained.\nIt is not a fixed-dimensional scalar capacity state. At singleton blocks\nell_r=0, the substitution is the identity on all original gradients; then\n(16), together with nonnegative multipliers and flow, is exactly the\noriginal full-certificate feasibility problem. Merely refining all the way\nto that matrix is a change of coordinates, not a solution of the profile.\n\n## 6. Precise implication for W and the remaining obstacle\n\nFor a fixed partition, or a chosen collection of partitions, define L_test(h)\nas the infimum of q>0 over all finite nonnegative arrays with the original\nflow(2) that pass the corresponding exact restricted matrices. Every true\nfull certificate is feasible for that relaxation. In particular the attained\ncertificate at q=W_n(h), supplied by accepted1252b8823bb5a290 including\nall zero steps, is feasible. Therefore\n\n                         W_n(h)>=L_test(h).                  (23)\n\nThis direction is justified by that explicit accepted attainment theorem,\nnot by an unspecified conic strong-duality slogan. No attainment of the\nrelaxed infimum is asserted or needed. A lower bound on q for just one\nselected array would not prove(23) with that particular array's value.\n\nTo prove the affirmative target using these tests one must still establish,\nfor every epsilon>0 and all sufficiently large n, uniformly in h>=0,\nthat their feasible flow/moment states cost at least(1-epsilon)/B_n.\nNeither(6), (18), nor(19) proves that estimate. A bounded-state separator\ndominated by the exact primitive/dominant F/G recursions has not been found.\nDropping moments is disproved by(10); direct Schur cone closure is disproved\nby(21). Retaining the entire singleton state leaves a theorem equivalent to\nthe original minimax comparison. That closure route is BLOCKED, not routine.\n\nDeliverable of this round: a quantitative single queried-cut necessity,\nan explicit simultaneous two-cut determinant with all actual moments,\ntheir exact singular/zero-step rules, and two scoped supplied-certificate\nobstructions to unjustified scalar closure. The all-horizon target remains\nunresolved; no new accepted fact or actual asymptotic match is claimed.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id":
