{"id": "a02dacdfa7402edf", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:55:28.808016+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Exact one-cut moment relaxation: the valid scalar tail cost is q>=mu_tail²/[4(1+2s_tail+(1+s_tail)C_in)], attained by the explicit h=(1,1),q=1/5 full certificate. The coupled query constraint admits a closed interval-distance condition after relaxing both target first moments. This explicitly pays the mass loss and does not assert simultaneous realizability or F/G closure across cuts.", "evidence": "# Quantitative queried cuts of full objective certificates\n\nStatus: complete self-contained candidate calculations, NOT verifier-accepted.\nCurrent TASK/master_guidance closes routine verification and numerical search;\nneither a solver nor fact_submit is used. Every new calculation below belongs\nto this single proof package, not to a chain of unverified premises. The only\naccepted mathematical dependency is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for the exact\nnormalized problem, interpolation necessity, and attained full certificates.\n\n## 1. The unchanged target and the full certificate hypothesis\n\nFor a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), W_n(h) is the\nsupremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\ndimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer\nx_star, and x_0 != x_star, using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).\nThe exact benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, with the original\nphi/psi recursions in accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d`. The affirmative\ntarget remains: for every epsilon>0, all sufficiently large n and EVERY\nnonnegative length-n schedule have W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n. A negative\nanswer requires a fixed positive relative improvement along unbounded\nhorizons by actual full-objective upper bounds. Neither outcome is claimed.\n\nWork at smoothness one and let x_0,g_0,...,g_n be independent formal vectors.\nSet x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All finite f_i\nare independent formal scalars. Put I={star,0,...,n}, lambda_ii=0, and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nA supplied full certificate consists of a finite real q_cert and ACTUAL\nlambda_ij>=0 for which\n\n M=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij                 (1)\n\nhas every value coefficient zero and is PSD on the full original formal\nspace. In particular the finite-node flows obey\n\n out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n).                                         (2)\n\nNo optimum-source edge is forbidden. No support, symmetrizability,\npositive-definiteness, h_i<2, or Huber equality assumption is made.\nZero-step gradients remain independent.\n\nNecessity of Q_ij<=0 on legal instances (accepted1252b8823bb5a290) gives\nW_n(h)<=q_cert for any such certificate. Conversely, that SAME accepted\nfact supplies a full certificate at q_cert=W_n(h) for every nonnegative\nh, including empty/all-zero schedules, by positive-step attainment and\nexplicit zero-step lifting. Thus any universal necessary inequality proved\nbelow can be applied to at least one attained certificate at the actual W.\nAn inequality involving the multipliers of a particular supplied certificate\nis not by itself a multiplier-free lower bound on W.\n\nEvery feasible q_cert is positive. Setting all gradients to zero first\ngives q_cert>=0. Summing (2) yields\nsum_i lambda_(i,star)=2+sum_i lambda_(star,i)>=2. If q_cert=0, set all\nfinite gradients to the same scalar1. The coefficient of scalar x_0 in\nM is then -sum_i lambda_(i,star)<0, so M is unbounded below. This excludes0.\n\n## 2. A nontrivial queried-cut capacity inequality\n\nFix a queried index j<n, write b_cut=h_j, and let T_cut={j+1,...,n}.\nIts complement in I includes the optimum and indices0,...,j. Define\n\n s_tail=sum_(j<t<n)h_t,\n ell_tail(k)=sum_(j<t<k)h_t for k in T_cut,\n C_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)lambda_ik,\n C_out=sum_(i in T_cut,k outside T_cut)lambda_ik,\n F_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(j,k),\n B_cut=sum_(k in T_cut)lambda_(k,j),\n I_cut=sum_(i!=j)lambda_(i,j),\n J_forward=sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_(j,k),\n J_in=sum_(i outside T_cut,k in T_cut)ell_tail(k) lambda_ik,\n mu_query=lambda_(j,star),\n mu_tail=sum_(i in T_cut)lambda_(i,star).\n\nThese are actual array sums, including every remote edge and optimum edge.\nSumming (2) over T_cut gives C_out-C_in=2. Set\n\n A_cut=I_cut-b_cut F_cut,\n D_cut=1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in,\n K_cut=b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward.                       (3)\n\nThe following matrix is necessarily PSD:\n\n              [ q_cert       -mu_query/2       -mu_tail/2 ]\n T_query   =  [ -mu_query/2    A_cut             K_cut/2   ].    (4)\n              [ -mu_tail/2    K_cut/2            D_cut     ]\n\nProof. Evaluate (1) on the formal subspace\n\n x_0=z,  g_i=0 (i<j),  g_j=u,  g_i=v (i>j).\n\nAll value coefficients have already canceled, so they may be set to0.\nOnly finite-source edges to star contribute z; their total is\n-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>. The coefficient of ||u||^2 in the norm\nterms is (out_j+in_j)/2=I_cut, since j<n. The only additional diagonal\nterms are -h_j lambda_(j,k)||u||^2 for k>j. This gives A_cut.\n\nFor the ||v||^2 coefficient, set u=z=0. A tail point k then has\nx_k=-ell_tail(k)v, and a non-tail finite point has position0. Within-tail\nposition terms sum to\n\n sum_(k in T_cut)ell_tail(k)(out_k^internal-in_k^internal).\n\nUse (2) to replace the internal divergence by\n2*1_(k=n)-out_k^cross+in_k^cross. The outgoing cross position terms\nadd back sum_k ell_tail(k)out_k^cross. This leaves\n2s_tail+J_in. The cross norm terms are\n(C_out+C_in)/2=1+C_in; internal norm terms are zero. Hence D_cut.\n\nFor the coefficient of <u,v>, norm terms give -(F_cut+B_cut).\nAn edge j->k with k in T_cut contributes -ell_tail(k)<u,v>, totaling\n-J_forward. An edge sourced in T_cut and targeting its complement\nhas the position shift +b_cut*u, totaling b_cut C_out<u,v>.\nAll other position terms have either zero source gradient or cancel\nthe common tail shift. These are exactly K_cut. Thus the restriction is\n\n q_cert||z||^2-<z,mu_query*u+mu_tail*v>\n                   +A_cut||u||^2+K_cut<u,v>+D_cut||v||^2,\n\nwhose scalar coefficient matrix is (4). Since the original polynomial\nis PSD on every formal vector, its restriction is PSD. This restriction\nis a necessary test, not an assertion that its formal vectors are legal\ntrajectories, and it deletes no edge from the original certificate.\n\nComplete the independent z square, using q_cert>0. Put\n\n a_cut=A_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert),\n d_cut=D_cut-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert),\n k_cut=K_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert).\n\nThen (4) is equivalent to\n\n a_cut>=0, d_cut>=0, k_cut^2<=4a_cut*d_cut.                     (5)\n\nIn particular, when d_cut>0 the quantitative incidence cost is\n\n I_cut >= b_cut F_cut + mu_query^2/(4q_cert)\n              + [b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward\n                       -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)]^2/(4d_cut). (6)\n\nWhen d_cut=0 the square numerator in (6) must be zero and the remaining\ncondition is I_cut>=b_cut F_cut+mu_query^2/(4q_cert). There is no division\nby a vanished residual and no strict-PSD assumption. Equivalently,\n\n |b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-J_forward\n                     -mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert)|\n       <=2 sqrt((I_cut-b_cut F_cut-mu_query^2/(4q_cert))\n                 *(1+2s_tail+C_in+J_in-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert))).   (7)\n\nIf every edge incident to j vanishes, I_cut=F_cut=B_cut=J_forward=\nmu_query=0. Then (5) forces b_cut C_out=0. Since C_out>=2, b_cut=0.\nThis recovers the qualitative incidence obstruction, now as the zero-cost\ncase of an exact quantitative condition at every finite certificate level.\nIf b_cut=0 there is no contradiction; the zero-step case is included.\n\nFor an alternative exact q cost define\n\n Z_cut=[ A_cut K_cut/2 ; K_cut/2 D_cut ],\n m_cut=(mu_query,mu_tail)^T.\n\nCondition (4) is equivalent to Z_cut>=0, m_cut in Range(Z_cut), and\n\n q_cert >= (1/4) m_cut^T Z_cut^+ m_cut.                         (8)\n\nHere Z_cut^+ inverts its positive eigenvalues and vanishes on its kernel.\nFor proof, diagonalize the real symmetric Z_cut. A component of m_cut\non a zero eigenvector would give an unbounded linear polynomial in that\nfree direction. Once these components vanish, complete one square for\neach positive eigenvalue. This proves necessity and sufficiency, including\nall singular cases. If 4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2>0, the right side is explicitly\n\n (D_cut*mu_query^2-K_cut*mu_query*mu_tail+A_cut*mu_tail^2)\n                         /(4A_cut D_cut-K_cut^2).             (9)\n\n## 3. Why total cut flow cannot replace its position moment\n\nHere is ONE exact supplied certificate. Take n=2, h=(1,1), q_cert=1/5,\nwith the following nonzero multipliers and every other multiplier zero:\n\n lambda_02=1, lambda_10=1, lambda_21=2,\n lambda_(1,star)=1, lambda_(2,star)=1.                          (10)\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative. The finite flows are respectively0,0,2.\nWith x_1=x_0-g_0 and x_2=x_0-g_0-g_1, direct expansion of (1) gives\n\n M=(1/5)||x_0-(5/2)(g_1+g_2)||^2\n                                      +(3/4)||g_1-g_2||^2.   (11)\n\nFor an explicit coefficient check, the sum of the position terms in (10)\nis -<x_0,g_1+g_2>-||g_0||^2+<g_0,g_1>+<g_0,g_2>\n+3<g_1,g_2>. The norm terms add ||g_0||^2+2||g_1||^2+2||g_2||^2\n-<g_0,g_1>-<g_0,g_2>-2<g_1,g_2>. Their sum is exactly the expansion\nof (11) after adding q_cert||x_0||^2. This is full PSD with the\noriginal independent g_0 present as a kernel direction.\n\nFor j=0, T_cut={1,2}, the exact data are\n\n b_cut=1, s_tail=1, C_in=1, C_out=3,\n F_cut=B_cut=I_cut=1, J_forward=J_in=1,\n mu_query=0, mu_tail=2,\n A_cut=0, D_cut=5, K_cut=0.\n\nThus (4) is the singular rank-one square (z-5v)^2/5, and (8) gives\nthe exact cost1/5. Dropping the target-position moment J_in would assert\n\n mu_tail^2 <= 4q_cert(1+2s_tail+C_in),                          (12)\n\nwhich here reads4<=16/5, false. Equivalently, that proposed scalar cost\nwould falsely require q_cert>=1/4. Dropping J_forward from K_cut likewise\nproduces K_cut=1 while A_cut=0, falsely rejecting the same full certificate.\nTherefore unweighted cross-cut flow cannot be substituted in these exact\ncapacity tests. The actual remote edge0->2, whose target lies one unit\nof step mass into the tail, supplies the missing moment. This refutes\n(12), not every conceivable scalar invariant or an existential re-selection.\n\n\n## 7. The correct mass-only relaxation, with its explicit loss\n\nOne CAN remove the actual first moments at a single cut if their loss is\npaid explicitly. Nonnegative h and lambda imply\n\n 0<=J_forward<=s_tail F_cut,\n 0<=J_in-J_forward<=s_tail(C_in-F_cut).\n\nIn particular the valid scalar tail bound is\n\n q_cert>=mu_tail^2/[4(1+2s_tail+(1+s_tail)C_in)].               (24)\n\nProof: in(4), the z,v principal minor gives mu_tail^2<=4q_cert D_cut,\nand D_cut<=1+2s_tail+(1+s_tail)C_in. The supplied certificate(10) has\nequality in(24). Thus(24), which pays the full possible elapsed mass,\nsurvives that example whereas the unweighted replacement(12) does not.\n\nThere is also a closed elimination of the TWO moment bounds from the\ncoupled query test. Write a=a_cut from(5) only within this paragraph, and\ndefine the scalar data\n\n R_mom=b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert),\n E_mom=1+2s_tail+C_in+s_tail(C_in-F_cut)-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert),\n U_mom=s_tail F_cut.\n\nEvery true certificate necessarily satisfies\n\n a>=0, E_mom+R_mom+a>=0,\n dist(R_mom+2a,[0,U_mom])^2<=4a(E_mom+R_mom+a).              (25)\n\nHere dist denotes the ordinary distance of a real number from the stated\nclosed interval. For proof put t=J_forward. Then0<=t<=U_mom and\nd_cut<=E_mom+t. Equation(5) gives\n\n (R_mom-t)^2<=4a(E_mom+t), E_mom+t>=0.\n\nCompleting the scalar square gives\n(t-(R_mom+2a))^2<=4a(E_mom+R_mom+a). For a>0, this is possible\nfor t in the interval exactly when the last two conditions in(25) hold;\nthe square inequality itself forces E_mom+t>=0. For a=0 the same\nconditions require t=R_mom in[0,U_mom] and E_mom+R_mom>=0, which is\nthe correct zero-residual rule. This proves(25) including degeneracy.\n\nConversely(25) is sufficient for the ABSTRACT moment relaxation of(5):\nchoose such a t and set J_forward=t, J_in=t+s_tail(C_in-F_cut).\nThese values satisfy the two displayed interval bounds and yield(5).\nNo claim is made that those abstract moments arise from actual target\nindices or a common array across several cuts. It is precisely that\ncross-cut realizability/correlation information that(25) relaxes.\nThe scalar relaxation is therefore a genuine necessary inequality,\nnot a constant-preserving F/G separator or an exact iterative closure.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["367cf22cf1bd54cc", "1c968f5b991dd461"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "local_memory/quantitative_query_cut_capacity.md", "status": "complete_candidate_unverified", "scope": "single-cut scalar moment relaxation only", "justification_type": "calculation", "is_fragile": false, "fragility_reason": "", "suggested_followup": "none", "new_solver_calls": 0, "new_fact_submit_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"R_mom": "b_cut C_out-F_cut-B_cut-mu_query*mu_tail/(2q_cert).", "E_mom": "1+2s_tail+C_in+s_tail(C_in-F_cut)-mu_tail^2/(4q_cert).", "U_mom": "s_tail F_cut, the upper bound on the forward target first moment.", "dist": "Euclidean distance of a real number from the specified closed real interval."}}
{"id": "7868e4af51fb3141", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:56:41.416825+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "C55 joint extension completed before reading the same-launch primal retask: allowing an arbitrary balanced seed pair (1,2), plus independent balanced pairs from p to BOTH q-1 and q-2 at every growing join, still fails for the prescribed non-pair H2 seed coefficients. For m>=100000 every full objective certificate in this enlarged family has q_cert>(2/5)^(2m) exp(2^(m-1)/(625m)). Complete full proof and all derivative constants included. UNVERIFIED; arbitrary seeds, unrestricted certificates and actual schedule remain open. Switching now to TASK's legal-primal repair lane.", "evidence": "# C55: the prescribed three-boundary queried-pair recurrence is insufficient\n\nStatus: **complete scoped proof candidate, not verifier-accepted**. No\noptimization or verifier allocation is used. The predecessor\n`1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the interpolation convention and the requirement\nthat an objective certificate be positive semidefinite on all the original\nformal vectors. Everything specific to this recursive family is derived\nbelow. Prior C53/C55 findings are motivation, not mathematical premises.\n\nThe result covers precisely the H2 seed and the repeated-copy join with the\nadded balanced pair described below, including arbitrary horizon-dependent\njoin parameters and storage choices. It does not cover arbitrary seeds,\nindependent internal array corrections, or unrestricted certificates for the\nsame schedule. The test vectors are formal vectors, not a legal function.\nSection 10 also allows an arbitrary balanced pair inside the seed and both\nof the last two queried points at every higher join, with an explicit larger\nhorizon threshold.\n\n## 1. Statement and notation\n\nFix an integer m>=128 and define\n\n    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    d_j=a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nAll steps are predetermined and strictly positive; the bridges grow with\nthe recursion level. No upper bound on the steps is assumed. The claims\nbelow concern this explicit family, not arbitrary nonnegative schedules.\n\nFor formal gradients substitute the actual GD recursion with smoothness\none and optimum jet zero. For distinct samples define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    C_ij=-2 Q_ji\n        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nThe accepted predecessor proves C_ij>=0 for legal smooth convex data.\nFormal vectors used to test a quadratic certificate need not be legal.\n\nAn array at level j has storage parameters u_j,v_j and star pattern\n\n    lambda_(star,i)=h_i              (i<n_j),\n    lambda_(star,n_j)=u_j,\n    lambda_(i,star)=0                (i<n_j),\n    lambda_(n_j,star)=s_j=S_j+u_j-v_j.                 (1)\n\nHere lambda_ik denotes a multiplier array on C_ik; the scalar lambda without\nindices denotes 1+sqrt(2). The full identity defines its remainder E_j:\n\n    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-u_j g_end||^2-2v_j f_end\n                   =sum lambda_ik C_ik+E_j,\n    Z_j=(u_j-1)^2+v_j,\n    z_j=sum_(i<n_j) h_i g_i+g_end.                    (2)\n\nStart from the exact H2 seed in Section 2, and for j=2,...,m-1 apply any\njoin in Section 3, with a balanced multiplier gamma_j>=0 on the queried\npair (p,q-1). The same child array is embedded on the left and multiplied\nby r_j>=0 on the right. All actual final multipliers must be nonnegative\nto give a certificate. One may use any real u_j,v_j, with v_m>0, and need\nnot require positive semidefinite child or local remainders.\n\nThen, if the complete objective matrix at level q_cert is PSD, necessarily\n\n    q_cert >= 1/k_m^2\n           > (2/5)^(2m) exp[2^(m-3)/m],              (3)\n\nwhere the signed numbers k_j are defined in Section 5. The last expression\ntends to infinity. In particular q_cert>1 for m>=128, so this family cannot\nsupply v_m>=M_m with E_m PSD, nor any useful bounded objective coefficient.\nThis is an obstruction for the enlarged C55 recurrence, not an upper or\nlower bound on the actual schedule's worst-case objective.\n\nThe proof retains every internal child square and every new pair term. Its\nkey change from C53 is that the last-query gradient is c times the terminal\ngradient, instead of equal to it.\n\n## 2. The exact seed, its signs, and its attained boundary form\n\nPut\n\n    b0=1+a, M=M_1=2+a, d=d_1=1-epsilon,\n    x=d/M, eta=x^2+2x-1, a0=(2-b0^2)/b0^2.\n\nThe one-step seed H1=(b0) has finite multipliers\n\n    lambda_01=M, lambda_10=1,\n\nstar multipliers lambda_(star,0)=b0, lambda_(star,1)=M, and no incoming\nstar multipliers. Set u_1=M, v_1=2M-1, hence Z_1=M^2. Direct expansion in\n(2) gives\n\n    E_1=(2-b0^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.\n\nTo obtain H2, use the join of Section 3 with gamma=0 and\n\n    A=1/x, B=2+x, r=3+x+1/x, t=1/x-2-x,\n    P=(1+M eta)/x, Q=M,\n    u_2=M_2=2M+d, v_2=2M_2-1.                        (4)\n\nHere 1<b0<sqrt(2), 3/8<x<sqrt(2)-1, and eta<0. For the upper bound on x,\nwith s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda, expansion gives\n\n    d-sM=-epsilon[1-2s^2/(1+epsilon)]<0.\n\nThe lower bound follows from d>=127/128 and M<5/2. Thus a0,t>0.\nMoreover eta>=-7/64, so 1+M eta>1-35/128>0. Consequently A,B,P,Q,r are\npositive. The physical star pattern has s_1=s_2=0, and the update described\nin Section 3 yields nonnegative actual star multipliers as well.\n\nFor the four gradients of H2 define\n\n    U=b0(g_0-g_1), V0=b0(g_2-g_3), D0=g_1-g_3.\n\nExpanding the complete join gives\n\n    E_2=a0||U||^2+(r a0+t)||V0||^2\n                         +M^2(-eta)||D0||^2.          (5)\n\nFor an explicit coefficient check, (4) gives H=-t(M+d), ell=-tM in (8)\nbelow. Substitute V=d g_1+b0 g_2+g_3, Z_1=M^2, Z_2=(2M+d)^2 into (8).\nThe mixed V0,D0 coefficient is A d-M=0; the D0 square coefficient is\nM^2(1-2x-x^2). The two seed remainders provide a0||U||^2+r a0||V0||^2.\nThe remaining term is t||V0||^2, establishing (5).\n\nChanging u_2,v_2 and only the two terminal star coefficients in (1) adds\n(M_2^2-Z_2)||g_3||^2 to (5). Thus the obstruction also permits these\nstorage changes at the seed. The finite seed coefficients remain fixed.\n\nWith (z,e,w)=(z_2,g_3,g_2), the boundary relation is\n\n    z-M_2 e=U+V0+(M+d)D0.\n\nAll three boundary vectors are independent. Set c0=M^2(-eta), l0=M+d.\nThe exact identity\n\n    a0||U||^2+c0||D0||^2\n      =a0 c0/(c0+a0 l0^2) ||U+l0 D0||^2\n           +||a0 l0 U-c0 D0||^2/(c0+a0 l0^2)\n\nhas its second square equal to zero for every prescribed value of\nU+l0 D0. Therefore the sharp, attained boundary form is\n\n    K_2(z,e,w)=A2||z-M_2 e-b0(w-e)||^2\n                         +B2||w-e||^2\n                         +(M_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2,\n    A2=[1/a0+(1+x)^2/(-eta)]^(-1),\n    B2=(r a0+t)b0^2.                                 (6)\n\nNo unattainability of boundary minimizers is assumed in what follows.\n\n## 3. The actual enlarged array and the full local identity\n\nAt a join write n=n_j, p=n, q=2n+1, S=S_j, M=M_j, d=d_j, b=1+d.\nChoose A,B,P,Q with\n\n    r=1+A+B, P-Q=b+(1-A)S.\n\nEmbed the old array on the left and r times that array on the right.\nFor each right queried index p<i<q add\n\n    A h_i to (p,i), B h_i to (q,i),\n    -(A+B)h_i to (star,i).\n\nAdd the six corrections\n\n    (p,q):P, (q,p):Q,\n    (p,star):-s_j, (q,star):s_(j+1)-r s_j,\n    (star,p):b-u_j, (star,q):u_(j+1)-r u_j.            (7)\n\nFinally add gamma>=0 to both (p,q-1) and (q-1,p). These are actual queried\nsamples. This balanced change preserves every function-value flow and\nevery star multiplier. It adds no incoming unqueried-terminal constraint.\n\nFor example A,B,P,Q>=0, u_j>=0 and s_j>=0 at the relevant levels suffice\nfor nonnegative actual arrays when the seed is nonnegative: right queried\nstar coefficients become (r-A-B)h_i=h_i; the two terminal star coefficients\nbecome b and u_(j+1); the two incoming terminal star coefficients become\nzero and s_(j+1). All other entries are copied or receive nonnegative\nadditions. The obstruction allows all real A,B,P,Q satisfying the flow\nidentity and r>=0, so it also covers choices whose signs are certified by\na more detailed inspection of the actual array.\n\nWrite g=g_p, e=g_q, w=g_(q-1), and define\n\n    t=A-B,\n    H=M+d-B(M-d)-tM,\n    ell=P-A,\n    V=x_p-x_q-g+e=d g+z_right.\n\nThe complete remainder identity is\n\n    E_next=E_left+r E_right\n       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g+ell e>\n       +(Z_j-rd^2)||g||^2+(rZ_j-Z_next)||e||^2\n       +2gamma<g-w,e+a w-V>.                          (8)\n\nHere a is the fixed parameter in b0=1+a, since every block ends with that\nsame step b0. To verify the new term, x_(q-1)=x_q+b0 w gives\n\n    -gamma(C_(p,q-1)+C_(q-1,p))\n       =2gamma[||g-w||^2-<g-w,x_p-x_(q-1)>]\n       =2gamma<g-w,e+a w-V>.\n\nFor completeness the old part of (8) can be checked before the pair is\nadded. Put D=x_p-x_q and W=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i=x_p-b g-x_q. The rank\ncorrection sums to\n\n    2AS f_p+2BS f_q-AS||g||^2-BS||e||^2\n                      -2<W,A(x_p-g)+B(x_q-e)>.\n\nThe value coefficient at p becomes\n\n    -2v_j+2AS+2(P-Q)-2s_j-2(b-u_j)\n            =2[(A-1)S+P-Q-b]=0.\n\nThe value coefficient at q becomes -2v_next using r=1+A+B and (1).\nAfter subtracting the correction from the new potential minus the two\nchild potentials, the quadratic coefficients are\n\n    D^2:t, (D,g):H-ell-t, (D,e):ell+t,\n    g^2:Z_j-rd^2+t-2H+2ell,\n    e^2:rZ_j-Z_next+t+2ell, (g,e):H-2ell-t,\n\nwhere a mixed coefficient multiplies twice the indicated inner product.\nSubstituting V=D-g+e proves the old part of (8). Thus (8) is an identity\non the full formal space, with every inherited remainder retained.\n\n## 4. Explicit eliminated matrix and every singular range condition\n\nThis section records the exact recurrence being obstructed, including\nits singular cases. Write an attained child boundary form as\n\n    K(z,e,w)=alpha||z||^2+2beta<z,e>+2chi<z,w>\n                        +delta||e||^2+2etaK<e,w>+phi||w||^2.\n\nIn (8) substitute E_left=K(L,g,u)+its internal remainder and\nE_right=K(R,e,w)+its internal remainder. The six-variable form has\nparent boundary z=L+d g+R. Eliminate X=(g,u,R) and retain Y=(z,e,w).\nAfter L=z-d g-R, its symmetric matrix is\n\n                         [ D_el  B_el ]\n                         [ B_el^T C_el].\n\nIts entries are exactly\n\n    D_el(1,1)=(alpha+t-r)d^2-2beta d+delta\n                                      +2d(H-ell-gamma)+Z_j,\n    D_el(1,2)=etaK-d chi,\n    D_el(1,3)=d(alpha+t)-beta+H-ell-gamma,\n    D_el(2,2)=phi,\n    D_el(2,3)=-chi,\n    D_el(3,3)=(1+r)alpha+t;\n\n    B_el=[ -d alpha+beta,  d ell+gamma, gamma(a+d);\n                 chi,          0,              0;\n               -alpha,     r beta+ell,       r chi+gamma ];\n\n    C_el=[ alpha,          0,                    0;\n              0, r delta+rZ_j-Z_next,  r etaK-gamma;\n              0,       r etaK-gamma,    r phi-2a gamma ].  (9)\n\nThe full six-variable matrix is PSD if and only if all of the following\nhold:\n\n    D_el is PSD;\n    B_el^T xi=0 for EVERY xi in ker(D_el);\n    C_el-B_el^T D_el^dagger B_el is PSD.                (10)\n\nThe middle condition is equivalently that every column of B_el belongs to\nthe range of D_el. The dagger is the inverse on its positive eigenspaces\nand zero on its kernel. To prove (10), first restrict to Y=0. A nonzero\ncross coefficient against a null vector of D_el makes the form unbounded\nbelow by scaling that null direction. Once these coefficients vanish,\ncomplete the square on the positive eigenspaces. This proves both\nnecessity and sufficiency, including every rank of D_el.\n\nWhen the children have exact attained decompositions with PSD internal\nremainders and r>=0, (10) is also equivalent to PSD of the entire parent\nremainder: the independent boundary data can be lifted while setting all\ninternal child squares to zero. The new sharp boundary form is the last\nSchur complement in (10). No such interpretation is needed for the\nobstruction below: it directly evaluates the original E_j and does not\nassume that any earlier elimination passed.\n\n## 5. A different formal seed survives every new queried pair\n\nDefine\n\n    k_2=M_2+6epsilon, theta=c.\n\nAt the H2 seed choose the following explicit four gradients, all multiples\nof an arbitrary vector e:\n\n    g_0=(2-c+6epsilon/b0)e,\n    g_1=e, g_2=c e, g_3=e.                            (11)\n\nThen the exact boundary is\n\n    z_2=k_2 e, g_end=e, g_last_query=c e.\n\nIndeed z_2=b0 g_0+(1+d_1)g_1+b0 g_2+g_3=(M_2+6epsilon)e.\nIn (5), D0=0, V0=b0(c-1)e, and U=[6epsilon-b0(c-1)]e.\nWe next prove the strict energy inequality\n\n    E_2 < (k_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2                         (12)\n\nfor e!=0, for every m>=128. This includes all internal seed squares.\n\nFirst, a>2/5. In fact sqrt(2)<10/7 gives lambda<17/7, so\n\n    a >= (127/129)(7/17)=889/2193 >2/5.\n\nAlso a<s=sqrt(2)-1, b0<sqrt(2), M>12/5 and c<5/2. To bound a0, use\n\n    b0=sqrt(2)-2s epsilon/(1+epsilon),\n    2-b0^2<4sqrt(2)s epsilon=4(2-sqrt(2))epsilon\n                                          <(12/5)epsilon,\n    b0^2>49/25.\n\nTherefore a0<(60/49)epsilon<(5/4)epsilon. Since 3/8<x<s<1/2,\n\n    r=3+x+1/x<37/6<25/4.\n\nFurthermore\n\n    s-x=epsilon[1-2s^2/(1+epsilon)]/M<(5/12)epsilon,\n    t=(s-x)(1+1/(sx))<(5/12)(23/3)epsilon<4epsilon.\n\nThe estimate on 1/(sx) uses s>2/5 and x>3/8. We have\n0<6epsilon<b0(c-1), because 6epsilon<=3/64 and b0,c-1>7/5. Hence\n\n    ||U||^2 <= b0^2(c-1)^2||e||^2,\n    b0^2(c-1)^2<2(3/2)^2=9/2.\n\nSubstitution into the FULL seed formula (5) gives\n\n    E_2-(M_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2\n       <=[(r+1)a0+t] b0^2(c-1)^2||e||^2\n       <[(29/4)(5/4)+4](9/2)epsilon||e||^2\n       =(1881/32)epsilon||e||^2\n       <60epsilon||e||^2.\n\nFinally M_2=5-epsilon+2a>5, so\n\n    k_2^2-M_2^2=12M_2 epsilon+36epsilon^2>60epsilon.\n\nThis proves (12). In particular the argument has not replaced the full\nseed by an unattainable boundary minimum or dropped a positive square.\n\n## 6. A uniform subcritical gap and its exact propagation\n\nSet\n\n    R0=k_2/(c d_2)=(M_2+6epsilon)/[(1-epsilon)c^2].\n\nWe have the explicit bound\n\n    0<R0<1-epsilon/4.                                (13)\n\nFor its proof expand the available gap using lambda^2=2lambda+1:\n\n    (c d_2-M_2)/epsilon\n      =[6sqrt(2)+epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2\n                                  -lambda^2 epsilon^3]/(1+epsilon).\n\nThis is greater than 8. Indeed after subtracting 8 and multiplying by\n1+epsilon, the numerator is\n\n    6sqrt(2)-8-7epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^3\n      >2/5-7/128-12/128^2-6/128^3>0.\n\nConsequently c d_2-k_2>2epsilon. Since c d_2=(1-epsilon)c^2<25/4,\n\n    1-R0>8epsilon/25>epsilon/4,\n\nas claimed.\n\nNow define signed numbers recursively by\n\n    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j, j>=2.                         (14)\n\nFor every block H_j there is a formal gradient assignment linear in its\nterminal vector e such that\n\n    z_j=k_j e, g_end=e, g_last_query=c e,\n    E_j <= (k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2.                        (15)\n\nThis holds simultaneously for every permitted choice of the join and\nstorage parameters, including choices depending on the target horizon.\nThe seed is (11)-(12). For the induction step put the right child on its\ntemplate with terminal e, and the left child on its template with terminal\n\n    g=-k_j e/d_j.\n\nThen V=d_j g+z_right=0, and the parent moment is\n\n    z_next=z_left+d_j g+z_right=k_j g=k_(j+1)e.\n\nIts last-query gradient remains c e. The crucial NEW pair term in (8) is\n\n    2gamma(-k_j/d_j-c)(1+a c)||e||^2.                 (16)\n\nIt is nonpositive at every level. At j=2, k_2>0 proves this directly. For\nj>=3, k_j<0; solving (14) gives\n\n    |k_j|=(1-epsilon)c^j R0^(2^(j-2)),\n    |k_j|/d_j=c R0^(2^(j-2))<c.                     (17)\n\nThus (16) is negative for gamma>0 and e!=0, since 1+a c=2-epsilon>0.\n\nThe other mixed local terms vanish because V=0. Since r>=0, the two\ncomplete child estimates in (15) retain their direction under addition.\nKeeping their full values and the nonpositive new pair term gives\n\n    E_next <= (k_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2\n                 +r(k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2\n                 +(Z_j-rd_j^2)||g||^2+(rZ_j-Z_next)||e||^2\n             = (k_j^4/d_j^2-Z_next)||e||^2,\n\nwhich is exactly (15) at the parent. No local PSD, nonsingularity, greedy\nstorage, or rank assumption was used.\n\nIn co-coercivity language the pair has\n\n    C_(p,q-1)+C_(q-1,p)\n          =2(2-epsilon)(c+k_j/d_j)||e||^2>=0.\n\nThis explains why a nonnegative weight on this actual pair cannot repair\nthis new formal template. The old C53 template had the opposite sign on\nsome such pairs; those are different formal vectors.\n\n## 7. The original initial-vector objective conversion\n\nLet u=u_m, v=v_m>0, Z=(u-1)^2+v and k=k_m. For the final array the full\nobjective certificate at any level q_cert is\n\n    M_cert=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n\n                     +sum_(i!=j) (2lambda_ji/v)Q_ij\n           =(q_cert-1/v)||x_0||^2\n                     +[||x_n-u g_n||^2+E_m]/v.        (18)\n\nThis follows directly from (2) and C_ij=-2Q_ji. In particular E_m PSD\nand nonnegative actual multipliers yield the bound q_cert=1/v. The\nnormalization is the project's 2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), by the\nscaling in predecessor `1252b8823bb5a290`.\n\nKeep x_0 independent of the formal template in (15), and set\nbeta0=k+u-1. Since z_m=k e,\n\n    x_n-u g_n=x_0-beta0 e.\n\nThe restriction of v M_cert is bounded ABOVE by the two-variable form\nwith matrix\n\n                  [ q_cert v    -beta0       ]\n                  [  -beta0    2k beta0-v    ].        (19)\n\nThe difference is a nonnegative multiple of ||e||^2, by (15). If the\noriginal M_cert is PSD, this upper matrix must therefore also be PSD.\nIts second diagonal is positive: if it were zero, PSD would force\nbeta0=0, making that diagonal -v<0. Its determinant now implies\n\n    q_cert >= beta0^2/[v(2k beta0-v)] >=1/k^2,\n\nwhere the last step is exactly\n\n    k^2 beta0^2-v(2k beta0-v)=(v-k beta0)^2>=0.\n\nThis includes arbitrary increases of q_cert and indefinite child or final\nremainders. It tests the original x_0 variable, not only the condensed\ngradient state.\n\nFrom (13),(17) and 1-x<=exp(-x),\n\n    |k_m|<c^m exp[-2^(m-4)/m].                        (20)\n\nThus (3) follows from c<5/2. The logarithm of its right side is\n2^(m-3)/m-2m log(5/2), which tends to infinity because 2^m/m^2 tends to\ninfinity. Its successive ratio is 2m^2/(m+1)^2>3/2 for m>=7.\nFor m>=128, (20) also gives |k_m|<1: log(5/2)<1 follows from\nexp(1)>1+1+1/2+1/6>5/2, and 2^(m-4)>m^2 for m>=128. The latter inequality\nholds at 128 and is preserved since 2m^2/(m+1)^2>1. Hence q_cert>1.\n\n## 8. Undeformed calibration and scope checks\n\nSet epsilon=0, so a=1/lambda, c=lambda. Then b0=sqrt(2), M_j=lambda^j,\nd_j=lambda^(j-1), a0=t=eta=0, and the physical seed E_2 is identically\nzero. At each higher join choose\n\n    A=B=lambda, r=lambda^2, t=H=ell=0,\n    P=lambda, Q=M_j,\n    u_j=(1+sqrt(4M_j^2-3))/2, v_j=u_j, Z_j=M_j^2.\n\nThe same storage choice can be made at H2 by its permitted terminal-star\nchange, preserving E_2=0. It has s_j=S_j>=0. The flow equation follows\nfrom M_j=lambda d_j, and (8) with gamma=0 gives E_next=0. Every actual\nmultiplier is nonnegative by Section 3. This is an exact valid undeformed\ncalibration of the full array and the objective conversion (18).\n\nIf one retains the physical H2 storage u_2=M_2,v_2=2M_2-1 instead, the\nseed still has Z_2=M_2^2; switching to the displayed u_j,v_j at the next\nlevel likewise changes only the two terminal star coefficients and is\nallowed. No calibration identity is being carried from a differently\nnormalized performance measure.\n\nAt epsilon=0 the obstruction template has k_2=M_2 and R0=1, so it yields\nonly q_cert>=lambda^(-2m), entirely compatible with the valid bound\n1/u_m of order lambda^(-m). The strict horizon-dependent gap in (13)\nis essential.\n\nThe hypothesis r>=0 is exactly the scaling restriction in the C55 state\nrecurrence, not an extra PSD assumption on its children. It is also\nforced by nonnegative final arrays in this prescribed construction. The\nseed has the positive coefficient lambda_01=M. At every larger level,\nthe first two queried samples retain this coefficient unchanged, because\nthey lie in the left child and none of the join corrections touches their\nordered pair. In a right child the corresponding ordered pair has\ncoefficient rM and is untouched by the parent's fan, sparse, and new-pair\ncorrections. In the final leftmost occurrence of any such join it remains\nuntouched by all higher joins. Thus a negative r at any level would leave\na negative actual coefficient in the final array. This also shows that\nthe proof covers arrays required to be nonnegative only at the final\nlevel. The case r=0 is already included in the energy induction.\n\nThe seed restriction matters: the result does not assert that every H2\narray has (5), or that every three-boundary seed is covered. A different\nseed has to be tested against the seed-energy and last-query conditions\n(12),(15). Arbitrary additional queried pairs can change (16); no claim\nabout those corrections is made here. Likewise the formal template need\nnot satisfy omitted interpolation constraints, so it is not an actual\nlower witness for H_m. Accepted terminal completion removes no queried\nprefix constraints and cannot justify dropping them.\n\nAn explicit violated pair for (11) is already present in its H2 seed:\ng_1=e, g_2=c e and x_1-x_2=(1+d_1)e give\n\n    C_12+C_21=2(1-c)(d_1+c)||e||^2<0.\n\nThis directly prevents interpreting the formal template as a legal\nadversary, regardless of how function values are assigned. The extra\nseed pair on these indices is addressed by a different template next.\n\nThis analysis makes no lower-bound claim for schedules with zero steps.\nThe accepted predecessor handles those schedules without identifying\nduplicated formal gradients. Any future unrestricted conclusion would\nstill have to retain both zero-step and arbitrarily-large-step cases.\n\nAll new mathematics in this file remains a proof candidate. The exact\nminimax/profile target remains unresolved, and the fact graph is unchanged.\n\n## 9. Reproducibility of the algebraic audit\n\nOne fixed rational substitution in the six-variable identity (9) was\nchecked by polarization of its 21 coefficients, and the four rational\nconstant margins in Sections 5-6 were checked exactly. These were tiny\narithmetic checks, not optimization or a numerical search. The displayed\nexpansions and inequalities are the proof; the checks are not premises.\n\nFor architecture context, the exact local text of Altschuler--Parrilo,\narXiv:2309.16530, Theorem 3.4 and its helper-lemma proof was reread. No\ntheorem from that paper is imported; its undeformed recursion motivated\nthe array, whose modified full identity is proved in Section 3.\n\n## 10. Seed-pair freedom and two trailing queries still do not suffice\n\nThis is a uniform enlargement of the SAME joint proof, not an invocation\nof its unverified earlier sections as accepted facts. All identities and\nthe objective conversion needed here have their derivations above.\n\nTake m>=100000. In the H2 seed allow any nonnegative common addition\ngamma_seed to lambda_12 and lambda_21. Keep its other finite coefficients\nas in Section 2 and permit the same arbitrary terminal-star changes. At\nevery higher join permit BOTH balanced queried pairs\n\n    (p,q-1), (q-1,p) with common multiplier gamma_1>=0;\n    (p,q-2), (q-2,p) with common multiplier gamma_2>=0.  (21)\n\nThe two gamma values may be chosen independently at every level and may\ndepend on m. Keep all other join freedoms in Section 3. This includes\nthe proposed three-boundary family with a freely reweighted seed pair,\nas well as the analogous family that retains the two last query gradients.\n\nEvery full PSD objective certificate in this enlarged family satisfies\n\n    q_cert > (2/5)^(2m) exp[2^(m-1)/(625m)] -> infinity. (22)\n\nNo bound on the sizes of the nonnegative gamma values is imposed. The\nproof does not require PSD of the modified seed or any intermediate child.\n\n### 10.1 Explicit replacement template and its uniform seed estimate\n\nPut sigma=1/100 and define the positive seed moment\n\n    k_2=d_1 c^2-sigma epsilon=c d_2-sigma epsilon.\n\nThe new four seed gradients are\n\n    g_0=[k_2-1-c(M+d_1)]e/b0,\n    g_1=c e, g_2=c e, g_3=e.                         (23)\n\nThey give z_2=k_2 e. In particular BOTH last query gradients equal c e.\nSince g_1=g_2, their balanced interpolation sum is identically zero,\nincluding its function-value cancellation. Thus gamma_seed changes\nneither the seed energy on (23) nor the following estimates.\n\nContinue writing d=d_1, M=M_1, and put\n\n    Uc=k_2-1-c(M_2-1),\n    c0=M^2-2Md-d^2,\n    B0=(r a0+t)b0^2+c0,\n    F(epsilon)=k_2^2-M_2^2-a0 Uc^2-B0(c-1)^2.        (24)\n\nFormula (5) evaluated on (23) has U=Uc e, V0=b0(c-1)e,\nD0=(c-1)e. Therefore F(epsilon)>=0 implies exactly\n\n    E_2 <= (k_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2,\n\nincluding the complete seed remainder and all seed-pair additions.\nWe now establish the explicit uniform lower bound\n\n    F(epsilon)>(6/25)epsilon for 0<epsilon<=1/100000.  (25)\n\nAll quantities in (24) are smooth functions of epsilon on [0,1/128].\nThe expression does not divide by a0 or eta, so it is regular at zero.\nWrite b=sqrt(2) for the next derivative computation only. At epsilon=0,\n\n    M_2=k_2=lambda^2, Uc=-(4+2b), c-1=b,\n    a0=t=c0=B0=0,\n    a0'=4-2b, t'=16-10b, c0'=6b-4,\n    B0'=36-10b,\n    k_2'-M_2'=6b-sigma.\n\nHere a=sqrt(2)-1 at zero and a'=-2a there; differentiating the explicit\ndefinitions in (4),(24) gives all the displayed derivatives. In particular\n\n    [a0 Uc^2+B0(c-1)^2]'(0)\n       =(4-2b)(4+2b)^2+2(36-10b)=104-4b,\n    F(0)=0,\n    F'(0)=40b-56-2sigma lambda^2>11/25.              (26)\n\nFor the strict rational margin, b>707/500 follows by squaring positive\nnumbers, and lambda^2<6. Hence 40b-56>14/25 and\n2sigma lambda^2<3/25.\n\nFor transparency, the following elementary derivative bounds hold\nthroughout [0,1/128]. The three columns are upper bounds for the absolute\nvalues of the quantity, its first derivative, and its second derivative.\n\n    quantity                  value     first     second\n    b0                           3/2       1          2\n    M                            5/2       1          2\n    d                            1         1          0\n    c                            5/2       5/2        0\n    M_2                          6         3          4\n    k_2                          7        20         40\n    a0                           1         4         20\n    d/M                          1/2       1          2\n    M/d                          3         4         12\n    r                            7         5         14\n    t                            6         5         14\n    b0^2                         3         3          8\n    c0                          13        14         22\n    r a0+t                      13        38        208\n    B0                          52       167       1000\n    Uc                          21        40         65\n    Uc^2                       441      1680       6000\n    (c-1)^2                      3         8         13. (27)\n\nHere are sufficient details to reproduce every derivative bound. We have\n2<M<5/2, 127/128<=d<=1, and\n\n    a'=-2(sqrt(2)-1)/(1+epsilon)^2,\n    a''=4(sqrt(2)-1)/(1+epsilon)^3,\n    a0=2/b0^2-1.\n\nThus |a'|<1, |a''|<2, |a0'|<4 and |a0''|<20, using b0>1. The quotient\nrules give\n\n    (d/M)'=d'/M-dM'/M^2,\n    (d/M)''=-2d'M'/M^2-dM''/M^2+2d(M')^2/M^3,\n    (M/d)'=M'/d+M/d^2,\n    (M/d)''=M''/d+2M'/d^2+2M/d^3.\n\nThese yield the corresponding rows of (27); r=3+d/M+M/d and\nt=M/d-2-d/M give the next rows. Since c0=M^2-2Md-d^2, its derivative\nbounds follow by the product rule: the second derivative is bounded by\n2(1+5)+2(2+2)+2=22. The product r a0+t has bounds 13,38,208.\nMultiplying it by b0^2 and adding c0 gives bounds\n\n    13*3+13=52,\n    38*3+13*3+14=167,\n    208*3+2*38*3+13*8+22=978<1000\n\nfor B0. For k_2=d c^2-sigma epsilon, direct differentiation gives\n|k_2'|<20 and |k_2''|<40. The definitions M_2=5-epsilon+2a and\nUc=k_2-1-c(M_2-1) give\n\n    |Uc|<7+1+(5/2)*5<21,\n    |Uc'|<20+(5/2)*5+(5/2)*3=40,\n    |Uc''|<40+2*(5/2)*3+(5/2)*4=65.\n\nThe last two rows of (27) then follow by differentiating squares.\n\nUsing (27) in (24),\n\n    |(a0 Uc^2)''|<20*441+2*4*1680+6000=28260,\n    |(B0(c-1)^2)''|<1000*3+2*167*8+52*13=6348,\n    |(k_2^2-M_2^2)''|<2*20^2+2*7*40+2*3^2+2*6*4=1426.\n\nTheir sum is less than 40000, so |F''|<40000. Integrating the second\nderivative twice, with (26), gives\n\n    F(epsilon)>(11/25)epsilon-20000epsilon^2\n                    >=(6/25)epsilon\n\non the claimed interval. This proves (25) without relying on numerical\napproximations or an unspecified continuity threshold.\n\n### 10.2 Both pairs have the sign that defeats every nonnegative weight\n\nStart (14) with the new k_2. Its critical ratio satisfies\n\n    0<R0=k_2/(c d_2)=1-sigma epsilon/(d_1 c^2)\n                                  <1-epsilon/625,    (28)\n\nbecause d_1 c^2<25/4 and sigma=1/100. Put the right child on its new\ntemplate and the left on the template with terminal g=-k_j e/d_j.\nThis again gives V=0, z_next=k_(j+1)e, and both trailing query gradients\nc e. Equation (17), with this R0, still gives\n\n    -k_j/d_j<=c\n\nat every level: k_2>0, and all later k_j are negative with absolute ratio\n|k_j|/d_j=c R0^(2^(j-2))<c.\n\nFor the second-last query write w1=g_(q-1), w2=g_(q-2). The last two\nphysical steps are b0 and 1+d_1. Its exact additional remainder is\n\n    -gamma_2(C_(p,q-2)+C_(q-2,p))\n      =2gamma_2<g-w2,e+b0 w1+d_1 w2-V>.              (29)\n\nThis follows by substituting\nx_(q-2)=x_q+b0 w1+(1+d_1)w2 into the pair identity. On the new template,\nthe first and second pairs' contributions are respectively\n\n    2gamma_1(-k_j/d_j-c)(1+a c)||e||^2,\n    2gamma_2(-k_j/d_j-c)[1+(b0+d_1)c]||e||^2.\n\nBoth are nonpositive. The seed pair contributes zero. Retaining all\nchild remainders in (8),(29) therefore proves (15) with the new k_j,\nword for word by the displayed addition in Section 6. This is an actual\nfull-remainder argument and does not depend on a particular method of\ncondensing its four boundary vectors.\n\nFinally the original x_0 test (18)-(19), with its proof above, implies\nq_cert>=1/k_m^2. From (28),\n\n    |k_m|<c^m exp[-2^(m-2)/(625m)],\n\nwhich gives (22). The bound on |k_m| is below one for m>=100000: the inequality\n2^(m-2)>625m^2 holds at m=128 and is preserved by the increasing ratio\n2m^2/(m+1)^2. Combining with log(5/2)<1 proves q_cert>1 in the stated\nrange.\n\nThis enlargement still fixes the non-pair finite coefficients of the H2\nseed. It does not close arbitrary H2 seeds or additional independent\ninterpolation changes. In particular it does not assert an obstruction\nfor all fixed-size boundary states. It rules out two concrete suggested\nrepairs with all their weights free, and leaves the unrestricted problem\nand the actual schedule distinct.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "C55_three_boundary_balanced_pair", "finding_ids": ["704f32e7f06cbf56"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "research_path": "research/c55_three_boundary_pair_obstruction.md", "scope": "free seed pair and both trailing-query pairs, fixed other seed coefficients; not arbitrary boundary states", "verification_status": "unverified joint candidate; no fact_submit", "budget_delta": {"optimization_calls": 0, "build_solve_seconds": 0, "fact_submit_calls": 0}}, "glossary": {"gamma_seed": "Arbitrary nonnegative balanced addition to the seed entries (1,2) and (2,1).", "gamma_1": "Arbitrary nonnegative balanced pair weight from p to the last queried sample q-1.", "gamma_2": "Arbitrary nonnegative balanced pair weight from p to the second-last queried sample q-2.", "sigma": "The fixed rational 1/100 in the new seed moment.", "k_2": "d_1 c^2-sigma epsilon in Section 10; prior Section 5 uses its explicitly different k_2.", "F": "The seed energy gap in (24), with F(epsilon)>(6/25)epsilon.", "Uc": "The seed coordinate k_2-1-c(M_2-1).", "B0": "The seed coefficient (r a0+t)b0^2+c0, defined in (24)."}}
{"id": "273cc121acfd549e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T20:59:22.836156+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "Complete NEW bulk-array obstruction candidate: every nonnegative mixture of normalized dense binary tensor kernels A=sum eta(I-tensor P_k)+diag(delta), with each P_k doubly substochastic and first column sum1, forces q>1/8 on ANY full objective certificate with h0>=7/5. It includes calibrated one-step Silver factors, arbitrary level/context-independent directed weights, arbitrarily many dense mixture terms, and arbitrary nonnegative optimum storage; it fails undeformed and actual critical words. Full original-space operator identity, all actual signs/value flows, elementary tensor product inequalities, a distinct symmetric-array obstruction, and an exact directed tensor screen are proved in one package. This is not an obstruction to all directed arrays or a legal lower witness.", "evidence": "# Bulk binary interpolation kernels: full-space identities and exact obstructions\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate, round12. No optimization and no\nfact_submit. This is an obstruction to the precisely defined multiplier\norganizations below, not a legal hard function or a decision of the project.\nOnly accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies a mathematical predecessor: its\nsigned smooth-convex interpolation residual and full-certificate framework.\nEvery new identity and bound used in this document is proved here jointly.\n\n## 1. Exact operator form on the original formal space\n\nFix n>=1, N=n+1, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and normalize L=1 and the\noptimum sample to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The N+1 vectors\nx_0,g_0,...,g_n remain independent. Put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nFor actual smooth-convex samples Q_ij<=0, by fact1252b8823bb5a290.\nLet lambda_ij>=0 denote the coefficient of Q_ij. A full upper certificate\nat level q has all finite value coefficients zero and is the PSD form\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.       (1)\n\nDefine the strictly lower triangular N by N matrix T by\nT_it=h_t for t<i and T_it=0 otherwise, and put V=I+T. For a real\nmatrix B, Sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. Let e_n be the last coordinate vector\nand 1 the vector with every coordinate equal to1.\n\nHere is an exact operator parameterization useful for specifying structured\narrays. Choose an N by N real matrix A satisfying\n\n    A_ij<=0 for i!=j,\n    b=A1>=0,\n    a=A^T1-2e_n>=0,                                  (2)\n\nwhere inequalities on vectors are coordinatewise. Set\n\n    lambda_ij=-A_ij  (finite distinct i,j),\n    lambda_(i,star)=b_i,  lambda_(star,i)=a_i.          (3)\n\nAll actual coefficients are nonnegative. With r_i=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij,\nc_i=sum_(j!=i)lambda_ji, one has A_ii=r_i+b_i and\na_i=r_i+b_i-c_i-2*1_(i=n). Thus the value coefficient at i in (1) is\nr_i+b_i-c_i-a_i-2*1_(i=n)=0, including at the terminal.\n\nExpanding every quadratic term in (1) gives the complete matrix\n\n    M(q) = [x_0;g]^T [[q, -b^T/2],[-b/2, G]] [x_0;g],\n    G=Sym(A V)-e_n e_n^T.                            (4)\n\nThe notation means sums of Euclidean inner products when the formal vectors\nhave dimension greater than1. To check (4), write K_ij=lambda_ij for i!=j\nand K_ii=0. The finite position terms equal\ng^T[diag(r)-K]Tg. The star position terms equal\n-<x_0,sum b_i g_i>+g^T diag(b)Tg. The squared-gradient terms equal\ng^T[diag(r+b)-Sym(K)-e_n e_n^T]g, using the just-checked value flow.\nAdding them proves (4). Conversely a finite nonnegative array with the\nrequired value flow gives (2)--(4). This identity alone is a reformulation,\nnot progress on unrestricted PSD; the restricted cone studied next is\nspecified independently of that unknown PSD inequality.\n\n## 2. A genuine bulk cone: directed binary-coordinate operators\n\nTake N=2^m, m>=1, n=N-1, and index the finite samples in chronological\nbinary order. At each bit k choose numbers\n\n    u_k>=0, v_k>=0, 0<=u_k-v_k<=2, kappa_k>=0,\n    A_k=[[u_k,-v_k],[-u_k,v_k+2]].                   (5)\n\nThe two column sums of A_k are0 and2; its two row sums are\nu_k-v_k and2+v_k-u_k, both nonnegative. Embed A_k at bit k with\nidentity matrices on the other bits. Choose arbitrary delta_i>=0 and put\n\n    A=sum_(k=1)^m kappa_k (I tensor ... tensor A_k tensor ... tensor I)\n                   +diag(delta_0,...,delta_n).       (6)\n\nAn off-diagonal entry in (6) is nonzero only between indices differing in\none bit. For each setting of the other m-1 bits, the edge0->1 at bit k\nhas coefficient kappa_k v_k, and the reverse edge has coefficient\nkappa_k u_k. This is a scalable bulk organization with m*2^(m-1)\ninterior cross-block pairs. It is not the endpoint-fan join. The same\ntwo-node factor is used in every context at a given bit; that equality\nof contexts is a real restriction.\n\nLet s=sum kappa_k. Then b=A1>=0. At a finite index i with binary bits i_k,\n\n    a_i=2sum_k kappa_k i_k+delta_i-2*1_(i=n).\n\nConsequently (2), hence all actual signs and all value coefficients, hold\nif and only if\n\n    t:=2s+delta_n >=2.                              (7)\n\nThere is no prohibition on outgoing optimum edges: all these a_i are\nretained. The delta_i permit arbitrary additional equal weights on\nQ_(i,star) and Q_(star,i). The initial coefficient q is free. Incoming\nterminal edges are allowed; no terminal or queried pair is deleted.\n\n## 3. Full one-step calibration\n\nThe cone contains a valid one-step seed throughout 0<H<=sqrt(2). Put\nM=1+H, take physical step h_0=H, and choose\n\n    A_H=[[2,-2/M],[-2,2+2/M]], delta=0, kappa=1.\n\nIts actual coefficients are\n\n    lambda_01=2/M, lambda_10=2,\n    lambda_(0,star)=2H/M, lambda_(1,star)=2/M,\n    lambda_(star,0)=lambda_(star,1)=0.\n\nDirect expansion proves\n\n    (1/M)||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n      = [||x_0-Hg_0-g_1||^2\n             +(2-H^2)||g_0-g_1||^2+M(M-1)||g_1||^2]/M.       (8)\n\nThe value coefficient at0 is2/M-2+2H/M=0, and that at1,\nincluding -2f_1, is2-2/M+2/M-2=0. Expanding the three squares verifies\nall gradient and initial-distance coefficients. Every square coefficient\nin (8) is nonnegative. In particular H=sqrt(2) gives the full undeformed\nSilver seed, and the actual critical h_0=1+a_m also lies in this interval\nfor m>=128, as checked below. The next obstruction therefore concerns\nthe proposed tensor extension, rather than an invalid local seed.\n\n## 4. Exact constant lower barrier for the entire cone (5)--(7)\n\nClaim. If h_0=H>=7/5, no array (5)--(7), at ANY depth m, can make the\nfull matrix (4) PSD with q<=1/8. All remaining steps may be arbitrary\nnonnegative numbers. All u_k,v_k,kappa_k and delta_i may depend on the\nhorizon. The local factors need not be calibrated as in Section3.\n\nProof. Define the nonnegative numbers\n\n    U=sum kappa_k u_k,\n    R=sum kappa_k v_k,\n    B=U-R>=0,\n    z=B+delta_0>=0,\n    t=2sum kappa_k+delta_n>=2.                       (9)\n\nThe symbol R in this section is only a scalar coefficient sum, not an\nobjective or a gradient remainder. At the all-zero binary index0,\n\n    b_0=z,\n    G_00=U+delta_0-HR=z-(H-1)R.                    (10)\n\nFor (10), every off-diagonal neighbor of index0 is later than0,\nT_j0=H at every such neighbor, and the total outgoing coefficient is R.\nAt the all-one index n,\n\n    b_n=t-B,\n    G_nn=t+R-1.                                    (11)\n\nFor (11), column n of V has only its diagonal entry1, and\nA_nn=sum kappa_k(v_k+2)+delta_n=t+R.\n\nThe source condition gives sum b_i-sum a_i=2. If q=0 and the full\nmatrix were PSD, every entry in its initial row would be zero, hence\nb=0, contradicting this condition and a>=0. A negative q also violates\nPSD. Thus suppose 0<q<=1/8.\n\nThe principal matrix in x_0,g_0 must be PSD. By (10), its determinant\nand lower diagonal require\n\n    z^2 <=4q[z-(H-1)R],\n    z-(H-1)R>=0.                                   (12)\n\nIn particular z<=4q and B<=z<=4q. Rearranging (12) as\n\n    (z-2q)^2+4q(H-1)R<=4q^2\n\nalso gives R<=q/(H-1). Since H>=7/5 and q<=1/8,\n\n    B<=1/2,  R<=5/16.                              (13)\n\nThe other principal matrix, in x_0,g_n, has positive lower diagonal\nt+R-1>=1. By (11), its determinant requires\n\n    q >= (t-B)^2/[4(t+R-1)].                        (14)\n\nUsing (13), t>=2, and t+R-1<t gives\n\n    q > (t-1/2)^2/(4t)\n       >= (3t/4)^2/(4t)\n       =9t/64 >=9/32.\n\nThe middle inequality uses t-1/2>=3t/4, which follows from t>=2.\nThis contradicts q<=1/8, completing the proof. No Schur complement\nof an omitted subspace, no Huber equality, and no positivity restricted\nto legal transcripts were used: these are necessary principal tests\non the full original formal space.\n\n## 5. Application to undeformed and actual critical words\n\nFor undeformed Silver, h_0=sqrt(2)>7/5. Thus the local full PSD seed\n(8) cannot be extended with (5)--(7) to any upper coefficient tending\nto0. In particular it cannot maintain the benchmark q=1/lambda^m\nonce lambda^m>=8, where lambda=1+sqrt(2).\n\nFor the assigned critical word, m>=128, epsilon=1/m,\nc=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c, and\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Its first physical step is\nH=1+a. The radical bounds12/5<lambda<17/7 imply\n\n    a >=127/(129lambda)>889/2193>2/5,\n    a <1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1.\n\nThe last rational comparison follows from4445>4386. Hence\n7/5<H<sqrt(2), so both the valid seed (8) and the obstruction apply.\nThe total shifted mass M_m=1+sum H_m is at least2^m, since every one\nof its2^m-1 steps exceeds1. Thus the desired q_m=O(1/M_m) tends to0\nand is excluded by q>1/8 for this cone. This conclusion does not use\nthe unverified C54 scalar comparison as a mathematical premise.\n\nScope. Independent context-dependent edge weights, connections changing\nmore than one bit, arbitrary dense tensor kernels, and independent changes\nto the local finite coefficient arrays are NOT excluded. In particular\nthe full PEP certificate cone is much larger. The scalar obstruction does\nnot prove that the actual critical schedule is bad. The structural defect\nof (6) is precise: the same coefficient totals R,U control both the first\ngradient and the terminal, while the terminal value source requires t>=2.\nIncreasing q or diagonal optimum storage cannot remove that defect at\nthe required vanishing scale.\n\n## 6. A separate exact screen for symmetric bulk multiplier arrays\n\nThis section concerns ANY array with lambda_ij=lambda_ji for all finite\ndistinct i,j, including arbitrary complete bipartite, dyadic hierarchical,\nor Walsh-diagonal nonnegative kernels. Optimum incidence may be arbitrary\nas long as the value flow holds. It is a different restriction from (6).\n\nIdentify R^2 with C, with <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). Set zeta=(1+i)/2 and\ndefine a formal trajectory\n\n    p_0=1, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i zeta)p_i,\n    x_i=p_i, g_i=zeta p_i, f_i=|p_i|^2/4.           (15)\n\nIt obeys the physical GD equations exactly and has ||x_0||=1. Since\nRe(zeta)=|zeta|^2=1/2, direct expansion gives\n\n    Q_(i,star)=Q_(star,i)=0,\n    Q_ij=(1/2)Im(conj(p_i)p_j)=-Q_ji.              (16)\n\nThe individual finite Q need not be nonpositive. These are formal test\nvectors, not asserted to be a legal smooth-convex transcript. On (15),\nthe quadratic form of a symmetric finite array equals\n\n    M(q)=q-|p_n|^2/2.\n\nFull PSD therefore requires q>=|p_n|^2/2, irrespective of every optimum\nedge. For the recursive word with h-levels1+a c^k, define\n\n    T_j=|p_(2^j-1)|^2,\n    d_k=(1+a^2 c^(2k))/2.\n\nThen T_0=1 and T_(j+1)=d_j T_j^2, since the word contains two\nidentical child products and the single bridge. Both undeformed Silver\n(a=1/lambda,c=lambda) and the actual critical word for m>=128 satisfy\na>2/5 and c>12/5. Consequently\n\n    d_0>4/7, d_1>19/20, d_2>3, d_3>15,\n    T_4=d_0^8 d_1^4 d_2^2 d_3\n         >(4/7)^8(19/20)^4*135 >6/5.                (17)\n\nThe four rational inequalities in (17) follow by evaluating\n(1+(2/5)^2(12/5)^(2k))/2 at k=0,1,2,3. For the final comparison,\nthe left rational product reduces to900778752/720600125; its numerator\ntimes5 minus6 times its denominator is180293010>0. For k>=4, d_k>1.\nIt follows inductively that for every j>=4,\n\n    T_j>(6/5)^(2^(j-4)),\n    q>(1/2)(6/5)^(2^(j-4)).                        (18)\n\nThus the symmetric multiplier organization fails even the undeformed\ncalibration, exponentially in the number of leaves. This is not a\nlimitation on Laplacian SLACK matrices produced from directed arrays.\n\nFor a general directed finite array, the same computation yields instead\nthe exact necessary directed-area inequality\n\n    q +(1/2)sum_(i<j)(lambda_ij-lambda_ji)\n                      Im(conj(p_i)p_j) >= T_m/2.    (19)\n\nEquation (19) identifies an actual contribution that is lost on replacing\nan array by its symmetric part. It is necessary only, not sufficient for\nfull PSD or interpolation feasibility.\n\n## 7. Exact tensor contraction for the remaining directed dense family\n\nFor the same recursive word and any complex zeta, let P_j be its scalar\nterminal GD polynomial on H_j and define\n\n    tau_(j+1)=(1-(1+a c^j)zeta)P_j, P_0=1.\n\nThe full sample-position vector p^(j) of length2^j obeys\n\n    p^(0)=(1), p^(j+1)=[p^(j);tau_(j+1)p^(j)],\n    p^(m)=(1,tau_m) tensor ... tensor(1,tau_1).     (20)\n\nThis is proved by applying the identical child GD polynomial after the\nbridge; P_(j+1)=tau_(j+1)P_j is its last entry.\n\nIf a proposed dense finite multiplier kernel (before discarding its\ndiagonal) is K=eta times the tensor product of real nonnegative2 by2\nmatrices B_m,...,B_1, then\n\n    p^* K p=eta product_k [B_k,00+B_k,11|tau_k|^2\n                             +B_k,01 tau_k+B_k,10 conj(tau_k)].       (21)\n\nThis follows by distributing the finite tensor sums. Diagonal entries\ncontribute real numbers and thus do not affect the imaginary part.\nAt zeta=(1+i)/2, (19) is equivalently\n\n    q+(1/2)Im(p^*Kp)>=T_m/2.                       (22)\n\nFor a finite nonnegative sum of such product kernels, sum the expressions\n(21). This is an analytic contraction test for a PRESCRIBED low-complexity\nkernel, not a sufficient PSD invariant. It leaves the genuinely directed\ndense product family open; applying (4) with arbitrary unspecified A and\ncalling its PSD a remaining lemma would merely restate the original PEP\nand is explicitly BLOCKED as a proof route.\n\n## 8. Literature and scope of this round\n\nRead as method context: Bok and Altschuler, \"Accelerating Proximal Gradient\nDescent via Silver Stepsizes\", arXiv:2412.05497, Theorem9, Definition13,\nLemma14 and its proof, with the local PDF and extracted text retained.\nThat work's Laplacian is a slack matrix in auxiliary proximal subgradients,\nnot a symmetric multiplier array. Its exact silver identities do not\nsupply an upper certificate for the critical deformation here. No theorem\nfrom the paper is used as a correctness premise in Sections1--7.\n\nThis package records two distinct scoped obstructions and a directed\ntensor contraction screen. It adds no verified fact and does not resolve\neither the uniform lower-bound target or a strict asymptotic separation.\n\n## 9. Dense tensor kernels and arbitrary nonnegative mixtures: the same barrier\n\nThe coordinate sum in (6) is not essential. Here is a strictly larger,\ngenuinely dense coefficient cone, with independent cross-block pairs\ndiffering in arbitrarily many bits. Its assumptions are explicit; no\narbitrary unknown full matrix is being hidden inside its definition.\n\nAt every bit k choose a two by two matrix\n\n    P_k=[[1-v_k,u_k],[v_k,c_k-u_k]],\n    0<=u_k<=v_k<=1,  u_k<=c_k<=1-v_k+u_k.           (23)\n\nAll entries are nonnegative, all row and column sums are at most1, and\nthe first column sum is exactly1. These properties follow directly:\nthe row sums are1-v_k+u_k and c_k+v_k-u_k; the column sums are1,c_k.\nConversely every nonnegative two by two matrix with these row/column\nproperties has the representation (23).\n\nTake any finite number of products, with factors P_(ell,k) satisfying\n(23), and choose eta_ell>=0 and delta_i>=0. (24)\n\nThus EVERY actual finite multiplier is the concrete nonnegative sum\n\n    lambda_ij=sum_ell eta_ell product_k P_(ell,k)[i_k,j_k], i!=j.   (25)\n\nThe diagonal of P is not an interpolation edge. The definition of A\nsets the star coefficients via (3), rather than treating a diagonal\nas a free nonnegative interpolation constraint. Its row sums b are\nnonnegative because each P_ell has row sums at most1. Every column\nsum of A is nonnegative. The only additional requirement for a>=0 is\n\n    t=sum_ell eta_ell(1-product_k c_(ell,k))+delta_n>=2.          (26)\n\nAt all other indices the subtraction2e_n in (2) is zero. Equations\n(24)--(26) therefore check every actual sign and all value coefficients.\nThe number of mixture terms and every parameter may depend on m; no\nbounded-rank or fixed-library assumption is imposed.\n\nThis cone contains the valid seed of Section3. For any0<H<=sqrt(2),\nput M=1+H, eta=4, and take a single factor\n\n    P_H=[[1/2,1/(2M)],[1/2,1/2-1/(2M)]].           (27)\n\nIt has u=1/(2M),v=c=1/2, satisfies (23), and\n4(I-P_H)=A_H. Equation (8) is its full one-step calibration. More\ngenerally the cone contains all coordinate sums (6), including their\nnonnegative limits: write a local two-node A_k as eta(I-P_k) with eta\nlarge enough to keep both diagonal entries of P_k nonnegative, and\nput identity factors at every other bit. Its row/column bounds follow\nfrom those of A_k. The delta remains unchanged.\n\nWe now prove that every full PSD certificate from (24)--(26), on any\nschedule with h_0>=7/5, still requires q>1/8.\n\nFor one product, temporarily omit the mixture label and put\n\n    D0=product_k(1-v_k), L0=product_k(1-v_k+u_k),\n    Dn=product_k(c_k-u_k), Cn=product_k c_k,\n    Ln=product_k(c_k+v_k-u_k).\n\nThese are respectively the first diagonal, first row sum, last diagonal,\nlast column sum, and last row sum of P. The first column sum is1.\nAll are nonnegative and the row and column sums are at most1.\n\nTwo elementary product inequalities are decisive:\n\n    0<=Ln-Cn<=1-L0,\n    0<=Cn-Dn<=L0-D0.                                (28)\n\nTo prove them, for nonnegative vectors s,d define\nF_d(s)=product_k(s_k+d_k)-product_k s_k. Its expansion is the sum\nover nonempty subsets J of product_(k in J)d_k times\nproduct_(k not in J)s_k. Each coefficient is nonnegative, so F_d\nis coordinatewise nondecreasing in s.\nFor the first inequality of (28), set d_k=v_k-u_k>=0.\nThen c_k<=1-d_k by (23), and\n\n    Ln-Cn=F_d(c)<=F_d(1-d)=1-product_k(1-d_k)=1-L0.\n\nFor the second, set d_k=u_k. Since c_k-u_k<=1-v_k,\n\n    Cn-Dn=F_u(c-u)<=F_u(1-v)=L0-D0.\n\nNow restore the mixture label and define\n\n    B_L=sum_ell eta_ell(1-L0_ell),\n    R_L=sum_ell eta_ell(L0_ell-D0_ell),\n    B_R=sum_ell eta_ell(Ln_ell-Cn_ell),\n    R_R=sum_ell eta_ell(Cn_ell-Dn_ell).\n\nEvery number is nonnegative. By (28),\n\n    B_R<=B_L, R_R<=R_L.                             (29)\n\nThe actual first and last coefficients of the full matrix (4) are\n\n    z=b_0=B_L+delta_0,\n    G_00=z-(h_0-1)R_L,\n    b_n=t-B_R,\n    G_nn=t+R_R-1.                                   (30)\n\nFor the first diagonal, the finite outgoing row sum at0 is R_L and\neach such target j>0 has T_j0=h_0. For the last diagonal use\nA_nn=sum eta(1-Dn)+delta_n=t+R_R and column n of V as before.\nThese prove (30) on the original formal space.\n\nIf0<q<=1/8 and the matrix were PSD, its x_0,g_0 principal submatrix\nwould imply exactly as in (12)\n\n    B_L<=z<=4q<=1/2,\n    R_L<=q/(h_0-1)<=5/16.\n\nEquations (29)--(30) and the x_0,g_n principal submatrix would then\ngive, using t>=2,\n\n    q >=(t-B_R)^2/[4(t+R_R-1)]\n       >(t-1/2)^2/(4t)>=9/32,\n\na contradiction. Levels q<=0 are excluded by the same source/initial-row\nargument in Section4. This proves q>1/8 for the entire dense mixture cone.\n\nThe critical and undeformed applications in Section5 follow unchanged.\nThe obstruction allows all directions of optimum incidence, all actual\ninterior pairs, arbitrarily many mixture terms, and arbitrary diagonal\noptimum storage. It rules out this normalized tensor CONE, not all\nnonnegative tensor-product arrays: a factor violating its first-column\nnormalization or its row/column bounds can leave the cone. Nor does it\ncover a correction with negative mixture weights even if the final edge\narray is nonnegative. The product inequalities (28), not a mere change\nof basis or an assumed Huber kernel, are the mechanism behind the bound.\n\n## 10. General first-query budget and required context variation\n\nFor context and a possible next mutation, the first part of the preceding\ntest is universal. In ANY nonnegative full certificate with h_0=H>1,\nwrite b_0=lambda_(0,star) and r_0=sum_(j=1)^n lambda_0j. The x_0,g_0\nprincipal submatrix is\n\n    [[q,-b_0/2],[-b_0/2,b_0-(H-1)r_0]].             (31)\n\nThis follows directly from the general expansion (4), with no restrictions\non incoming terminal edges, optimum-source edges, or the rest of the array.\nSince q>0, its determinant implies\n\n    b_0<=4q, r_0<=q/(H-1).                          (32)\n\nThe proof is the identity\n(b_0-2q)^2+4q(H-1)r_0<=4q^2. The earlier arbitrary-q queried-cut\ndraft367cf22cf1bd54cc retains more variables and all target-position\nmoments; (31)--(32) are compatible with that larger interface. They are\nrederived here jointly, not imported from an unverified draft.\n\nIf a contextual extension of the binary-coordinate array has first\ntotals B_L,R_L and terminal totals B_R,R_R as in (30), t>=2, and\nobeys B_R<=K B_L and R_R<=K R_L for some K>=1, then the same proof\nshows\n\n    q>1/(8K), provided h_0>=7/5.                    (33)\n\nIndeed q<=1/(8K) would imply B_R<=1/2,R_R<=5/16 and also q<=1/8,\nwhich reproduces the contradiction. Thus a useful q=O(1/M_m) in this\ncontextual extension requires K at least a constant times M_m. A bounded\nchange of context weights cannot repair the cone.
